{"id": "366067681df90ad9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:25:02.263064+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Nonstationary signed-history extension: freely choose every modulation v_i in[1,6/5], set alpha_i=(901/900)^i v_i and b_i=13/10+(23/9)alpha_(i+1)/alpha_i. A full positive-value Moreau relay gives W_(2m)>=(85/121)(901/900)^(2m), although every b_i is in[3,5] and all quadratic errors vanish.", "evidence": "# Robust nonstationary positive-value relay with exponential growth\nStatus: complete explicit construction, UNVERIFIED; no new verifier allocation.\nThis extends the periodic signed-history example by allowing independently varying modulation at every stage and a strictly growing gradient scale. All proof ingredients are included here.\n\n## 1. Schedule class and value\nFix\na=9/10, T=23/10, kappa=1/10, chi=6/5, q_g=901/900.\nChoose any m>=1 and ANY numbers v_i in[1,chi],0<=i<=m, with v_0=1.\nDefine alpha_i=q_g^i v_i and\nb_i=T-1+(T/a)alpha_(i+1)/alpha_i,0<=i<m.\nThe supplied schedule is h=(1,b_0,...,1,b_(m-1)), of length2m.\nEvery b_i belongs to[3,5]; these are variable checkpoints, not a constant schedule.\n\nThen one explicitly defined nonseparable convex1-smooth function in R^(m+1) gives\nW_(2m)(h)>=(85/121) q_g^(2m) v_m^2\n          >=(85/121)(901/900)^(2m).                     (1)\nIts exact closest-minimizer normalization is used. Meanwhile the entire quadratic-class objective is at most\nmax{1/(2 e m),(4/5)^(2m)} ->0.                           (2)\nThus the nonlinear error grows exponentially on a robust nonstationary class with freely chosen v_i.\n\nEquivalently, this is an explicit condition on a supplied checkpoint schedule: the multiplicative partial products\nv_i=product_(j=0)^(i-1) [(9/(23 q_g))(b_j-13/10)]\nmust all lie in[1,6/5]. The empty product v_0=1 is fixed. This condition is sufficient, not asserted for arbitrary schedules.\n\n## 2. One global function and exact residual identity\nLet zeta_0=e_0 and\nzeta_(i+1)=-a zeta_i+sqrt(1-a^2)e_(i+1).\nTheir Gram matrix is real PSD with\n<zeta_i,zeta_j>=(-a)^|i-j|.\nSet omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i and\nJ(z)=max{0,max_i(<omega_i,z>-kappa alpha_i^2)}, f=env_1 J.\nThis is one finite nonnegative convex max-affine function, so its envelope is convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with minimum0. The direct proximal optimality condition proves that if plane i is active at x-omega_i then\ngrad f(x)=omega_i, f(x)=<omega_i,x>-(1/2+kappa)alpha_i^2.\nSubgradient monotonicity gives the1-Lipschitz gradient estimate, ensuring full global legality, not only local interpolation.\n\nStart at X_0=T alpha_0 zeta_0=T zeta_0, and set the intended block starts\nX_(i+1)=X_i-(1+b_i)omega_i.\nLet Z_i=X_i-T omega_i. Define\nB_i=(T/a)alpha_(i+1), D_i=B_i(1-a^2).\nThen\nZ_0=0, Z_(i+1)=Z_i-B_i(zeta_i+a zeta_(i+1)).\nConsequently\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for j>=i,\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=-S_(j,i) for j<i,\nS_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1)D_l(-a)^(l-j).                   (3)\nThis follows by taking inner products of the update: zeta_l+a zeta_(l+1) is orthogonal to every later zeta_j, and its earlier inner products are(1-a^2)(-a)^(l-j).\n\nAt either real query x=X_i-u omega_i,u=0,1, the proposed proximal point is Z_i+d omega_i with d=T-u-1. The terminal query has d=T-1. Put delta=T-2=3/10, so all these d are>=delta.\nThe current plane has value(d-kappa)alpha_i^2>=alpha_i^2/5>0.\nFor an old index j its deficit relative to the current plane is\nd alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)\n +alpha_j S_(j,i)-kappa alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2,        (4)\nwhere C_ij=(-a)^(i-j). Future indices have the same expression with S=0.\n\n## 3. Uniform estimates for the signed history\nPut theta=a q_g=901/1000 and K=(T/a)(1-a^2)=437/900.\nFor L=i-j>0,\nS_(j,i)/q_g^j=K q_g sum_(r=0)^(L-1)(-theta)^r v_(j+r+1).\nDefine the negative constant\nS_floor=K q_g(1-theta chi)/(1-theta^2).\nUsing v in[1,chi] on positive and negative terms gives:\nif L is even,\nS_(j,i)/q_g^j >= S_floor(1-theta^L);                    (5)\nif L is odd,\nS_(j,i)/q_g^j >= S_floor\n +K q_g theta^(L-1)(theta chi-theta^2)/(1-theta^2)\n >=S_floor.                                           (6)\nThus old residuals may be positive; only their exact quantitative cost is bounded.\n\nThe following rational bounds will suffice:\ntheta^2<13/16, theta^2>4/5,\ntheta chi-1<13/160,\nK q_g<39/80,\n|S_floor|<169/800.                                     (7)\nFor the last inequality divide the product of the preceding bounds by\n1-theta^2>3/16:\n(39/80)(13/160)/(3/16)=169/800.\nAll numbers here are exact finite rational inequalities.\n\nEvery coefficient of d in(4), including future comparisons, is positive.\nFor even separation in a past comparison,\nC_ij alpha_j/alpha_i=(a/q_g)^L v_j/v_i<=a^2 chi<1.\nFor a future even separation the ratio is\ntheta^L v_j/v_i<=theta^2 chi<39/40<1.\nOdd correlations are negative. Therefore checking d=delta suffices.\n\nFuture planes pass for arbitrary amplitude ratios. At even separation their deficit at d=delta is\n(delta-kappa)alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2-delta C_ij alpha_i alpha_j.\nThis quadratic form is positive definite because\ndelta^2 C_ij^2<=delta^2 a^4=59049/1000000\n <4kappa(delta-kappa)=80000/1000000.\nAt odd separation every term has a nonnegative sign.\n\nFor past comparisons normalize(4) by q_g^(2j). Set\nz=q_g^L v_i>=v_i>=1, y=v_j in[1,chi].\nIf L is odd, (6) and the positive cross term give the lower bound\n(delta-kappa)z^2+kappa y^2+S_floor y\n >=3/10-(169/800)(6/5)=93/2000>0.                       (8)\n\nIf L is even, the part\n(delta-kappa)z^2-delta a^L z y\nincreases with z>=1, since its derivative is at least\n2/5-(3/10)a^2 chi>0.\nReplace z by v_i; then replace a^L by the larger theta^L to lower the expression further. Using(5), the result is\nF(v_i,y,w)=(delta-kappa)v_i^2+kappa y^2\n -delta w v_i y+S_floor(1-w)y,\nwhere w=theta^L lies in[0,theta^2].\nThis is affine in w, so only endpoints need checking.\nAt w=0, the estimate in(8) applies.\nAt w=theta^2, F increases with v_i on[1,chi]:\n2(delta-kappa)v_i-delta theta^2 y\n >=2/5-(3/10)(13/16)(6/5)>0.\nIt decreases with y on that interval, since\n2kappa y-delta theta^2 v_i+S_floor(1-theta^2)\n <6/25-(3/10)(4/5)=0.\nIts minimum is therefore at v_i=1,y=chi. By(7),\nF(1,chi,theta^2)\n>43/125-(3/10)(13/16)(6/5)-(39/80)(13/160)(6/5)\n=127/32000>0.                                          (9)\nThis proves every old-plane comparison at every lag, including those with S<0.\n\nThe zero, current, past and future checks establish all prescribed gradients. In particular at each checkpoint\nJ(X_i-2omega_i)=alpha_i^2/5>0,\nso the new mechanism carries a positive proximal value rather than resetting to the minimum.\n\n## 4. Exact minimizer distance and objective\nThe minimizer set equals\nP_min={z:<omega_i,z><=kappa alpha_i^2 for all i}.\nBecause alpha_0=1 and alpha_i>=1, y_proj=kappa zeta_0 belongs to P_min: odd correlations are negative; even ones are at most1. Constraint0 is active and\nX_0-y_proj=(T-kappa)omega_0.\nThis is a nonnegative active-normal certificate, so y_proj is the exact closest minimizer. The squared initial distance is\n(T-kappa)^2=(11/5)^2=121/25.\nThe exact terminal value is\n2f(X_m)=2(T-kappa-1/2)alpha_m^2=(17/5)alpha_m^2.\nDividing gives(1).\n\nThe gradients omega_0,...,omega_m are independent with adjacent nonzero correlations, and their squared norms alpha_i^2 vary. Independence allows each zero/plane hinge to be isolated; its envelope Hessian is the rank-one projector onto omega_i. Adjacent projectors do not commute, proving nonseparability in every fixed orthonormal basis.\n\n## 5. Actual negative history and the quadratic comparison\nThis family strictly escapes the old sign restriction. Choose any eligible consecutive modulation pattern\nv_j=chi,v_(j+1)=1,v_(j+2)=chi.\nThen\nS_(j,j+2)=K q_g^(j+1)(1-theta chi)<0.\nNevertheless(8)--(9) certify the full old plane. The construction therefore does not merely hide a nonpositive-residual assumption behind new notation.\n\nThe checkpoints obey\n13/10+(23/9)q_g(5/6)<=b_i<=13/10+(23/9)q_g(6/5),\nan interval contained in[3,5]. For the quadratic multiplier\n(1-lambda)(1-b_i lambda),\non0<=lambda<=1/5 it is nonnegative and<=exp(-lambda); on1/5<=lambda<=1 its absolute value is<=4/5 by the endpoints and its single quadratic vertex. Thus spectral diagonalization gives(2) for every finite-dimensional convex quadratic.\n\nThis is a rigorous candidate lower bound for the displayed nonstationary class, not the exact target theorem. The bounded-modulation/partial-product condition is explicitly retained; no claim says arbitrary schedules satisfy it or can be partitioned into such blocks without losing the target constant.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_growing_signed_history_relay.md", "extends": "8cce883b9f93f556", "mechanism": "positive checkpoint values, variable gradient energies, actual positive old residuals", "status": "complete nonstationary construction, unverified", "verification_allocation": "unchanged", "scope": "explicit bounded multiplicative partial-product class; no all-schedule comparison"}, "glossary": {"q_g": "Fixed geometric gradient-amplitude growth factor901/900.", "v_i": "Free modulation factor in[1,6/5], with v_0=1; unrelated to earlier complex interpolation values.", "theta": "The effective signed-sum ratio a q_g=901/1000.", "S_floor": "Explicit negative bound K q_g(1-theta chi)/(1-theta^2) for normalized old history sums.", "K": "The rational coefficient(T/a)(1-a^2)=437/900 in this finding, not the earlier single-cut functional."}}
{"id": "8a77ec65ac2eeb52", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:27:26.915025+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Requested interval/descent compatibility screen: exact star-compensated dictionary yields M_new=M_old+Deltaq||x0||^2+K(step change)-H(C,h_new). The interval diagonalization leaves the full mixed cut terms intact; an inhomogeneous elimination is available, but signs and inactive-interpolation costs remain.", "evidence": "# Compatibility screen: interval matrices and full joint descent\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED algebraic screen requested by master_guidance31b8d52469caa452.\nIt uses max4's cd0f93f08a50828f as a lead, not as a verified building block.\nAll identities needed below are derived explicitly. No numerical or verifier\nallocation is used.\n\n## 1. Exact dictionary, including the star edges\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Use\nQ_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.\nA full certificate has lambda>=0, finite flow out-in=2 e_n, and\nM(q,lambda,h)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij>=0.\n\nLet C be an (n+1)-square real matrix with C1=0; its off-diagonal\nentries may have either sign. Put b=C^T1. Define T(h) by T_ii=1,\nT_ij=h_j when j<i, and T_ij=0 when j>i, and set\nH(C,h)=CT(h)+T(h)^T C^T.\n\nThe exact sign dictionary is\n\n2 sum_(i!=j) C_ij Q_ij\n  =-2 sum_i b_i f_i - sum_(i,j)H_ij<g_i,g_j>\n    +sum_i b_i||g_i||^2.                             (1)\n\nFor verification, the function terms are -2b^Tf because row sums\nvanish. The position term is 2sum C_ij<g_i,x_j-x_i>\n=2sum C_ij<g_i,x_j>, since C1=0. Substituting\nx_j=x_0+g_j-(Tg)_j and again using C1=0 gives\n2sum C_ij<g_i,g_j>-2sum(CT)_ij<g_i,g_j>.\nThe squared-gradient difference contributes\nb^T(||g_i||^2)-2sum C_ij<g_i,g_j>. Combining proves (1).\n\nThus the following simultaneous finite/star multiplier change preserves\nall flow equations and keeps mu_i=lambda_(i,star) fixed:\n\nlambda'_ij=lambda_ij+2C_ij   (finite distinct i,j),\nnu'_i=nu_i-2b_i,             nu_i=lambda_(star,i).     (2)\n\nIndeed finite out-in changes by -2b_i, which is canceled by the\nchange -2b_i in incoming star flow. Also sum b_i=0.\n\nAt a FIXED schedule, the exact complete matrix change is simply\n\nM(q,lambda',h)-M(q,lambda,h)=-H(C,h),                  (3)\n\nembedded in the gradient block, with the x_0 row unchanged. The\nfunction terms and b_i||g_i||^2 terms in (1) cancel against\nsum(-2b_i)Q_(star,i). This is why the star adjustment must not be\nomitted.\n\nThe ACTUAL sign constraints are\n\nlambda_ij+2C_ij>=0  (i!=j),   nu_i-2b_i>=0.           (4)\n\nThey are neither positivity of interval coordinates nor positivity of\nevery C entry. Negative C entries are allowed exactly to the extent\nthat the old finite multipliers supply them.\n\n## 2. Simultaneously changing the schedule\n\nLet h'=h+a>=0. Define the full cut vector\n\nz_t(lambda)=sum_(i,j)lambda_ij\n (1_(t<i)-1_(t<j))g_i,\n\nwhere either indicator is zero at the star. Define the quadratic form\n\nK(a;lambda)=sum_(t<n)a_t<g_t,z_t(lambda)>.\n\nThen (1)--(3), now applied at h', give the exact identity\n\nM(q',lambda',h') =\nM(q,lambda,h)+(q'-q)||x_0||^2+K(a;lambda)-H(C,h').    (5)\n\nThis includes all remote forward-cut terms. For i<j, the matrix entries\nof K in the gradient block are\n\n2K_ij=a_i[mu_j+sum_(l<=i)lambda_(j,l)]\n       -a_j sum_(l>j)lambda_(i,l),\nK_ii=-a_i sum_(j>i)lambda_(i,j).                     (6)\n\nThe expression in (6) follows directly by collecting the two cut\ncontributions to <g_i,g_j>.\n\nConsequently a diagonal H(C,h') does NOT diagonalize the full\njoint perturbation: K still has the mixed entries (6). The exact\nzero-mixed-entry target is H_ij=K_ij for i!=j, not H_ij=0.\n\nFor example, if every finite forward edge is zero, (6) becomes\n2K_ij=a_i c_(i,j), where\nc_(i,j)=mu_j+sum_(l<=i)lambda_(j,l).\nSumming flow over the suffix shows sum_(j>i)c_(i,j)\n=2+sum_(j>i)nu_j>0. Thus if H is diagonal AND K-H is\nrequired to have no mixed entries, each a_i must be zero.\nThis is a precise incompatibility of those two simultaneous\nrequirements. It does not rule out allowing a non-diagonal PSD\nremainder in (5).\n\n## 3. Inactive-constraint costs survive this coordinate change\n\nSuppose the old full certificate matches one legal stationary primal:\n||x_0||=1, 2f_n=q, M vanishes on its data, lambda_ij Q_ij=0, and\n<g_t,z_t(lambda)>=0. Put sigma_ij=-Q_ij>=0 on these SAME data.\n\nIf (4) holds, evaluating the fixed-h change (3) on the old data gives\n\nsum_(i,j)H(C,h)_ij<g_i,g_j>\n =sum_(i,j)lambda'_ij sigma_ij>=0.                  (7)\n\nThe sum on the right includes the star edges. At the new schedule,\nPSD of (5) necessarily gives\n\nq'-q >= sum_(i,j)H(C,h')_ij<g_i,g_j>.                (8)\n\nWriting DeltaT=T(h')-T(h), the right side is exactly the nonnegative\ncost in (7) plus\n\n2 sum_(i,j)(C DeltaT)_ij<g_i,g_j>.\n\nThus a descent q'<q must pay every inactive-interpolation cost through\nthis bilinear term. Diagonalization does not erase the earlier\njoint-descent obstruction.\n\n## 4. The triangular elimination can be made inhomogeneous\n\nAssume h'_i>1 for i<n. Specify the strict upper entries U_ij=C_ij.\nFor j>i define L_(j,i)=sum_(l<=i)C_(j,l), L_(j,-1)=0.\nThe desired off-diagonal matrix H has the exact equation\n\n(h'_i-1)L_(j,i)+L_(j,i-1)\n =U_ij+h'_j sum_(l>j)U_(i,l)-H_ij.                 (9)\n\nAt j=n the displayed sum is empty and the auxiliary h'_n is irrelevant.\nIndeed (CT)_ij=U_ij+h'_j sum_(l>j)U_(i,l), while\n(CT)_ji=-(h'_i-1)L_(j,i)-L_(j,i-1), proving (9).\n\nTaking H_ij=K_ij makes (9) a scalar elimination for the mixed\ncancellation in (5). After it, one STILL has to check every sign in\n(4), all diagonal entries or the full remaining PSD matrix, and\nstrict output improvement. Nothing in the elimination proves those\nconditions automatically.\n\n## 5. Compatibility with the actual new deformation\n\nThe independently derived candidate8c998d29efd3da99 uses\nC_(j,k)=eta p_j/2 for j>k and C_(j,j)=-eta p_j/2, with all other\nentries zero. It has b_k=eta/2 and b_j=-eta p_j/2.\nThese are exactly the star changes in that candidate.\n\nIts H(C,h') is generally NOT diagonal. The specific suffix step\nrescalings make its mixed entries equal to K_ij, and its remaining\ndiagonal difference is eta diag(p). This is a concrete solution of\n(5), whereas max4's homogeneous H_ij=0 modes do not by themselves\nsupply that cancellation.\n\nConclusion of the bounded screen: the interval coordinates give a\nuseful exact dictionary and an inhomogeneous elimination, but the\npublished zero-remainder interval subclass is not a ready-made repair\nfor the new deformation's cut-profile mismatch. It retains the same\naggregate-sign and inactive-cost obligations. No all-schedule descent,\nHuber saturation, or exact-profile lower bound follows.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_interval_descent_compatibility.md", "responds_to": "31b8d52469caa452", "finding_ids": ["cd0f93f08a50828f", "8c998d29efd3da99", "1dc29ea19aa3894a"], "status": "bounded algebraic screen complete; no generic descent claimed", "scope": "Does not assert necessity of zero PSD remainder, triangular flow, or interval-coordinate positivity"}, "glossary": {"C": "Real finite-index matrix with zero row sums, encoding changes of finite interpolation multipliers.", "b": "Column-sum vector C^T1; star-outgoing multipliers change by -2b.", "T": "Lower-triangular trajectory matrix T_ii=1 and T_ij=h_j for j<i.", "H": "H(C,h)=CT(h)+T(h)^TC^T, acting on gradient coordinates.", "K": "Full step-perturbation form sum_t a_t<g_t,z_t(lambda)>.", "U": "Strict upper triangle of C in the inhomogeneous elimination.", "L": "L_(j,i)=sum_(l<=i)C_(j,l) only in Section4; smoothness is normalized to1."}}
{"id": "cd577f86c5896600", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:29:11.871514+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "The full two-step suffix has an exact joint descent allowing adjacent forward multipliers. More generally, any positive star-outgoing weight at n-2 in a full stationary tuple requires a quantified remote forward flow from an earlier, negatively correlated gradient; at n=2 such a star weight is impossible.", "evidence": "# Two-step suffix: a full joint move and the remote-flow obstruction\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED. All statements are conditional on a complete certificate\nor on a complete matching stationary primal, as explicitly specified below.\nThis is a hostile test and extension of J1, not a target theorem.\n\n## 1. Full notation\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let h>=0 and q>0.\nSet Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\nA full certificate means lambda_ij>=0, out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n),\nand M=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij PSD after the GD\nsubstitution. Write mu_j=lambda_(j,star), nu_j=lambda_(star,j).\n\nLet n>=2, k=n-2, a=h_k, b=h_(k+1). Define\n\nc_1=mu_(k+1)+sum_(l<=k)lambda_(k+1,l),\nc_2=mu_n+sum_(l<=k)lambda_(n,l),\nc_3=c_2+lambda_(n,k+1)>0,\nr_01=lambda_(k,k+1), r_02=lambda_(k,n), r_12=lambda_(k+1,n).\n\nThe last assertion follows from terminal flow:\nc_3=2+nu_n+sum_(l<n)lambda_(l,n).\n\nFor the explicit deformation in Section2 only, assume\n\nlambda_(i,j)=0 whenever 0<=i<k<j<=n.                 (R)\n\nThus no forward edge from an EARLIER prefix vertex crosses into the\ntwo final gradient vertices. All three forward edges within\n{k,k+1,n} remain allowed. The condition is vacuous at n=2.\n\n## 2. Explicit finite deformation allowing local forward edges\n\nAssume (R), a>1, and nu_k>0. First suppose c_2>0. Choose v>0 and define\n\nu=(v/c_2)[c_1 - b r_02(c_2+v)/((a-1)(c_3+v))].       (1)\n\nRequire u>=0 and u+v<=nu_k. Change\n\nnu'_k=nu_k-u-v,   nu'_(k+1)=nu_(k+1)+u,\nnu'_n=nu_n+v,\nlambda'_(k+1,k)=lambda_(k+1,k)+u,\nlambda'_(n,k)=lambda_(n,k)+v,                         (2)\n\nwith all other multipliers unchanged, and set\n\na'=(a c_2+v)/(c_2+v),   b'=b c_3/(c_3+v).            (3)\n\nKeep all earlier steps fixed. All new coefficients are nonnegative,\nflow is preserved, a'>1, and 0<=b'<=b.\n\nThe complete matrix identity is\n\nM(q,lambda',h')-M(q,lambda,h)\n =(a-a')(r_01+r_02)||g_k||^2\n  +[u+(b-b')r_12]||g_(k+1)||^2+v||g_n||^2.           (4)\n\nEvery coefficient is nonnegative and v>0.\n\nHere are all mixed-entry checks. For the pair (k,n), the coefficient\nchange is (a'-a)c_2+v(a'-1)=0. For (k+1,n), it is\n(b'-b)c_3+b'v=0. For (k,k+1), it is\n(a'-a)c_1+u(a'-1)-(b'-b)r_02=0 by (1).\nEvery pair involving an earlier prefix index is unchanged by (R):\na change of a or b could only couple to such an index through one\nof the forward edges excluded by (R). The only changed outgoing\nfinite masses are +u at k+1 and +v at n; the diagonal dependence\non the changed steps contributes the remaining terms in (4).\nThe x_0 row is unchanged because every mu_j is unchanged.\n\nThe condition in (1) is explicit. In particular, if\n\n(a-1)c_1 c_3 > b r_02 c_2,                            (5)\n\nthen all sufficiently small v>0 have u>=0, and they also satisfy\nu+v<=nu_k. If r_02=0, (1) reduces to u=v c_1/c_2>=0\nwithout needing a strict inequality in (5). Thus adjacent forward\nedges do not obstruct this move; the skip edge k->n is different.\n\nIf c_2=0, then c_1>0 by the cut flow. Choose 0<u<=nu_k,\ntake v=0, and instead set\n\na'=(a c_1+u)/(c_1+u),   b'=b.\n\nThe same changes (2) give\nM_new-M_old=(a-a')(r_01+r_02)||g_k||^2+u||g_(k+1)||^2>=0.\nNo division by c_2 is used in this case.\n\nThese identities imply an actual strict worst-case improvement,\nnot just feasibility at level q. For c_2>0 let\nB=product_(t<n)(1+h'_t). On every new legal trajectory with\nR=||x_0||>0 and E=2f_n/R^2, one has ||x_n||<=BR and\nf_n<=||g_n||||x_n||. Therefore (4) implies\n\nq-E>=v E^2/(4B^2),\n\nhence\nW_n(h')<=2q/[1+sqrt(1+v q/B^2)]<q.                   (6)\n\nFor c_2=0 use ||g_n||<=(1+b')||g_(k+1)|| to obtain the\nsame conclusion with v replaced by u/(1+b')^2.\nThese elementary bounds use the full old PSD matrix evaluated on\nthe new formal vectors. They do not pretend that the old matching\nprimal follows the new schedule.\n\n## 3. A stronger necessary condition at a full stationary tuple\n\nNow DROP (R). Suppose the certificate matches a SINGLE legal primal\ntranscript with ||x_0||=1, 2f_n=q>0, M zero on its data, and\nlambda_ij Q_ij=0. Assume positive last steps and all cut stationarity\nequalities <g_t,z_t(lambda)>=0, where\n\nz_t(lambda)=sum_(j>t)(mu_j+sum_(l<=t)lambda_(j,l))g_j\n            -sum_(i<=t)(sum_(j>t)lambda_(i,j))g_i.\n\nFirst, <g_(n-1),g_n>=0. This follows from the complete system,\nwithout differentiability of W: vary only the last step by s in\nboth signs on the same legal function. Its new final gradient\nchanges by O(|s|), and f_n(s)=f_n-s<g_(n-1),g_n>+O(s^2).\nThe old PSD form has value O(s^2) on the changed formal vectors\nbecause it annihilates the old data. The first-order change of the\nmatrix itself has zero pairing by the last cut equality, so its\nnew value is also O(s^2). Since all new Q_ij<=0 and the same lambda\nis nonnegative, the certificate identity implies\n2f_n(s)<=q+O(s^2). Taking s in both signs gives the orthogonality.\n\nSuppose nu_k>0. Complementarity gives\n\nf_k=||g_k||^2/2.\n\nThe descent inequality then shows y=x_k-g_k is a minimizer. If\n0<a<1, Lipschitz continuity of the gradient at the two endpoints\ny and x_k forces g_(k+1)=(1-a)g_k: the two Lipschitz inequalities\nhave lengths summing to ||g_k||, so equality in the triangle\ninequality fixes the vector. At a=1 the next point is a minimizer.\nFor 0<a<1, the last orthogonality makes g_n perpendicular to g_k,\nwhile x_n-y=(1-a)(1-b)g_k; cocoercivity against y then forces\ng_n=0. Both cases contradict q>0. Therefore\n\na>1.                                                 (7)\n\nPut d=||g_k||^2, A=||g_(k+1)||^2, Bn=||g_n||^2,\nu0=<g_k,g_(k+1)>, v0=<g_k,g_n>, and\nF_i=lambda_(i,k+1)+lambda_(i,n) for i<=k.\nAll d,A,Bn are positive when q>0.\n\nInterpolation Q_(k+1,k)<=0 and Q_(n,k)<=0, using f_k=d/2\nand last-gradient orthogonality, gives\n\n-(a-1)u0>=f_(k+1)+A/2,\n-(a-1)v0>=f_n+Bn/2.                                  (8)\n\nThe kth cut stationarity equality is\n\nc_1 u0+c_2 v0-F_k d-sum_(i<k)F_i<g_k,g_i>=0.\n\nCombining with (8) yields the quantitative REMOTE-flow obligation\n\n-sum_(i<k)F_i<g_k,g_i>\n >=F_k d+\n   [c_1(f_(k+1)+A/2)+c_2(f_n+Bn/2)]/(a-1)\n >0.                                                  (9)\n\nFor strict positivity, summing the cut flow gives\nc_1+c_2=2+nu_(k+1)+nu_n+sum_(i<=k)F_i>0,\nand f_(k+1)+A/2 and f_n+Bn/2 are strictly positive.\n\nThus some earlier index i<k must satisfy BOTH F_i>0 and\n<g_i,g_k><0. If there is no such remote forward edge, nu_k\ncannot be positive at a full stationary tuple. In particular:\n\n- At n=2, the sum in (9) is empty, so every full positive-step\n  stationary tuple with q>0 has nu_0=0. No triangular-multiplier\n  hypothesis is needed.\n- At arbitrary n, a positive nu_(n-2) requires a genuine earlier\n  forward edge into the last two samples and an oppositely directed\n  earlier gradient. Local forward edges alone cannot support it.\n- This gives the exact failure point for extending a suffix-only\n  multiplier argument: the missing term is the signed remote sum\n  in (9), not an unspecified PSD or regularity issue.\n\nThe conclusion does not eliminate terminal nu_n, does not force\nq=1/(2sum h+1), and does not resolve the exact benchmark profile.\nIt only narrows the full-feasible obstruction.\n\nAll elementary inequalities used above follow from convexity and\n1-smoothness. In particular, f(x-gnabla f(x))<=f(x)-||gnabla f(x)||^2/2;\nand <grad f(x),x-y>>=||grad f(x)||^2 at any minimizer y follows by\nadding the two smooth-convex interpolation inequalities with y.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_two_step_remote_flow.md", "plan_id": "max2_J1_J2", "related_findings": ["8c998d29efd3da99", "8a77ec65ac2eeb52", "1dc29ea19aa3894a"], "status": "complete conditional perturbation and structural obstruction; unverified", "scope": "No global Huber tightness claim; remote earlier edges remain and terminal star flow is not excluded", "verification_allocation": "unchanged"}, "glossary": {"k": "The index n-2, preceding the final two updates.", "a": "h_(n-2), the first step in the tested suffix.", "b": "h_(n-1), the final step.", "c_1": "mu_(n-1)+sum_(l<=n-2)lambda_(n-1,l).", "c_2": "mu_n+sum_(l<=n-2)lambda_(n,l).", "c_3": "mu_n+sum_(l<n)lambda_(n,l).", "u": "The nonnegative amount transported from star->k to star->k+1, as defined in Section2.", "v": "The positive amount transported from star->k to star->n in Section2.", "F_i": "lambda_(i,n-1)+lambda_(i,n), the forward mass from prefix index i into the two terminal samples.", "Bn": "Squared norm of the last gradient in Section3, not the benchmark denominator B_n."}}
{"id": "116539ec76b9ab76", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:29:50.095657+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Sharp repeated-block boundedness threshold: for arbitrary ordinary steps in[0,1] of total s followed by b, each block is Fejer when b<=s+2; a Huber cycle persists at equality; above it, a growing-dimensional Markov Moreau construction gives W>=((2s+1)/(s+1)^2)((b-1)/(s+1))^m.", "evidence": "# Exact boundedness threshold for small-step blocks followed by one long step\nStatus: complete candidate, UNVERIFIED. No additional verifier call.\nThis is a scoped matching upper/lower classification, not the unrestricted exact-profile target.\n\n## 1. Statement\nLet A=(a_1,...,a_l) be a finite list with0<=a_j<=1 and total s. Repeat the block(A,b) with fixed b>=0.\nFor every finite-dimensional convex differentiable1-smooth function with a minimizer, one complete block never increases the distance to that minimizer whenever\nb<=s+2.                                                 (1)\nConsequently W_(m(l+1))((A,b)^m)<=1 for every m>=1 in this range.\n\nAt b=s+2, a single fixed symmetric Huber objective gives, at every block endpoint, normalized error\n(2s+1)/(s+1)^2>0.                                        (2)\nFor every b>s+2 and every m>=1, an explicit nonseparable convex1-smooth function in R^(m+1) realizes\nW_(m(l+1))((A,b)^m)\n >=[(2s+1)/(s+1)^2][(b-1)/(s+1)]^m,                     (3)\nwhich grows exponentially.\nThe lower constructions only require the ordinary steps to be nonnegative with total s; the upper implication(1) additionally requires each a_j<=1. The distinction is retained.\n\n## 2. Self-contained inequalities for the upper bound\nNormalize the chosen minimizer to0 and minimum to0.\nThe descent estimate follows by integrating the1-Lipschitz gradient along a segment. Applied to the convex function f(z)-<grad f(y),z>, which is minimized at y, it gives\nf(x)>=f(y)+<grad f(y),x-y>+||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2/2.    (4)\nAdding its two orientations gives cocoercivity.\nAgainst a minimizer, (4) yields\n<x,g(x)>>=f(x)+||g(x)||^2/2, f(x)>=||g(x)||^2/2.          (5)\n\nConsider a small step x_next=x-alpha g with0<alpha<=1 and let v=g(x_next). Cocoercivity implies\nalpha<g-v,g>>=||g-v||^2,\nand hence <g-v,v>>=(1/alpha-1)||g-v||^2>=0.\nThus <g,v>>=||v||^2 and ||g||>=||v||. Formula(4) also gives\nf(x)-f(x_next)>=alpha||v||^2.\nA zero step has the same properties directly.\n\nLet x_t be the ordinary-block points, y its endpoint, and k=g(y). Put P_t equal to the sum of ordinary steps preceding x_t. Summing the preceding estimates gives\n||g(x_t)||>=||k||,\nf(x_t)>=f(y)+(s-P_t)||k||^2.                             (6)\nFor one ordinary step of size alpha, (5) gives\n||x_next||^2-||x||^2\n<=-2alpha f(x)+alpha(alpha-1)||g(x)||^2.\nBecause alpha(alpha-1)<=0, (6) may be substituted into both terms. Summing all ordinary steps and using\n2 sum_t a_t(s-P_t)=s^2+sum_t a_t^2\nyields\n||y||^2-||x_0||^2<=-2s f(y)-s(s+1)||k||^2.              (7)\nFor the long step z=y-bk, (5) gives\n||z||^2-||y||^2<=-2b f(y)+b(b-1)||k||^2.\nTogether with(7), this is the precise block inequality\n||z||^2-||x_0||^2\n<= -2(s+b)[f(y)-||k||^2/2]\n   +(b-s-2)(b+s)||k||^2.                                (8)\nBoth terms are nonpositive under(1), proving the distance assertion. Applying it at every block keeps the endpoint distance bounded by its initial value. Integrating the gradient from a minimizer also gives f(z)<=||z||^2/2, so the normalized endpoint objective is at most1.\n\nNo last-iterate convergence rate below the threshold is asserted here; (8) proves boundedness and its exact boundary.\n\n## 3. Boundary witness\nLet tau=1/(s+1) and\nf(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=tau,\nf(x)=tau|x|-tau^2/2 for |x|>=tau.\nIts derivative clips x to[-tau,tau], so it is convex differentiable1-smooth.\nStart at x_0=1. Through an ordinary block the derivative is constantly tau and the points decrease from1 to tau, because the cumulative ordinary sum lies in[0,s]. The step b=s+2 then sends tau to\ntau-(s+2)tau=-1.\nOn the next block the signs reverse. Thus block endpoints alternate between1 and-1 for all repetitions. Their normalized objective is\n2tau-tau^2=(2s+1)/(s+1)^2,\nestablishing(2) on one fixed scalar function.\n\n## 4. Exponential high-dimensional witness above the boundary\nAssume b>s+2 and put\nT=s+1, q=sqrt((b-1)/(s+1))>1, a=1/q in(0,1).\nConstruct independent Markov directions in R^(m+1):\nzeta_0=e_0,\nzeta_(i+1)=-a zeta_i+sqrt(1-a^2)e_(i+1).\nLet\nalpha_i=q^i/T, omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i,\nJ(z)=max{0,max_(0<=i<=m)<omega_i,z>}, f=env_1 J.\nStart at X_0=T omega_0=e_0 and define\nX_(i+1)=X_i-(s+b)omega_i.\nWe verify that every query in ordinary block i and its checkpoint has gradient omega_i, and that the terminal gradient is omega_m.\n\nSet Z_i=X_i-T omega_i. A direct update gives\nZ_0=0,\nZ_(i+1)=Z_i-B_i(zeta_i+a zeta_(i+1)),\nB_i=(b-1)alpha_i=Tq^2 alpha_i.\nWrite D_i=B_i(1-a^2)=T(q^2-1)alpha_i.\nThen D_(i+1)=q D_i, so D_i=aD_(i+1).\nTaking Markov inner products shows\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for j>=i,\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=-sum_(l=j)^(i-1)D_l(-a)^(l-j)<=0 for j<i.\nFor the last inequality, consecutive terms cancel exactly because aD_(l+1)=D_l; an odd-length sum has a positive final singleton. Thus every old residual is nonpositive, and this fact has been proved for all lags.\n\nAt a query after ordinary cumulative sum u in[0,s], the proposed proximal point is\nX_i-(u+1)omega_i=Z_i+d omega_i,\nwhere d=T-u-1=s-u>=0.\nThe current plane has value d alpha_i^2>=0.\nFor a future plane j>i,\n<omega_j,omega_i>/alpha_i^2=(-a)^(j-i)alpha_j/alpha_i=(-1)^(j-i),\nso its value is at most the current value, with equality at even separation.\nFor a past plane j<i, its residual term is nonpositive and\n<omega_j,omega_i>/alpha_i^2=(-1)^(i-j)q^(-2(i-j))<=1;\nagain it is at most the current value. Thus ALL planes, and the zero plane, are dominated. The active-plane subgradient condition gives grad f=omega_i, and the block update is exact.\n\nAt the terminal point X_m the same check has d=T-1=s>=0, giving\nf(X_m)=(T-1/2)alpha_m^2.\nThe Moreau envelope is globally convex differentiable1-smooth by the proximal optimality and monotonicity argument. Its minimum is0 on the cone where every normal has nonpositive inner product. The origin is the closest minimizer to X_0: X_0=T omega_0 is a positive multiple of an active normal at0, so for any minimizing z,\n<X_0,z><=0 and ||X_0-z||^2>=||X_0||^2=1.\nConsequently\n2f(X_m)=(2T-1)q^(2m)/T^2,\nwhich is exactly(3).\n\nThe omega_i are independent, with nonzero adjacent inner products. Isolated hinges against the zero plane have noncommuting rank-one Hessian projectors, so this is nonseparable in any fixed orthonormal basis and uses genuinely growing dimension. The ordinary gradients are constant on each block, but the energies grow geometrically from block to block. This is not a sequence of independently selected Hessians.\n\n## 5. Relation to the central target\nThe threshold b=s+2 is exact for boundedness of the repeated prescribed block with ordinary steps in[0,1]. It is not an exact formula for W at a finite horizon, and it does not cover arbitrary long-step chronology. In particular it cannot substitute for a uniform lower bound matching U_n^ZJ. It supplies a sharp structural test for one class, with matching global upper and high-dimensional lower arguments.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_small_block_threshold.md", "status": "complete matching restricted-class upper/lower proof, unverified", "verification_allocation": "unchanged", "scope": "boundedness of repeated small-step blocks; not arbitrary schedules or exact minimax profile"}, "glossary": {"A": "Fixed list of ordinary steps, each in[0,1].", "s": "Total ordinary step mass in A; not a benchmark profile.", "T": "The virtual budget s+1 in the explicit Markov construction.", "q": "The amplitude growth factor sqrt((b-1)/(s+1)) for this construction.", "a": "Magnitude1/q of its negative Markov correlation.", "k": "Gradient at the end of the ordinary block in the upper proof."}}
{"id": "776c3d8ba2d52ebc", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:38:07.443597+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Corrected two-sided residual join now recovers the ordinary ConPP certificate exactly. It keeps both blocks' gradient-aggregate residuals and uses four aggregate interpolation identities; an auxiliary endpoint coefficient may be negative only where a positive aggregate coefficient offsets it on the same actual interpolation edge. The resulting explicit five-variable quadratic test is sufficient, but no competitive finite-residual recursion is yet found.", "evidence": "# Two-sided residual join with aggregate interpolation cancellation\n\nStatus: unverified exact certificate identity and successful recovery of the ordinary ConPP join on its structural subspace. No improved recursive family has been found. This continues the failed aggregate-only rule4be2737d7e8764a6 without silently reusing its insufficient positivity restrictions.\n\nFor a GD block and nonnegative proof weights u, write D=sum u_i, d=x_start-x_end, U=sum u_i g_i, e=U-d, and\n\n  E=2sum_i u_i(f_i-f_end)+sum_i u_i||g_i||^2\n      +2sum_i u_i<g_i,x_start-x_i>-||U||^2.\n\nThen E>=0 implies the objective guarantee W<=1/D by the squared-distance identity reproduced in research/max4_nonlocal_tail_transport.md. All formulas below use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0, at smoothness1.\n\n## 1. Two arbitrary blocks and the exact new state\n\nLet block a end at y with gradient g. A bridge of physical length tau>=1 moves to y'=y-tau g, and block b starts there and ends at z with gradient q. Let the blocks have states(D_a,e_a,E_a) and(D_b,e_b,E_b); denote the tail gradient aggregate by U_b and its displacement by d_b, so e_b=U_b-d_b. The tail's proof-weight vector is v and its terminal entry is v_end. Assume certificates\n\n  E_a>=nu_a||e_a-K_a g||^2,\n  E_b>=nu_b||e_b-K_b q||^2,\n\nwhere the nu coefficients are nonnegative. Exact structural relations such as e_a=K_a g may instead be retained as relations, without replacing them by a finite quadratic penalty.\n\nForm new proof weights by keeping the prefix weights, adding eta to the prefix terminal entry, scaling all tail weights by beta, and adding gamma to the final entry. Require\n\n  beta>=0,\n  prefix_terminal_weight+eta>=0,\n  beta*v_end+gamma>=0,\n  D_c=D_a+eta+beta D_b+gamma>0.\n\nThe new residual is exactly\n\n  e_c=e_a+(eta-tau)g+(beta-1)U_b+e_b+gamma q.         (1)\n\nThe change in energy, before eliminating the function difference Delta=f_y-f_z, is\n\n  E_c=E_a+beta E_b+2(D_a+eta)Delta+R_0,\n\nwhere\n\n  R_0=eta(1-eta)||g||^2-2eta<e_a,g>\n      +beta(1-beta)||U_b||^2\n      +2beta<U_b,(tau-eta)g-e_a>\n      +gamma(1-gamma)||q||^2\n      -2gamma<q,e_a+(eta-tau)g+(beta-1)U_b+e_b>.      (2)\n\nEquation(2) follows by expanding the definition of E under the displayed changes of proof weights; it involves no smoothness inequality yet.\n\n## 2. Four aggregate interpolation identities\n\nDefine\n\n  A_y=-2sum_(i in tail) v_i Q_(i,y),\n  A_z=-2sum_(i in tail) v_i Q_(i,z),\n  P=-2Q_(z,y), N=-2Q_(y,z).\n\nSubstitution of y-x_i=tau g+(y'-x_i) and d_b=U_b-e_b gives\n\n  A_y=2D_b Delta-E_b-||U_b||^2\n         -2(tau-1)<U_b,g>-D_b||g||^2,\n\n  A_z=-E_b+||U_b||^2-2<U_b,e_b-q>-D_b||q||^2,\n\n  P=2Delta-||g||^2-||q||^2+2(1-tau)<g,q>\n          -2<q,U_b>+2<q,e_b>,\n\n  N=-2Delta+(2tau-1)||g||^2-||q||^2\n           +2<g,U_b>-2<g,e_b>+2<g,q>.              (3)\n\nChoose scalars c,m,t_y,t_z satisfying\n\n  c>=0, m>=0, t_z>=0, t_y+c*v_end>=0,\n  c D_b+t_y-t_z=D_a+eta.                            (4)\n\nAlthough t_y can be negative, the actual interpolation multipliers in c A_y+m A_z+t_y P+t_z N are all nonnegative. At the edge(z,y), A_y already contributes terminal coefficient c*v_end, so the total coefficient is c*v_end+t_y. At each other tail-to-y edge the coefficient is c*v_i; at tail-to-z edges it is m*v_i; and the edge(y,z) has coefficient t_z. Self-edges vanish. These are the signs that matter, rather than the signs of all four aggregate coordinates separately.\n\nThe function-coefficient equality in(4) eliminates Delta from(2).\n\n## 3. A finite-dimensional sufficient certificate test\n\nDefine the following quadratic form in(e_a,e_b,g,U_b,q):\n\n  R=R_0\n    +c[||U_b||^2+2(tau-1)<U_b,g>+D_b||g||^2]\n    +m[-||U_b||^2+2<U_b,e_b-q>+D_b||q||^2]\n    +t_y[||g||^2+||q||^2+2(tau-1)<g,q>\n                       +2<q,U_b>-2<q,e_b>]\n    +t_z[-(2tau-1)||g||^2+||q||^2-2<g,U_b>\n                       +2<g,e_b>-2<g,q>].          (5)\n\nEquations(2)–(4) give the exact identity\n\n  E_c=E_a+(beta+c+m)E_b+R\n                  +c A_y+m A_z+t_y P+t_z N.        (6)\n\nConsequently, for any nu_c>=0 and real K_c, it suffices that the quadratic form\n\n  R+nu_a||e_a-K_a g||^2\n      +(beta+c+m)nu_b||e_b-K_b q||^2\n      -nu_c||e_a+(eta-tau)g+(beta-1)U_b\n                          +e_b+(gamma-K_c)q||^2    (7)\n\nbe positive semidefinite. Any exact structural relations from the input blocks may first be substituted into(7); imposing positivity only on that known subspace is then legitimate. For free residual variables, positivity means the full symmetric5-by5 coefficient matrix is PSD, in every Euclidean dimension. This implies E_c>=nu_c||e_c-K_c q||^2 and W<=1/D_c.\n\nThis is a certificate construction, not a new algorithm with auxiliary state: all u,v and residuals are proof quantities. The actual algorithm is precisely the predetermined concatenation[a,tau,b].\n\n## 4. Exact ordinary-ConPP calibration\n\nTake genuinely primitive input blocks of capacities K=1+sum(a) and A=1+sum(b), with their standard proof weights(a,K) and(b,A). Then\n\n  D_a=2K-1, D_b=2A-1,\n  e_a=K g, e_b=Aq,\n  E_a=V_a>=0, E_b=V_b>=0.\n\nLet r>0 be the unique root\n\n  r^2+(A+K)r-AK=0,\n\nso the usual bridge is tau=1+r. Since r<A, set\n\n  J=K+r,\n  beta=1, eta=tau-K, gamma=J,\n  K_c=K+A+r,\n  c=m=J/(A-r),\n  t_y=c(1-A), t_z=c r.                             (8)\n\nAll actual weight conditions hold: the new bridge weight is K+eta=tau; the final weight is A+gamma=K_c. The interpolation conditions(4) also hold, because\n\n  t_y+c*v_end=c(1-A)+c A=c>0,\n  c D_b+t_y-t_z=c(A-r)=J=D_a+eta.\n\nAfter substitution of e_a=Kg and e_b=Aq, the quadratic remainder(5) vanishes identically. One can check this by the equivalent two scalar identities\n\n  r(K+A+r)=KA,\n  K+r=2Ar/(A-r).\n\nIndeed the resulting energy identity is exactly\n\n  V_c=V_a+(1+2c)V_b\n          -2c sum_(i in tail, i!=end) h_i(Q_(i,y)+Q_(i,z))\n          -2c Q_(z,y)-2cr Q_(y,z),                 (9)\n\nwhere V_c is the saturated primitive energy for the combined physical sum K+A+r-1. Every multiplier on -Q in(9) is nonnegative. Thus the rule recovers the successful ordinary ConPP certificate on its actual structural identities, with capacity F(K,A)=K+A+r.\n\nThe negative aggregate coefficient t_y in(8) is essential when A>1, even though the final multiplier on its interpolation edge is positive. Earlier screens that required every aggregate coefficient nonnegative excluded this calibration. This is the same distinction exposed by the signed interval-coordinate test cd0f93f08a50828f.\n\n## Remaining task\n\nThe calibration does not prove closure for arbitrary finite residual penalties. At the exact primitive point e_a=Kg and e_b=Aq are identities, not estimates with a finite nu. A candidate involving nonprimitive blocks must either retain its actual additional boundary relations or satisfy the full matrix(7). No parameters giving a competitive recursive nonprimitive family have been found, and no claim of an improved denominator is made. The endpoint-only weight template is separately refuted by12ea2963a6e53e67; it cannot be substituted for this full internal-weight accounting.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"research_path": "research/max4_two_sided_residual_join.md", "plan_id": "max4_nonlocal_tail_residual_composition", "attempt_type": "direct", "status": "exact interface and structural ConPP calibration; nonprimitive accelerated closure remains open", "related_findings": ["4be2737d7e8764a6", "cd0f93f08a50828f", "12ea2963a6e53e67"], "verification_status": "unverified; no new verifier or numerical calls", "key_stuck_points": ["Ordinary primitive calibration uses exact residual identities, so it does not establish finite-residual closure.", "At each genuine nonprimitive join independent residual penalties can lose strength; cross-block correlation or additional boundary relations may be needed."]}, "glossary": {"E_a,E_b": "Intrinsic objective energies of the two blocks, defined in the evidence.", "e_a,e_b": "Proof-weight gradient aggregates minus physical displacements for the respective blocks.", "U_b": "Tail proof-weight gradient aggregate.", "A_y,A_z": "Nonnegative interpolation sums from every tail iterate to the bridge anchor y and terminal anchor z.", "P,N": "The endpoint interpolation quantities -2Q_(z,y), -2Q_(y,z).", "c,m,t_y,t_z": "Aggregate interpolation coefficients; t_y may be negative only if t_y+c*v_end>=0.", "R": "Explicit quadratic remainder in five vector variables(e_a,e_b,g,U_b,q)."}}
{"id": "6192a365859c116f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:41:39.640574+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Actual terminal robustness from remote planes: for every even m>=32, append any nonnegative final stepsize t to the positive-value periodic relay. One fixed legal function gives W_(2m+1)>=(245/19008)^2>1/10000 uniformly in t; the two-plane cancellation proof retains the exact closest-minimizer distance.", "evidence": "# Positive-value periodic Markov relay with signed history\nStatus: complete explicit construction, UNVERIFIED. No new verifier call.\nThis tests the specific repair suggested after the minimum-reset and residual-sign obstructions: allow positive proximal function values, and check actual past-plane deficits even when the signed residual is positive.\n\n## 1. A parameterized model\nFix a in(0,1), chi>=1, T>=2, and kappa in[0,T-2]. Assume\nT>=2a chi,                                               (1)\n(T-2-kappa)-(T-kappa)chi^2+T chi/a>=0.                     (2)\nFor any number m>=1 of blocks, in R^(m+1) set\nzeta_0=e_0,\nzeta_(i+1)=-a zeta_i+sqrt(1-a^2)e_(i+1).\nDefine alpha_i=chi for even i and alpha_i=1 for odd i, omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i. Thus\n<zeta_i,zeta_j>=(-a)^|i-j|\nand all omega_i are independent.\n\nEach real block consists of one unit step followed by\nb_i=T-1+(T/a)alpha_(i+1)/alpha_i.\nTake the ONE finite max-affine function\nJ(z)=max{0,max_(0<=i<=m)(<omega_i,z>-kappa alpha_i^2)}\nand f=env_1 J. Start at X_0=T alpha_0 zeta_0.\nWe prove that block i queries X_i and X_i-omega_i with gradient omega_i at both, and that\nX_(i+1)=X_i-(1+b_i)omega_i.\nThe terminal point is X_m with gradient omega_m.\n\nThe function f is legal globally: J is finite nonnegative convex with J(0)=0, and its envelope is finite convex differentiable1-smooth. For precision, the proximal minimization has a unique minimizer by the quadratic term; its subgradient equation implies grad f(x)=x-prox_J(x), and monotonicity gives\n||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>.\nAt any point where plane i is active at x-omega_i, that subgradient equation yields the claimed gradient and value\nf(x)=<omega_i,x>-alpha_i^2/2-kappa alpha_i^2.\nThus it remains to compare every affine plane at the indicated proximal points.\n\n## 2. Exact signed residuals\nLet Z_i=X_i-T omega_i and put\nB_i=(T/a)alpha_(i+1), D_i=B_i(1-a^2).\nThe update gives\nZ_0=0,\nZ_(i+1)=Z_i-B_i(zeta_i+a zeta_(i+1)).\nDirect multiplication of Markov correlations proves\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for j>=i,\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=-S_(j,i) for j<i,\nwhere\nS_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1)D_l(-a)^(l-j).                     (3)\nIndeed zeta_l+a zeta_(l+1) is orthogonal to all zeta_j with j>=l+1, and its inner product with earlier zeta_j is(1-a^2)(-a)^(l-j). This proves(3) by summing the update; no sign of S is assumed.\n\nAt a real query x=X_i-u omega_i with u=0 or1, its proposed proximal point is\nZ_i+d omega_i, d=T-u-1>=T-2.\nThe terminal query has d=T-1 and obeys the same lower bound.\nThe current affine plane has value(d-kappa)alpha_i^2>=0.\nThe current-minus-j-th plane difference is\nd alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)\n +alpha_j S_(j,i)-kappa alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2          (4)\nfor j<i, with C_ij=(-a)^(i-j); for j>i the same formula has S=0.\nEvery coefficient of d is positive: at even separation the amplitudes agree and C<1, while at odd separation C<0. Hence it suffices to check d=T-2.\n\nFuture planes are already controlled. At even separation their offsets agree and their deficit is d alpha_i^2(1-C)>=0. At odd separation the deficit is\n(d-kappa)alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2+d a^|i-j| alpha_i alpha_j>=0.\nThe substantive point is the full past history.\n\n## 3. All past planes, including positive residuals\nFor even separation i-j=2q, q>=1, let z=alpha_j=alpha_i and y be the opposite amplitude. Formula(3) is the exact geometric sum\nS_(j,i)=(T/a)(y-a z)(1-a^(2q)).\nSubstituting into(4) gives, at d=T-2,\n(1-a^(2q)) z[-2z+(T/a)y]>=0\nby(1), since z/y is either chi or1/chi.\n\nFor odd separation i-j=2q+1, q>=0, let z=alpha_j and y=alpha_i be the opposite amplitude. Now\nS_(j,i)=(T/a)[y-a z+a^(2q)(a z-a^2 y)].\nSubstitution into(4), still at d=T-2, gives\nF_infty(y,z)+a^(2q)[T z^2-2a yz],                       (5)\nwhere\nF_infty(y,z)=(T-2-kappa)y^2-(T-kappa)z^2+(T/a)yz.\nThe second term in(5) is nonnegative by(1).\nAmong the two choices(y,z)=(1,chi),(chi,1), the first has smaller F_infty: the difference is\n2(T-1-kappa)(chi^2-1)>=0.\nIts nonnegativity is precisely(2). This proves every old-plane comparison, at every lag, with the signed residual retained. Together with the future and zero-plane checks it proves the entire trajectory in Section1.\n\nThe exact sampled values are\nf(X_i-u omega_i)=(T-u-1/2-kappa)alpha_i^2.\nAt a checkpoint query u=1, the nonsmooth proximal value is\nJ(X_i-2omega_i)=(T-2-kappa)alpha_i^2.                    (6)\nIt is strictly positive whenever kappa<T-2. Thus these checkpoints are not minimum resets.\n\n## 4. Closest minimizer and payoff\nThe minimizer set of f is exactly\nP_min={z:<omega_i,z><=kappa alpha_i^2 for every i}.\nThe point y_proj=kappa alpha_0 zeta_0 lies in P_min: for even i the amplitude equals alpha_0 and the correlation is at most1; for odd i the correlation is negative. Constraint0 is active, and\nX_0-y_proj=(T-kappa)omega_0\nis a positive multiple of its normal. For any z in P_min this gives\n<X_0-y_proj,z-y_proj><=0,\nso squared-distance expansion proves that y_proj is the unique closest minimizer and\ndist(X_0,P_min)^2=(T-kappa)^2 alpha_0^2.\nThe normalized terminal value of this one instance is therefore exactly\n2(T-kappa-1/2)alpha_m^2 / [(T-kappa)^2 alpha_0^2].        (7)\nFor even m, alpha_m=alpha_0 and this is\n[2(T-kappa)-1]/(T-kappa)^2.\nAll smooth-convex interpolation inequalities, including the optimum sample, hold because all samples have been checked on this one explicit envelope.\n\n## 5. Rational parameters, quadratic stability, and a strict failure of the old test\nChoose\na=9/10, chi=6/5, T=23/10, kappa=1/10.\nCondition(1) has positive margin\nT-2a chi=7/50.\nThe left side of(2) is exactly37/375>0.\nThe alternating checkpoint sizes are\nb_even=463/135, b_odd=131/30,\nboth in[3,5]. Therefore for m=2r and every integer r>=1 the length4r schedule\n(1,463/135,1,131/30) repeated r times\nhas the explicitly realized normalized error\nW_(4r)(h)>=85/121.                                     (8)\nAt every checkpoint its proximal value is alpha_i^2/5>0.\n\nThis model violates the earlier nonpositive-residual criterion. For any even j with j+2<=m,\nS_(j,j+2)=(T/a)(1-a^2)(1-a chi)<0\nbecause a chi=27/25>1. Thus\n<Z_(j+2),zeta_j> >0,\nand D_j>=a D_(j+1) is false at every such pair. Nevertheless the exact plane comparisons above hold. This is an explicit repair, not an assumption that local correlation checks suffice.\n\nFor comparison, each quadratic block multiplier is(1-lambda)(1-b_i lambda).\nFor0<=lambda<=1/5 it is nonnegative and at most exp(-lambda).\nFor1/5<=lambda<=1, its positive value is at most8/25, since b_i>=3; the magnitude of its negative minimum is at most4/5, since b_i<=5 and max_(b<=5,b>=3)(b-1)^2/(4b)=4/5.\nHence the full finite-dimensional quadratic normalized objective after m blocks is at most\nmax{1/(2 e m),(4/5)^(2m)} ->0.\nThe nonlinear persistent value is not a large quadratic product.\n\nThe omega_i are independent, adjacent ones have negative inner product, and their nonnegative-offset max-affine system has isolated zero/plane hinges. At such hinges the envelope has rank-one Hessian projectors onto omega_i. Adjacent projectors do not commute, proving that this function is nonseparable in every fixed orthonormal basis. Independence permits exposing each hinge by solving for one affine value0 and all others strictly negative. The dimension m+1 grows with the horizon and the gradient energies alternate between36/25 and1.\n\n## 6. Scope\nThis is a parameterized, fully signed-history construction with positive checkpoint values and a concrete rational periodic test outside the earlier residual-sign interface. It does not claim that(1)--(2), or a generalization of them, can realize an arbitrary schedule at the benchmark profile. The endpoint values of the exact all-past comparison now show where positive residuals can be paid for by real face margins and offsets. A future extension must solve those inequalities quantitatively for nonperiodic data, rather than imposing S>=0 again or assuming feasibility at target strength.\n\n## 7. Uniform survival of one arbitrary final step\nFor the rational parameters in Section5, let m be even and m>=32. Append ANY prescribed finite nonnegative step t after the2m-step periodic prefix. The same one fixed function above gives\nW_(2m+1)(h,t) >= (245/19008)^2 >1/10000,                 (9)\nuniformly in t and m.\nThis uses actual old planes and does not assume a monotone terminal objective.\n\nWrite L_j(z)=<omega_j,z>-kappa alpha_j^2 for the affine pieces of J and set c_tail=a^m. Since m is even, alpha_m=alpha_0=6/5. At the endpoint X_m, the exact residual sums from Section3 give\nL_0(X_m)=38/375+(46/15)c_tail,\nL_(m-1)(X_m)=-19/6.\nThe directional slopes along omega_m are\n<omega_0,omega_m>=(36/25)c_tail,\n<omega_(m-1),omega_m>=-27/25.\nFor clarity, the first value follows from\nS_(0,m)=-(46/225)(1-c_tail),\n<Z_m,omega_0>=(92/375)(1-c_tail),\n<omega_m,omega_0>=(36/25)c_tail,\nand X_m=Z_m+T omega_m. The second follows directly from the preceding block update.\n\nDefine\nlambda_tail=3/(3+4c_tail),\nw_tail=lambda_tail omega_0+(1-lambda_tail)omega_(m-1),\neta_tail=lambda_tail kappa alpha_0^2+(1-lambda_tail)kappa alpha_(m-1)^2.\nThe affine combination L_tail=lambda_tail L_0+(1-lambda_tail)L_(m-1) has zero slope along omega_m, since\nlambda_tail(36/25)c_tail-(1-lambda_tail)(27/25)=0.\nThus, for EVERY real t,\nL_tail(X_m-t omega_m)\n=[38/125-(52/15)c_tail]/(3+4c_tail).                     (10)\n\nFor m>=32, c_tail<=1/20. Indeed a^8<9/20 and(9/20)^4<1/20. Consequently the numerator in(10) is at least49/375 and its denominator is at most16/5, so\nL_tail(X_m-t omega_m)>=49/1200=:d_tail.\nMoreover convexity of the squared norm gives\n||w_tail||^2<=lambda_tail alpha_0^2+(1-lambda_tail)alpha_(m-1)^2<=36/25=:C_tail.\n\nHere is the envelope lower bound without an external formula. Since J is at least both0 and L_tail, it is at least theta_tail L_tail for any theta_tail in[0,1]. Completing the square in its Moreau minimization gives, at x=X_m-t omega_m,\nf(x)>=theta_tail L_tail(x)-theta_tail^2||w_tail||^2/2.\nTake theta_tail=d_tail/C_tail=49/1728<1. Then\n2f(x)>=d_tail^2/C_tail.\nThe initial squared distance to the exact nearest minimizer, from Section4, is4356/625. Division yields\n2f(x)/(4356/625)\n >=[(49/1200)/( (6/5)(66/25) )]^2\n =(245/19008)^2>1/10000.\nBecause grad f(X_m)=omega_m was proved above, x=X_m-t omega_m is exactly the result of the appended GD step. This proves(9), including t=0 and arbitrarily large finite t.\n\nThe construction is therefore robust to any one-step terminal repair of this periodic prefix. A general arbitrary-length suffix is not covered by this argument: after the first appended step its gradient can change, so the cancellation of one fixed direction cannot be reused without a new proof.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_periodic_arbitrary_final_step.md", "extends": "8cce883b9f93f556", "status": "complete extension with full original construction included; unverified", "verification_allocation": "unchanged", "scope": "one arbitrary final step; no arbitrary-length suffix claim"}, "glossary": {"c_tail": "The positive remote Markov correlation a^m for even m.", "lambda_tail": "The convex-combination weight3/(3+4c_tail) cancelling the terminal gradient direction.", "w_tail": "The combined normal lambda_tail omega_0+(1-lambda_tail)omega_(m-1).", "L_tail": "The corresponding affine lower plane of J, constant along the appended-step line.", "d_tail": "Uniform positive affine value49/1200.", "C_tail": "Upper bound36/25 for the squared norm of w_tail."}}
{"id": "b750ce63e2821fa8", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:58:57.145921+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Concrete joint boundary storage is available for the primitive pair(3/2,3/2): its energy V controls the displacement error sharply by V>=||d-3q||^2/9, and it also controls the dual slack H through V>=(4/5)H+||d-3q||^2/36. Both are exact nonnegative interpolation identities with an explicit inherited 3x3 storage matrix. The existing join now also recovers ConPD exactly, hence the full finite-horizon F/G benchmark, but no improving recursion is established.", "evidence": "# Exact retained storage from a two-step primitive block\n\nStatus: unverified research identities. No new verifier or numerical PEP call has been used. These formulas do not yet certify an asymptotic improvement.\n\nAll functions below are convex and differentiable with 1-Lipschitz gradient. Write\n\n    Q_ij = f_i-f_j + <g_i,x_j-x_i> + ||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0.\n\n## 1. Primitive dual residual, with the actual interpolation coefficients\n\nFor a GD block of nonnegative physical steps h, let S=sum h_i, A=S+1, d=x_0-x_n, p=g_0 and q=g_n. Define its saturated primitive energy\n\n    V = 2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)\n        + sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2 - S A ||q||^2.\n\nThe identity below is algebraic for every block; the primitive assumption is V>=0. Put\n\n    H = 2(f_0-f_n)-2S||q||^2-||p-(d+q)/A||^2,\n    A_start = -2[sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,0)+Q_(n,0)],\n    A_end   = -2 sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,n).\n\nThen\n\n    H = (2A-1)/A^2 V + A_start/A + (A-1)/A^2 A_end.       (1)\n\nIn particular H>=0 for an actual primitive block. This follows without importing a quadratic-product condition or identifying the ambient primitive and s-composable classes.\n\nFor a direct check, put Z=d+q and use proof weights (h,1). Their intrinsic energy is V+A(A-1)||q||^2. Expanding the two interpolation sums gives\n\n    A_start = 2A(f_0-f_n)-V-A(A-1)||q||^2\n              -||Z||^2+2<Z,p>-A||p||^2,\n    A_end = -V+||Z||^2-A^2||q||^2.\n\nSubstituting proves (1). This is the exact identity obtained before the optimum interpolation is added in Grimmer--Shu--Wang, arXiv:2410.16249, Appendix A.1, Lemma17. That proof was read, including its definitions and Proposition6 input; the calculation here checks applicability to the present V definition.\n\n## 2. The pair (3/2,3/2) has a sharp nonzero boundary remainder\n\nFor this specific two-step block let p=g_0, m=g_1, q=g_2. Its displacement is d=(3/2)(p+m), and S=3, A=4. Define the retained displacement error\n\n    t=d-3q=(3/2)(p+m-2q).\n\nThe exact certificate is\n\n    V = 4(-Q_10)+9(-Q_12)+16(-Q_21)+(-Q_02)\n        + ||t||^2/9.                                (2)\n\nAll coefficients are nonnegative. Expanding each Q verifies the function coefficients (3,3,-6), gradient diagonal coefficients (-3/4,-3/4,-12), and zero off-diagonal coefficients after adding the displayed square. Thus\n\n    V >= ||d-3q||^2/9.                               (3)\n\nThe coefficient 1/9 is sharp over all legal functions. Take the one-dimensional function with derivative g(x)=x for x<=1 and g(x)=1 for x>=1, normalized by f(0)=0. It is convex, 1-smooth, and minimized at zero. Starting at x_0=5/2 gives x_1=1, x_2=-1/2, gradients (1,1,-1/2), and values (2,1/2,1/8). Therefore V=9/4 and t=9/2, giving V=||t||^2/9.\n\nThis is a new retained term, not merely a second naming of the objective residual. It vanishes on both the flat-gradient and full-curvature equality trajectories, but need not vanish on a general trajectory.\n\n## 3. Objective and dual slacks are correlated\n\nFor the same pair, set r=p-(d+q)/4 and H=2(f_0-f_2)-6||q||^2-||r||^2. Equation (1) becomes\n\n    H = (7/16)V + (3/4)(-Q_10)+(1/2)(-Q_20)\n                    +(9/16)(-Q_02)+(9/16)(-Q_12).   (4)\n\nThere is also the exact reverse-side combination\n\n    V-(4/5)H\n      = (2/5)(-Q_01)+(9/5)(-Q_10)+(26/5)(-Q_12)\n          +(48/5)(-Q_21)+||t||^2/36.                (5)\n\nThus this actual block supplies both H>=0 and\n\n    V >= (4/5)H + ||t||^2/36.                        (6)\n\nUnlike separate assumed squared penalties, (5) preserves a concrete correlation between the objective energy and the dual slack. Every actual interpolation multiplier is displayed; no negative interpolation inequality is used.\n\nEquivalently, with Delta=f_0-f_2 and boundary vector (p,d,q),\n\n    V-(8/5)Delta >= (p,d,q)^T J (p,d,q),\n\n    J = [ -4/5    1/5     1/5  ]\n        [  1/5   -1/45   -2/15 ]\n        [  1/5   -2/15   -23/5 ].                   (7)\n\nHere the matrix is an inherited storage term and is not asserted PSD. Discarding it or resetting its displacement coordinate at a later join would be invalid. The same clipped-quadratic example in part2 attains (5) at equality: H=135/64, V=9/4 and t^2/36=9/16.\n\nFor checking (2) and (5), the following one-parameter coefficient identity is useful. For any real kappa,\n\n    V = (1-4kappa)(-Q_01)+(1/2+2kappa)(-Q_02)\n        +(7/2+2kappa)(-Q_10)+(17/2+2kappa)(-Q_12)\n        +(1-4kappa)(-Q_20)+(14+8kappa)(-Q_21)\n        + kappa ||p+m-2q||^2.                       (8)\n\nEquation (2) uses kappa=1/4. Combining (8) with (4), using kappa=5/52 in the factor (13/20)V, proves (5). This was checked by a fixed nine-equation rational coefficient calculation, not by an optimization or numerical search.\n\n## 4. Calibration also recovers the full dominant recursion\n\nThe existing exact join in max4_two_sided_residual_join.md recovers ConPP. Its ordinary ConPD specialization does not require an independent tail residual penalty.\n\nLet the prefix be genuinely primitive with capacity K, energy V_a and weights (a,K), so D_a=2K-1 and e_a=K g. Let the tail have arbitrary nonnegative intrinsic proof weights v, mass D_b>0 and energy E_b>=0. Define\n\n    beta=(1+sqrt(1+8K/D_b))/2,\n    c=2K/D_b=beta^2-beta,\n    tau=1+K/beta.\n\nKeep the prefix physical weights, change its terminal weight from K to K+1, and scale every tail weight by beta. Its new mass is\n\n    D_new=2K+beta D_b=beta^2 D_b=G(K,D_b).\n\nFor A_y=-2 sum_(i in tail) v_i Q_(i,y), where y is the bridge start, direct expansion gives\n\n    E_new=V_a+beta^2 E_b+c A_y.                       (9)\n\nIndeed the general join has eta=1, gamma=0, m=t_y=t_z=0; its quadratic remainder has coefficients -2K+cD_b=0 on g^2, beta(1-beta)+c=0 on U_b^2, and beta(tau-1-K)+c(tau-1)=0 on <g,U_b>. All actual weights and interpolation coefficients are nonnegative. The standard squared-distance identity therefore gives W<=1/D_new.\n\nConsequently the exact boundary interface recovers both scalar F and G operations and hence every finite-horizon DP denominator B_n, not merely a dyadic exponent or a rate with a fixed asymptotic loss. Equations (2)--(7) are extra block information; their profitable propagation is the outstanding construction task.\n\n## 5. Terminal-storage literature: a precise finite reduction\n\nScherer, arXiv:2207.01363, Theorem5.1 in the HTML numbering (Theorem11 in the main-agent PDF pointer), equations30--35 and AppendixA.5, reduces its finite terminal-cost expression to a doubly hyperdominant matrix M: off-diagonal entries are nonpositive and all row and column sums are nonnegative. Its filter is initialized at zero. The proof and the conjecture in Section7 were read; no completeness conjecture or nonzero-initial-state extension is used here.\n\nThe connection to the project's actual interpolation cone can be checked directly. For samples including a minimizer, translate x_star=0 and f_star=0 and set\n\n    z_i=x_i-g_i, F_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nThen Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,z_j-z_i>. For a doubly hyperdominant M on the non-optimum samples define weights\n\n    a_ij=-M_ij (i!=j),\n    a_i,star=sum_j M_ij,\n    a_star,i=sum_j M_ji.\n\nAll weights are nonnegative. Their incoming and outgoing masses agree at every vertex, including the optimum. Thus the F coefficients cancel, and exactly\n\n    sum_(i,j) a_ij(-Q_ij)=sum_(i,j) M_ij<g_i,z_j>.   (10)\n\nThe finite hyperdominant terminal form therefore gives an actual combination of the existing smooth-convex interpolation inequalities. It is a potentially useful parametrization of retained correlations, not an additional nonlinear premise. Equation (10) says nothing about completeness of the five-variable join or of any finite memory compression.\n\nAt a split, a one-step memory example already shows what must be retained:\n\n    sum_(i=m)^n <g_i,z_i-z_(i-1)> -(F_n-F_(m-1))\n      =sum_(i=m)^n (-Q_(i,i-1)) >=0.                (11)\n\nBoth z_(m-1) and F_(m-1) are inherited. Replacing them by zero at an internal join changes the identity. For this project, the concrete new candidate storage is (7), with the displacement and both endpoint gradients retained, rather than a bare appeal to a stability theorem.\n\n## Current boundary\n\nThere is still no closed improving family. The next direct attempt is to transport the actual pair storage through more than one bridge while permitting an indefinite intermediate storage matrix, and to require a valid nonnegative final certificate in the original objective normalization. All intermediate identities in this note remain unverified; they must be supplied in full in any eventual verification package.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_joint_objective_dual_storage", "research_path": "research/max4_joint_boundary_storage.md", "related_findings": ["776c3d8ba2d52ebc", "b4017ddb6896138e"], "verification_status": "unverified; no new verifier or solver calls", "next_action": "Transport the actual pair storage through multiple bridges; keep the matrix indefinite only at intermediate joins and close a full objective certificate."}, "glossary": {"t": "For the pair(3/2,3/2), the displacement error d-3q, with d=x_0-x_2 and q=g_2.", "H": "Primitive dual slack 2(f_0-f_n)-2(A-1)||q||^2-||p-(d+q)/A||^2, A=1+sum h.", "J": "The explicit inherited quadratic storage matrix in equation7 of the evidence, acting on the start gradient, block displacement, and terminal gradient."}}
{"id": "975866a657152425", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:59:39.978265+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "The exact remote-kernel counterexample also has a continuous full-certificate repair retaining its remote edges: for 1<=r<=5/4, h=(2,1+r,3) has explicit nonnegative multipliers and full PSD slack proving W=4r^2. Starting at the same bad sparse-transport certificate, simultaneous quadratic/cubic flow reweighting removes the extra kernel and gives strict descent.", "evidence": "# Complete-certificate obstruction to the unchanged remote-edge extension\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED exact counterexample. This refutes one specified extension\nof the suffix transport, not the existence of some descent and not Huber\ntightness at global minimizers. It directly addresses TASK.md's unrestricted\ntwo-step-suffix test.\n\n## 1. A complete tight certificate\n\nTake n=3, L=1, h=(2,9/4,3), q=25/4, and zeta=sqrt(78)/5.\nUse Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2, with\nx_star=g_star=f_star=0. All unspecified multipliers below are zero:\n\nmu_0=lambda_(0,star)=25/2+5sqrt(78)/8,\nmu_1=lambda_(1,star)=5sqrt(78)/8,\nnu_0=lambda_(star,0)=5sqrt(78)/4+1,\nnu_1=lambda_(star,1)=9/2,\nnu_2=lambda_(star,2)=5,\n\nlambda_10=25/2-5sqrt(78)/8,\nlambda_21=8, lambda_32=2,\nlambda_02=5, lambda_20=4.                            (1)\n\nAll are nonnegative; lambda_10>0 because sqrt(78)<9.\nThe finite flow equations can be checked explicitly:\nat0, out=in=35/2+5sqrt(78)/8;\nat1, out=in=25/2;\nat2, out=in=12;\nat3, out-in=2.\n\nFor independent formal vectors (x_0,g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3), define\n\nR0=x_0-g_0,\nU=g_0+g_1,\nV=g_2+(5/4)g_1,\nZ=g_3+2g_2.\n\nThe FULL matrix has the exact sum-of-squares representation\n\nM=q||x_0||^2-2f_3+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n =(25/4)||R0-(sqrt(78)/20)U||^2\n   +(1/32)||U-16V||^2+||Z||^2.                     (2)\n\nHere GD substitution and the displayed flows cancel every f coefficient.\nThus this is a full PSD certificate, with no omitted prefix or remote entry.\n\nA short derivation of (2) also checks every coefficient. At the first step2,\nthe multiplier set\nmu_0=2+zeta/2, mu_1=zeta/2, nu_0=zeta,\nlambda_10=2-zeta/2\ngives the identity\n||x_0||^2-2f_1+sum lambda Q\n =||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2.\nMultiply it by25/4. For a step of length1+r, r>=1, direct expansion gives\n\n2r^2 f_i-2f_(i+1)+2Q_(i+1,i)+2(r^2-1)Q_(star,i)\n =||r g_i+g_(i+1)||^2.\n\nAdd four times this identity with i=1,r=5/4 and once with i=2,r=2.\nThis gives a base certificate with the same q and the same multipliers\nas (1), except lambda_02=lambda_20=0, nu_0=5sqrt(78)/4,\nand nu_2=6. Its slack is\n\nM_base=(25/4)[||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2]\n        +4||V||^2+||Z||^2.                         (3)\n\nFinally add the exact zero-flow multiplier change\n5Q_02+4Q_20+Q_(star,0)-Q_(star,2).\nIts quadratic form is\n-5||U||^2-<U,V>+4||V||^2.\nCombining with (3), and using\n(25/4)(1-zeta^2/16)=161/32, gives (2).\n\nThe certificate is TIGHT on the globally legal one-dimensional function\nf(x)=x^2/2, with x_0=1. Its GD data are\n\n(x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)=(1,-1,5/4,-5/2),\n(g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(1,-1,5/4,-5/2),\n(f_0,f_1,f_2,f_3)=(1/2,1/2,25/32,25/8).\n\nEvery Q_ij, including star pairs, is zero on these data, (2) vanishes,\nand 2f_3=q. Thus W_3(h)=25/4. This is a complete legal matching\nprimal, not merely a PSD principal block.\n\nThe selected star multiplier is nu_(n-2)=nu_1=9/2>0.\nThere is a genuine remote prefix-to-suffix edge lambda_02=5.\n\n## 2. The precise extension being refuted\n\nApply the previously valid c_2=0 two-step transport to k=1 while\nleaving the remote edge unchanged. In this certificate,\n\nc_1=mu_2+lambda_20+lambda_21=12,\nc_2=mu_3+lambda_30+lambda_31=0,\nc_3=lambda_32=2.\n\nFor any 0<eta<=9/2, the proposed extension changes\n\nh'_1=(27+eta)/(12+eta),    h'_0=2, h'_2=3,\nlambda'_21=8+eta,\nnu'_1=9/2-eta, nu'_2=5+eta,                          (4)\n\nand leaves every other multiplier and q unchanged. All steps and\nmultipliers remain nonnegative, and every flow equation remains exact.\nThus the only issue is the full PSD condition.\n\nDirect expansion gives\n\nM_new-M\n =eta||g_2||^2\n   +[25 eta/(4(12+eta))]<g_0,g_1>.                  (5)\n\nThe second term is the previously absent remote cut contribution\n(h_1-h'_1)lambda_02<g_0,g_1>. It cannot be discarded.\n\n## 3. Exact negative direction in the OLD full kernel\n\nUse one-dimensional formal vectors with\n\ng_0=47/40,  g_1=-7/40,  g_2=9/32,  g_3=-9/16,\nx_0=47/40+sqrt(78)/20.                              (6)\n\nThese need not be a legal new primal: they are a legitimate test of\nPSD, which is required on ALL formal vectors.\n\nHere U=1, V=1/16, Z=0, and R0=sqrt(78)/20.\nConsequently the OLD FULL slack (2) is exactly zero.\nOn (6), equation(5) is exactly\n\nM_new=eta(81 eta-344)/[1024(12+eta)].                (7)\n\nIt is negative for every 0<eta<344/81. In particular eta=1 is allowed\nby (4), all actual multipliers remain nonnegative, but\nM_new=-263/13312<0. There is therefore no sufficiently small positive\nmove of this unchanged transport that preserves PSD.\n\nOn the actual matching quadratic data from Section1, in contrast,\n(5)/eta is 25/16-25/[4(12+eta)]>0. Thus checking only the\nmatching primal would falsely suggest a gain. The second full-kernel\ndirection is essential.\n\n## 4. Scope and a different successful action on this example\n\nThis is NOT a stationary tuple and NOT a globally optimal schedule.\nIt does not refute some other simultaneous descent. Indeed one exists\nwith a different certificate re-selection.\n\nFirst continuously undo the last zero-flow adjustment in Section1,\nmoving from (1) to its base certificate (3) at the SAME h,q.\nEvery intermediate actual multiplier is nonnegative, and every\nintermediate slack is a convex combination of the PSD matrices (2)\nand (3). The same legal quadratic remains matching throughout.\nThe base slack (3) has no extra kernel beyond that quadratic direction,\nbecause zeta^2/16=39/200<1.\n\nNow use the schedule h'=(2,28/13,3), corresponding to eta=1 in (4).\nAn explicit full certificate with value q'=900/169<25/4 is obtained\nby keeping the first-step multiplier pattern\n(mu_0,mu_1,nu_0,lambda_10)\n=(2+zeta/2,zeta/2,zeta,2-zeta/2),\nmultiplying it by q'=4(15/13)^2, and then adding the two\nfunction-growth identities from Section1:\nfour times the i=1 identity with r=15/13, and once the i=2\nidentity with r=2.\n\nIts slack is\n\nq'[||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2]\n +4||(15/13)g_1+g_2||^2+||2g_2+g_3||^2 >=0,\n\nall multiplier signs hold since15/13>1 and zeta<4, and its\nfunction-value flow is exactly2e_3. The quadratic again attains q'.\nThis is a real fixed-length improvement for this example.\n\nThe obstruction therefore distinguishes two mechanisms:\nthe same sparse transport can fail at an unnecessarily degenerate\noptimal dual certificate, whereas reselecting the dual certificate\ninside the fixed-h optimum face can make a different descent available.\nNo theorem is claimed that such re-selection removes remote edges\nfor every schedule, nor that the maximal-rank dual face always has\nthe needed kernel structure.\n\nNext action: analyze certificate re-selection together with the\ninactive-interpolation costs before attempting a universal descent.\nDo not add a remote-edge-absence hypothesis and call the original\nextension repaired. The eventual exact-profile mass-cap issue remains\nseparate even if global Huber tightness were established.\n\n## 5. A continuous repair that RETAINS the remote edges\n\nThe same obstruction can be repaired by a different, explicit simultaneous\nreweighting, without first setting the remote multipliers to zero.\nThis supplies a concrete alternative mechanism on this example only.\n\nFor 1<=r<=5/4, take h(r)=(2,1+r,3), q(r)=4r^2, retain\nzeta=sqrt(78)/5, and set z(r)=64r^2/25. Choose\n\nmu_0=4r^2(2+zeta/2),  mu_1=2r^2 zeta,\nnu_0=4r^2 zeta+z(r)(r-1),\nnu_1=8(r^2-1),        nu_2=6-z(r)(r-1),\n\nlambda_10=4r^2(2-zeta/2),  lambda_21=8, lambda_32=2,\nlambda_02=r z(r),          lambda_20=z(r).            (8)\n\nAll unlisted multipliers are zero. They are nonnegative throughout the\ninterval: z<=4, r-1<=1/4, so nu_2>=5; and zeta<4.\nDirect substitution gives out-in=0 at0,1,2 and2 at3. At r=5/4\nthese are exactly (1), and both remote edges stay positive for\nevery r in the interval.\n\nUse R0=x_0-g_0, U=g_0+g_1, V_r=g_2+r g_1, Z=g_3+2g_2.\nThe full slack is\n\nM(r)=4r^2||R0-(zeta/4)U||^2+||Z||^2\n     +A(r)||U||^2-(r-1)z(r)<U,V_r>+(4+z(r))||V_r||^2,\n\nA(r)=r^2(161-128r)/50.                              (9)\n\nTo derive (9), scale the first-step identity by4r^2, use the two\nfunction-growth identities with factors r and2, and add the\nfinite/star flow change corresponding to\nC_02=r z/2, C_20=z/2.\nIts quadratic contribution is\n-z[r||U||^2+(r-1)<U,V_r>-||V_r||^2].\nCompleting the same first square yields (9).\n\nThe remaining two-by-two matrix in (U,V_r) has positive A(r), and\nits determinant is exactly\n\nr^2(5-4r)(512r^3+1664r^2+16r+3220)/1250 >=0.          (10)\n\nThus the FULL M(r) is PSD. It has a positive determinant in this\nremaining block when r<5/4, so the extraneous kernel responsible\nfor (7) is removed. The legal quadratic f(x)=x^2/2 follows\n(1,-1,r,-2r), satisfies every active interpolation equality, and\nattains q(r)=4r^2. Consequently (8)--(10) give the exact identity\n\nW_3(2,1+r,3)=4r^2  for 1<=r<=5/4.\n\nIn particular moving r downward from5/4 is a continuous strict\nsame-length descent starting from the original degenerate certificate,\nwith nonnegative flow and both remote finite edges retained.\nThe failed sparse update and this successful update start from the\nSAME full certificate. Their difference is the simultaneous scaling\nof the prefix-source coefficients and the remote weights (quadratic\nand cubic in r), which changes the full kernel correctly.\n\nThis does not assert that a comparable homogeneous reweighting exists\nfor an arbitrary near-optimal schedule. {"id": "df2a5653a864f938", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:02:54.776279+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Linear-length arbitrary-suffix protection on the SAME periodic prefix: a new growing-energy Markov Moreau function gives W_(2m+k)>=(5/198)^2 for every suffix of k arbitrary nonnegative finite steps with k L_prop<=m-2600. Explicit old affine guards force bounded backward propagation of active gradient indices.", "evidence": "# Correlated Moreau relay surviving arbitrary suffixes of linear length\nStatus: complete new constructive candidate, UNVERIFIED. No additional verifier call.\nThis responds to the fixed-witness two-step failure50b0bb4cd67cac9f and the quantifier reminder d7b99ad37dff6ae5. The adversary is replaced by a different correlated function on the SAME periodic prefix. The stronger uniform suffix property is proved below, not imposed as a requirement on all adversaries.\n\n## 1. Precise statement\nFix rational constants\na_0=9/10, q=10001/10000, a=a_0 q,\ntheta=a q=a_0 q^2,\nchi=6/5, T=23/10, kappa=1/10.\nDefine the integer\nL_prop=1+ceil(max{log(2400)/(2log q), log(9600)/log(10/9)}).   (1)\nLet m>=2600 be even. Take the2m-step prefix\nh_pre=(1,463/135,1,131/30) repeated m/2 times.\nLet k>=0 satisfy\nk L_prop<=m-2600,\nand append ANY prescribed finite nonnegative suffix(t_1,...,t_k).\nThere is one explicitly defined convex differentiable1-smooth function in R^(m+1), already fixed by m, which gives\nW_(2m+k)(h_pre,t_1,...,t_k)>=(5/198)^2=25/39204.            (2)\nThus even arbitrary and unbounded suffix entries cannot repair this prefix within the stated linear number of steps. All constants are absolute and the threshold is independent of the suffix.\nThis is a restricted schedule theorem, not the unrestricted exact-profile target.\n\nThe fixed function used here differs from the one annihilated in50b0bb4cd67cac9f. Its gradient amplitudes grow by q and its negative correlation changes from-a_0 to-a=-a_0 q, leaving the actual prefix steps unchanged.\n\n## 2. Explicit function and prefix data\nIn R^(m+1) construct\nzeta_0=e_0,\nzeta_(i+1)=-a zeta_i+sqrt(1-a^2)e_(i+1).\nPut v_i=chi at even i and v_i=1 at odd i,\nalpha_i=q^i v_i, omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i,\nE_i=alpha_i^2,\nL_i(x)=<omega_i,x>-kappa E_i,\nJ(x)=max{0,L_0(x),...,L_m(x)},\nf(x)=min_z{J(z)+||x-z||^2/2}.\nThis gives one finite max-affine function with explicit nonnegative offsets and an explicitly real PSD Gram matrix\n<omega_i,omega_j>=alpha_i alpha_j(-a)^|i-j|.\nThe vectors omega_i are independent.\n\nThe envelope is globally convex differentiable1-smooth: its unique proximal minimizer exists by the quadratic term, the optimality equation gives grad f(x)=x-prox_J(x), and subgradient monotonicity gives\n||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>.\nIts minimum is0 and its minimizer set is\nP_min={x:L_i(x)<=0 for all i}.\nFor every x, its gradient belongs to conv{0,omega_0,...,omega_m}; more precisely it is a convex combination of normals active at prox_J(x).\n\nStart at X_0=T omega_0. Set\nb_i=T-1+(T/a)alpha_(i+1)/alpha_i\n    =T-1+(T/a_0)v_(i+1)/v_i.\nThese are exactly463/135 at even i and131/30 at odd i, because the q factors cancel.\nDefine X_(i+1)=X_i-(1+b_i)omega_i. We now prove that these are the actual block starts, with both block-i queries having gradient omega_i.\n\n## 3. Complete legality check of the prefix\nLet Z_i=X_i-T omega_i,\nB_i=(T/a)alpha_(i+1), D_i=B_i(1-a^2).\nThen\nZ_0=0, Z_(i+1)=Z_i-B_i(zeta_i+a zeta_(i+1)).\nTaking inner products gives\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for j>=i,\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=-S_(j,i) for j<i,\nS_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1)D_l(-a)^(l-j).                    (3)\n\nWrite delta=T-2=3/10 and Lambda=(T/a)(1-a^2)q.\nThe following exact rational inequalities hold:\n4/5<theta^2<13/16,\ntheta chi-1<13/160,\nLambda<=437/900<39/80.\nFor the first two use9/10<theta<901/1000.
