For the third,\nLambda=(T/a_0)(1-a_0^2 q^2)<(T/a_0)(1-a_0^2)=437/900.\nDefine\nS_floor=Lambda(1-theta chi)/(1-theta^2)<0.\nThen\n|S_floor|<(39/80)(13/160)/(3/16)=169/800.                 (4)\n\nAt a query X_i-u omega_i,u=0 or1, the proposed proximal point is\nZ_i+d omega_i,d=T-u-1>=delta.\nThe terminal query X_m has d=T-1 and is also covered.\nThe current plane has value(d-kappa)E_i>=E_i/5>0.\nFor j<i, its deficit above plane j is\nd alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)+alpha_j S_(j,i)\n -kappa E_i+kappa E_j, C_ij=(-a)^(i-j);                  (5)\nfuture indices use the same expression with S=0.\n\nThe coefficient of d is positive. At even separation, for a past j the ratio C_ij alpha_j/alpha_i is at most a_0^2 chi<1, and for a future j it is at most theta^2 chi<39/40. At odd separation C_ij<0. Hence d=delta is the worst endpoint.\n\nFuture comparisons at even separation are positive definite quadratic forms:\n(delta-kappa)alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2-delta C_ij alpha_i alpha_j>0,\nsince delta^2 C_ij^2<=delta^2 a^4<(9/100)(13/16)^2<4kappa(delta-kappa).\nAt odd separation all three displayed terms have nonnegative signs.\n\nFor the past, put ell=i-j and normalize the deficit by q^(2j). Write z=q^ell v_i>=1 and y=v_j in[1,chi]. Finite geometric sums, using v in[1,chi], give\nS_(j,i)/q^j>=S_floor(1-theta^ell) if ell is even,\nS_(j,i)/q^j>=S_floor if ell is odd.                       (6)\nFor odd ell, the cross term is positive, so the deficit is at least\n(delta-kappa)z^2+kappa y^2+S_floor y\n >=3/10-(169/800)(6/5)=93/2000>0.\nFor even ell, the expression\n(delta-kappa)z^2-delta a^ell z y\nincreases with z>=1. Replace z by v_i, and then replace a^ell by the larger theta^ell. The resulting lower bound is\nF=(delta-kappa)v_i^2+kappa y^2\n -delta w v_i y+S_floor(1-w)y, w=theta^ell in[0,theta^2].\nIt is affine in w. At w=0 the preceding93/2000 bound applies. At w=theta^2 it increases with v_i and decreases with y on[1,chi], since\n2/5-(3/10)(13/16)(6/5)>0,\nand\n6/25-(3/10)(4/5)=0\nwhile the omitted S_floor term is strictly negative.\nIts minimum is at v_i=1,y=chi and exceeds\n43/125-(3/10)(13/16)(6/5)-(39/80)(13/160)(6/5)\n=127/32000>0.\nThus all old, future and zero planes are controlled. The active-plane subgradient equation proves every prescribed gradient and the entire prefix.\n\nThis verification uses the signed sums themselves. They can be negative; no nonpositive-residual hypothesis is substituted.\n\nThe initial closest minimizer is y_star=kappa omega_0. For even j,\n<omega_j,y_star>/ (kappa E_j)=(a/q)^j=a_0^j<=1,\nand odd inner products are negative. Thus y_star belongs to P_min. Constraint0 is active and\nX_0-y_star=(T-kappa)omega_0\nis its positive normal. Squared-distance expansion proves that y_star is closest. Its squared distance is\n(T-kappa)^2 E_0=(11/5)^2(6/5)^2=4356/625.                (7)\n\n## 4. Exact endpoint affine values\nPut\nB_even=S_floor/chi,\nB_odd=Lambda(chi-theta)/(1-theta^2),\nA_even=-B_even-kappa,\nA_odd=-B_odd-kappa,\nC_end=T+B_even.\nFor even m, equation(3) gives at X_m:\nL_j(X_m)/E_j=A_even+C_end theta^(m-j) if j is even,\nL_j(X_m)/E_j=A_odd-chi C_end theta^(m-j) if j is odd.     (8)\nThese are obtained by summing the two-period geometric series; for an even j,\nS_(j,m)/alpha_j=B_even(1-theta^(m-j)),\nwhile an odd j has\nS_(j,m)/alpha_j=B_odd+chi B_even theta^(m-j).\n\nWe need the following uniform bounds:\nA_even>=19/270, |A_even|<1, |A_odd|<=22/25<1.             (9)\nThe last two follow from(4) and\nB_odd<(39/80)(3/10)/(3/16)=39/50.\nFor the first, put z=q^2. Direct algebra gives\nB_even(q)-B_even(1)\n=(T/chi)(z-1)(a_0 z-chi)/(1-a_0^2 z^2)<=0,\nbecause a_0z=theta<chi.\nAt q=1, B_even(1)=-23/135, so A_even>=23/135-1/10=19/270.\n\n## 5. Affine guards annihilating every later direction\nFix an even index j with j+51<=m. Let\nr_guard=chi a_0^51,\nlambda_guard=1/(1+r_guard),\nw_j=lambda_guard omega_j+(1-lambda_guard)omega_(j+51),\nG_j=lambda_guard L_j+(1-lambda_guard)L_(j+51).\nThen G_j is an affine lower bound for J.\nThe normal w_j is orthogonal to EVERY omega_l with l>=j+51:\nthe Markov products cancel because51 is odd and\n(1-lambda_guard)alpha_(j+51)=lambda_guard alpha_j a^51.\n\nEquation(8) therefore gives, with all decaying endpoint terms cancelling,\nG_j(X_m)=q^(2j) d_guard,\nd_guard=[chi^2 A_even-chi theta^51(B_odd+kappa)]/\n        [1+chi a_0^51].                                (10)\nNow theta^51<1/100: theta<1-99/1000 gives theta^51<exp(-5), while the degree6 Taylor sum for exp(5) exceeds100. Also a_0^51<1/100 and q^51<chi; for the latter,\nq^51<=(1-51/10000)^(-1)<6/5,\nusing the binomial bound by the geometric series.\nConsequently\nd_guard>=[38/375-132/12500]/(253/250)\n=1702/18975>2/25.                                      (11)\nThe guard normal has ||w_j||^2<=E_j, since both constituent normals have norm at most alpha_j (q^51<chi).\n\nFor later use, its inner products obey the explicit bounds\n|<w_j,omega_l>|<=E_j a_0^(j-l) for l<j,\n|<w_j,omega_l>|<=E_j theta^(l-j) for j<=l<j+51,\n<w_j,omega_l>=0 for l>=j+51.                            (12)\nTo check the middle formula before taking absolute values, it is\nlambda_guard alpha_j alpha_l(-a)^(l-j)\n [1-a^(2(j+51-l))].\nFor l<j replace that last factor by1-a^102 and use the corresponding Markov product. The amplitude factors then give (12).\n\n## 6. A uniform lower bound on the proximal value after any late-direction perturbation\nFor r>=2600, let\nV_r=span{omega_i:r<=i<=m}.\nTake ANY x in X_m+V_r, with no bound on the coefficients of its displacement. Put g=grad f(x), z=x-g=prox_J(x).\n\nChoose the largest even J_0 with J_0+51<=r. Take50 guards with starts\nJ_0, J_0-52,...,J_0-52*49.\nAll starts are nonnegative because r>=2600, and each guard is constant on X_m+V_r by its orthogonality. Let E_top=chi^2 q^(2J_0).\nTheir energies are at least(12/25)E_top, because\nq^(-5096)>=1-5096/10000>12/25.\nBy(10)--(11), each guard value at x is at least E_j/18. Hence their average value is at least(2/75)E_top.\n\nFor any one normal omega_l, (12) bounds the sum of the50 absolute guard inner products by(100/99)E_top. Here is a direct justification of the uniform sum bound. The starts are52 apart, every guard to the left of l by at least51 contributes0, and at most one interval can contain l. At position d in[0,51] within that interval the remaining geometric bound is\ntheta^d+a_0^(52-d)/(1-a_0^52).\nThis is a convex function of d, so its maximum is at an endpoint. At d=0 it is1/(1-a_0^52)<100/99; at d=51 it is less than1/100+(9/10)/(1-1/100)<100/99. Before all intervals the single geometric-series bound is also<100/99.\nBecause g belongs to conv{0,omega_0,...,omega_m}, the average guard normal has inner product with g at most(2/99)E_top.\n\nSince J(z) is at least the average guard value at z=x-g, we obtain\nJ(prox_J(x))>= (2/75-2/99)E_top=(16/2475)E_top.\nAlso r-J_0<=52 and q^(-104)>49/50, so\nE_top>=(49/50)chi^2 q^(2r).\nIn particular\nJ(prox_J(x))> chi^2 q^(2r)/200.                         (13)\nThis holds for every x in the whole affine space X_m+V_r, independently of its displacement size. It is the key difference from a merely local covariance check.\n\n## 7. Bounded backward propagation of active indices\nLet E_r^ref=chi^2 q^(2r). For j<r, the Markov structure and(8) imply that at x in X_m+V_r there is one real scalar Q_x such that\nL_j(x)/E_j=A_even+(chi/v_j)Q_x theta^(r-j) for even j,\nL_j(x)/E_j=A_odd-(chi/v_j)Q_x theta^(r-j) for odd j.       (14)\nFor completeness one may take\nQ_x=C_end theta^(m-r)+\n    (-1)^r <zeta_r,x-X_m>/(chi q^r).\nFor j<r the inner product with any later normal factors through zeta_r, giving(14).\n\nFor ANY gradient g=grad f(x), the full Gram matrix gives\n|<omega_j,g>|<=chi E_j,\nbecause |<omega_j,omega_l>|/E_j is at most\nchi theta^(l-j) for l>=j or chi a_0^(j-l) for l<j.\nThus for ell=r-j>0,\nL_j(prox_J(x))/E_r^ref\n <=3q^(-2ell)+chi |Q_x| a_0^ell.                       (15)\n\nIf |Q_x|<10, (13) is a lower bound E_r^ref/200 for the active level.\nIf |Q_x|>=10, choose j in{r-1,r-2} with even parity for Q_x>0 or odd parity for Q_x<0. Equations(9),(14) and the bound on <omega_j,g> give\nL_j(prox_J(x))\n >=E_j[(4/5)|Q_x|-11/5]\n >=(|Q_x|/2)E_j\n >=(|Q_x|/4)E_r^ref,                                   (16)\nwhere the last inequality uses q^(-4)/chi^2>1/2.\n\nBy the definition(1),\nq^(-2L_prop)<1/2400, a_0^L_prop<1/9600.\nFor ell>=L_prop, (15) is therefore< E_r^ref/400 if |Q_x|<10. If |Q_x|>=10, its value divided by |Q_x| is< E_r^ref/4000. In either case it is strictly below the active level furnished by(13) or(16).\nTherefore no plane with index j<=r-L_prop is active at prox_J(x). The zero plane is not active either by(13). We conclude\ngrad f(x) belongs to span{omega_i:i>=r-L_prop}\nfor every x in X_m+V_r and r>=2600.                    (17)\n\nThis is a proved restriction on actual gradients of the one global function. It retains all old planes; the index exclusion comes from comparing their values with a certified positive active level.\n\n## 8. Arbitrary suffix and normalized lower bound\nRun any prescribed finite suffix steps from X_m. After t suffix steps, induction using(17) shows\nx^(t) belongs to X_m+V_(m-t L_prop)\nas long as m-t L_prop>=2600. Indeed each update adds a scalar multiple of a gradient in the displayed larger span; the scalar can be arbitrarily large.\n\nThe guard G_0 uses normals0 and51, and is orthogonal to every omega_i with i>=51. Hence it remains CONSTANT throughout every allowed suffix, with value d_guard>=2/25. Its normal satisfies ||w_0||^2<=chi^2=36/25.\nTo transfer this affine lower bound through the envelope, note that J is at least both0 and G_0. For any lambda in[0,1], completing the square gives\nf(x)>=lambda G_0(x)-lambda^2||w_0||^2/2.\nTake lambda=(2/25)/(36/25)=1/18. At the terminal point this yields\n2f(x)>= (2/25)^2/(36/25)=1/225.\nDivide by the exact initial squared distance4356/625 from(7):\n2f(x)/||X_0-y_star||^2 >=(1/225)/(4356/625)\n=25/39204=(5/198)^2.\nThis proves(2) for every allowed suffix, including zero steps and arbitrarily large finite entries.\n\nIndependence of the omega_i and their nonzero adjacent correlations also show nonseparability: isolate each zero/plane hinge, whose envelope Hessian is the rank-one projector onto omega_i. Adjacent such projectors do not commute. Thus no fixed orthonormal coordinates separate the function.\n\n## 9. Scope and exact-profile relevance\nThis gives a full-suffix construction on a precise unbounded-horizon class, with a number of arbitrary repair steps proportional to the prefix length. It replaces, rather than assumes robustness of, the witness refuted by the two-step annihilator.\n\nUsing the verified benchmark bound B_n>=2n+1 from fact0be8b54e0a33381c, the candidate implies W_n/U_n^ZJ>= (25/39204)(2n+1) on this padded-prefix family, tending to infinity. That consequence remains conditional on verification of this new candidate.\n\nThe unrestricted problem is still open here. No assertion says an arbitrary near-optimal schedule contains this periodic prefix, or can be transformed to it without cost. The new reusable mechanism is the explicit affine guards and the finite backward propagation estimate(17), with all matrix, value, minimum and suffix conditions included.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_linear_suffix_guard.md", "repairs": "50b0bb4cd67cac9f", "responds_to": "d7b99ad37dff6ae5", "related_candidates": ["366067681df90ad9", "6192a365859c116f"], "fact_ids": ["0be8b54e0a33381c"], "status": "complete candidate, unverified", "verification_allocation": "unchanged", "scope": "periodic prefix plus a fixed positive fraction of arbitrary suffix steps; not all schedules"}, "glossary": {"a_0": "Base correlation magnitude9/10 fixed by the actual periodic checkpoint steps.", "q": "Gradient-amplitude growth factor10001/10000 in this candidate.", "a": "Actual Markov correlation magnitude a_0 q.", "theta": "Effective history ratio a q=a_0 q^2.", "L_prop": "Explicit integer1+ceil(max(log2400/(2logq),log9600/log(10/9))) controlling backward propagation per suffix step.", "V_r": "Span of gradient normals with indices at least r.", "G_j": "Affine guard combining planes j and j+51 at an even j.", "w_j": "The normal of affine guard G_j, orthogonal to every later gradient normal from j+51 onward.", "E_r^ref": "Reference energy chi^2 q^(2r), independent of r's parity.", "Q_x": "Real scalar expressing the effect of a late-direction displacement on all older plane values."}}
{"id": "3c02ccd1fb137184", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:14:32.308229+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "A genuine finite objective residual is now constructed at unchanged Huber mass: physical pair(3/2,3/2), proof weights(3/2-epsilon,3/2,4+epsilon), D=7, and E>=(1/(2epsilon))||e-4g_2||^2 for0<epsilon<=1/64. The proof retains the dual slack and adjacent interpolation cycles, with all five actual multipliers and a full2x2 PSD matrix. A new exact finite-residual block transport is calibrated to ConPD with no fixed limiting loss, but its mass is strictly below G(K,D_b), so this particular closed subrule cannot supply an improvement.", "evidence": "# A genuine finite objective residual and its exact block transport\n\nStatus: unverified exact identities and a scoped limitation. No solver or verifier calls. The eleven-step rule with bridge factors(7/10,1,1/5) is closed by max3's explicit counterexample, which was read and matches the present primitive energy definition. None of the constructions below attempts to repair that rule.\n\nUse smoothness1 and Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0. For nonnegative proof weights u on a GD block write\n\n    D=sum u_i, d=x_start-x_end, U=sum u_i g_i, e=U-d,\n    E=2sum u_i(f_i-f_end)+sum u_i||g_i||^2\n       +2sum u_i<g_i,x_start-x_i>-||U||^2.\n\nFor any minimizer, direct expansion gives\n\n    ||x_start-x_star||^2+2sum u_i Q_(i,star)-2D(f_end-f_star)\n       =||x_end-x_star-e||^2+E.                      (1)\n\nThus E>=0 yields the original last-iterate bound W<=1/D.\n\n## 1. A nonprimitive proof-weight state with no loss of Huber mass\n\nThe physical schedule is the pair(3/2,3/2). Write its three gradients as p,m,q, its displacement as d=(3/2)(p+m), and define\n\n    t=d-3q, r=p-(d+q)/4.\n\nFor 0<epsilon<=1/64, choose actual proof weights\n\n    u=(3/2-epsilon, 3/2, 4+epsilon).\n\nThen D=7 and\n\n    e=4q-epsilon(p-q), v=e-4q=-epsilon(r+t/4).       (2)\n\nThese are not the primitive weights(h,4), although the physical block is primitive. All three weights are positive. The exact interpolation identity is\n\n    E=(21epsilon/2)(-Q_01)+(1-5epsilon/4)(-Q_02)\n        +(4+29epsilon/4)(-Q_10)\n        +(9+29epsilon/4)(-Q_12)+(16+4epsilon)(-Q_21)\n        +(r,t)^T M_epsilon (r,t),                    (3)\n\nwhere the full symmetric matrix is\n\n    M_epsilon =\n      [ 2epsilon-epsilon^2, -31epsilon/12-epsilon^2/4 ]\n      [ -31epsilon/12-epsilon^2/4,\n                         1/9+7epsilon/36-epsilon^2/16 ].\n\nEvery actual interpolation coefficient in (3) is nonnegative. Equation (3) follows either by expanding E and the five Q terms directly, or by the following retained-slack derivation, which also explains the cross term.\n\nLet V be the pair's physical primitive energy and H=2(f_0-f_2)-6||q||^2-||r||^2. Define the adjacent cycle quantities C_01=-Q_01-Q_10 and C_12=-Q_12-Q_21. They satisfy\n\n    C_01=3<q,r>-<q,t>/4-||r||^2\n                         +(7/6)<r,t>-13||t||^2/144,\n    C_12=-(3/2)<q,r>+(5/8)<q,t>+||r||^2/2\n                         -(5/12)<r,t>+25||t||^2/288.\n\nThe correlated identity in max4_joint_boundary_storage.md gives, more generally,\n\n    V-epsilon H\n      =(epsilon/2)(-Q_01)+(1-5epsilon/4)(-Q_02)\n        +(4-11epsilon/4)(-Q_10)\n        +(9-19epsilon/4)(-Q_12)+(16-8epsilon)(-Q_21)\n        +(16-15epsilon)||t||^2/144.                  (4)\n\nThis can be checked using the one-parameter primitive identity there with\nkappa=(16-15epsilon)/(64-28epsilon), so that\n(1-7epsilon/16)kappa=(16-15epsilon)/64.\n\nThe exact change from V to E has q-cross terms 12epsilon<q,r>+5epsilon<q,t>. The nonnegative combination 10epsilon C_01+12epsilon C_12 has exactly those same q-cross terms. Subtracting its quadratic expression, and adding it to the actual interpolation coefficients in (4), yields (3). This is why keeping only the two scalar slack bounds would have missed the finite objective state: the adjacent cycles supply the required correlations with q.\n\nSet nu=1/(2epsilon). The matrix after subtracting nu||v||^2 from (3) is\n\n    M_epsilon-(epsilon/2)(1,1/4)(1,1/4)^T.           (5)\n\nIts first diagonal entry is (3/2)epsilon-epsilon^2>0, and its determinant is\n\n    epsilon[1/6-(1037/144)epsilon-(29/18)epsilon^2]. (6)\n\nFor epsilon<=1/64 the bracket is at least1321/24576>0. Thus (5) is positive definite, proving the concrete finite residual certificate\n\n    E >= nu ||e-4q||^2, D=7, nu=1/(2epsilon).        (7)\n\nThe mass7 is exactly the physical Huber mass2sum(h)+1. No limiting argument is used to make (7) valid: every indicated positive epsilon gives an actual finite certificate. The family has nu tending to infinity as the weights tend to the primitive weights.\n\n## 2. A transport with a nonprimitive input and an inherited quadratic term\n\nLet a prefix have nonnegative weights, D_a=2K-1 with K>=1, and the actual finite certificate\n\n    E_a >= nu||e_a-Kg||^2, nu>0,\n\nwhere g is the gradient at its endpoint y. The pair construction above provides K=4 and any nu=1/(2epsilon), epsilon<=1/64.\n\nTake an arbitrary tail block, starting at y'=y-tau g and ending at z. Its own nonnegative weights v have mass D_b>0, gradient aggregate U_b, energy E_b>=0, start gradient p_b and final gradient q_b. The four vectors g,p_b,q_b,U_b remain distinct. Nothing below identifies the tail start gradient with the bridge gradient, or replaces its aggregate by an endpoint gradient.\n\nPut\n\n    sigma=1/(nu+1), k=1+1/nu, eta=1-sigma=1/k,\n    c=(2K-sigma)/D_b,\n    beta=(1+sqrt(1+4k c))/(2k),\n    tau=1+K/(k beta).\n\nThus k beta^2=beta+c. Add eta to the prefix terminal proof weight and scale every tail weight by beta. All new proof weights are nonnegative and\n\n    D_new=2K-sigma+beta D_b=k beta^2 D_b.             (8)\n\nWrite v_a=e_a-Kg. The actual tail-to-anchor interpolation sum is\n\n    A_y=-2sum_(i in tail) v_i Q_(i,y)\n       =2D_b(f_y-f_z)-E_b-||U_b||^2\n          -2(tau-1)<U_b,g>-D_b||g||^2.              (9)\n\nExpansion of the new intrinsic energy and substitution of (9) gives exactly\n\n    E_new=(E_a-nu||v_a||^2)+(beta+c)E_b+c A_y\n          +(1/nu)||nu v_a-eta g-beta U_b||^2\n          +sigma(2K-1)||g||^2.                      (10)\n\nThis is a finite-horizon certificate with actual multipliers c v_i on -2Q_(i,y). It preserves the squared boundary quantity nu v_a-eta g-beta U_b; resetting it at a join is not part of the identity. The tail's p_b and q_b have zero explicit rows in the quadratic remainder because (9) has eliminated the full tail value sum exactly, not because those gradients were equated.\n\nFor completeness, the full nonzero boundary matrix in(v_a,g,U_b) is\n\n    [ nu,       -eta,                         -beta          ]\n    [ -eta, eta^2/nu+sigma(2K-1),          eta beta/nu       ]\n    [ -beta,   eta beta/nu,                  beta^2/nu      ].\n\nIt is the sum of the outer product (nu,-eta,-beta)(nu,-eta,-beta)^T/nu and diag(0,sigma(2K-1),0), hence PSD in every Euclidean dimension. If the independent gradients p_b,q_b are added to the boundary vector, their rows and columns are zero. Equation (1) now gives W<=1/D_new for the actual concatenated physical schedule.\n\nTo verify (10) without trusting a completion of squares, the raw quadratic coefficients before subtracting the square are\n\n    ||v_a||^2: nu,\n    <v_a,g>: -2eta,       <v_a,U_b>: -2beta,\n    ||g||^2: sigma(2K-sigma),\n    ||U_b||^2: c+beta-beta^2=beta^2/nu,\n    <g,U_b>: 2[beta(tau-eta-K)+c(tau-1)]\n               =2eta beta/nu.\n\nThe g-diagonal remainder equals sigma(2K-1), using eta=nu/(nu+1).\n\n## 3. Calibration succeeds, but this closed subrule cannot improve ConPD\n\nFor fixed K and D_b, as nu tends to infinity the parameters in part2 tend to the ordinary ConPD parameters and\n\n    D_new -> G(K,D_b).\n\nThere is no fixed calibration loss forced by a finite nonprimitive input: the actual pair family in part1 supplies arbitrarily large finite nu while retaining D_a=7. If desired, nu may be selected separately for each prescribed horizon.\n\nNevertheless, the specific transport in part2 always gives strictly LESS mass than the ordinary ConPD join. Let b_0>0 solve b_0^2-b_0=2K/D_b, so G(K,D_b)=2K+b_0 D_b. Since k>1 and c=(2K-sigma)/D_b<2K/D_b, its positive root beta satisfies beta<b_0. Hence\n\n    D_new=2K-sigma+beta D_b < G(K,D_b).               (11)\n\nThus (10) is an actual finite-residual propagation and calibration test, not an improving upper-bound rule. Repeating this inequality with no further correlations cannot certify a strict gain over the corresponding primitive-prefix G operations.\n\nThere is also a legal hard-instance explanation for the lack of a free local gain. For a prefix with physical sum K-1, one bridge tau=1+r, and a tail with physical sum T, the accepted one-cut fact b98bb807d514e80d gives\n\n    W >= r^2/[K^2(2T+1)].\n\nIf a proposed certificate were Huber-tight with mass2(K+T+r)+1, it would have to satisfy\n\n    r^2[2(K+T+r)+1] <= K^2(2T+1).                  (12)\n\nWhen the tail's own mass is2T+1, ordinary ConPD meets this condition at equality. The affine prefix and tail portions of this witness have t=H=0 for every embedded pair. Consequently the retained pair terms cannot by themselves permit a larger Huber-tight single bridge. This statement concerns this binary setting and does not exclude coordinated multi-bridge certificates or nonsaturated objective weights.\n\n## Current next step\n\nThe candidate storage is now an actual finite-weight state with a full PSD transport, rather than just a formal five-variable test. Its unchanged scalar subrule is quantitatively dominated by ConPD. Further work must use additional cross-block interpolation or the explicit inherited vector nu v_a-eta g-beta U_b to produce a different closed state; retuning the parameters in (10) cannot give the required strict improvement.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_joint_objective_dual_storage", "research_path": "research/max4_finite_pair_residual_transport.md", "related_findings": ["b750ce63e2821fa8", "776c3d8ba2d52ebc"], "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "verification_status": "unverified; no new numerical optimization or verifier call", "status": "concrete input and finite PSD transport established in draft; unchanged subrule strictly dominated by G"}, "glossary": {"v_a": "Prefix objective residual e_a-Kg in the finite transport.", "M_epsilon": "Explicit two-by-two matrix of the pair's retained dual residual r and displacement error t.", "sigma": "1/(nu+1), the finite-residual discount in the displayed transport.", "nu": "Positive residual coercivity coefficient; for the pair construction nu=1/(2epsilon)."}}
{"id": "56f99268b45fbce8", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:17:51.116576+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Whole optimal-certificate set: a matching full stationary primal forces quantitative terminal source/slack rigidity across EVERY optimal certificate. If any certificate has nu_(n-2)>0, the adjacent forward edge has an explicit strictly positive old interpolation slack and vanishes in the entire optimal set; re-selection cannot restore that capacity.", "evidence": "# Whole optimal-certificate set: quantitative terminal rigidity and a common barrier\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED, self-contained candidate calculations. No new verifier\nallocation is assumed. This is the bounded face-level output requested by the\nupdated TASK.md. It neither proves Huber tightness nor resolves the exact\nZhang--Jiang profile comparison.\n\n## 1. Full setting and a matching stationary instance\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Fix a positive length-n schedule h,\nn>=1, and q>0. For distinct sample indices i,j in {star,0,...,n}, use\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.\n\nA full certificate has lambda_ij>=0, finite-node flow\n\n    sum_j lambda_ij - sum_j lambda_ji = 2*1_(i=n),\n\nand a PSD quadratic form after x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t:\n\n    M_lambda=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n            =q||x_0||^2-<x_0,sum_i mu_i g_i>\n                         +sum_(i,j) A_ij <g_i,g_j>,\n\nwhere mu_i=lambda_(i,star). Let C(h,q) denote ALL such certificates. Its\ngradient Schur complement is Z_lambda=A-mu mu^T/(4q)>=0.\n\nAssume there is an ACTUAL legal convex 1-smooth function, normalized by\n||x_0||=1, whose GD trajectory attains 2f_n=q. Suppose one certificate\nlambda^0 in C(h,q) satisfies every stepsize stationarity equation\n\n    D_t(lambda^0;g)=0, 0<=t<n,\n\nwhere D_t is the derivative, with all formal vectors fixed, of M_lambda\nwith respect to h_t. This is a full matching stationary tuple hypothesis.\nIt does not assert that arbitrary fixed schedules or arbitrary matching\nprimals are stationary. A globally minimizing schedule to which the usual\nfull stationary interface applies satisfies this hypothesis.\n\nEvery lambda in C(h,q) matches THIS SAME actual instance: its quadratic\nvalue is sum lambda_ij Q_ij<=0, and PSD makes it nonnegative. Consequently\n\n    lambda_ij Q_ij=0 for all i,j,\n    sum_i mu_i g_i=2q x_0,                         (1)\n    Y Z_lambda=0,                                 (2)\n\nwhere Y is the linear map from R^(n+1) to the ambient Euclidean space\ndefined by Y r=sum_i r_i g_i. To prove (1)--(2), complete the square:\n\n    M_lambda=q||x_0-(sum_i mu_i g_i)/(2q)||^2\n                    +sum_(i,j)(Z_lambda)_ij <g_i,g_j>.\n\nBoth summands are nonnegative and have sum zero. The second gives\nY Z_lambda^(1/2)=0, hence (2). No strict-complementarity assumption is made.\n\nWrite p=g_(n-1), g=g_n and b=h_(n-1)>0. Last-step stationarity implies\n\n    <p,g>=0.                                      (3)\n\nHere is a proof retaining the full matrix. Change only the final step by\na small signed u on the SAME actual function. Its terminal point changes\nby -u p; its terminal gradient changes by O(u), and\nf_n(u)=f_n-u<p,g>+O(u^2). Let v_u be the full formal-vector realization.\nPSD and matching give M_lambda(v_0)=0 and M_lambda v_0=0, so\nM_lambda(v_u)=O(u^2). Since the matrix is affine in the final step,\n\n    q-2f_n(u)+sum lambda_ij Q_ij(u)\n       =u D_(n-1)(lambda;g)+O(u^2).\n\nEvery Q_ij(u)<=0. Taking both signs of u proves\n\n    D_(n-1)(lambda;g)=2<p,g>                      (4)\n\nfor EVERY lambda in C(h,q). Applying stationarity for lambda^0 gives (3).\nThus last-cut stationarity, unlike unproved earlier-cut stationarity,\nsurvives re-selection with this same matching instance.\n\nDefine B_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t), with B_0=1. Gradient Lipschitzness and\ng_star=0 give ||x_i||,||g_i||<=B_i. Convexity and 2f_n=q give\n\n    ||g||>=q/(2 B_n)>0,\n    ||p||>=||g||/(1+b)>=q/[2B_n(1+b)].           (5)\n\nThe second inequality uses ||g-p||<=b||p||.\n\n## 2. A quantitative invariant of the ENTIRE certificate set\n\nChoose any reference certificate lambda^r in C(h,q), with source mu^r, and put\n\n    L_face=span( {range Z_lambda : lambda in C(h,q)}\n                  union {mu-mu^r : lambda in C(h,q)} ).\n\nThis is an ordinary linear span in the finite-dimensional gradient\ncoefficient space R^(n+1). Equations (1)--(2) imply\n\n    L_face subset ker Y.                          (6)\n\nLet J={0,...,n-2}, and T={n-1,n}; J is empty if n=1.\nLet H_J=(<g_i,g_j>)_(i,j in J), which is the actual prefix-gradient Gram\nmatrix, not an assumed replacement for the full primal.\n\nFor EVERY r in L_face, including every source difference, (3) and (6) give\nthe exact identity\n\n    ||p||^2 r_(n-1)^2 + ||g||^2 r_n^2\n            = r_J^T H_J r_J.                      (7)\n\nIndeed r_(n-1)p+r_n g=-sum_(i in J)r_i g_i; square both sides and use\n<p,g>=0. In particular,\n\n    r_n^2 + r_(n-1)^2/(1+b)^2\n       <= [4 B_n^2/q^2] (sum_(i in J) B_i |r_i|)^2. (8)\n\nThis explicit estimate says how much earlier source coefficients must\nchange to permit a specified change of the two terminal source coefficients.\nIt is simultaneous over every optimal certificate; it does not require\nthe reselected certificate to be stationary on all earlier cuts.\n\nConsequences include\n\n    L_face intersect span{e_(n-1),e_n}={0},\n    dim L_face<=n-1,                              (9)\n\nand injectivity of mu -> mu_J on the entire source-coefficient set.\nFor n=1 every source coefficient is fixed across the whole set, and every\nZ_lambda is zero. For larger n the conclusion allows nonzero earlier\nSchur slack and source variation. It does not imply Z_lambda=0.\n\nApplying (7) to the columns of a PSD square root of any Z_lambda gives\nthe additional exact identity\n\n    ||p||^2 (Z_lambda)_(n-1,n-1)\n       +||g||^2 (Z_lambda)_(n,n)\n           = trace(H_J (Z_lambda)_JJ).             (10)\n\nThus, for example,\n\n    (Z_lambda)_(n,n)\n      +(Z_lambda)_(n-1,n-1)/(1+b)^2\n    <= [4 B_n^2/q^2] (sum_(i in J)B_i^2)\n                                  trace((Z_lambda)_JJ). (11)\n\nEquations (7), (8), (10), (11) are quantitative restrictions on the full\noptimal set, not a claim that its common kernel equals the observed\nprimal range. Additional common kernel directions are permitted.\n\nThe fixed-coordinate graph formulation is also useful. Because projection\nL_face -> R^J is injective, the terminal coordinates of r in L_face are\nlinear functions of r_J. These functions can be extended to R^J. Hence\nthere are fixed vectors u,v in R^J such that\n\n    r_(n-1)=-u^T r_J,  r_n=-v^T r_J for all r in L_face.\n\nThe vectors k_p=(u,1,0), k_n=(v,0,1) annihilate every Z_lambda, and\nmu^T k_p and mu^T k_n are constant over C(h,q). In particular\n\n    Z_JT=-Z_JJ [u v],\n    Z_TT=[u v]^T Z_JJ [u v]                       (12)\n\nfor every certificate in the set, with the SAME u,v. This uses the full\nSchur matrix, including singular prefix blocks.\n\n## 3. Certificate mixing gives an explicit descent test without stationarity\n\nThe exact covariance identity explains why different certificates cannot\nbe inspected in isolation. For finitely many certificates lambda^a at\nthe same h,q, positive weights theta_a summing to one, and their average,\n\n    Z_bar=sum_a theta_a Z_a\n          +(1/(4q))sum_a theta_a(mu^a-mu_bar)(mu^a-mu_bar)^T. (13)\n\nThis follows by expanding A_bar-mu_bar mu_bar^T/(4q). Every multiplier\nof the average is nonnegative, its flow is correct, and its FULL matrix\nis PSD, being the average of the full matrices.\n\nBy choosing finitely many certificates whose ranges and source differences\nspan L_face, one gets range Z_bar=L_face. One direct justification is\nthat the range of a sum of PSD matrices is the sum of their ranges;\nthe covariance range is the span of the included source differences.\n\nFor completeness, a terminal vector in this range yields an actual\nsame-length descent, without a stationary-instance assumption.\nSuppose for some averaged certificate\n\n    Z_bar >= kappa (a e_(n-1)+s e_n)(a e_(n-1)+s e_n)^T,\n\nwhere kappa>0, a>0 and s in {+1,-1}. Put B=B_n, Bprev=B_(n-1), and\n\n    C=q/(2B^2)+1/kappa.\n\nChoose a positive d with\n\n    d<=a/C,\n    d<=q^2/(8 a B^2 Bprev^2),\n    d<b if s=+1.\n\nReplace b by b-s d, leaving every earlier step fixed. Then\n\n    W_n(h_0,...,h_(n-2),b-s d)\n        <=q-d q^2/(8 a B^2)<q.                    (14)\n\nHere is the full uniform argument. For ANY legal instance normalized by\n||x_0||=1, put E=2f_n and R=a p+s g. The averaged PSD certificate gives\nq-E>=kappa||R||^2. Convexity gives ||g||^2>=E^2/(4B^2).\nThe identity for ||R||^2 gives\n\n    -2s<p,g> >= (||g||^2-||R||^2)/a.\n\nThe new endpoint is x_n+s d p, so smoothness gives\n\n    E_new<=E-(d/a)[E^2/(4B^2)-(q-E)/kappa]+d^2 Bprev^2.\n\nInsert E^2>=q^2-2q(q-E), then the displayed d bounds. This proves (14)\nuniformly over dimensions and functions. The smoothness inequality can\nalso be written as one additional interpolation inequality plus the\nnonnegative square ||g_new-g-s d p||^2/2. Thus the argument retains a\nfull interpolation/PSD certificate with an auxiliary old endpoint.\n\nA vector supported only on e_n in range Z_bar contradicts tightness:\nq-E>=kappa||g||^2>=kappa E^2/(4B^2) uniformly forces E<=r<q.\nA vector supported only on e_(n-1) gives the same contradiction with\nkappa/(1+b)^2. Consequently a nonzero intersection of L_face with the\nterminal two-coordinate plane either improves the tight bound at the\nsame schedule or gives the genuine final-step descent (14).\n\nFor exactly TWO certificates, if their source difference is\nDelta mu=A e_(n-1)+B0 e_n with A*B0!=0, the equal-weight average has\n\n    kappa=B0^2/(16q),\n    a=|A/B0|, s=sign(A/B0)\n\nafter rescaling the direction. Formula (14) is therefore an explicit\nquantitative way of exploiting re-selection. It cannot occur under\nthe matching stationary hypothesis of Sections1--2, by (8).\n\n## 4. A common strict forward-edge barrier two steps from the end\n\nAssume now n>=2, set k=n-2 and a=h_k, and retain the actual stationary\ninstance of Section1. Suppose SOME certificate in C(h,q) has\n\n    nu_k=lambda_(star,k)>0.\n\nComplementarity for that certificate on the common instance forces\n\n    delta_k=f_k-||g_k||^2/2=0.                    (15)\n\nMeanwhile Q_(n,n-1)<=0 and (3) imply\n\n    delta_(n-1)=f_(n-1)-||p||^2/2\n                  >=q/2+||g||^2/2>0.              (16)\n\nFirst a>1. To see this, the unit-step descent inequality from (15) shows\nthat y=x_k-g_k is a minimizer. For 0<=a<=1, Lipschitz gradient equality\nbetween x_k and y forces p=(1-a)g_k. A detailed justification: the two\nLipschitz bounds along the segment sum to ||g_k||, so equality in the\ntriangle inequality forces the stated vectors. At a=1, p=0 contradicts\nf_n>0. At a<1, (3) implies <g_k,g>=0; since x_n=x_k-a g_k-b p, the\nconstraint Q_(n,k)<=0 becomes q/2+||g||^2/2<=0, again impossible.\nThus a>1.\n\nDefine sigma_ij=-Q_ij>=0 for the common actual instance. Direct expansion\nof the exact GD relation x_(k+1)=x_k-a g_k gives\n\n    Q_(star,k+1)+(1/a)Q_(k+1,k)+((1-a)/a)Q_(k,k+1)\n           -Q_(star,k)\n      =(1/a)||p-(1-a)g_k||^2.\n\nUsing (15) and (16),\n\n    sigma_(k,k+1)\n      =[a delta_(k+1)+sigma_(k+1,k)\n                     +||p+(a-1)g_k||^2]/(a-1)\n      >=[a/(a-1)](q/2+||g||^2/2)\n      >=[a/(a-1)](q/2+q^2/(8B_n^2))>0.          (17)\n\nTherefore EVERY certificate in C(h,q) has\n\n    lambda_(k,k+1)=0,  nu_(k+1)=0.                (18)\n\nThe first is a common strict inactive edge, not a missing edge in one\nbad extreme certificate. Re-selection at unchanged h,q cannot restore\nthat adjacent capacity. This obstruction is compatible with remote\nforward edges and does not prove that a simultaneous full motion fails.\n\n## 5. The inactive cost remains after re-selection\n\nThe precise finite joint-perturbation identity is recorded here to prevent\na representation change from hiding the cost in (17). Use the stationary\nbase lambda^0 and its common actual instance. For a new full certificate\n(q',lambda',h'), let dh=h'-h and dlambda=lambda'-lambda^0. Define\n\n    z_t(lambda)=sum_(j>t)[mu_j+sum_(l<=t)lambda_(j,l)]g_j\n                   -sum_(i<=t)[sum_(j>t)lambda_(i,j)]g_i,\n    B_mix=sum_(t<n)dh_t <g_t,z_t(dlambda)>.\n\nHere z_t is linear in lambda, and D_t(lambda;g)=<g_t,z_t(lambda)>.\nThe full matrix is affine separately in h and lambda; pairing it with\nthe OLD Gram realization, using flow, old matching, and all old\nstationarity equations, gives exactly\n\n    <M(q',lambda',h'),G_old>\n       =q'-q-sum_ij lambda'_ij sigma_ij+B_mix.\n\nFull PSD therefore requires\n\n    q'-q >= sum_ij lambda'_ij sigma_ij-B_mix.      (19)\n\nIn the setting of Section4 this includes the explicit charges\n\n    sum_ij lambda'_ij sigma_ij\n       >= lambda'_(k,k+1) [a/(a-1)](q/2+q^2/(8B_n^2))\n                     +nu'_(k+1)(q/2+q^2/(8B_n^2)). (20)\n\nThe two edges are distinct and every omitted charge is nonnegative.\nIf one instead uses a reselected base certificate that is not stationary\non this same primal at earlier cuts, the linear term\nsum dh_t D_t(lambda_reselected;g) must be retained. Formula (19) with\nthe original stationary base already includes it inside B_mix.\nNo unsupported stationarity transfer across all cuts has been made.\n\n## 6. Scope\n\nThe identities and explicit bounds (7)--(14), together with the strict\ncommon barrier (17)--(20), are new face-level quantitative constraints.\nThey survive arbitrary re-selection in the full nonnegative-flow/PSD set.\nThey allow extra kernel directions, all remote edges, and non-Huber\nlevels q>1/(2sum h+1).\n\nThey do not show that a global minimizer has a positive nu_(n-2), that\nevery other star-outgoing multiplier vanishes, that the full slack is\nrank one, or that q equals the Huber value. Even Huber tightness would\nstill leave the exact/asymptotic DP mass restriction. The remaining\nsearch must pay (20) by a genuine joint schedule/flow motion, or obtain\na different uniform comparison; convex-face terminology alone does\nnot supply that bridge.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["65226df802e529dc", "0459cada985e4bd8", "cfe9f969be95cc9a"], "artifact": "research/max2_whole_optimal_face.md"}, "glossary": {"C(h,q)": "All full nonnegative-flow interpolation certificates at the same schedule h and objective level q, with full PSD slack.", "L_face": "Span of all gradient Schur-complement ranges and all differences of star-directed source vectors across C(h,q).", "Y": "Map r to sum_i r_i g_i for the one actual matching stationary primal.", "H_J": "Actual Gram matrix of the earlier gradients g_0,...,g_(n-2).", "B_i": "Elementary trajectory radius product_(t<i)(1+h_t).", "sigma_ij": "Nonnegative interpolation slack -Q_ij of the fixed matching primal.", "B_mix": "Exact bilinear schedule/multiplier change paired with that matching primal.", "mu_i": "Source coefficient lambda_(i,star).", "nu_i": "Star-outgoing coefficient lambda_(star,i)."}}
{"id": "792aa4b6dbbad8bf", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:27:50.742511+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Exact terminal re-selection candidate: for every positive schedule, some tight FULL certificate has all incoming multipliers lambda_(j,n)=0, including nu_n. The reduced PEP has the exact same value via a fixed-prefix maximal conjugate extension; the selected certificate has an exact terminal square and a complete PSD prefix remainder.", "evidence": "# Exact terminal re-selection through the fixed-prefix maximal extension\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED self-contained candidate. No new fact_submit is allocated.\nThis is a precise positive support result for the full optimal certificate set.\nIt removes only incoming edges at the UNQUERIED final sample, not arbitrary\nremote forward edges at queried samples. No descent or profile theorem is claimed.\n\n## 1. The reduced primal retains the complete prefix\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be a\nfinite positive schedule, n>=1, and let P={star,0,...,n-1}.\nAs usual x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nConsider the full Gram primal on (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with G>=0,\n||x_0||^2<=1, and objective maximize 2f_n. Keep\n\n    Q_ij<=0 for every distinct i,j in P,\n    Q_(n,j)<=0 for every j in P.                    (1)\n\nDelete every Q_(j,n)<=0, j in P, including Q_(star,n).\nNo other constraint is removed. In particular all remote forward and\nbackward interpolation constraints inside the prefix remain.\n\nThe value of this reduced primal equals the true unrestricted W_n(h).\n\n## 2. Exactness by an explicit globally legal extension\n\nTake any feasible reduced transcript and realize its Gram matrix by\nfinite-dimensional Euclidean vectors. For j in P put\n\n    a_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j,\n    H_j(v)=a_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2,\n    H(v)=max_(j in P) H_j(v),\n    F(x)=sup_v {<x,v>-H(v)}.                       (2)\n\nEach H_j is a quadratic with Hessian I, so H is finite, continuous and\n1-strongly convex. Its quadratic growth makes the supremum defining F\nfinite and attained at a unique vector v(x). The optimality relation is\nx in partial H(v(x)). Strong convexity gives\n\n    <x-x',v(x)-v(x')> >= ||v(x)-v(x')||^2,\n\nand hence ||v(x)-v(x')||<=||x-x'||. The maximizing affine functions in (2)\nthen show grad F(x)=v(x): the lower bound from testing v(x) and upper\nbound from testing v(x') squeeze the first-order difference, with an\nO(||x-x'||^2) error. Thus F is globally convex, differentiable, and\n1-smooth.\n\nFor any i,j in P,\n\n    H_j(g_i)-a_i=Q_ij<=0.\n\nConsequently H(g_i)=a_i, and x_i is a subgradient of H at g_i, since the\ni-th quadratic is active there and has gradient x_i. It follows that\ngrad F(x_i)=g_i and F(x_i)=f_i. The star sample gives F(0)=0 and\ngrad F(0)=0, so zero is a global minimizer. This proves legality of the\ncomplete prefix without invoking an external interpolation black box.\n\nBecause GD through x_n uses only g_0,...,g_(n-1), the SAME predetermined\nschedule on F follows all the supplied positions through x_n.\nThe retained terminal inequalities say\n\n    H_j(g_n)<=<x_n,g_n>-f_n for every j in P.\n\nTherefore\n\n    f_n<=<x_n,g_n>-H(g_n)<=F(x_n).                  (3)\n\nThe new actual terminal value is at least the reduced transcript's\nobjective. Thus every reduced feasible value is at most W_n(h).\nConversely any legal trajectory is reduced feasible, proving exactness.\n\nThe constructed F is also the pointwise maximal smooth-convex extension\nof the fixed prefix. Indeed, for any legal extension K, the elementary\nsmooth-convex interpolation inequality between each fixed sample j and\nan arbitrary sample x with gradient grad K(x) gives\nK(x)<=<x,grad K(x)>-H(grad K(x))<=F(x).\nMaximality is useful motivation but (3) alone proves reduced-PEP exactness.\n\nAt an optimal reduced transcript, 2f_n=W_n(h), both inequalities in (3)\nare equalities. Uniqueness of the conjugate maximizer implies\ng_n=grad F(x_n) and f_n=F(x_n). Hence an optimal reduced primal is itself\nfully interpolable: the deleted incoming terminal inequalities hold at\noptimality. This statement is about optimal transcripts, not all reduced\nfeasible transcripts.\n\n## 3. The reduced dual attains the SAME value\n\nHere are the necessary finite-dimensional details, so re-selection is\nnot an appeal to an unattained dual infimum.\n\nA strict reduced primal exists when every h_t>0. Choose n+2 distinct\nnumbers theta_l in (0,1), take a quadratic with diagonal Hessian\ndiag(theta_l), and an initial vector with every coordinate nonzero and\nnorm less than one. The columns x_0,g_0,...,g_n are evaluations of\n\n    1, z, z product_(t<1)(1-h_t z), ...,\n                       z product_(t<n)(1-h_t z)\n\nat the distinct nodes, times nonzero initial coordinates. These n+2\npolynomials have degrees 0,1,...,n+1 with nonzero leading coefficients,\nso their evaluation matrix is invertible. Thus G is positive definite.\n\nNo two positions among star and 0,...,n are equal: their distinct\nprefix polynomials, each of degree at most n, cannot agree at all n+2\ndistinct nodes; none of those polynomials vanishes at all nodes either.\nFor the strictly convex quadratic with Hessian strictly below I,\ndirect substitution gives\n\n    Q_ij=-1/2 (x_i-x_j)^T Hquad(I-Hquad)(x_i-x_j)<0\n\nfor every distinct pair. In particular every retained inequality is strict.\n\nThe reduced optimal value is finite by Section2 and the elementary GD\nbound W_n(h)<=product_t(1+h_t)^2. Its dual attainment now follows directly\nfrom separating an open convex set, as follows. Let E_red be the retained\nordered pairs. The open convex set of (a,b,c) satisfying, for some G>=0,f,\n\n    a_ij>Q_ij(f,G) (ij in E_red),\n    b>G_00-1,\n    c<2f_n\n\ndoes not contain (0,0,q), where q=W_n(h). A separating hyperplane has\nnonnegative coefficients lambda_ij,r,eta, not all zero, and yields\n\n    sum_(E_red) lambda_ij Q_ij(f,G)\n             +r(G_00-1)-eta*2f_n >= -eta*q\n\nfor all G>=0,f. The strict primal point rules out eta=0. Normalize eta=1.\nArbitrary values f then force out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), and arbitrary PSD G\nforces the full matrix M(r,lambda,h)>=0. Testing G=f=0 gives r<=q;\nweak duality and reduced exactness give r>=q. Thus r=q and the dual\nattains the exact objective value.\n\nExtend this reduced certificate to the original full format by setting\nall omitted multipliers to zero. We have proved the precise re-selection:\n\n    At every positive h there EXISTS a tight full certificate with\n        lambda_(j,n)=0 for ALL j in P.             (4)\n\nIn particular nu_n=0 can be imposed, and the remaining outgoing\nterminal weights sum to exactly two. This does not say that every\noptimal certificate already has this support. It does not assert that\na source vector can be held fixed during the re-selection.\n\nThis argument is the ordinary finite-dimensional separation proof only\nfor the reduced system just justified. It does not repeat or claim as\nnew the historical global minimizer/KKT interface.\n\n## 4. Exact terminal square and monotone probability coordinates\n\nFor a certificate satisfying (4), write\n\n    alpha_j=lambda_(n,j)/2 >=0, j in P,\n    sum_(j in P)alpha_j=1,\n    y_j=x_j-g_j,  delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2,\n    V_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in P)alpha_j y_j.            (5)\n\nLet lambda^P denote the unchanged multipliers on ordered pairs inside P.\nDirect expansion, with delta_star=y_star=0, gives the FULL identity\n\n    M(q,lambda,h)\n      =||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha,                  (6)\n\nwhere\n\n    R_alpha=q||x_0||^2\n                  -2sum_(j in P)alpha_j delta_j-||V_alpha||^2\n                  +sum_(i,j in P)lambda^P_ij Q_ij. (7)\n\nThe prefix flow cancels all function values in R_alpha. In detail,\nfor each finite i<n it is\n\n    out_i^P-in_i^P=2alpha_i,\n\nbecause the removed terminal outgoing edge n->i supplied incoming flow\n2alpha_i in the full certificate. R_alpha is thus a quadratic form\nin (x_0,g_0,...,g_(n-1)).\n\nFull PSD is EQUIVALENT to R_alpha>=0: one direction follows from (6),\nand the other follows by minimizing the full quadratic over the\nindependent formal vector g_n. This is an exact Schur complement,\nnot the omission of an unobserved kernel direction.\n\nThe same identity is a necessary-and-sufficient certificate\nparameterization of this selected subface: nonnegative prefix\nmultipliers with the displayed prefix flow, alpha in the probability\nsimplex, and the complete PSD remainder R_alpha.\n\nFor explicit chronological coefficients define\n\n    w_(-1)=alpha_star,\n    w_t=alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j, 0<=t<n.\n\nThen 0<=w_(-1)<=w_0<=...<=w_(n-1)=1, and\n\n    V_alpha=alpha_star x_0\n            -sum_(t<n)[h_t w_t-(w_t-w_(t-1))]g_t. (8)\n\nThis keeps all earlier gradient coordinates. In particular\nlambda_(n,star)=2alpha_star<=2. The formula is not a scalar-mass\nreplacement for the prefix PSD condition (7).\n\nOn ANY actual matching worst case for this selected certificate,\n\n    g_n=V_alpha,  R_alpha=0,\n    alpha_j Q_(n,j)=0, lambda^P_ij Q_ij=0.          (9)\n\nThus re-selection gives an actual terminal gradient formula for the\nmatching primal. It does not impose such a formula on every legal\ntrajectory; away from equality the square in (6) measures its defect.\n\n## 5. Relation to the remote-edge obstruction and source variation cost\n\nFor a two-step suffix k=n-2, the selected support eliminates remote\nforward edges i->n and the local edge (n-1)->n. It does NOT eliminate\nremote i->(n-1) for i<k. Those edges are within the complete prefix\nand remain in (7). In particular the old three-step counterexample\nat h=(2,9/4,3) already has no incoming edges at its final sample3:\nits hostile edge0->2 survives this reduction unchanged.\n\nAt a full stationary matching tuple with nu_k>0, the common strict\nbarrier derived directly from interpolation still applies:\nlambda_(k,k+1)=0 across the full optimal set, with positive old slack.\nThe terminal re-selection may alter earlier source coefficients and\nmay alter which actual matching primal supplies earlier-cut stationarity.\nOne cannot transport all stationarity equations to an arbitrarily\nchosen reselected certificate.\n\nFor any joint change from a fixed stationary base, the exact old-primal\nidentity remains\n\n    q'-q >= sum lambda'_ij(-Q_ij(old))\n                  -sum_t (h'_t-h_t)<g_t,z_t(lambda'-lambda)>.\n\nNeither the terminal square (6) nor dropping the redundant terminal\nincoming inequalities cancels this inactive cost or bilinear term.\n\n## 6. Literature check and scope\n\nThe explicit maximum-of-quadratics conjugate construction is consistent\nwith Taylor, Hendrickx and Glineur, Smooth strongly convex interpolation\nand exact worst-case performance of first-order methods,\narXiv:1502.05666, Theorem4 and Remark2. Their Theorem6 uses a full-rank\nquadratic transcript to establish fixed-step PEP duality. Those sections\nwere read in the downloaded paper. All construction, reduced-system\nexactness, separation, and full square identities needed here are\nwritten out, rather than imported as unexplained external theorems.\nA targeted Matlas query returned unrelated results; no such result was used.\n\nThis is a concrete, validly scoped route to re-selection and a complete\nmatrix parameterization of the resulting subface. It is not a descent\ntheorem at a global minimizer, does not remove the surviving remote\nprefix edges, and does not resolve Huber tightness or the exact DP mass\nrestriction.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["798907612a01ea26", "56f99268b45fbce8", "85e7cc28c72d0d8d"], "artifact": "research/max2_terminal_reselection.md", "external_source": "arXiv:1502.05666 Theorem4 Remark2 Theorem6, read locally; construction re-derived"}, "glossary": {"P": "Prefix index set {star,0,...,n-1}, which retains every ordered interpolation inequality.", "H_j": "Quadratic conjugate lower model a_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||²/2.", "F": "The globally convex 1-smooth maximal prefix extension H*, constructed explicitly in this finding.", "alpha_j": "Nonnegative terminal outgoing multiplier lambda_(n,j)/2; sums to one.", "V_alpha": "x_n-sum_(j in P)alpha_j(x_j-g_j).", "R_alpha": "The complete prefix PSD quadratic remainder in the exact identity M=||g_n-V_alpha||²+R_alpha.", "w_t": "Cumulative terminal weights alpha_star+sum_(j<=t)alpha_j; w_(n-1)=1."}}
{"id": "364ad0d46c4224cb", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:33:27.563333+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "The four-mode f3 obstruction also gives exact rigidity within the intrinsic certificate class: both the nonnegative proof weights and the full finite-edge interpolation certificate are unique at D=6+4sqrt2, and its PSD remainder is zero. A useful indefinite state nevertheless survives: E>=2(1+sqrt2)|<g_2-g_3,sqrt2 g_1+g_2>|, with an explicit interval of actual nonnegative edge coefficients.", "evidence": "See added Sections5-6 of research/max4_tight_benchmark_storage_obstruction.md. Equality and Q_i,star=0 force U=x_0 on four independent gradient rows, uniquely fixing u=(sqrt2,2+sqrt2,2+sqrt2,2+sqrt2). The middle-cut Huber restricts positive finite interpolation edges to01,10,21,31,23,32; coefficient matching forces (2,2lambda,2lambda,2lambda,0,D), where lambda=1+sqrt2. This does not establish uniqueness for a full PEP certificate allowing optimum-outgoing edges. The exact three-point quantity T=I21+I32-I31=<g2-g3,sqrt2*g1+g2>, where Iij=-Qij, gives E-theta*T=E_prefix+(2lambda-theta)I21+(D-theta)I32+(2lambda+theta)I31>=0 for |theta|<=2lambda. Thus a family of indefinite boundary forms survives even though every positive quadratic gradient-memory remainder vanishes. A profitable cross-block use of that family is still open.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"research_path": "research/max4_tight_benchmark_storage_obstruction.md", "related_findings": ["7e5ba92b7db8211a", "b02755c6f17e5694"], "status": "exact scoped rigidity and signed-slack state; no improving join yet", "verification_status": "unverified, no extra verifier call"}, "glossary": {"T": "The indefinite three-point form <g_2-g_3,sqrt(2)g_1+g_2> for the displayed three-step schedule.", "I_ij": "The nonnegative smooth-convex interpolation slack -Q_ij."}}
{"id": "6dd1df44d30531f8", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:43:35.738143+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "The all-cross interpolation plan has an exact finite moment update and a concrete obstruction to endpoint compression. Two explicit legal h=(1,1,1) trajectories share(D,E,d,U,e,p,q,f_start,f_end,V,H), including both objective and primitive dual energies, but their reverse-anchor interpolation sums differ by1/225. Rank-one cross sums require the weighted proximal-point and primal-value moments, or an equivalent correctly signed mixed-moment bound.", "evidence": "# Exact cross-block moments and an endpoint-state collision\n\nStatus: unverified exact identities and an explicit counterexample to a proposed compression. No new optimization or verifier call. A competitive closed moment certificate is not yet available.\n\nWork at smoothness1 with\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.\n\nDefine transformed sample coordinates and values\n\n    z_i=x_i-g_i, F_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nThen the actual interpolation slack is\n\n    I_ij=-Q_ij=F_j-F_i-<g_i,z_j-z_i> >=0.           (1)\n\n## 1. A finite exact moment tuple\n\nFor any nonnegative weights u on actual samples define\n\n    D=sum u_i, U=sum u_i g_i, Z=sum u_i z_i,\n    M=sum u_i F_i,\n    N=sum u_i(<g_i,z_i>-F_i),\n    C=M+N=sum u_i<g_i,z_i>.\n\nThe letters M,N here denote weighted function moments, not a PSD matrix or a horizon. N is the conjugate-value moment; this notation avoids reusing the primitive capacity K.\n\nFor a block beginning at x_start and ending at x_end with value f_end, its intrinsic objective energy satisfies exactly\n\n    E=2<U,x_start>-||U||^2-2N-2D f_end.             (2)\n\nFor two weighted sets a,b of samples from the SAME function, rank-one sums of every directed cross edge satisfy\n\n    T_ab=sum_(i in a,j in b) u_i v_j I_ij\n        =D_a M_b+D_b N_a-<U_a,Z_b> >=0,\n    T_ba=D_b M_a+D_a N_b-<U_b,Z_a> >=0.             (3)\n\nThese equations follow by expanding (1) and factoring the product weights. In particular the internal all-pairs sum is\n\n    T_aa=D_a C_a-<U_a,Z_a> >=0.                    (4)\n\nThe moment tuple has an exact additive update. If the old head weights are scaled by alpha, the tail weights by beta, and extra weights eta,gamma are placed at the bridge point y and final point z, then\n\n    D_new=alpha D_a+beta D_b+eta+gamma,\n    U_new=alpha U_a+beta U_b+eta g_y+gamma g_z,\n    Z_new=alpha Z_a+beta Z_b+eta z_y+gamma z_z,\n    M_new=alpha M_a+beta M_b+eta F_y+gamma F_z,\n    N_new=alpha N_a+beta N_b\n             +eta(<g_y,z_y>-F_y)+gamma(<g_z,z_z>-F_z). (5)\n\nHere z_y=y-g_y and z_z=z-g_z; the repeated z is a point name in the subscript and a transformed coordinate on the right. All positions and function values in (5) refer to their actual common global coordinates. There is no reset at the start of the tail. The actual final proof weights must be nonnegative. Equation(2) then computes the combined objective energy exactly.\n\nThis supplies a precise interface for the all-cross plan. It does not establish that the elementary inequalities(2)--(4) characterize all legal moments or provide a closed accelerated certificate.\n\n## 2. Endpoint information, even including the dual slack, does not determine (3)\n\nConsider the fixed physical schedule h=(1,1,1), start x_0=12/5, minimizer0 and proof weights u=(1,1,1,1). Construct two one-dimensional convex1-smooth functions A and B, each with f(0)=0. Their sample data are:\n\n|i|x_i for A|g_i for A|f_i for A|x_i for B|g_i for B|f_i for B|\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n|0|12/5|1|293/200|12/5|1|293/200|\n|1|7/5|7/10|123/200|7/5|23/30|47/72|\n|2|7/10|1/2|39/200|19/30|13/30|283/1800|\n|3|1/5|1/5|1/50|1/5|1/5|1/50|\n\nBoth columns satisfy x_(i+1)=x_i-g_i exactly.\n\nFor function A, linearly interpolate the displayed gradients between successive points. The three slopes from the first interval to the last are3/10,2/7,3/5, all in[0,1]. Set g(x)=x for x<=1/5 and g(x)=1 for x>=12/5. Integrating this continuous monotone1-Lipschitz derivative gives precisely the displayed values: its three drops f_i-f_(i+1) are17/20,21/50,7/40.\n\nFor function B, on each interval[x_(i+1),x_i] let L_i=g_i be its length, let delta_i=g_i-g_(i+1), and place a slope1 ramp of length delta_i after a lower flat piece of length a_i. Before the ramp the derivative is g_(i+1); after it the derivative is g_i. Choose\n\n    (a_0,a_1,a_2)=(289/420,11/100,1/10).\n\nThe corresponding (L_i,delta_i) are(1,7/30),(23/30,1/3),(13/30,7/30). Each a_i lies in[0,L_i-delta_i]. The area under the derivative on such an interval is\n\n    g_i L_i-delta_i^2/2-delta_i a_i.\n\nIt is respectively731/900,223/450,247/1800 for the three intervals, yielding the displayed function values from f_3=1/50. Extend the derivative by x below1/5 and by1 above12/5. This again gives one globally defined continuous, nondecreasing1-Lipschitz derivative, and its integral is minimized at zero. Thus both constructions satisfy the full function assumptions, not just adjacent interpolation tests.\n\n## 3. All proposed endpoint data agree, but a needed reverse sum differs\n\nFor both trajectories,\n\n    D=4, d=x_0-x_3=11/5, U=12/5, e=U-d=1/5,\n    p=g_0=1, q=g_3=1/5,\n    f_0=293/200, f_3=1/50, f_0-f_3=289/200,\n    E=443/100.\n\nThe physical primitive energy, for S=3 and capacity4, is V=E-12q^2=79/20 on both instances. Their primitive dual expression is also the same:\n\n    H=2(f_0-f_3)-6q^2-|p-(d+q)/4|^2=249/100.\n\nThese equalities use the direct definitions; they do not assume any unverified primitive theorem for the schedule.\n\nNevertheless their weighted proximal-point sums and primal moments differ:\n\n    Z_A=23/10, M_A=281/200,\n    Z_B=67/30, M_B=2497/1800.\n\nThe conjugate moment N is the same, as equation(2) already implies from the shared endpoint data. At the terminal point z_3=x_3-g_3=0 and F_3=f_3-q^2/2=0. Consequently the reverse-anchor sum is\n\n    sum_i u_i I_(3,i)=M-qZ.\n\nIt equals189/200=1701/1800 on A and1693/1800 on B, a difference of1/225. Thus no formula depending only on the common tuple(D,E,d,U,e,p,q,f_0,f_3,V,H) can recover this reverse interpolation sum for all legal trajectories.\n\nThis is a counterexample to exact endpoint compression, not to inequalities that bound that sum using the tuple, and not to carrying the larger moment tuple from part1.\n\n## 4. The open quantitative step\n\nEquation(3) makes the missing quantities explicit. Substituting M=C-N trades the missing primal value moment for the within-block mixed moment C=sum u_i<g_i,z_i>. The inequality(4) provides only a lower bound on C. In a proposed energy certificate the coefficient of C after subtracting a positive cross-edge sum can have the opposite sign, so (4) cannot silently be used as an upper bound.\n\nA successful all-cross join must retain a valid bound with the correct sign for this moment, or preserve C and its function-value counterpart through the next join. Additivity(5) alone is not that bound. This is the precise remaining mechanism in the moment plan; no exact-profile improvement is claimed.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_cross_block_moment_join", "research_path": "research/max4_cross_block_moment_state.md", "related_findings": ["90d0a912690abdbc", "7e5ba92b7db8211a"], "status": "exact interface and counterexample; quantitative sign-controlled closure remains open", "verification_status": "unverified; only tiny exact rational checks, no solver or verifier call"}, "glossary": {"Z": "Weighted proximal-point sum sum u_i(x_i-g_i).", "M": "Weighted transformed-value moment sum u_i(f_i-||g_i||^2/2).", "N": "Weighted conjugate-value moment sum u_i(<g_i,x_i-g_i>-f_i+||g_i||^2/2).", "C": "Within-block mixed moment M+N=sum u_i<g_i,x_i-g_i>.", "T_ab": "The nonnegative rank-one weighted sum of every interpolation slack from block a to block b."}}
{"id": "a73ea9d1697aa969", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:47:40.172642+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Selected terminal reduction package is now self-contained for EVERY nonnegative schedule, including zero steps and n=0. The zero-step certificate lift selects positive-step query gradients and original terminal n, embeds the compressed PSD matrix into the original formal space, and imposes no duplicate-gradient equalities.", "evidence": "# Selected terminal reduction package, including zero and empty schedules\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED; ONE fact_submit authorized by TASK.md and master_guidance aa9a56da67cdfc12.\n\n## Statement\n\nExact unqueried-terminal reduction and full-certificate re-selection for arbitrary nonnegative gradient-descent schedules.\n\nFor every integer n>=0, every L>0, and every finite h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) in [0,infinity)^n, let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). The empty schedule n=0 is allowed.\n\nFor the following finite formulations use normalized smoothness1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, normalized sampled values f_i, formal gradients g_i, and x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. Put I_n={star,0,...,n}, J_pre={star,0,...,n-1}, and Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 for distinct i,j in I_n. When n=0, J_pre={star}.\n\n(a) The finite Gram maximization of 2f_n subject to G_pr>=0, G_pr_(x0,x0)<=1, all Q_ij<=0 with distinct i,j in J_pre, and all Q_(n,j)<=0 with j in J_pre, has attained optimal value W_n(h). Here G_pr is the full Gram matrix of the n+2 formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with no rank constraint. No incoming terminal inequality Q_(j,n)<=0 is imposed. For any feasible prefix jets in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space, define cjet_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j, H_j(v)=cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2, H(v)=max_(j in J_pre)H_j(v), and F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)). Then F is an everywhere finite convex differentiable 1-smooth function interpolating all prefix jets, F(0)=0=min F, and it is the pointwise maximal such prefix interpolant. For every reduced-feasible terminal pair (g_n,f_n), F(x_n)>=f_n. At an optimal reduced transcript, F(x_n)=f_n and grad F(x_n)=g_n; hence every optimal reduced transcript is fully interpolable and obeys the deleted incoming terminal inequalities.\n\n(b) There exist nonnegative multipliers lambda_ij on all distinct pairs in I_n, with lambda_(j,n)=0 for every j in J_pre, finite-node flow sum_j lambda_ij-sum_j lambda_ji=2*1_(i=n), and a FULL positive semidefinite quadratic certificate\nM_cert=W_n(h)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\nafter the GD substitution. This certificate is PSD on the original formal space (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including every gradient at a zero step, without imposing equality between duplicated formal gradients. Thus some tight full certificate has no incoming edge at the unqueried terminal sample for every nonnegative schedule. For schedules with positive steps this follows from an explicit strict primal and a separation argument. If there are zero steps, choose the original index of each positive step as the representative of its compressed query and the original index n as the terminal representative; embedding a certificate for the positive-step compression gives the asserted full-space certificate. The all-zero and empty schedules have W_n(h)=1 and the direct certificate lambda_(n,star)=2, all other lambda zero, with M_cert=||x_0-g_n||^2.\n\n(c) For any real certificate level q_cert and any h as above, certificates with no incoming terminal multipliers are parameterized exactly as follows. Choose alpha_j>=0 for j in J_pre, with sum_j alpha_j=1, and nonnegative prefix multipliers lambda^pre_ij for distinct i,j in J_pre whose finite prefix flow is out_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i for 0<=i<n. Define y_j=x_j-g_j, delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2, V_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j y_j, with y_star=delta_star=0, and\nR_alpha=q_cert||x_0||^2-2sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j delta_j-||V_alpha||^2+sum_(i!=j in J_pre)lambda^pre_ij Q_ij.\nThe prefix flow cancels all f_i in R_alpha. Set lambda_(n,j)=2alpha_j, all incoming terminal multipliers zero, and retain lambda^pre. The complete identity is\nM_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha.\nConsequently full PSD of M_cert is equivalent to PSD of the entire prefix quadratic R_alpha in (x_0,g_0,...,g_(n-1)). For n>=1, with w_(-1)=alpha_star and w_t=alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j, one has 0<=w_(-1)<=w_0<=...<=w_(n-1)=1 and\nV_alpha=alpha_star x_0-sum_(t<n)[h_t w_t-(w_t-w_(t-1))]g_t.\nFor n=0, alpha_star=1, V_alpha=x_0, and R_alpha=(q_cert-1)||x_0||^2. Any actual normalized trajectory attaining a feasible q_cert has g_n=V_alpha, R_alpha=0, and multiplier complementarity.\n\nThis result deletes no interpolation inequalities inside the queried prefix. It asserts existence of a selected optimal certificate, not that every optimal certificate has the selected support. It neither establishes Huber tightness nor removes earlier remote forward edges, transfers earlier-cut stationarity under arbitrary re-selection, or proves the project's asymptotic exact-profile conjecture.\n\n## Proof\n\n1. Normalization, interpolation necessity, and elementary bounds.\n\nFor an original instance let R=||x_0-x_star||>0. The normalized function\nF_0(z)=[f(x_star+R z)-f(x_star)]/(L R^2)\nis convex differentiable with gradient Lipschitz constant1, a minimizer0,\nand initial norm1. Its GD uses the same h and its normalized objective\nis 2F_0(z_n). This also proves the converse scaling correspondence.\nWe may work with smoothness1 and the optimum sample zero.\n\nFor a differentiable function K with 1-Lipschitz gradient, integration\nof its gradient along a segment gives\nK(x+d)<=K(x)+<grad K(x),d>+||d||^2/2.\nFor convex K, apply this to K(z)-<grad K(x_i),z>, whose minimum is\nat x_i, taking a unit negative-gradient step from x_j. Its minimum\nvalue is no larger than the stepped value, so\nK(x_j)-K(x_i)-<grad K(x_i),x_j-x_i>\n    >=||grad K(x_j)-grad K(x_i)||^2/2.\nThis is exactly Q_ij<=0 for the actual samples, with our stated\norientation of Q.\n\nFor a normalized legal instance, put D_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t), D_0=1.\nSince grad K(0)=0, ||g_i||<=||x_i|| and ||x_(i+1)||<=(1+h_i)||x_i||.\nThus ||x_i||,||g_i||<=D_i, and\n0<=K(x_i)<=||x_i||^2/2.\nIn particular W_n(h)<=D_n^2<infinity.\nIt is strictly positive: choose the scalar quadratic K(x)=kappa*x^2/2\nwith kappa=1/[2(1+sum_t h_t)] in (0,1], starting at1.\nEvery factor 1-kappa h_t is positive, and its normalized error is\nkappa*product_t(1-kappa h_t)^2>0.\n\n2. Explicit interpolation and the maximal prefix extension.\n\nLet any finite collection of prefix jets (x_j,g_j,f_j), j in J_pre,\nsatisfy every ordered prefix inequality and include the zero optimum jet.\nDefine cjet_j,H_j,H,F as in the statement, on the same finite-dimensional\nEuclidean space. The function H(v)-||v||^2/2 is the maximum of finitely\nmany affine functions and hence is convex. H is continuous, finite,\n1-strongly convex, and has quadratic growth. For each x, the function\n<v,x>-H(v) therefore has a unique maximizer, denoted v(x).\n\nThe maximizer condition is x in partial H(v(x)). For any x,x',\nthe two strong subgradient inequalities for H, added together, give\n<x-x',v(x)-v(x')>>=||v(x)-v(x')||^2.\nConsequently ||v(x)-v(x')||<=||x-x'||.\nThe definition of F, evaluated at v(x) and v(x'), gives\n0<=F(x')-F(x)-<v(x),x'-x>\n    <=<v(x')-v(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.\nThus F is differentiable with grad F(x)=v(x), and its gradient is\n1-Lipschitz. It is convex as a supremum of affine functions and finite\nby the stated quadratic growth.\n\nFor i,j in J_pre direct expansion gives H_j(g_i)-a_i=Q_ij.\nThe term with j=i equals zero and every other term is nonpositive;\nhence H(g_i)=a_i. The active quadratic H_i has gradient x_i at g_i,\nand H>=H_i with equality there, so x_i is a subgradient of H at g_i.\nThe unique maximizer defining F(x_i) is consequently g_i, and\nF(x_i)=<x_i,g_i>-a_i=f_i. This proves interpolation of all prefix jets.\nIn particular F(0)=0 and grad F(0)=0, so zero is a global minimizer.\nThe same construction, for any finite index set including the zero\njet, proves sufficiency of ALL pairwise Q inequalities for a legal\nconvex 1-smooth interpolant; repeated positions cause no exception.\n\nIf K is any other legal function interpolating the prefix, apply the\nnecessity calculation in paragraph1 between its sample at an arbitrary\nx and each prefix jet j. Writing g=grad K(x), those inequalities give\nH_j(g)<=<x,g>-K(x) for every j. Thus\nK(x)<=<x,g>-H(g)<=F(x).\nThis proves the claimed pointwise maximality without importing an\ninterpolation or conjugacy theorem from a paper.\n\n3. Exact value of the reduced Gram problem and primal attainment.\n\nRealize any feasible Gram matrix by vectors in dimension at most n+2;\nif its rank is zero, one may embed its zero vectors in R.\nThe complete prefix inequalities give the function F of paragraph2.\nIts gradients at every queried prefix point equal the supplied g_i,\nso the GD recursion reaches the same supplied x_n. No terminal gradient\nis used to determine x_n, even when some steps vanish.\n\nFor the reduced terminal pair, the retained inequalities give\nH_j(g_n)<=<x_n,g_n>-f_n for all j in J_pre. Therefore\nf_n<=<x_n,g_n>-H(g_n)<=F(x_n).\nIf ||x_0||>0, normalization of this actual function yields\n2F(x_n)<=W_n(h)||x_0||^2<=W_n(h).\nIf ||x_0||=0, all actual prefix iterates and x_n are the minimizer0,\nso F(x_n)=0 and 2f_n<=0<W_n(h).\nThus the reduced supremum is at most W_n(h).\nEvery actual legal normalized trajectory is feasible for the reduced\nGram problem, so the reverse inequality holds.\n\nFor clarity the supremum is attained. Consider first the FULL Gram\nproblem, including every incoming terminal inequality. Its feasible\nset is closed. Paragraph2 applied to the entire finite index set\nI_n realizes every feasible point as a legal function; hence paragraph1\nbounds all sampled gradients, all Gram entries by Cauchy--Schwarz,\nand all values 0<=f_i<=D_i^2/2. The feasible set is therefore compact\nin finite-dimensional matrix/value space. It is nonempty and has an\nobjective maximizer. Necessity and the interpolation construction\nshow its value is exactly W_n(h). The same full optimizer is reduced\nfeasible, so it also attains the reduced value.\n\nAt any reduced optimizer, the chain\nW_n(h)=2f_n<=2F(x_n)<=W_n(h)||x_0||^2<=W_n(h)\nhas equal endpoints and W_n(h)>0. Thus ||x_0||=1,\nf_n=F(x_n), and <x_n,g_n>-H(g_n)=F(x_n).\nThe maximizer of that conjugate expression is unique, so\ng_n=grad F(x_n). The supplied terminal jet therefore belongs to the\nsame legal F as the prefix and satisfies every deleted incoming\ninequality. This also proves the assertion for every reduced optimizer,\nnot just one optimizer chosen from the full feasible set.\n\n4. Dual attainment for positive schedules, by separation.\n\nAssume n>=1 and h_t>0 for every t<n. Let E_red be the retained\nordered pair set:\n{(i,j): i,j in J_pre, i!=j} union {(n,j):j in J_pre}.\nUse the matrix/value variables of the reduced primal.\n\nThere is a strictly feasible point with G_pr positive definite,\nG_pr_(x0,x0)<1, and every retained Q strictly negative.\nChoose n+2 distinct real theta_l in (0,1), let A_quad be the diagonal\nmatrix with these entries, let K(x)=<x,A_quad x>/2, and choose x_0\nwith every coordinate nonzero and norm less than1.\nWrite Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), including Poly_0=1.\nThe columns (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) are evaluations at the theta_l of the\nn+2 polynomials 1,z Poly_0,...,z Poly_n, times nonzero initial\ncoordinate factors. These polynomials have distinct degrees\n0,1,...,n+1 and nonzero leading coefficients. A polynomial of degree\nat most n+1 vanishing at n+2 distinct nodes is zero, so the evaluation\nmatrix is invertible and G_pr is positive definite.\nFor i!=j finite, Poly_i-Poly_j is a nonzero polynomial of degree\nat most n; it cannot vanish at all chosen nodes. Poly_i cannot vanish\nat all nodes either. Thus all finite sample positions are distinct\nfrom each other and from star. Direct substitution for this quadratic\ngives\nQ_ij=-<x_i-x_j,A_quad(I-A_quad)(x_i-x_j)>/2<0.\nThis proves the full stated strictness.\n\nLet w_opt=W_n(h), which is finite by paragraph1. Consider the open\nconvex set O of tuples (s,t,c) for which there exist G_pr>=0 and real\nsample values f with\ns_ij>Q_ij(f,G_pr) for ij in E_red,\nt>G_pr_(x0,x0)-1, and c<2f_n.\nIt is open since it is a union of open orthants; it is convex by\nlinearity of every Q in the Gram/value variables and convexity\nof the PSD cone. The point (0,0,w_opt) is not in O.\nFinite-dimensional separation of a point from a nonempty open convex\nset gives a nonzero linear functional, chosen nonnegative on O\nrelative to that point. Independent upward changes of each s_ij\nand of t, and downward changes of c, force its coefficients to be\n(lambda_ij,rho,-eta) with lambda_ij,rho,eta>=0.\nTaking limits to the boundary of each strict inequality yields,\nfor ALL G_pr>=0 and ALL real sample values f,\nsum_(E_red)lambda_ij Q_ij(f,G_pr)\n    +rho(G_pr_(x0,x0)-1)-eta*2f_n >= -eta*w_opt.       (S)\n\nIf eta=0, some lambda_ij or rho is positive, since the functional is\nnonzero. Substitution of the strict primal point constructed above\nmakes the left side of (S) strictly negative, a contradiction.\nHence eta>0 and we divide the coefficients by eta to set eta=1.\n\nThe f_i are unrestricted real variables in (S). Their coefficients\nmust vanish:\nout_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), for 0<=i<=n.\nAfter their cancellation, (S) says\ntrace(M_cert(rho,lambda,h) G_pr)-rho>=-w_opt for every G_pr>=0.\nIf this matrix had a negative quadratic direction, scaling the\ncorresponding rank-one PSD G_pr would contradict this inequality.\nIt is therefore PSD. Taking G_pr=0 gives rho<=w_opt.\nConversely the flow, lambda>=0, and full PSD give, for every reduced\nfeasible point,\n2f_n<=rho G_pr_(x0,x0)<=rho\nbecause rho>=0. Thus w_opt<=rho. Equality follows.\nSetting lambda_(j,n)=0 on the omitted pairs gives the required\ntight full certificate on the original positive-step formal space.\n\n5. Zero-step compression and lifting in the ORIGINAL formal space.\n\nSuppose n>=1 and exactly m>=1 of the original steps are positive.\nList their original indices as\n0<=i_0<i_1<...<i_(m-1)<=n-1,\nand define the positive compressed schedule hhat_s=h_(i_s),\n0<=s<m. Deleting zero steps changes no actual iterate; therefore\nW_m(hhat)=W_n(h) by the same function/initial-point trajectories in\nboth directions.\n\nApply paragraph4 to hhat, obtaining a tight incoming-terminal-free\ncertificate lambdahat on {star,0,...,m}.\nDefine the injective index map\niota(star)=star, iota(s)=i_s for 0<=s<m, and iota(m)=n.\nOn the original formal space define\nxhat_0=x_0, ghat_s=g_(i_s) for s<m, ghat_m=g_n.\nIts compressed formal positions satisfy\nxhat_s=x_0-sum_(r<s)h_(i_r)g_(i_r)=x_(i_s) for s<m,\nand\nxhat_m=x_0-sum_(r<m)h_(i_r)g_(i_r)=x_n.\nThese are polynomial identities in independent original formal vectors:\nthe omitted coefficients h_t are ZERO. They do not use any equality\nbetween gradients sampled at identical actual positions.\n\nPut lambda_(iota(s),iota(t))=lambdahat_(s,t) on mapped pairs, and\nput every other original multiplier equal to zero.\nAt each mapped finite node the original flow equals its compressed\nflow; at each unmapped node both in-flow and out-flow vanish.\nThe mapped terminal is n, so the original finite flow is exactly\nout_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n). No multiplier points into original n.\nEvery mapped Q expression equals the corresponding compressed\nQ expression after identifying the mapped values, whose coefficients\ncancel by flow.\n\nLet T_pick be the linear map selecting\n(x_0,g_(i_0),...,g_(i_(m-1)),g_n)\nfrom (x_0,g_0,...,g_n). The ORIGINAL full certificate matrix is precisely\nT_pick^T Mhat_cert T_pick and is PSD because Mhat_cert is PSD.\nUnmapped gradient columns are zero. No duplicate-gradient constraint\nor auxiliary squared penalty is imposed or needed. This proves the\nclaimed original-index, full-formal-space lift, including leading,\ninterior, and trailing zero steps.\n\nIf m=0, including the empty schedule n=0, every actual iterate is x_0.\nSmoothness at the minimizer gives 2f(x_0)<=||x_0||^2, and the scalar\nquadratic x^2/2 attains equality. Thus W_n(h)=1.\nSet lambda_(n,star)=2 and every other multiplier to zero.\nIt has exactly the required flow and no incoming terminal edges.\nSince x_n=x_0 as a formal identity, its full quadratic is\n||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,star)=||x_0-g_n||^2.\nThis treats all-zero schedules and n=0 without any Slater assertion\nabout duplicated points. Parts(a) and the maximal extension remain\nvalid independently for these schedules by paragraphs2--3.\n\n6. Exact terminal-square and full prefix-PSD parameterization.\n\nTake ANY certificate whose incoming terminal multipliers vanish.\nThe finite terminal flow gives sum_(j in J_pre)lambda_(n,j)=2.\nConsequently alpha_j=lambda_(n,j)/2 is a nonnegative probability\nvector. All other multipliers lie inside the prefix, and their finite\nflow is out_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i for i<n.\nFor any such alpha and prefix multipliers define y_j,delta_j,V_alpha,\nR_alpha as in the statement, taking the star quantities equal to zero.\n\nExpanding the outgoing terminal sum gives\n2sum_j alpha_j Q_(n,j)\n =2f_n-2sum_j alpha_j f_j\n       +2<g_n,sum_j alpha_j x_j-x_n>\n       +sum_j alpha_j||g_n-g_j||^2\n =2f_n-2sum_j alpha_j delta_j\n       +||g_n-V_alpha||^2-||V_alpha||^2.\nSubstituting into the full certificate proves\nM_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha.\nIn R_alpha the coefficient of each finite f_i is\n(out_i^pre-in_i^pre)-2alpha_i=0; it contains no f_n.\nIt is the complete quadratic form on ALL original prefix vectors.\nThe vector V_alpha is independent of the formal terminal gradient g_n.\nHence full PSD is equivalent to prefix PSD: prefix PSD implies full\nPSD from the square identity, and full PSD implies prefix PSD by\nsetting the free formal vector g_n equal to V_alpha.\nThis is true even with zero-step duplicates; it is not a test only\non legal transcripts.\n\nConversely any alpha and nonnegative prefix multipliers with the\nstated prefix flow define a full multiplier family by\nlambda_(n,j)=2alpha_j and incoming terminal multipliers zero.\nTheir full finite flow is the required objective flow. If R_alpha\nis PSD the full square identity certifies it.
