This proves both\ndirections of the parameterization for any real q_cert.\n\nFor n>=1, expand positions in\nV_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j(x_j-g_j).\nThe coefficient of x_0 is\n1-sum_(j=0)^(n-1)alpha_j=alpha_star.\nFor t<n, the coefficient of g_t is\n-h_t+alpha_t+h_t sum_(j=t+1)^(n-1)alpha_j\n =-h_t[alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j]+alpha_t.\nWith w_t as in the statement, alpha_t=w_t-w_(t-1), giving\nthe claimed chronological formula and the nondecreasing w sequence.\nFor n=0 the probability simplex contains only alpha_star=1,\nthere are no prefix pairs, V_alpha=x_0, and\nR_alpha=(q_cert-1)||x_0||^2.\n\nFinally take an actual normalized trajectory attaining a feasible\ncertificate level q_cert. The certificate identity evaluated there is\nM_cert=sum lambda_ij Q_ij<=0, while full PSD makes it nonnegative.\nEvery term lambda_ij Q_ij is nonpositive, so each is zero.\nThe square identity and prefix PSD then imply\ng_n=V_alpha and R_alpha=0. This is an assertion about matching\nactual trajectories, not a presumed rank condition on a primal.\n\n7. Scope and proof dependencies.\n\nThe proof uses elementary smoothness, the displayed explicit convex\nconjugate construction, finite-dimensional linear algebra, compactness\nof the bounded finite Gram system, and a fully specified separating\nhyperplane argument. No historical project KKT claim or unverified\nfinding is a premise. No external paper theorem is used as a black\nbox. In particular the separation argument is used only for genuinely\npositive compressed steps, and the zero-step conclusion is obtained\nby an explicit embedding into the original formal space.\n\nOnly incoming constraints at n, which is unqueried by the recursion,\nare removed. Every ordered interpolation constraint inside J_pre\nremains. The statement implies neither Huber tightness nor a removal\nof earlier remote edges or a transfer of earlier-cut stationarity\nunder arbitrary re-selection; it does not decide the asymptotic\nexact-profile problem.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["792aa4b6dbbad8bf"], "master_guidance": "aa9a56da67cdfc12", "artifact": "research/max2_terminal_reselection_selected.md", "verification": "One selected submission authorized; not yet submitted or accepted."}, "glossary": {"Q_ij": "Signed normalized smooth-convex interpolation residual f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||²/2.", "J_pre": "Prefix sample index set {star,0,...,n-1}, or {star} for n=0.", "E_red": "All distinct ordered prefix pairs together with all outgoing terminal pairs (n,j), j in J_pre.", "M_cert": "Full formal-vector quadratic certificate q_cert||x_0||²-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij after GD substitution.", "q_cert": "A real objective upper-certificate level; selected tight level is W_n(h).", "w_opt": "The exact reduced/full objective value W_n(h) used in the separating-hyperplane argument.", "H_j": "Conjugate lower quadratic cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||²/2 for a prefix jet.", "v(x)": "Unique vector maximizing <x,v>-H(v) in the explicitly constructed conjugate F.", "D_i": "Elementary normalized trajectory bound product_(t<i)(1+h_t).", "A_quad": "Diagonal positive quadratic Hessian with distinct eigenvalues strictly between0 and1, used for strict feasibility.", "alpha_j": "Nonnegative terminal outgoing probability lambda_(n,j)/2 for j in J_pre.", "lambda^pre": "Nonnegative interpolation multipliers on the complete queried prefix.", "y_j": "Prefix proximal point x_j-g_j, with y_star=0.", "delta_j": "Prefix value f_j-||g_j||²/2, with delta_star=0.", "V_alpha": "x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j y_j, independent of the formal terminal gradient.", "R_alpha": "The complete prefix quadratic remainder in M_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||²+R_alpha.", "w_t": "Cumulative terminal probability alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j, with w_(-1)=alpha_star.", "hhat": "Positive-step compression of the original nonnegative schedule.", "i_s": "Original index of the s-th positive step in the zero-step compression argument.", "iota": "Injection of compressed sample indices into the original indices, mapping the compressed terminal to original n.", "T_pick": "Linear coordinate-selection map from all original formal vectors to those used by the compressed certificate.", "lambdahat": "Tight full certificate for the positive compressed schedule.", "Mhat_cert": "Full PSD certificate matrix for the positive compressed schedule.", "cjet_j": "Conjugate jet value <g_j,x_j>-f_j in this terminal-extension result.", "G_pr": "Full primal Gram matrix of the formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), or its fixed point in the separation argument.", "lambda_ij": "Nonnegative multiplier for the signed ordered interpolation residual Q_ij."}}
{"id": "e1af9b59ad43e2c0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:04:21.647156+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "The mixed-gradient-moment storage P_theta has an exact nonnegative physical bridge law and a complete 2x2 terminal objective certificate. Its price is a factor 1+theta. For every finite balanced Silver horizon, the certified bound becomes strictly worse under its positive infinitesimal deformation; the derivative loss has an explicit uniform positive lower bound. This rules out that local route, not finite deformations or the unrestricted target.", "evidence": "# Mixed gradient-moment storage: exact transport and local loss\n\nStatus: candidate derivation, not verifier-accepted. No solver or new verifier\ncall is used. This completes the unfinished calculation in the previous round's\nlocal notes. It does not establish the project's unrestricted target.\n\n## Definitions\n\nNormalize smoothness to 1. For a nonnegative GD block h with samples x_i,\ng_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), let\n\n    S=sum h_i, A=S+1, q=g_n,\n    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)\n        +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-S A||q||^2,\n    b_h=sum_(i<n) h_i(h_i-2),\n    B_h=sum_(i<n) h_i(h_i-2)||g_i||^2,\n    P_theta(h)=V_h-theta(B_h-b_h||q||^2).\n\nHere B_h is a trajectory quantity, whereas b_h is a schedule scalar; neither is\nthe project's benchmark denominator B_n. For actual samples of the same convex\n1-smooth function set\n\n    I_ij=f_j-f_i-<g_i,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2/2 >=0.\n\nThe proof of this inequality can be given without an external theorem: subtract\nthe affine function with gradient g_i from f, so x_i is a minimizer of the\nresulting convex 1-smooth function. Its one-step descent bound from x_j with\nstep 1 gives f_j-f_i-<g_i,x_j-x_i> >= ||g_j-g_i||^2/2.\n\n## 1. A complete objective certificate, including the price\n\nTranslate a chosen minimizer to zero and its value to zero. Write X=x_n.\nTelescoping squared distances and expanding the optimum slacks gives\n\n    V_h=||x_0||^2-||X||^2-2S f_n-S A||q||^2\n        -2 sum h_i I_(i,*),\n    B_h=||X||^2-||x_0||^2\n        +2 sum h_i[I_(i,*)+I_(*,i)].\n\nThus the following is an EXACT equality:\n\n    (1+theta)||x_0||^2-2(2S+1)f_n\n      =P_theta(h)\n       +2(1+theta) sum h_i I_(i,*)\n       +2theta sum h_i I_(*,i)+2A I_(n,*)\n       +(X,q)^T M_theta (X,q),                         (1)\n\n    M_theta = [[1+theta, -A],[-A, A^2-theta b_h]].\n\nFor theta>=0 all displayed optimum-edge coefficients are nonnegative. The\nmatrix is PSD exactly when theta[A^2-(1+theta)b_h]>=0. Therefore, if\n\n    P_theta(h)>=0 on every legal block trajectory,\n    theta>=0,  b_h<=A^2/(1+theta),\n\nthen W_n(h)<=(1+theta)/(2A-1). In particular the extra factor 1+theta cannot be\nomitted. Equation (1) gives the actual full boundary matrix and all new\ninterpolation multipliers, not only a distance estimate.\n\nNegative theta is not certified by this conversion: its incoming optimum\ncoefficients are negative. Even the boundary matrix fails for -1<theta<0,\nsince b_h<=S^2-2S=A^2-4A+3<A^2 and hence\ntheta[A^2-(1+theta)b_h]<0. This is a restriction on (1), not on every conceivable\nconversion of P_theta.\n\n## 2. Generalized physical primitive join with all actual multipliers\n\nTake physical head a of capacity K=1+sum a_i, ending at y with gradient g,\nthen a bridge of length 1+r, then physical tail b of capacity A=1+sum b_i,\nending at z with gradient q. Tail internal indices i mean all its gradient\nquery samples before z. Let\n\n    0<=r<A,\n    c=(K+r)/(A-r),  k=1+2c,\n    Delta=r(K+A+r)-KA,\n    J=2c[sum_(i internal to b) b_i(I_(i,y)+I_(i,z))\n          +I_(z,y)+r I_(y,z)].\n\nEvery multiplier in J is nonnegative. Repeated or coincident samples are\ninterpreted as the actual sample data; no inherited point or value is reset.\nDirect expansion yields\n\n    V_(a,1+r,b)=V_a+k V_b+c Delta(||q||^2-||g||^2)+J.   (2)\n\nFor checking (2), subtract V_a+k V_b from the left. Its function part is\n2(K+r)(f_y-f_z)-4c sum b_i(f_i-f_z). Its gradient part is\n\n    (K^2-K-r-r^2)||g||^2\n    -2c sum b_i(1-b_i)||g_i||^2\n    +[-(K+A+r-1)(K+A+r)+k(A-1)A]||q||^2.\n\nExpand J, use x_i=y-(1+r)g-sum_(j<i in b)b_j g_j and\nz=y-(1+r)g-sum_(j in b)b_j g_j. The terms containing internal gradient products\ncancel because c(A-r)=K+r. The remaining coefficients are precisely the\ndisplayed ones after c Delta(||q||^2-||g||^2) is added.\n\nMoreover\n\n    b_(a,1+r,b)=b_a+b_b+r^2-1,\n    B_(a,1+r,b)=B_a+B_b+(r^2-1)||g||^2.\n\nConsequently (2) gives the exact new storage identity\n\n    P_theta(a,1+r,b)=P_theta(a)+k P_(theta/k)(b)+J\n       +[c Delta+theta(b_a+r^2-1)](||q||^2-||g||^2).    (3)\n\nThe scalar closure equation is therefore\n\n    c Delta+theta(b_a+r^2-1)=0.                       (4)\n\nInputs require DIFFERENT labels theta and theta/k. Same-label copying is not a\nvalid consequence of (3). At theta=0, (4) is the usual primitive join, including\nits exact Silver scaling. No loss is inserted into that calibration.\n\n## 3. Nontrivial finite calibration and an exact equality test\n\nFor two empty inputs K=A=1, equation (4) reduces to\n\n    (1-theta)r^2+2(1+theta)r-(1+theta)=0.\n\nAt theta=1 it gives r=1/2, i.e. the step h=3/2, and (3) reads\n\n    P_1(3/2)=6 I_10+3 I_01 >=0.\n\nAt theta=0 it gives h=sqrt(2). This exhibits a real capacity gain when theta\nis added, but the objective certificate at theta=1 is only 2/4=1/2.\n\nFor any block and the legal full-curvature function f(x)=||x||^2/2, define\nPi_h=product_(i<n)(1-h_i). Then\n\n    P_theta(h)=[1+theta-(A^2+theta(1-b_h))Pi_h^2]||x_0||^2. (5)\n\nThis follows directly from the two identities in section 1: every optimum\nslack vanishes and q=x_n=Pi_h x_0. If a recursively constructed block is built\nfrom empty blocks using only (3)-(4), all interpolation slacks also vanish on\nthis quadratic, so the bracket in (5) must be exactly zero. It is a useful\nhostile equality check for any proposed parameters; no numerical search is\nneeded.\n\n## 4. The balanced deformation has a well-defined finite first variation\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), t=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda. For each fixed m, define a local\nfamily h_m(theta), of length 2^m-1, recursively near theta=0:\n\n    h_0(theta)=(),\n    h_(m+1)(theta)=(h_m(theta),1+r,h_m(theta/k)),\n\nwhere r,k solve (4) and k=1+2(K+r)/(A-r), with\n\n    K=A_m(theta), A=A_m(theta/k),\n    A_m(theta)=1+sum h_m(theta).\n\nThis is an implicit finite family, not an assertion that a common fixed\npositive theta works for every m. Its smooth local existence follows\ninductively from the implicit-function theorem. At theta=0, one has\n\n    K=A=lambda^m, r=t lambda^m, c=lambda, k=lambda^2.\n\nThe partial derivative of the first equation with respect to r is\n2lambda sqrt(2)lambda^m>0. Its derivative with respect to k is zero: the tail\nargument theta/k has zero k-derivative at theta=0. The derivative of the second\nequation with respect to k is 1 for the same reason. Thus the two-by-two\nJacobian is invertible. All physical steps, k and A-r remain positive in a\nsmall neighborhood for each fixed m. Inductively (3) then supplies a complete\nnonnegative interpolation certificate for P_theta. The input labels retain\nthe sign of theta. For sufficiently small theta>=0, the objective matrix in\n(1) is also PSD because b_h<A^2 at theta=0.\n\nDefine a_m=A_m'(0) and write b_m=b_(h_m(0)). The undeformed recurrence gives\n\n    b_0=0, b_(m+1)=2b_m+t^2 lambda^(2m)-1,\n    b_m=beta lambda^(2m)-gamma 2^m+1,\n    beta=(8sqrt(2)-11)/7,\n    gamma=(8sqrt(2)-4)/7.\n\nDifferentiating the capacity join at theta=0 yields\n\n    a_0=0,\n    a_(m+1)=sqrt(2)a_m\n       -[b_m+t^2 lambda^(2m)-1]/[2lambda sqrt(2)lambda^m]. (6)\n\nTo see every derivative in (6), the standard capacity map\nOmega(K,A)=(K+A+sqrt((K+A)^2+4KA))/2 has both first derivatives lambda/2\nat K=A. The derivatives of the two child capacities are a_m and\na_m/lambda^2, so their combined contribution is sqrt(2)a_m. The direct\ntheta-derivative of (4) is the second term of (6); derivatives of c disappear\nthere because Delta=0.\n\nPut eta=sqrt(2)/lambda=2-sqrt(2), d=beta+t^2=(10-6sqrt(2))/7, and\nv_m=a_m/lambda^m. Then exactly\n\n    v_(m+1)=eta v_m+[-d+gamma eta^(2m)]/[2lambda^2 sqrt(2)],\n    v_m={-d(1-eta^m)/(1-eta)\n           +gamma(eta^m-eta^(2m))/(eta-eta^2)}\n          /[2lambda^2 sqrt(2)].                       (7)\n\nIn particular\n\n    lim_(m->infty) v_m=-(10-6sqrt(2))/[14lambda sqrt(2)]<0. (8)\n\nThe operations here are: differentiate at theta=0 for each finite m, then\ntake the m-limit of the exact formula (7). No interchange with an unproved\nfixed-theta asymptotic limit is being used.\n\n## 5. Every finite balanced first variation loses after objective conversion\n\nCompare the certified bound to its own undeformed primitive bound:\n\n    R_m(theta)=(1+theta)(2lambda^m-1)/(2A_m(theta)-1).\n\nFor m>=1,\n\n    R_m'(0)=1-2a_m/(2lambda^m-1).                    (9)\n\nThis is strictly positive for every m. An explicit uniform derivative bound\nis available. Formula (7) gives v_1=1/(lambda^3 sqrt(2)). For m>=1 its forcing\nterm is at most the m=1 forcing term, and\n\n    gamma eta^2-d < (1-eta)(gamma-d),\n\nbecause the difference is gamma(eta^2+eta-1)-d eta<0, with\neta^2+eta-1=7-5sqrt(2)<0. Induction therefore gives v_m<=v_1 for all m>=1.\nIf v_m is negative, (9) is greater than 1. Otherwise the factor\n2lambda^m/(2lambda^m-1) is at most its m=1 value. Hence\n\n    R_m'(0)>=1-2/[lambda^2 sqrt(2)(2lambda-1)]>0.    (10)\n\nThus for EACH fixed finite balanced horizon, this concrete deformation is\nworse for every sufficiently small positive theta. Its large-horizon\nderivative has the limit\n\n    lim R_m'(0)=1+(10-6sqrt(2))/[14lambda sqrt(2)]>1. (11)\n\nThis closes the proposed infinitesimal balanced improvement in the specific\nP_theta storage and conversion (1). It does not rule out finite deformations,\nunbalanced trees, another conversion, or more general moment storage. In\nparticular (10) alone supplies no common neighborhood in theta for all m.\n\n## Literature and scope\n\nThe active mechanism was checked against the already downloaded exact text of\nFazlyab et al., arXiv:1705.03615, Theorem 3.1 and its full proof. Its method\nrequires a proved supply inequality, nonnegative coefficients, and an actual\nterminal PSD bound. Equations (1)-(4) give those objects for this narrower\ncandidate; that paper supplies no completeness theorem or asymptotic\nimprovement. Matlas returned no relevant long-step composition result on the\nnew targeted query, and web search provided no additional useful mechanism.\nNo external theorem is a proof premise of the algebra above.\n\nThe practical next question is whether a function-value-coupled moment can be\ntransported without paying the same initial factor, or whether a concrete\nnonlocal correlation changes the bridge closure. Re-optimizing (4) near the\nbalanced zero-label branch cannot supply the desired improvement.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_cross_block_moment_join", "local_artifact": "workers/max4/local_memory/mixed_moment_deformation.md", "related_findings": ["6dd1df44d30531f8", "90d0a912690abdbc"], "verification_status": "unverified; no solver or verifier call", "scope": "Each fixed finite balanced horizon and derivative-then-horizon limit; no common positive-theta neighborhood claimed"}, "glossary": {"P_theta": "For a physical GD block h, V_h-theta(B_h-b_h||g_n||^2), with the complete definitions in the evidence.", "B_h": "The trajectory moment sum h_i(h_i-2)||g_i||^2; distinct from the benchmark B_n.", "b_h": "The schedule scalar sum h_i(h_i-2).", "M_theta": "The 2x2 terminal boundary matrix [[1+theta,-A],[-A,A^2-theta b_h]], A=1+sum h.", "R_m": "Ratio of the deformed certified objective bound to the undeformed balanced primitive bound, explicitly defined in section 5."}}
{"id": "40601f4c6f496ab8", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:06:47.691338+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Exact last-step minimax candidate from accepted terminal fact: for every fixed nonnegative prefix, the best fixed final-step worst case equals the unrestricted worst case with exact final line search; an attained maximal interpolant in dimension<=n+1 supplies a common last-step stationary primal and forces lambda_(star,n-1)=0 in EVERY tight full certificate.", "evidence": "# Exact terminal hull and one-step minimax continuation\n\nStatus: complete candidate derivation, NOT verifier-accepted. The only proof\npredecessor is fact `1252b8823bb5a290`. The selected terminal-reduction submission\nwas already accepted in the preceding round; its one-request allocation is used.\nThis file contains one self-contained follow-on candidate and no new submission.\n\n## 1. Quantifiers and the complete prefix\n\nFix an integer n >= 1 and any fixed nonnegative prefix\n\n    h_pre = (h_0,...,h_(n-2)).\n\nThe prefix is empty when n=1. Set k=n-1. The last step b_last ranges over\n[0,infinity), and the full schedule is (h_pre,b_last). Use the normalized\nsetting L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0 and initial squared distance at most one.\nThe quantity W_n is exactly the normalized, arbitrary-finite-dimensional\nworst-case objective in fact 1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nLet C_pre be the set of pairs C=(G_pre,(f_i)_(0<=i<=k)) satisfying:\n\n* G_pre is the PSD Gram matrix of all formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_k);\n* its x_0 diagonal is at most one;\n* x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t for 0<=i<=k;\n* EVERY ordered inequality Q_ij<=0 is retained on J_pre={star,0,...,k},\n  where Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nThese conditions are imposed on Gram/value coordinates, so C_pre is a\nfinite-dimensional convex closed set. It is compact: the interpolation\nconstruction in the cited fact realizes every feasible set of prefix jets\nas a legal convex 1-smooth function. With D_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t),\none has ||x_i||,||g_i||<=D_i and 0<=f_i<=D_i^2/2. Cauchy--Schwarz bounds\nevery Gram entry. The set contains the zero data and is nonempty.\nThis argument allows all zero steps and imposes no strictness condition.\n\nWrite Delta_pre for the probability simplex on J_pre. For every C and\nalpha in Delta_pre set\n\n    y_j=x_j-g_j,    delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2,\n    y_star=0,      delta_star=0,\n    X_alpha=x_k-sum_j alpha_j y_j,\n    z_pre=g_k,\n    Phi_term(C,b_last,alpha)\n      =2 sum_j alpha_j delta_j+||X_alpha-b_last z_pre||^2.       (1)\n\nNorms and inner products in this display are Gram expressions, independent\nof the choice of a vector realization. For fixed (b_last,alpha), (1) is\nlinear in C. For fixed C, it is convex jointly in (b_last,alpha), because\nits vector inside the squared norm is affine and its other term is linear.\nThe function is continuous in all finite coordinates. Moreover delta_j>=0\nfollows from Q_(star,j)=-delta_j<=0.\n\n## 2. The exact hull formula, with its proof\n\nThe maximal prefix interpolant in the cited fact is F_C=H_C^*, where\n\n    H_C(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(j in J_pre)(<y_j,v>-delta_j).\n\nFor every x in the realizing Euclidean space,\n\n    2F_C(x)=min_(alpha in Delta_pre)\n                  [2 sum_j alpha_j delta_j\n                    +||x-sum_j alpha_j y_j||^2].              (2)\n\nHere is a direct proof, including attainment. Let v=grad F_C(x), the unique\nmaximizer of <x,v>-H_C(v) supplied by the predecessor. Let A be the nonempty\nset of indices maximizing <y_j,v>-delta_j. One has\n\n    x-v in conv{y_j:j in A}.                                  (3)\n\nTo prove this without a subgradient formula, suppose (3) fails. Separation\nfrom the finite convex hull gives a vector d such that\n<x-v,d> > max_(j in A)<y_j,d>. For small positive epsilon, the right\nderivative of the maximum of the affine functions at v in direction d is\nmax_(j in A)<y_j,d>: the finitely many strictly inactive indices remain\ninactive for all sufficiently small epsilon. The right derivative of\n<x,v+epsilon d>-H_C(v+epsilon d) is therefore positive. This contradicts\nmaximality of v. Thus choose alpha supported on A with\nx-v=sum alpha_j y_j. Its value in the right side of (2) equals 2F_C(x),\nbecause the weighted active affine values equal their maximum and\n\n    F_C(x)=<x,v>-||v||^2/2\n              -sum alpha_j(<y_j,v>-delta_j)\n          =||v||^2/2+sum alpha_j delta_j.\n\nConversely, for any alpha, H_C(u)>=||u||^2/2+\n<sum alpha_j y_j,u>-sum alpha_j delta_j for every u. Maximizing\n<x,u>-H_C(u) gives the upper bound in (2). The constructed active alpha\nattains it. For ANY minimizing alpha in (2), completing the square gives\n\n    grad F_C(x)=x-sum_j alpha_j y_j.                           (4)\n\nIndeed equality in the upper bound at the unique conjugate maximizer\nrequires precisely that equality of vectors; alternatively strict\nconvexity in the barycenter proves that all minimizing barycenters agree.\n\nThe predecessor says F_C interpolates the entire prefix, is convex and\n1-smooth, and is pointwise maximal among all such interpolants. Therefore\n\n    W_n(h_pre,b_last)\n       =max_(C in C_pre) min_(alpha in Delta_pre)\n                           Phi_term(C,b_last,alpha).          (5)\n\nFor the <= direction, any original function produces a prefix in C_pre\nand is bounded above at the resulting terminal point by F_C. For the >=\ndirection, F_C is itself legal and has the prescribed prefix and terminal\nposition. If ||x_0||<1, its unnormalized value is bounded by W times\n||x_0||^2, and scaling every vector and every value gives the converse\nnormalization at any positive maximizer. A zero initial vector gives zero\ndata by the recursion. Maxima exist by compactness and (2).\n\nEach such maximizing function lives in dimension at most n+1, the number\nof prefix formal vectors. All information at the unqueried terminal is\nconstructed in this same space. No queried-prefix inequality is removed.\n\n## 3. An elementary compact minimax lemma\n\nThe following finite-dimensional argument will be used here, rather than\nan unexplained imported minimax theorem. Let C and A be nonempty compact\nconvex sets and let P(c,a) be continuous, affine in c, and convex in a.\nThen\n\n    max_c min_a P(c,a)=min_a max_c P(c,a),                      (6)\n\nand both extrema are attained. To prove this, put m=max_c min_a P(c,a).\nFor each c let K_c={a in A:P(c,a)<=m}. Suppose some finite collection\nc_1,...,c_s has empty intersection of these sets. The set\n\n    U={u in R^s: some a in A has u_i>=P(c_i,a)-m for all i}\n\nis convex, by convexity in a, and closed, by compactness of A. It does not\ncontain zero. It has a closest point u_* to zero, with ||u_*||>0. Since\nU is invariant under addition of any vector with nonnegative coordinates,\nthe projection inequality <u_*,u-u_*>>=0 for u in U implies every coordinate\nof u_* is nonnegative. Let p_i=(u_*)_i/sum_j(u_*)_j. These are nonnegative\nand sum to one. Evaluating the same projection inequality at\nu=(P(c_i,a)-m)_i gives, for every a,\n\n    sum_i p_i P(c_i,a)\n      >=m+||u_*||^2/sum_i(u_*)_i > m.\n\nBut c_bar=sum_i p_i c_i belongs to C, and the left side is P(c_bar,a).\nThis contradicts the definition of m. Thus the closed sets K_c have the\nfinite intersection property in compact A and have a common point a_*.\nIt satisfies max_c P(c,a_*)<=m. The reverse inequality is the direct\nmin-max inequality. Continuity and compactness give all stated extrema.\n\nApplying (6) to (5) gives the exact terminal-weight minimax formula\n\n    W_n(h_pre,b_last)\n      =min_(alpha in Delta_pre) max_(C in C_pre)\n                           Phi_term(C,b_last,alpha).          (7)\n\nThis retains the full prefix Gram matrix. It does not identify that state\nwith any scalar statistic of the prefix schedule.\n\n## 4. Unbounded final steps, attainment, and one legal saddle function\n\nDefine\n\n    rho_last(h_pre)=inf_(b_last>=0) W_n(h_pre,b_last).\n\nThe value function in b_last is continuous. This follows from (5), since\nPhi_term is uniformly continuous on C_pre times Delta_pre times any\nbounded closed stepsize interval. It is convex: in (7), the maximum over\nC of the jointly convex functions in (1) is convex, and partial\nminimization over the convex simplex preserves convexity. Equivalently,\n(5) is a maximum over C of the convex functions 2F_C(x_k-b_last g_k).\n\nThe outer infimum is attained at a finite b_last. For a direct coercivity\ncheck choose\n\n    theta=1/[2(1+sum_(t<k)h_t)],\n    P_theta=product_(t<k)(1-theta h_t)>0.\n\nThe scalar legal function f(x)=theta x^2/2 starting at one yields\n\n    W_n(h_pre,b_last)>=theta P_theta^2(1-theta b_last)^2,\n\nwhich tends to infinity. Continuity now gives an attained minimum.\nIts value is positive: for each finite full schedule the cited fact proves\nW_n>0 (or choose a sufficiently small positive curvature avoiding all\nfinitely many roots). Positivity at an attained minimizer gives rho_last>0.\n\nThe exact unbounded minimax relation is\n\n    rho_last(h_pre)\n      =max_(C in C_pre) min_(b_last>=0, alpha in Delta_pre)\n                              Phi_term(C,b_last,alpha).       (8)\n\nHere are all compactness details. For each positive integer B, apply (6)\nto C_pre and [0,B] times Delta_pre. Let rho_B be the common value and\nlet C_B maximize the inner minimum. The values rho_B decrease to\nrho_last by (7). Along a subsequence with B tending to infinity,\nC_B converges to C_* in compact C_pre. For every fixed finite b_last\nand every fixed alpha, the defining inequality\nPhi_term(C_B,b_last,alpha)>=rho_B holds for all sufficiently large B.\nPassing to the limit proves Phi_term(C_*,b_last,alpha)>=rho_last for\nevery pair. This and the direct min-max inequality prove (8).\n\nChoose ANY optimal finite b_* and any alpha_* attaining (7) there.\nThen\n\n    Phi_term(C,b_*,alpha_*)<=rho_last\n      =Phi_term(C_*,b_*,alpha_*)\n      <=Phi_term(C_*,b_last,alpha)                             (9)\n\nfor every feasible C,b_last,alpha. The equality follows by applying the\ntwo inequalities to each other at the middle point. Thus an actual saddle\npair exists without a strictly feasible Gram hypothesis and without any\ncompactness assumption on the last-step ray.\n\nBy (2), the SINGLE maximal prefix interpolant F_(C_*) satisfies\n\n    min_(b_last>=0) 2F_(C_*)(x_k-b_last g_k)\n      =2F_(C_*)(x_k-b_* g_k)=rho_last.                        (10)\n\nIts initial norm is one: if it were smaller and nonzero, scaling its\nprefix to norm one would scale its positive inner minimum upward,\ncontradicting (8). If the initial vector were zero, the inner value would\nbe zero. The constructed function needs dimension at most n+1.\n\nConsequently, in the ORIGINAL normalization and arbitrary finite\ndimensions, with the first n-1 GD steps fixed in advance,\n\n    inf_(b>=0) W_n(h_pre,b)\n      =sup_(legal f,x_0,x_star)\n          inf_(b>=0) 2[f(x_k-(b/L)grad f(x_k))-f(x_star)]\n                           /(L||x_0-x_star||^2).             (11)\n\nThe supremum on the right is attained by the function in (10), after\nnormalization. Its inner infimum is attained at b_*. For an arbitrary\nlegal f, maximal extension bounds its values at EVERY point of this\nray by F_C for the same prefix, so its inner infimum is bounded by the\nright side of (8). Conversely F_(C_*) attains (8). This proves (11)\ndirectly; it does not exchange a stepsize with the changing earlier GD\nqueries. Thus optimizing the final fixed step has exactly the unrestricted\nworst-case value of allowing exact line search for that final step.\n\n## 5. Explicit geometry and a consequence for the complete dual set\n\nThe inner minimum in (8) has the closed form\n\n    min_(alpha in Delta_pre) [2sum alpha_j delta_j+||X_alpha||^2\n                      -(<X_alpha,z_pre>_+)^2/||z_pre||^2]    (12)\n\nwhen z_pre!=0; the fraction is replaced by zero when z_pre=0. This is\nobtained by minimizing a scalar quadratic in b_last, whose minimizing\nstep is <X_alpha,z_pre>_+/||z_pre||^2. Here t_+=max(t,0).\nFor z_pre=0, the actual point x_k is a minimizer of F_C and the inner\nvalue is zero (also choose alpha_k=1). Thus z_pre!=0 at C_*.\n\nLet v_*=grad F_(C_*)(x_k-b_* z_pre). Since the value in (10) is positive,\nv_*!=0. The minimizing last step is interior: at b=0 the derivative of\nF_(C_*)(x_k-b z_pre) is -||z_pre||^2<0. Differentiability and (10) give\n\n    <v_*,z_pre>=0.                                           (13)\n\nAdding the two interpolation inequalities between x_k and its terminal\npoint gives\n<z_pre-v_*,b_* z_pre>>=||z_pre-v_*||^2. Therefore\n\n    b_* >= 1+||v_*||^2/||z_pre||^2 > 1.                     (14)\n\nThis holds for EVERY minimizing last step, since any one of them can be\npaired with C_* in (9). The outgoing terminal-to-k interpolation inequality\nalso gives\n\n    delta_k >= rho_last/2+||v_*||^2/2 > 0.                 (15)\n\nIndeed its gradient-position term is b_*<v_*,z_pre>=0, and\n||v_*-z_pre||^2=||v_*||^2+||z_pre||^2.\n\nNow take ANY tight full nonnegative-flow PSD certificate at the schedule\n(h_pre,b_*), not necessarily one with the terminal support selected in\nthe predecessor. Evaluate its full quadratic identity on the actual\nnormalized trajectory of F_(C_*). The objective gap equals its certificate\nlevel rho_last, every Q_ij is nonpositive, and the quadratic is PSD.\nThus every nonnegative multiplier satisfies complementarity. Since\nQ_(star,k)=-delta_k is strictly negative by (15), EVERY such certificate\nhas\n\n    lambda_(star,k)=0.                                       (16)\n\nFor certificates with no incoming terminal multipliers, the accepted\nsquare identity gives v_*=V_alpha. Their formal last-step matrix derivative\nevaluated on this same transcript is 2<z_pre,v_*>=0. Thus one common\nactual primal supplies last-step stationarity for every selected optimal\ncertificate. No corresponding earlier-cut stationarity is claimed.\n\n## 6. A complete two-dimensional test and the role of dimension\n\nTake n=1 and in R^2 choose\n\n    x_0=(1,0),\n    z_pre=(1/3,sqrt(2)/6),\n    y_0=x_0-z_pre=(2/3,-sqrt(2)/6),\n    delta_0=1/6,    f_0=1/4.\n\nThen ||z_pre||^2=1/6, <x_0,z_pre>=1/3, and the two prefix inequalities\nare Q_(0,star)=0 and Q_(star,0)=-1/6. Define the fixed legal function by\n\n    F(x)=[v -> ||v||^2/2+max(0,<y_0,v>-1/6)]^*(x).\n\nThe cited fact proves that it is convex and 1-smooth, interpolates the\nstated initial jet, and has minimum zero at the origin. Formula (2) is\n\n    2F(x)=min_(0<=alpha<=1) [alpha/3+||x-alpha y_0||^2].\n\nAt b=3/2 and alpha=1/2, the residual is\n\n    v_*=(1/6,-sqrt(2)/6),\n    ||v_*||^2=1/12,\n    <z_pre,v_*>=0,\n    <y_0,v_*>=1/6.\n\nBoth derivatives of the jointly convex quadratic\nalpha/3+||x_0-b z_pre-alpha y_0||^2 vanish at this pair. It is feasible,\nso its global minimum on b>=0, alpha in [0,1] is 1/6+1/12=1/4.\nThis is an explicit function whose normalized error after exact line\nsearch is 1/4.\n\nFor the matching universal upper bound at b=3/2, all unlisted multipliers\nzero, set lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=lambda_(1,0)=1. They have the\ncorrect nonnegative flow, and on the FULL formal space\n\n    (1/4)||x_0||^2-2f_1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)\n           =||(1/2)x_0-g_0-g_1||^2.\n\nThis proves the exact value rho_last(empty)=1/4 without an external\none-step performance theorem.\n\nIn dimension ONE, by contrast, exact line search from any nonoptimal\npoint reaches a minimizer: the derivative has the same sign as its\ndisplacement from any chosen minimizer, so the nonnegative negative-\ngradient ray contains that minimizer. If the derivative is zero, the\npoint is already a minimizer. This applies after ANY fixed prefix.\nHence the scalar supremum of the exact-line-search error is zero.\nThe positive value (11) therefore depends essentially on permitting\nhigher finite dimensions; its minimax interchange must not be applied\nto a rank-one-restricted Gram set, which is not convex.\n\n## 7. Scope, literature audit, and unresolved bridge\n\nThis candidate derives (2), (7), and (11) together with the explicit\nattained saddle and all boundary details in a single proof. Its only\nverified building block is 1252b8823bb5a290. All additional arguments are\nincluded here; none is being silently promoted from an earlier finding.\n\nFor background the Matlas convex-conjugacy/minimax query returned generic\nduality results, none needed as a proof premise. Maurice Sion's original\n\"On general minimax theorems\", Pacific J. Math. 8 (1958), pp.171-176,\nespecially Section3, was downloaded and its definitions and proof read\nthrough the browser's PDF extraction. Its broader one-compact-set theorem\nis consistent with this finite convex setting, but Section3 above supplies\nthe particular elementary separation proof used here, and Section4 supplies\nthe unbounded-ray limit and attainment separately. No paper theorem is\nused as a black box.\n\nThe full queried-prefix state remains in C_pre. Earlier steps change this\nfeasible set, so (11) cannot be iterated backward as a scalar recurrence.\nNeither (12) nor (16) establishes Huber tightness, eliminates remote forward\nprefix edges, or proves the Zhang--Jiang exact-profile lower bound. The next\ntarget-facing question is whether the geometric inner value (12) admits\na useful lower bound under a realizable, explicitly constructed family\nof full prefixes. Its dimension-two one-step witness shows why restricting\nthat question to scalar exact-line-search adversaries would lose everything.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "plan_id": "36cbeeb6e095e35c", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/terminal_hull_linesearch.md", "status": "Complete scoped candidate, unverified; no new verifier allocation", "scope": "All earlier steps remain fixed and all queried-prefix constraints retained; no earlier-step minimax interchange or exact-profile claim", "subgoals": ["Exact terminal simplex/hull formula with active-plane proof", "Compact convex minimax using finite-dimensional separation", "Unbounded final-step saddle and original-normalization exact-line-search equality", "Common attained primal, final step strictly greater than1, and whole-dual-set penultimate star-flow exclusion", "Explicit 2D one-step value1/4 versus scalar exact-line-search value0"], "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none"}, "glossary": {"C_pre": "Compact convex set of full prefix Gram/value data with every ordered queried-prefix interpolation constraint.", "G_pre": "PSD Gram matrix of (x_0,g_0,...,g_(n-1)), retaining all original sample coordinates.", "h_pre": "The fixed nonnegative first n-1 steps, before the final stepsize is optimized.", "b_last": "The nonnegative final stepsize, with the queried prefix held fixed.", "Delta_pre": "Probability simplex indexed by {star,0,...,n-1}.", "X_alpha": "x_(n-1)-sum_j alpha_j y_j.", "z_pre": "The last queried gradient g_(n-1).", "Phi_term": "2sum_j alpha_j delta_j+||X_alpha-b_last z_pre||², affine in prefix Gram/value data and jointly convex in (b_last,alpha).", "rho_last": "inf_(b_last>=0) W_n(h_pre,b_last).", "F_C": "Pointwise maximal convex1-smooth interpolant of a feasible full prefix C, supplied by fact1252b8823bb5a290.", "v_*": "Terminal gradient of the attained saddle interpolant at an optimal final step."}}
{"id": "eedba6be86f2ff0a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:17:47.986800+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Unequal-block extension of the two-bridge primitive obstruction: for any three independently chosen nonnegative quadratic-balanced blocks with capacities A,B,C, every primitive join(a,1+r,b,1+s,c), r,s>=0, has total capacity at most max{Omega(Omega(A,B),C),Omega(A,Omega(B,C))}. The three exact quadratic/cut tests already have this sharp scalar maximum.", "evidence": "# Unequal quadratic-balanced blocks cannot improve a two-bridge primitive join\n\nStatus: self-contained candidate obstruction, not verifier-accepted. This\nextends finding2599c72fdf8542a0 from three identical blocks to three independent\nblocks. No solver or verifier call is used.\n\nLet a,b,c be finite nonnegative physical GD blocks, with capacities\n\n    A=1+sum a_i, B=1+sum b_i, C=1+sum c_i.\n\nAssume each block is quadratic-balanced in the explicit sense\n\n    |product_i(1-a_i)|=1/A,\n    |product_i(1-b_i)|=1/B,\n    |product_i(1-c_i)|=1/C.                           (1)\n\nEmpty blocks are allowed. For r,s>=0, form\n\n    h=(a,1+r,b,1+s,c), T=1+sum h_i=A+B+C+r+s.\n\nIf h is primitive, in the exact sense that the energy\n\n    V_h=2 sum_i h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n        -(T-1)T||g_n||^2\n\nis nonnegative for every convex 1-smooth GD trajectory, then\n\n    T <= max{Omega(Omega(A,B),C),Omega(A,Omega(B,C))}, (2)\n\nwhere Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2. This Omega is the exact shifted\nprimitive recurrence in verified fact b98bb807d514e80d. The obstruction below\ndoes not require the individual blocks to be primitive; only (1) and their\nnonnegative physical steps are needed.\n\n## 1. The three exact necessary tests\n\nFor a general nonnegative primitive schedule with capacity T, choose a step\n1+u, u>0, having prefix sum P and tail sum R. Define\n\n    a_minus=u/(R+1), g(x)=min(1,max(-a_minus,x)),\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, x_0=P+1.\n\nThe derivative is continuous, monotone, and 1-Lipschitz. The prefix stays on\nthe positive affine ray and ends at1. The selected step lands at-u. Since\n(R+1)a_minus=u and all tail partial sums lie in[0,R], the tail stays on the\nnegative affine ray and ends at-a_minus. Every sample satisfies\n\n    f_i-g_i x_i+g_i^2/2=0,\n\nand the terminal point is in the quadratic core. Expanding the GD squared\ndistance telescope gives exactly\n\n    V_h=(P+1)^2-T^2 a_minus^2.\n\nConsequently\n\n    T u <= (P+1)(R+1).                              (3)\n\nAt u=0 the same inequality is automatic. Applied to the two bridges, (3)\ngives\n\n    T r <= A(B+C+s),  T s <= C(A+B+r).               (4)\n\nOn the full-curvature quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, the same expansion gives\nV_h=1-T^2 product_i(1-h_i)^2. By (1), this implies\n\n    T r s <= ABC.                                   (5)\n\nAll three tests use actual fixed one-dimensional functions.\n\n## 2. Solve the scalar feasible region exactly\n\nPut H=A+B+C and R=T-H=r+s. For fixed T the cut constraints (4) are precisely\n\n    r<=u_A(T)=A(T-A)/(T+A),\n    s<=u_C(T)=C(T-C)/(T+C).                          (6)\n\nLet M be the right side of (2), and suppose for contradiction T>M.\nBecause Omega(B,C)>B+C and Omega(A,B)>A+B, monotonicity of Omega gives\n\n    T>Omega(A,B+C), T>Omega(C,A+B).\n\nThese inequalities are equivalent to\n\n    T(T-H)>A(B+C), T(T-H)>C(A+B),\n\nrespectively. They imply R>u_A(T) and R>u_C(T). Thus any feasible r must lie\nin the nonempty interval\n\n    L=R-u_C(T) <= r <= U=u_A(T),   0<L<=U<R.         (7)\n\nThe product r(R-r) is concave in r, so its minimum on (7) is attained at L\nor U. At U the first cut is an equality. Writing s_U=R-U, this equality and\nthe total-capacity equation give\n\n    B+C+s_U=F_A(T),  F_A(T)=T(T-A)/(T+A).\n\nFor T>=A this function is strictly increasing: its derivative is\n1-2A^2/(T+A)^2>=1/2. If D=Omega(B,C), the defining quadratic equation for\nOmega gives F_A(Omega(A,D))=D. Since T>Omega(A,D),\n\n    s_U>D-B-C.\n\nThe positive number rho=D-B-C solves rho(B+C+rho)=BC. Therefore\n\n    T U s_U=A(B+C+s_U)s_U>ABC.                     (8)\n\nAt L the second cut is an equality. The identical argument with A and C\ninterchanged gives r_L>Omega(A,B)-A-B and\n\n    T L(R-L)=C(A+B+r_L)r_L>ABC.                    (9)\n\nConcavity now implies T r(R-r)>ABC throughout (7), contradicting (5).\nThis proves (2), including r=0 or s=0 since under T>M the interval is\nstrictly inside(0,R).\n\n## 3. Sharpness of the necessary-test bound\n\nFor the parenthesization a,(b,c), put\n\n    D=Omega(B,C), s=D-B-C,\n    T=Omega(A,D), r=T-A-D.\n\nBoth bridges are positive, Tr=A(B+C+s), and Trs=ABC. The other cut also\nholds. One direct check is as follows: the binary equations give rT=AD and\nsD=BC, so Ts=ABC/r. The required Ts<=C(A+B+r) is equivalent to\nAB<=r(A+B+r). Since r(T)=AD and T=A+B+C+r+s, subtracting gives\n\n    r(A+B+r)-AB = AD-r(C+s)-AB\n                           = (A-r)(C+s)>0,\n\nsince r=AD/T<A. Interchanging A and C proves feasibility for the other\nparenthesization as well. Hence M is the exact maximum of the three scalar\ntests. If the input blocks themselves are primitive, the usual binary\ncomposition constructs primitive schedules attaining these capacities; the\nupper bound (2) needs no such assumption or attainment claim.\n\nThe scalar quantity in (2) is exactly the larger capacity of the two binary\nparenthesizations. In particular unequal block sizes cannot evade the earlier\nequal-copy obstruction while each block retains exact quadratic balance.\n\n## Scope and next opening\n\nThis is a primitive-energy obstruction, not a bound on every direct objective\ncertificate. It also does not apply when any input has\n|product(1-h_i)|<1/(1+sum h_i). Then (5) weakens. The next candidate is a\nslightly shortened two-step block (u,u), u<3/2, whose exact additional\ngradient-variation storage is derived separately. Its loss of quadratic\nbalance is real and quantitative; it cannot be ignored when testing bridges.\n\nNo external theorem is required for the proof. Matlas returned no relevant\nresult for the targeted query. The exact local GSW2410.16249 definitions of\nbalanced composability provide terminology only; no general equivalence with\nthis primitive definition is assumed.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_unequal_three_block_screen", "related_findings": ["2599c72fdf8542a0", "168103a931d3b9ea"], "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "local_artifact": "workers/max4/local_memory/unequal_three_block_screen.md", "verification_status": "unverified; no verifier or solver call", "scope": "Primitive energy and quadratic-balanced input blocks only"}, "glossary": {"T": "Total physical capacity A+B+C+r+s of the three-block join.", "r,s": "Nonnegative bridge excesses, so the physical bridge steps are1+r and1+s.", "quadratic-balanced": "A block h of capacity1+sum h has |product(1-h_i)| equal to the reciprocal of that capacity."}}
{"id": "4339b7cebe8d6905", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:18:40.819327+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "A concrete nonsaturated primitive input now retains a stronger joint objective/dual matrix. For the physical pair(u,u),29/20<=u<=3/2, exact nonnegative interpolation identities give V>=0,H>=0 and V>=(4/5)H+(p,m,q)^T J_u(p,m,q), with explicit PSD J_u and two positive gradient-difference directions when u<3/2. The added physical slack is epsilon=(3/2-u)(u+1/2); quadratic balance is strictly unsaturated. No profitable two-bridge completion is claimed.", "evidence": "# Extra storage from shortening the primitive pair\n\nStatus: complete candidate identity, not verifier-accepted. This is a proposed\ninput for the remaining joint boundary transport, not an improved schedule.\nNo solver or verifier call is used.\n\nFor 0<=u<=3/2 consider the physical pair h=(u,u). Write\np=g_0, m=g_1, q=g_2 and define\n\n    K=1+2u,\n    V=2u(f_0+f_1-2f_2)+u(1-u)(||p||^2+||m||^2)\n        -2u(1+2u)||q||^2,\n    epsilon=(3/2-u)(u+1/2)>=0,\n    I_ij=f_j-f_i-<g_i,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nOn samples of one convex 1-smooth function I_ij>=0. The following exact\nidentity retains two independent gradient differences:\n\n    V=I_02+(1+2u)I_10+(3+4u)I_12+(4+8u)I_21\n       +(1/4)||p+m-2q||^2\n       +epsilon[||p-m||^2+4||m-q||^2].               (1)\n\nAll four actual interpolation coefficients are nonnegative, and every square\ncoefficient is nonnegative in the stated range. Thus this is a full candidate\nprimitive certificate for the pair. At u=3/2 it reduces to the existing pair\nidentity in finding b750ce63e2821fa8; the epsilon term is the new retained\nslack from changing the physical steps, not the proof weights.\n\nFor completeness, here is a coefficient proof of (1). Use x_1=x_0-u p and\nx_2=x_0-u(p+m). Then\n\n    I_02=f_2-f_0+(u-1/2)||p||^2+u<p,m>+<p,q>-(1/2)||q||^2,\n    I_10=f_0-f_1-(1/2)||p||^2-(1/2)||m||^2+(1-u)<p,m>,\n    I_12=f_2-f_1+(u-1/2)||m||^2+<m,q>-(1/2)||q||^2,\n    I_21=f_1-f_2-(1/2)||m||^2-(1/2)||q||^2+(1-u)<m,q>.\n\nThe weighted sum in (1) has function coefficients(2u,2u,-4u). Subtracting\nits gradient part from V leaves a quadratic form on a=p-q and b=m-q:\n\n    (a,b)^T R_u(a,b),\n    R_u=[[alpha,1/2-alpha],[1/2-alpha,5alpha-1]],\n    alpha=1+u-u^2=1/4+epsilon.\n\nTherefore\n\n    R_u=(1/4)[[1,1],[1,1]]+epsilon[[1,-1],[-1,5]],\n\nwhich is the sum of squares in (1). Its determinant is\n4alpha^2-1/4 and its trace is6alpha-1. At u<3/2 it is positive definite,\nbecause alpha>1/4; at u=3/2 it has rank1. More explicitly,\n[[1,-1],[-1,5]] has determinant4 and positive upper-left entry1, so its\nquadratic form is ||a-b||^2+4||b||^2.\n\n## Quadratic slack and the precise changed hypothesis\n\nThe pair's quadratic response is Pi_u=(1-u)^2. Its quadratic-balance ratio is\n\n    kappa(u)=K Pi_u=(1+2u)(1-u)^2.\n\nFor 0<u<3/2, kappa(u)<1, since\n\n    1-kappa(u)=u^2(3-2u)>0.\n\nAt u=3/2, kappa=1. If u=3/2-delta with delta small and positive,\n\n    K=4-2delta,\n    kappa=1-(9/2)delta+6delta^2-2delta^3,\n    epsilon=2delta-delta^2.\n\nThus shortening the pair supplies explicit positive gradient-variation\nstorage and strictly weakens the quadratic product test. The unequal-block\nobstruction requiring exact quadratic balance cannot be applied to these\ninputs without changing its hypotheses.\n\nFor three equal copies with bridges1+cK and1+dK, put M=3+c+d. The two\nnecessary cut tests remain\n\n    Mc<=2+d, Md<=2+c,\n\nwhile the quadratic test becomes\n\n    Mcd<=kappa(u)^(-3)                              (2)\n\nwhen kappa(u)>0; at u=1 the quadratic response is zero and this test is\nvacuous. Passing (2) is not sufficient for a primitive certificate. It only\nidentifies a concrete unresolved regime not closed by the balanced result.\n\n## Joint objective and primitive-dual storage\n\nThere is also an exact correlated-slack identity throughout the explicit\ninterval 29/20<=u<=3/2. Define\n\n    H=2(f_0-f_2)-4u||q||^2\n         -||p-(u(p+m)+q)/K||^2.\n\nFirst, direct expansion gives the primitive-dual identity\n\n    H=(4u+1)V/K^2+(2u/K)I_10+(2/K)I_20\n                          +(4u^2/K^2)(I_02+I_12).   (3)\n\nTogether with (1), it proves H>=0 for this actual pair. More information is\nretained by the following identity:\n\n    V-(4/5)H\n      =(2/5)I_01+(2u-6/5)I_10\n        +(4u-4/5)I_12+(8u-12/5)I_21\n        +(1/4)[P||z||^2+2D<z,w>+Q||w||^2],         (4)\n\nwhere the ACTUAL gradient combinations and scalar coefficients are\n\n    z=p+m-2q, w=p-m,\n    P=(-20u^2+36u-8+4/K^2)/5,\n    Q=4(3-2u)(5u-1)/5,\n    D=-4(3-2u)(5u^2+u-1)/(5K).\n\nThe four interpolation coefficients in (4) are nonnegative on the stated\ninterval. The quadratic matrix (1/4)[[P,D],[D,Q]] is PSD, and is positive\ndefinite when u<3/2. Here is an explicit check. Put delta=3/2-u, so\n0<=delta<=1/20. Since K=4-2delta,\n\n    P=[1+24delta-20delta^2+4/K^2]/5>=1/4.\n\nFor delta>0, Q>0 and\n\n    D^2/Q=4(3-2u)(5u^2+u-1)^2/[5K^2(5u-1)]\n          <=4418/38025 <1/4 <=P.\n\nThe displayed upper bound uses 3-2u<=1/10,\n5u^2+u-1<=47/4, K>=39/10, and 5u-1>=25/4. Thus its determinant is strictly\npositive. At delta=0, P=1/4 and D=Q=0, leaving ||z||^2/16. This is exactly\nthe older u=3/2 correlated storage, and the second eigenvalue becomes\npositive on shortening the physical steps.\n\nFor a full matrix on the distinct variables(p,m,q), set\n\n    t11=(P+Q+2D)/4, t12=(P-Q)/4, t22=(P+Q-2D)/4.\n\nThen the remainder in (4) is\n\n    (p,m,q)^T J_u(p,m,q),\n    J_u=[[t11,t12,-t11-t12],\n         [t12,t22,-t12-t22],\n         [-t11-t12,-t12-t22,t11+2t12+t22]].          (5)\n\nIt is PSD by (4); its kernel is the constant-gradient subspace when\nu<3/2. Equivalently, one may replace m by d_block/u-p to carry the actual\ndisplacement d_block=u(p+m). No initial or terminal gradient of a neighboring\nblock is identified with these variables.\n\nFor a coefficient audit of (4), the following two-parameter identity is\nconvenient. Choose scalars b0,e0 and put\n\n    a0=2-2b0, c0=2u+2-b0-e0,\n    d0=4+4u-b0-e0, f0=4+8u-2e0.\n\nThese lower-case subscripted letters in this audit are interpolation\ncoefficients, not function values or block capacities. The exact expansion is\n\n    V=a0 I_01+b0 I_02+c0 I_10+d0 I_12+e0 I_20+f0 I_21\n       +(p-q,m-q)^T R(p-q,m-q),\n\n    R11=2-u^2-(1-u)b0,\n    R22=6+4u-5u^2-(2-u)(b0+e0),\n    2R12=-[4-2u^2+(2u-3)b0-(1-u)e0].\n\nSet L0=20u^2+4u+1, b0=16u^2/L0 and e0=8K/L0. Substitute this expansion\nin (1-(4/5)(4u+1)/K^2)V and then subtract the other terms of (4/5)H from\n(3). The I_02 and I_20 coefficients cancel exactly; the four remaining\ncoefficients are those in (4). Rewriting the remainder using z,w gives P,D,Q\nas displayed. These are algebraic identities, not appeals to an unverified\nprimitive residual lemma.\n\n## What a next join must prove\n\nThe retained variables in (1) and (4) are the ACTUAL p,m,q of each block. Since\nd_block=u(p+m), they can also be stored as(p,d_block,q) for u>0, without\nidentifying the next block's start gradient with the bridge gradient. An\nattempt to spend the epsilon term across two bridges must preserve these\ndistinct variables and the four displayed interpolation coefficients. It\nmust finish with a nonnegative full matrix and an original-objective bound.\nNo such two-bridge completion or asymptotic improvement is proved here.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_joint_objective_dual_storage", "related_findings": ["b750ce63e2821fa8", "eedba6be86f2ff0a"], "local_artifact": "workers/max4/local_memory/shortened_pair_storage.md", "verification_status": "unverified; two tiny exact rational coefficient audits only; no solver or verifier call", "next_action": "Carry the actual start/middle/end gradients or equivalent displacement through two bridges and close a full certificate; do not assume scalar cut and product feasibility is sufficient."}, "glossary": {"u": "The common physical stepsize of the two-step block(u,u), here29/20<=u<=3/2.", "p,m,q": "The actual start, middle and terminal gradients of the two-step block.", "J_u": "The explicit3x3 PSD joint-storage matrix in equation5 of the evidence.", "kappa(u)": "Quadratic-balance ratio(1+2u)(1-u)^2, strictly below1 for0<u<3/2.", "epsilon": "Positive added gradient-variation storage coefficient(3/2-u)(u+1/2)."}}
{"id": "3e06d97b1142c19c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:23:49.556326+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Quantitative final-stage candidate for EVERY nonnegative prefix: one explicit fixed 2D Huber/capped-quadratic product has error >=q_S+kappa_S(b-b_S)^2 for every appended b>=0, with b_S=psi(S,0), q_S=1/[1+2(S+b_S)], and its full-prefix maximal terminal extension has exact optimized value q_S.", "evidence": "# A quantitative final-stage guard for every nonnegative prefix\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No new submission is\nauthorized. This proof constructs and analyzes the function directly. It does\nnot use the unverified minimax finding 40601f4c6f496ab8 as a premise. The final\nmaximal-extension identification uses only accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 1. Statement in the original normalization\n\nFix any integer k>=0, any L>0, and any finite nonnegative predetermined prefix\nh_pre=(h_0,...,h_(k-1)). The empty prefix is allowed. Write S=sum_t h_t. Define\n\n    t_S=(sqrt(8S+9)-1)/4,\n    b_S=1+t_S=(3+sqrt(8S+9))/4,\n    c_S=1/(S+1),\n    q_S=1/(2t_S+1)^2=1/[1+2(S+b_S)],\n    omega_S=1/(1+q_S t_S),\n    kappa_S=q_S^2/[t_S(1+q_S t_S)].                      (1)\n\nAll these quantities are positive, 0<omega_S<1, and t_S>=1/2. The identities\n\n    t_S(2t_S+1)=S+1,\n    c_S^2 t_S^2=q_S                                   (2)\n\nwill be used repeatedly.\n\nThere is an explicit SINGLE convex differentiable L-smooth function f on R^2,\nwith a minimizer x_star=0 and an initial point x_0 of norm one, such that for\nEVERY nonnegative final stepsize b, the GD trajectory for (h_pre,b) obeys\n\n    2[f(x_(k+1)(b))-f(x_star)]/L\n             >= q_S+kappa_S(b-b_S)^2.                    (3)\n\nEquality with q_S holds at b=b_S, which is therefore the unique exact-line-\nsearch minimizer for this one function after the supplied prefix. In particular,\n\n    inf_(b>=0) W_(k+1)(h_pre,b) >= q_S.                   (4)\n\nThe complete fixed-prefix maximal-interpolation game for the particular jets\nconstructed below has EXACT optimized normalized value q_S. Thus (4) is backed\nby one fixed realizable prefix and a tight terminal completion, not merely by\nchoosing a different scalar adversary after observing b.\n\nFor the Zhang--Jiang bridge notation in the project, b_S=psi(S,0). This is\na final-stage bound depending on the actual prefix sum S; it asserts no bound\non that sum in terms of k and does not prove the all-schedule exact profile.\n\n## 2. The fixed two-dimensional function\n\nIt suffices first to set L=1. Define the even scalar Huber function A_S and\nthe one-sided capped quadratic C_S by\n\n    A_S(u)=u^2/2                         if |u|<=q_S,\n           q_S|u|-q_S^2/2               if |u|>=q_S;\n\n    C_S(u)=u^2/2                         if u<=c_S,\n           c_S u-c_S^2/2                if u>=c_S.\n\nTheir derivatives are respectively the clamp of u to [-q_S,q_S] and min(u,c_S).\nThey are continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz. The functions are convex\nand differentiable, nonnegative, and minimized at zero.\n\nUse the fixed product function and initial point\n\n    F(u,v)=omega_S A_S(u/sqrt(omega_S))\n              +(1-omega_S) C_S(v/sqrt(1-omega_S)),\n\n    x_0=(sqrt(omega_S),sqrt(1-omega_S)).                   (5)\n\nIts gradient is 1-Lipschitz: each coordinate gradient has Lipschitz constant\nat most one in that coordinate, so the sum of squared gradient differences\nis at most the squared Euclidean displacement. It is convex, differentiable,\nand has minimum zero at the origin. The initial squared distance is exactly\none. The two scalar coordinates evolve independently under the SAME supplied\nstepsizes, starting at scalar value one in both coordinates.\n\n## 3. Every supplied prefix step is legal, in its actual order\n\nLet P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t for 0<=i<=k. Nonnegativity gives 0<=P_i<=S. In the two\nscalar coordinates the entire prefix trajectory is\n\n    u_i=1-q_S P_i,    grad A_S(u_i)=q_S,\n    v_i=1-c_S P_i,    grad C_S(v_i)=c_S.                  (6)\n\nIndeed c_S=1/(S+1) gives v_i>=c_S, including equality at i=k. Also\n\n    q_S(S+1)=t_S/(2t_S+1)<1,\n\nso u_i>=1-q_S S>q_S. Thus the derivative formulas justify (6) inductively\nunder every step, regardless of its magnitude or its position in the prefix.\nZero steps merely repeat the same legal data; no strictness assumption occurs.\n\nAfter the prefix the two scalar coordinates are\n\n    u_k=1-q_S S,    v_k=c_S.\n\nFor a final step b>=0 they become\n\n    u_(k+1)(b)=1-q_S(S+b),\n    v_(k+1)(b)=c_S(1-b).                                (7)\n\nThe second coordinate always belongs to the quadratic region u<=c_S, including\narbitrarily negative values. Consequently its normalized error curve is exactly\n\n    B(b):=2C_S(v_(k+1)(b))=c_S^2(1-b)^2.                 (8)\n\nLet A(b):=2A_S(u_(k+1)(b)). This is a convex differentiable function of b,\nbeing a convex differentiable scalar function composed with an affine map.\n\n## 4. A uniform bound against every final step\n\nAt b=b_S the identities (1)-(2) give\n\n    u_(k+1)(b_S)=(1+q_S)/2>q_S,\n    A(b_S)=q_S,\n    A'(b_S)=-2q_S^2,\n\n    v_(k+1)(b_S)=-c_S t_S,\n    B(b_S)=q_S,\n    B'(b_S)=2c_S^2 t_S.                                (9)\n\nConvexity supplies a GLOBAL tangent lower bound\n\n    A(b)>=q_S-2q_S^2(b-b_S)                             (10)\n\nfor every b>=0, even when (7) has left the Huber linear region or crossed zero.\nThe exact quadratic (8) expands as\n\n    B(b)=q_S+2c_S^2 t_S(b-b_S)+c_S^2(b-b_S)^2.          (11)\n\nThe choice of omega_S in (1) is precisely\n\n    omega_S q_S^2=(1-omega_S)c_S^2 t_S.                 (12)\n\nMultiplying (10) by omega_S and (11) by 1-omega_S cancels the linear terms.\nFor the ACTUAL single product function (5), its normalized error is\nomega_S A(b)+(1-omega_S)B(b). We obtain\n\n    2F(x_(k+1)(b))\n       >=q_S+(1-omega_S)c_S^2(b-b_S)^2\n        =q_S+kappa_S(b-b_S)^2.\n\nAt b=b_S both channel values equal q_S, so the error is exactly q_S.\nSince kappa_S>0, this minimizer is unique. This proves (3) and (4) for L=1.\nFor arbitrary L take f=L F; the iteration x_next=x-(h/L)grad f(x) is the same\nnormalized iteration, and the division by L in (3) cancels the rescaling.\n\nThe terminal gradients also expose why the construction resists line search:\n\n    g_k=(sqrt(omega_S)q_S, sqrt(1-omega_S)c_S),\n    g_(k+1)=(sqrt(omega_S)q_S,\n                         -sqrt(1-omega_S)c_S t_S).\n\nEquation (12) makes their inner product zero, while the objective stays\nq_S/2>0. Both coordinates belong to one fixed legal function throughout.\n\n## 5. The maximal completion of this full prefix is also tight\n\nApply fact1252b8823bb5a290 to ALL the queried prefix jets of (5), including\ntheir actual values, and let F_max be the pointwise maximal interpolant.\nIt is legal and agrees with F on every queried point. Since its gradient\nat x_k is the same, its possible final points are still (7). Pointwise\nmaximality and (3) imply\n\n    2F_max(x_(k+1)(b))>=q_S+kappa_S(b-b_S)^2.             (13)\n\nWe prove equality at b_S directly by its conjugate construction. Let w be\nthe terminal gradient of F at b_S, and use the usual prefix y_j=x_j-g_j\nand delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2. The two outgoing terminal residuals are\n\n    Q_(k+1,star)=Q_(k+1,k)=0.                           (14)\n\nFor the Huber coordinate, both the last query and the terminal point lie\non the same positive linear region, giving equality in both inequalities.\nFor the capped coordinate, the last query is c_S and the terminal point\nis negative, both lying in its quadratic region, again giving equality.\nWeighted addition of the two scalar residuals yields (14).\n\nAll other prefix residuals with this terminal sample are nonpositive\nbecause F is an actual smooth-convex function. Thus in\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_j(<y_j,v>-delta_j),\n\nthe optimum and last-query planes are both active at v=w, and no other\nprefix plane has a larger value. Directly from (6),\n\n    y_k=(sqrt(omega_S)[1-q_S(S+1)],0).\n\nSet alpha_k=2t_S/(2t_S+1) and alpha_star=1/(2t_S+1). The identity\n\n    x_(k+1)(b_S)=w+alpha_k y_k                          (15)\n\nholds in both coordinates. In the second coordinate it states equality\nof the position and gradient in the quadratic region. In the first it\nreduces to\n\n    1-q_S(S+b_S+1)\n        =[2t_S/(2t_S+1)] [1-q_S(S+1)],\n\nwhich follows by substituting (1)-(2).\n\nSince (15) is a convex combination of gradients of the two active\nquadratics at w, it makes x_(k+1)(b_S) a subgradient of H at w. This can\nalso be checked by taking the weighted sum of their quadratic supporting\ninequalities, with the displayed nonnegative weights. Therefore w is\nthe conjugate maximizer at this point. Equality (14) identifies its value\nas F(x_(k+1)(b_S))=q_S/2. Hence F_max has the same value there.\n\nTogether with (13), this proves the exact optimized fixed-prefix maximal\nvalue q_S, using the accepted fact rather than an unverified minimax exchange.\nAll earlier queries remain in H, even though the two stated planes suffice\nto certify the terminal equality for this PARTICULAR construction.\n\n## 6. Quantitative use and precise limit\n\nThis gives a dimension-two function depending only on the fixed prefix sum S\nwhose error is bounded below uniformly over an arbitrary final step. Its\nquadratic excess also gives the necessary condition\n\n    W_(k+1)(h_pre,b)<=q_S+epsilon\n       => |b-b_S|<=sqrt(epsilon/kappa_S)\n\nfor every epsilon>=0. No randomization or adaptive algorithmic choice is\nbeing substituted for the predetermined schedule; (5) is one deterministic\nfunction and works for all b at once.\n\nFor S=0 the formulas give t_S=1/2, b_S=3/2, q_S=1/4,\nomega_S=8/9, and kappa_S=1/9, recovering an explicit one-step line-search\nobstruction. Leading, interior, and trailing zero steps in the prefix do\nnot alter this conclusion.\n\nFor larger prefixes q_S~1/(2S). Consequently this construction alone does\nnot control an unusually large prefix sum, does not rule out arbitrary\nremote forward edges, and does not yield the required exact-profile bound\nas a function of k. Its role is a quantitative final-stage boundary\ncondition for a recurrence that must still constrain the COMPLETE earlier\ntrajectory. It complements the general minimax candidate and the three-anchor\nobstruction without assuming either unverified result in its proof.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/prefix_sum_terminal_guard.md", "task": "TASK.md terminal value game", "status": "Complete candidate, unverified; no new request", "scope": "All nonnegative prefix orders and zero steps; actual prefix sum S; no sum-versus-length cap or exact-profile claim", "proof_dependency_note": "Direct elementary construction; does not use unverified minimax40601f4c6f496ab8"}, "glossary": {"t_S": "(sqrt(8S+9)-1)/4, satisfying t_S(2t_S+1)=S+1.", "b_S": "1+t_S=psi(S,0), unique optimal final step of the explicit fixed function.", "c_S": "1/(S+1), the capped-quadratic threshold.", "q_S": "1/(2t_S+1)^2=1/[1+2(S+b_S)], the exact constructed final-stage value.", "omega_S": "1/(1+q_S t_S), the positive Huber coordinate weight.", "kappa_S": "q_S^2/[t_S(1+q_S t_S)], the positive uniform quadratic final-step penalty.", "A_S": "Even scalar Huber function of threshold q_S.", "C_S": "Scalar function quadratic below c_S and linear with slope c_S above it.", "F_max": "Maximal smooth-convex interpolant of all actual prefix jets in the displayed product construction."}}
{"id": "29a1f4a5269032b1", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:32:04.430959+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "New concrete symbolic seed candidate: h=(3/2,3/2,1+r,3/2,3/2,1+s,10/7,10/7), with exact radicals in the evidence, has capacity T>82/5>9^rho and passes ALL eight primitive single-cut tests plus full-curvature equality. Only the final pair is shortened; its exact joint objective/dual input matrix is positive definite on two gradient differences. There is NO primitive certificate yet: the intermediate tail is nonprimitive, so a full cross-block repair or an explicit legal counterexample is required.", "evidence": "# Concrete asymmetric eight-step candidate and the required hostile checks\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED SCHEDULE CANDIDATE ONLY. There is no universal upper bound.\nThe following is a symbolic construction, not a reopened numerical screen.\n\nThe equal balanced three-block obstruction was extended in candidate\needba6be86f2ff0a. The actual shortened-pair matrix in4339b7cebe8d6905 suggests\nchanging only the final pair, so that a nonprimitive intermediate tail could\nbe repaired by retained cross-block information.\n\n## 1. Exact physical schedule\n\nSet\n\n    u=10/7, C=1+2u=27/7, Pi=(1-u)^2=9/49,\n    D=(165+sqrt(65641))/42,\n    s=D-55/7,\n    T=(4+D+sqrt((4+D)^2+16D))/2,\n    r=T-4-D.\n\nThe proposed eight steps, in chronological order, are\n\n    h=(3/2,3/2,1+r,3/2,3/2,1+s,10/7,10/7).        (1)\n\nTheir physical capacity is exactly T. The scalar identities are\n\n    D^2-(55/7)D-196/9=0,\n    sD=196/9,\n    rT=4D,\n    T r s=784/9.                                  (2)\n\nThis makes the first bridge's primitive cut test an equality and also makes\nthe full-curvature quadratic primitive test an equality: the two initial\npairs each have quadratic product1/4, and the last has product9/49, so\n\n    T |product_i(1-h_i)|=T r s (9/784)=1.\n\nThe second bridge's cut test passes strictly. Indeed 10<D<101/10, and the\npositive root equation r^2+(4+D)r-4D=0 gives 12/5<r<5/2. Therefore\n\n    r(8+r)>624/25>5488/243,\n\nwhich is equivalent, using (2), to Ts<C(8+r). The internal pair-step cut tests\nalso pass, as follows. The upper bound D<101/10 gives T<17 by substituting17\ninto the defining quadratic for Omega(4,101/10). At the first two pair\nqueries, the right-hand sides of the cut are T-3/2 and(5/2)(T-3),\nrespectively, both at least T/2. At the two middle pair queries, the prefix\ncapacity is greater than5 and the tail capacity is greater than C, so their\nproduct exceeds5C>17/2>T/2. At the last two queries the right-hand sides are\n(17/7)(T-20/7) and T-10/7, respectively. These exceed the left-hand side\n3T/7 since T>82/5>170/49 and T>5/2. Thus all eight original-index cuts have\nbeen checked.\n\n## 2. The physical sum clears the amplification threshold\n\nThe first polynomial in (2) is negative at D=10, so D>10. Monotonicity of\nOmega therefore gives\n\n    T>Omega(4,10)=7+sqrt(89)>82/5.\n\nLet rho=log_2(1+sqrt(2)), the Silver exponent. The exact identity\n\n    (1+sqrt(2))^11=8119+5741sqrt(2)<2^14\n\nimplies rho<14/11. The upper bound on the power follows, for example, from\nsqrt(2)<10/7: 8119+57410/7<16384. Finally\n\n    82^11-9^14 5^11=10042975364984229043>0,\n\nso 9^rho<9^(14/11)<82/5<T. This is only a physical-sum statement. A genuine\nprimitive proof for (1), if one exists, would satisfy the seed hypothesis of\nthe previously recorded conditional amplification argument. Physical sum and\nthe tests in part1 do not establish that proof.\n\n## 3. An available input matrix at the exact shortened pair\n\nFor the last pair, the joint identity from the complete coefficient formulas\nin shortened_pair_storage.md still holds at u=10/7. With its actual gradients\np,m,q and z=p+m-2q, w=p-m, it is\n\n    V-(4/5)H\n      =(2/5)I_01+(58/35)I_10+(172/35)I_12+(316/35)I_21\n        +(1/4)[P||z||^2+2D0<z,w>+Q||w||^2].        (3)\n\nHere D0 names a matrix coefficient, distinct from the capacity D in (1).\nDirect rational substitution in the displayed coefficient formulas gives\n\n    P=102916/178605, D0=-2084/6615, Q=172/245,\n    (P Q-D0^2)/16=278302/14586075>0.\n\nTheir positivity is a finite exact check, not a numerical feasibility result\nfor the joined eight-step schedule. The full3x3 matrix on(p,m,q) is the\nexplicit lift in equation5 of shortened_pair_storage.md.\n\n## 4. Stronger hybrid witness, and why uniform shortening is unprofitable locally\n\nThere is an additional exact hostile test which is stronger than the global\nquadratic near the balanced baseline. Let the first two blocks have arbitrary\nnonnegative physical steps and capacities A,B, and let the last pair be(u,u),\n1<u<=3/2. Use bridges1+r and1+s and total capacity\nT=A+B+(1+2u)+r+s. If\n\n    rs<=B, (u-1)s<=1,\n\ntake g(x)=clip(x,-r/B,1), f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, x_0=A. The first block\nstays on the positive plateau and ends at1; the first bridge and middle block\ntravel from-r to-r/B with constant negative gradient-r/B. The second bridge\nlands at rs/B. The two final pair steps stay in the quadratic core by the\ndisplayed assumptions. The terminal point and gradient are\n\n    q=(u-1)^2 rs/B.\n\nEvery sampled optimum interpolation residual is zero, so its primitive energy\nis exactly A^2-T^2 q^2. Therefore primitive validity NECESSARILY requires\n\n    T rs (u-1)^2<=AB.                              (4)\n\nFor (1), rs=(784/9)/T>B=4, so this particular witness does not apply. The\nfailure of its core condition must not be hidden.\n\nFor three equally shortened pairs u=3/2-delta, K=1+2u, define normalized\nbridges r=Kc,s=Kd and M=3+c+d. The balanced right-associated baseline has\n\n    delta=0, d0=sqrt(2)-1,\n    M0=Omega(1,1+sqrt(2)), c0=M0-3-d0>d0.\n\nBoth core assumptions for (4) hold strictly there. Along any differentiable\nprimitive family through this baseline, they continue to hold for small\ndelta>=0. The first cut and (4) give\n\n    Mc<=2+d,\n    Mcd<=1/kappa(u), kappa(u)=K(u-1)^2.\n\nBoth inequalities are equalities at delta=0. Since kappa'(0)=-9/2, their\none-sided derivative constraints imply\n\n    M'(0)<= (9/2)/p',\n    p'=2sqrt(2)[1-2/(M0+1)^2]>46sqrt(2)/25.\n\nFor detail, M'=c'+d'. Differentiating the cut gives\nd'>=(M0+c0)M'/(M0+1). Differentiating the product then gives\nd0(M0+c0)M'+M0(c0-d0)d'<=9/2. Substituting the lower bound for d' yields\nthe displayed p', using M0 c0=2+d0.\n\nHence the actual capacity K M has derivative\n\n    (K M)'(0)<=-2M0+18/p'<-8+225/(23sqrt(2))<0.     (5)\n\nThis rejects the positive infinitesimal improvement obtained by shortening\nALL three pairs together near that baseline, even though the full-curvature\ntest alone misleadingly leaves room. It does not reject shortening only the\nlast pair in (1).\n\n## 5. The exact unresolved proof obligation\n\nA proof for (1) must use the last pair's actual matrix (3) across BOTH\nbridges. The intermediate tail(middle pair,1+s,last pair) is not being assumed\nprimitive. Its ordinary two-block primitive cut test fails, so applying the\nusual primitive recursion to it would be invalid. The outer anchor and\nretained cross-block interpolation must repair that signed deficit in a\nsingle complete certificate, or a legal function must refute the candidate.\nAll samples, function values and gradients must belong to one fixed function.\nNo additional solver or verifier request is made.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_asymmetric_shortened_tail_seed", "related_findings": ["4339b7cebe8d6905", "eedba6be86f2ff0a", "2973c2322cdee545"], "local_artifact": "workers/max4/local_memory/asymmetric_shortened_tail_candidate.md", "verification_status": "unverified schedule candidate; no solver or verifier call", "independent_attack_needed": "Find one legal full smooth-convex function with V_h<0, or derive the full nonnegative interpolation certificate.
