It does not assert\nthat R_replaced is PSD on every formal prefix; the false step being tested\nis treating it as a lower bound on R.\n\n## What remains possible\n\nEquation(1) is an exact full terminal closure with all moment data retained.\nThe scoped failure is the proposed deletion of C_a using(2). A useful\nimplementation must either retain C_a with a valid upper supply bound,\nretain an additional signed value/storage inequality, or use richer local\nprefix multipliers whose contribution is accounted for in full. Adding the\naccepted terminal square alone does not fix this missing sign.\n\nFor physical weights u_i=h_i over a contiguous block, one exact alternative\nis C_a=(||x_start||^2-||x_end||^2+B_h)/2, where\nB_h=sum h_i(h_i-2)||g_i||^2. It retains the gradient moment B_h and the\nglobal-position dependence. Bounding that moment by the earlier scalar\nP_theta mechanism pays its explicitly recorded initial-distance factor;\none cannot silently discard it here either.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_cross_block_moment_join", "master_guidance": "8d2dacb475d6eb19", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "related_findings": ["6dd1df44d30531f8", "e1af9b59ad43e2c0"], "local_artifact": "workers/max4/local_memory/terminal_cross_moment_sign.md", "verification_status": "unverified new identity and example; no verifier or solver call", "scope": "Sign obstruction to this particular moment elimination; not an impossibility for retaining the complete moments or using richer local prefix storage."}, "glossary": {"t_a": "Nonnegative probability coefficient for group a, alpha_i=t_a u_i.", "k_ab": "Actual nonnegative rank-one multiplier coefficient for directed cross edges from group a to group b.", "L_a": "Nonnegative coefficient2t_a+nu_a+sum_b k_ba D_b of the negative mixed moment C_a.", "C_a": "Actual within-group mixed moment sum u_i<g_i,x_i-g_i>.", "eta_a,nu_a": "Actual groupwise coefficients of prefix-to-optimum and optimum-to-prefix interpolation multipliers, respectively."}}
{"id": "72acacb33061e3ab", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:27:39.391572+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Order-sensitive polynomial prefix with a free terminal witness: an explicit permutation of every Chebyshev multiset Chi_n, n>=100, admits one fully legal 2D mu-strongly-convex prefix at a fixed nonreal z and one maximal interpolant whose normalized error is >=J_n=beta^2 cosh((2n+1)asinh(2beta))/(8(2n+1)^2|z|^3) for EVERY appended final step. J_n grows exponentially/n^2, while the sum-only Huber and entire ordinary quadratic channels on the same family with final step1 are O(n^-2) and miss the exact benchmark. Same z is illegal for the increasing permutation for all sufficiently large n. Complete candidate; no new verified fact.", "evidence": "# A chronological complex prefix with a uniform free-terminal lower bound\n\nStatus: complete candidate argument, NOT verifier accepted. No fact submission\nis allocated. Accepted inputs are `dfde2fa326c908bf` (cycle calculus and the\nChebyshev polynomial identity), `1252b8823bb5a290` (full-prefix interpolation\nand maximal terminal completion), and `b98bb807d514e80d` (benchmark growth).\nNo unverified terminal minimax assertion is used.\n\n## Statement and concrete data\n\nIdentify the complex plane with the real Euclidean plane by\n`<a,b> = Re(conj(a)b)`. Set\n\n    K = 65536,  phi = pi/K,  beta = tan(phi)/2,\n    z = 1/2 + i beta,  mu = 1/4,\n    h_c = 1/(1/4 + beta^2) = 4 cos^2(phi),\n    u = asinh(2 beta).\n\nThese constants do not depend on the horizon. For every integer n >= 100 let\n\n    theta_j = (2j-1)pi/[2(2n+1)],\n    a_j = sec^2(theta_j),                 1 <= j <= n.\n\nThe multiset {a_j} is the accepted Chebyshev schedule Chi_n. Partition its\nindices into the four classes\n\n    C_s: 1 <= a_j <= 3,\n    C_w: 3 < a_j <= h_c,\n    E_w: h_c < a_j < 6,\n    E_s: 6 <= a_j.\n\nThe subscripts s and w mean strong and weak radial contraction/expansion.\nList each class in increasing original index j. Pair the r-th C_w entry with\nthe r-th C_s entry and place each pair in the order (C_w,C_s). Concatenate\nthese pairs, then the unused C_s entries. Next pair the r-th E_w entry with\nthe r-th E_s entry, place each pair in the order (E_w,E_s), and finally append\nthe unused E_s entries. The counts below prove that every required pair\nexists. Denote this specified permutation by h^(n)=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)).\n\nDefine the actual chronological points and gradients by\n\n    P_0(t) = 1,\n    P_i(t) = product_(r<i)(1-h_r t),\n    xi_i = P_i(z),   g_i = z xi_i,         0 <= i <= n,\n    xi_star = g_star = 0.\n\nThere is an explicitly specified globally convex differentiable 1-smooth\nfunction f_n on R^2 with minimizer 0, f_n(0)=0, and strong-convexity parameter\nmu, whose gradients at ALL these points are g_i. Its values, and the\nfunction itself, are constructed below from a finite maximum over paths.\nIn particular the points are the actual GD trajectory for h^(n), with\n||xi_0||=1.\n\nOne fixed pointwise maximal interpolant F_n of these prefix jets satisfies,\nfor EVERY appended step b >= 0,\n\n    2 F_n(xi_n-b g_n) >= J_n,\n    J_n = beta^2 cosh((2n+1)u) / [8(2n+1)^2 |z|^3].                (1)\n\nIndeed the assertion holds for every real b. This uses a terminal vector\northogonal to g_n and checks its lower affine value against EVERY prefix\nanchor, including the optimum. The appended endpoint is not required to\nhave gradient z times its position.\n\nConsequently\n\n    inf_(b>=0) W_(n+1)(h^(n),b) >= J_n -> infinity.                 (2)\n\nFor the explicit schedules (h^(n),1), both the canonical sum-based Huber\nchannel and the entire ordinary convex quadratic channel are O(n^-2), and\ntheir ratios to U_(n+1)^ZJ tend to zero. The new lower bound instead has\nJ_n/U_(n+1)^ZJ -> infinity.\n\nLegality here depends on the order of the prefix. The SAME fixed z fails\nthe interpolation cycle criterion for the increasing ordering of Chi_n\nfor all sufficiently large n, although its terminal polynomial and sum are\nidentical. This is a scoped statement about this concrete ansatz; no upper\nbound on W for the increasing permutation is asserted.\n\n## 1. Elementary constant and count checks\n\nOn [0,pi/4], tan(t) <= 2t: its derivative sec^2(t) is at most 2 and tan(0)=0.\nThus\n\n    0 < beta <= phi < 4/65536 = 1/16384 < 1/10000.\n\nAlso 3 < h_c < 4. The strict upper bound follows from beta>0; for the lower\nbound, 1/4+beta^2 < 1/3.\n\nThe theta_j have spacing d=pi/(2n+1), with first point d/2 and last point\npi/2-d. An interval of length ell contains at most ell/d+1 grid points;\nthe interval between its endpoints loses at most two grid points relative\nto length/d. In particular:\n\n* C_s contains every theta_j <= pi/4, so |C_s| >= n/2-1.\n* C_w lies inside (pi/4,pi/3), since its steps are greater than 3 and less\n  than 4. Hence |C_w| <= (2n+1)/12+1 <= n/6+2.\n* E_s contains every theta_j >= 5pi/12, because\n  cos^2(5pi/12)=(2-sqrt(3))/4 < 1/6. Thus |E_s| >= n/6-2.\n\nFor E_w put\n\n    theta_c = acos(sqrt(1/4+beta^2)),\n    theta_6 = acos(1/sqrt(6)).\n\nWe have theta_6 < 3pi/8: indeed\ncos^2(3pi/8)=(2-sqrt(2))/4 < 1/6, since sqrt(2)>4/3.\nFor t in [pi/3-1/100,pi/3], sin(t)>1/2. Integration gives\n\n    cos(pi/3-1/100) > 1/2+1/200 = 0.505,\n\nwhereas sqrt(1/4+beta^2)<0.505. As cosine is strictly decreasing on this\ninterval, theta_c > pi/3-1/100. Therefore\n\n    |E_w| <= (2n+1)(1/24+1/(100pi))+1\n           < (2n+1)(1/24+1/300)+1\n           <= 9n/100+2.\n\nFor n>=100 the displayed bounds imply |C_s|>=|C_w| and |E_s|>=|E_w|.\nThis establishes the specified permutation without computing any roots or\nperforming a search.\n\n## 2. Uniform radial sparsity of the chronological prefix\n\nWrite r(h)=|1-hz|. Direct expansion gives\n\n    r(h)^2 = 1-h+h^2(1/4+beta^2).\n\nThus r(h)<=1 for 0<=h<=h_c and r(h)>1 for h>h_c. On [1,3] the right side\nis convex in h, so it is bounded by the larger endpoint value:\n\n    r(h)^2 <= 1/4+9beta^2 < 9/25,\n    r(h) < 3/5.                                                   (3)\n\nFor h>=6,\n\n    r(h) >= |1-h/2| = h/2-1 >= 2.                                 (4)\n\nEvery step in the first phase contracts radii, and weak steps are never\nadjacent. Every four successive steps wholly in that phase contain at\nleast two strong contractions. By (3), their total radial factor is less\nthan 9/25 < 1/2. Therefore, for any R>0, the first phase contributes at\nmost four prefix points with radius in (R/2,R].\n\nEvery step in the second phase expands radii, weak steps are never\nadjacent, and every two successive steps contain a strong expansion. By\n(4), their total radial factor is at least 2. That phase contributes at\nmost two prefix points in (R/2,R]. Count the shared boundary point in both\nphases if necessary. The complete prefix therefore satisfies the convenient\nlooser bound\n\n    #{i in {0,...,n}: R/2 < |xi_i| <= R} <= M,  M=8, for every R>0.  (5)\n\nAll xi_i are nonzero, since z is nonreal and all h_i are real. The count\nis a count of indexed points, so repeated points would also be counted.\n\n## 3. Every-cycle area bound from radial sparsity\n\nHere is a direct lemma for an arbitrary finite indexed planar point set\ncontaining the origin and satisfying (5). For any simple directed cycle C,\nput\n\n    Area_C = sum_(v->w in C) Im(conj(v)w),\n    Energy_C = sum_(v->w in C) |w-v|^2.\n\nThen\n\n    |Area_C| <= (16 M^2/3) Energy_C.                               (6)\n\nIf the cycle has only the origin, both sides vanish. Otherwise let R be\nits largest vertex radius. Summing over the disjoint annuli\n(R/2^(s+1),R/2^s], s>=0, gives\n\n    sum_(v in C)|v|^2 <= M R^2 sum_(s>=0)4^(-s) = 4M R^2/3.        (7)\n\nFirst suppose some vertex has radius at most R/2. Starting at a vertex of\nradius R, follow the directed cycle to its first such vertex. At most M\nvertices before that first arrival have radius greater than R/2, so this\npath has at most M edges. Its endpoint distance is at least R/2. Cauchy--\nSchwarz for the sum of its edge vectors gives\n\n    Energy_C >= R^2/(4M).\n\nThe inequality |Area_C|<=sum_(v in C)|v|^2 follows from\n|Im(conj(v)w)|<=|v||w| and 2|v||w|<=|v|^2+|w|^2. Combine with (7).\n\nIf instead every vertex has radius greater than R/2, the cycle has r<=M\nvertices. Translate one vertex to zero; the total Area_C is unchanged,\nsince the cross terms telescope, and Energy_C is unchanged. Each translated\nvertex is the sum of at most r edges, so its squared norm is at most\nr Energy_C. The previous area bound in the translated coordinates is\ntherefore at most r^2 Energy_C <= M^2 Energy_C, which implies (6).\n\nThe proof applies to every ordering of the vertices of each cycle, not\nonly chronological adjacent edges. With M=8 its constant is C=1024/3.\n\n## 4. All ordered interpolation conditions on the same real data\n\nSet\n\n    z' = (z-mu)/(1-mu) = 1/3 + (4 beta/3)i,\n    alpha' = 1/3,  beta' = 4 beta/3,\n    kappa' = alpha'-|z'|^2 = (2-16beta^2)/9,\n    g'_i = z' xi_i,  g'_star = 0.\n\nThe point list is still xi_i=P_i(z), NOT P_i(z'). Define edge weights on\nthe complete directed index graph I={star,0,...,n} by\n\n    q'_ij = <g'_j,xi_i-xi_j> + |g'_i-g'_j|^2/2,\n\nwith q'_ij attached to the directed edge j->i. Expanding real inner\nproducts, just as in accepted fact dfde2fa326c908bf but without requiring\na recurrence for z', gives\n\n    q'_ij = alpha'(|xi_i|^2-|xi_j|^2)/2\n             + beta' Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i)\n             - kappa' |xi_i-xi_j|^2/2.                            (8)\n\nThe norm terms telescope on every cycle. Our constants obey\n\n    kappa' >= 2 beta' C,\n\nbecause after multiplication by 9 this is\n\n    2-16beta^2 >= 8192 beta.\n\nThe left side exceeds 1 and the right side is less than 8192/10000<1.\nEquations (6) and (8) consequently imply that EVERY directed simple cycle\nhas nonpositive q'-weight. Repeated cycles split into simple cycles and\nhave the same property.\n\nFor i in I let ell'_i be the maximum weight of a directed path from i to\nstar, allowing the empty path at star, and set v'_i=-ell'_i. Removing any\ncycle cannot decrease a path weight, so this is a finite maximum over\nsimple paths. In particular ell'_star=0. Concatenating an edge j->i with\na maximizing path from i gives\n\n    ell'_j >= q'_ij+ell'_i,\n    v'_i-v'_j >= q'_ij.                                          (9)\n\nThus (9) checks every ordered smooth-convex interpolation inequality on\nexactly these planar points, gradients, and values. The real Gram matrix\nof any chosen list of these vectors is PSD because it is their ordinary\nEuclidean Gram matrix in R^2. The initial squared distance is |xi_0|^2=1;\nneither moments nor pairwise conditions are substituted for these checks.\n\nFor complete specification define\n\n    H'(v) = max_(j in I) { <g'_j,xi_j>-v'_j\n                         + <xi_j,v-g'_j> + |v-g'_j|^2/2 },\n    F'(x) = sup_v { <x,v>-H'(v) }.\n\nBy (9) and the accepted interpolation construction in 1252b8823bb5a290,\nF' is a finite convex differentiable 1-smooth function satisfying\nF'(xi_i)=v'_i, grad F'(xi_i)=g'_i, and F'(0)=min F'=0. That construction\nrequires only legal finite jets; it does not require that they form a GD\ntrajectory for z'. Finally put\n\n    f_n(x) = (1-mu)F'(x) + mu |x|^2/2,\n    v_i = (1-mu)v'_i + mu |xi_i|^2/2.\n\nThis function is mu-strongly convex, has 1-Lipschitz gradient, and is\nminimized at zero with value zero. At every prefix point,\n\n    grad f_n(xi_i) = ((1-mu)z'+mu)xi_i = z xi_i = g_i.\n\nIts sampled values are v_i, all its ordered interpolation inequalities\nhold by its globally constructed smooth convexity, and\nxi_(i+1)=xi_i-h_i g_i. This is a single legal function on a single real\nGram representation, for all the chronological data simultaneously.\n\n## 5. Free terminal completion and the orthogonal vector\n\nFor the ORIGINAL jets (xi_j,g_j,v_j) just constructed put\n\n    y_j = xi_j-g_j,  delta_j = v_j-|g_j|^2/2,\n    H(v) = |v|^2/2 + max_(j in I)(<y_j,v>-delta_j),\n    F_n = H^*.\n\nThe star values are y_star=delta_star=0. Accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290\nstates that F_n is a legal convex 1-smooth interpolant of all these prefix\njets, with minimum zero, and F_n(x)>=f_n(x) at every x.\n\nWe also have H(v)<=f_n^*(v)<=|v|^2/(2mu). The first inequality follows\neither by conjugating the pointwise maximality statement (H=H** because\nH is finite, continuous and convex), or directly from each conjugate\nsupport quadratic in accepted interpolation. The second follows from\nf_n(x)>=mu|x|^2/2 and maximizing <x,v>-mu|x|^2/2.\n\nLet a=xi_n and g=g_n=z a. Neither is zero. The following vector is real\nin the above R^2 identification:\n\n    d = a - (<a,g>/|g|^2)g\n      = a(1-z/(2|z|^2)),\n    w = mu d.\n\nIt obeys <w,g>=0, <a,d>=|d|^2, and\n\n    |d|^2 = beta^2 |a|^2/|z|^2.\n\nFor EVERY anchor j, including star, the bound on H(w) implies\n\n    |w|^2/2 + <y_j,w>-delta_j <= |w|^2/(2mu).\n\nRearranging gives the requested simultaneous terminal inequalities\n\n    2delta_j + 2<a-y_j,w> - |w|^2\n       >= 2<a,w> - |w|^2/mu\n       = mu |d|^2.                                               (10)\n\nFor every b>=0, conjugate evaluation at this same w gives\n\n    2F_n(a-bg) >= 2<a-bg,w>-2H(w)\n                 >= mu |d|^2\n                 = mu beta^2 |P_n(z)|^2/|z|^2.                   (11)\n\nNo minimax interchange or terminal linear-gradient constraint is used.\nThe function and vector do not depend on b. Formula (10) explicitly keeps\nevery remote prefix anchor; none is discarded.\n\n## 6. Exact payoff and comparison with the benchmark\n\nLet m=2n+1. Accepted fact dfde2fa326c908bf gives\n\n    P_n(t) = (-1)^n T_m(sqrt(t))/(m sqrt(t)),\n    sum_i h_i = 2n(n+1)/3,\n\nwhere T_m is the Chebyshev polynomial with T_m(cos s)=cos(ms).\nTake s=pi/2-i u, where sinh(u)=2beta. Then cos(s)=2i beta and\nz=(1+cos(s))/2, so cos(s/2) is a square root of z. As m is odd,\n\n    |T_m(sqrt(z))|^2\n      = |cos(m pi/4-i m u/2)|^2\n      = 1/2+sinh^2(m u/2)\n      = cosh(m u)/2.\n\nConsequently\n\n    |P_n(z)|^2 = cosh(m u)/(2m^2 |z|).\n\nSubstitution in (11), with mu=1/4, is exactly (1). Because u>0 is fixed,\ncosh(m u)>=exp(m u)/2, proving (2) and exponential growth divided by m^2.\n\nFor t in [0,1], the same Chebyshev identity gives\n\n    t P_n(t)^2 <= 1/m^2,\n\nincluding t=0 by continuity. On the schedule with the appended step b=1,\nthe ordinary convex quadratic channel is therefore\n\n    sup_(0<=t<=1) t (1-t)^2 P_n(t)^2 <= 1/m^2.                    (12)\n\nFor an arbitrary finite-dimensional convex quadratic with Hessian between\n0 and the identity, orthogonal diagonalization writes its normalized\nobjective as a nonnegative weighted average of the scalar terms in (12).\nThe weights sum to one after initial-distance normalization. Thus (12)\nalso bounds the entire ordinary convex quadratic family, not just scalar\nquadratics. Flat directions contribute zero.\n\nThe canonical sum-only Huber channel is\n\n    1/[1+2(2n(n+1)/3+1)] <= 3/(4n(n+1)).                          (13)\n\nAccepted fact b98bb807d514e80d gives\nB_N=1/U_N^ZJ <= 1+4N^p, p=log_2(3)<2, and B_N>=1. Multiplying (12) and\n(13) by B_(n+1) proves that both channel-to-benchmark ratios tend to zero.\nIn contrast J_n/U_(n+1)^ZJ=J_n B_(n+1)>=J_n tends to infinity.\n\nFor arbitrary prescribed L>0, replace F_n by L F_n; the normalized error\nand the GD points for normalized steps are unchanged.\n\n## 7. The same fixed spectral point is illegal in a different ordering\n\nConsider the increasing ordering a_1,...,a_n of exactly the same multiset.\nIts prefix points will be denoted X_i. The multiplier at h_c is\n\n    c = 1-h_c z\n      = -cos(2phi)-i sin(2phi)\n      = exp(i(pi+2pi/K)).\n\nSince K is a power of two and p_0=K/2+1 is odd, gcd(p_0,K)=1. Hence\nc^r, 0<=r<K, are the K distinct K-th roots of unity.\n\nChoose grid indices j_n so that theta_(j_n+1) tends to the interior point\ntheta_c=acos(1/sqrt(h_c)). Since K is fixed and the grid spacing tends to\nzero, for every r=0,...,K-2 the steps a_(j_n+1+r) tend to h_c. For all\nsufficiently large n these indices exist in {1,...,n}. None of the X_i\nvanishes, and the K normalized consecutive prefix points satisfy\n\n    X_(j_n+r)/X_(j_n) -> c^r,                  0<=r<K.             (14)\n\nFix the permutation of r which arranges the limit roots in positive angular\norder: its r-indices are p_0^(-1)s modulo K for s=0,...,K-1. The resulting\ncycle uses distinct indices for every n. Its limiting regular polygon has\n\n    Area/Energy = (1/2)cot(pi/K) = (1/2)cot(phi).\n\nThis follows directly by dividing K sin(2pi/K) by\n4K sin^2(pi/K). For the ORIGINAL spectral point z,\nkappa=Re(z)-|z|^2=1/2-|z|^2. The cycle weight in (8) with z in place of z'\nis therefore, at the limiting regular polygon,\n\n    beta Area - kappa Energy/2\n       = Energy[beta cot(phi)-kappa]/2\n       = Energy[1/2-(1/2-|z|^2)]/2\n       = Energy |z|^2/2 > 0.                                    (15)\n\nCycle area and energy are continuous in all vertices. By (14)-(15), for\nall sufficiently large n this actual normalized cycle has positive weight.\nUndoing normalization multiplies its weight by |X_(j_n)|^2>0, preserving\nthe sign. It violates a necessary smooth-convex interpolation cycle, so\nno values can make the increasing-order prefix with gradients z X_i legal.\n\nThe chosen radially sparse permutation was fully legal at the same z.\nThe endpoint polynomial and step sum are symmetric in the step multiset;\nthis legality distinction resides in the chronological prefix geometry.\n\n## Scope and open bridge\n\nThis gives one specified family and a quantitative terminal witness; it does\nnot prove the arbitrary-schedule exact profile. It does not assert that the\nincreasing-order schedule has small unrestricted worst-case error, nor that\nthe stronger single-checkpoint Huber channels miss this family. The next\nuniform problem is to extract comparable radial or other correlated data\nfrom arbitrary schedules, including repeated/zero and near-optimal steps.\nThe radial density constant M in this construction is controlled by the\nchosen permutation, not by the endpoint polynomial alone.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "master_guidance": "68efbfee40ba0708", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/radially_sparse_chebyshev_prefix.md", "verification_status": "Unverified; no routine fact_submit allocated", "mechanism": "Bounded radial density controls every interpolation cycle; strong-convex interpolation gives an orthogonal terminal vector and all-anchor affine lower bounds."}, "glossary": {"K": "Fixed integer65536.", "phi": "Angle pi/K.", "beta": "Positive imaginary part tan(phi)/2 of z.", "z": "Fixed nonreal spectral parameter1/2+i beta.", "mu": "Strong-convexity parameter1/4.", "h_c": "Unit-modulus multiplier threshold1/(1/4+beta^2).", "h^(n)": "Explicit strong/weak paired permutation of the n Chebyshev steps.", "C_s,C_w,E_w,E_s": "Strong/weak contraction and weak/strong expansion classes defined in evidence.", "P_i": "Chronological prefix polynomial product_(r<i)(1-h_r t).", "xi_i": "Actual planar prefix position P_i(z).", "M": "Upper bound8 on indexed prefix points in any radial annulus(R/2,R].", "C": "Every-cycle area-energy constant16M^2/3=1024/3.", "z'": "Transformed spectral value(z-mu)/(1-mu), used at the SAME xi_i.", "q'_ij": "Complete directed interpolation edge weight for gradients z'xi_j.", "ell'_i": "Maximum q'-path weight from i to the optimum star.", "v'_i": "Interpolating value-ell'_i for transformed jets.", "F'": "Convex1-smooth interpolant of transformed jets given by the explicit conjugate formula.", "f_n": "Globally1-smooth mu-strongly-convex function(1-mu)F'+mu||x||^2/2.", "F_n": "Pointwise maximal original-prefix interpolant from accepted1252b8823bb5a290.", "w": "mu times projection of xi_n onto the orthogonal complement of g_n; the one terminal test vector for every final step.", "J_n": "Explicit uniform final-step objective lower bound in the claim.", "u": "asinh(2beta).", "X_i": "Prefix points for the increasing permutation, used only in the order-sensitivity obstruction."}}
{"id": "bc047aa498ba614a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:35:17.996479+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Increasing Chebyshev order also has a fully legal 2D free-terminal adversary: with z_n=1/2+i/(4096 sqrt(n)), one fixed maximal interpolant gives inf_(b>=0) W_(n+1)(Chi_n increasing,b) >= exp(sqrt(n)/2048)/(144*4096^2*n^3) -> infinity, although sum-only Huber and all ordinary quadratic channels at b=1 are O(n^-2). New complete-cycle lemma is |Area|<=3M Energy for radial density M, and the actual increasing prefix has M<=32sqrt(n). Every anchor and the orthogonal terminal vector are checked. Unverified candidate.", "evidence": "# Increasing Chebyshev prefixes: a growing legal free-terminal witness\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, unverified. No new fact submission is\nallocated. This strengthens the mechanism in candidate72acacb33061e3ab but\ndoes NOT use that unverified candidate as a premise. The proof below includes\nevery new geometric step and uses accepted facts1252b8823bb5a290,\ndfde2fa326c908bf, and b98bb807d514e80d only.\n\n## Result\n\nFor every integer n>=100, use the INCREASING Chebyshev schedule\n\n    h_(j-1)=sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2n+1)]),       j=1,...,n.\n\nPut\n\n    beta_n=1/(4096 sqrt(n)),  z_n=1/2+i beta_n,  mu=1/4,\n    P_0(t)=1,  P_i(t)=product_(r<i)(1-h_r t),\n    xi_i=P_i(z_n),  g_i=z_n xi_i,            i=0,...,n,\n    xi_star=g_star=0,\n    m=2n+1,  u_n=asinh(2 beta_n).\n\nThere exist explicit values v_i and one globally convex differentiable\nmu-strongly-convex 1-smooth function f_n on the real plane, minimized at\nzero with value zero, interpolating all these points, gradients and values.\nThe data are the actual ordered GD prefix, with |xi_0|=1. The pointwise\nmaximal interpolant F_n of these same jets has, for every real appended\nstep b, the bound\n\n    2F_n(xi_n-b g_n) >= J_n,\n    J_n = beta_n^2 cosh(m u_n)/(8 m^2 |z_n|^3)\n        >= exp(sqrt(n)/2048)/(144*4096^2*n^3).                    (1)\n\nOne explicit terminal test vector w_n, orthogonal to g_n, satisfies the\ncorresponding inequality against EVERY prefix anchor. Neither the function\nnor w_n depends on b. In particular\n\n    inf_(b>=0) W_(n+1)(h,b) >= J_n -> infinity.                    (2)\n\nAt b=1, the complete ordinary convex quadratic channel is at most\n1/(2n+1)^2, and the canonical sum-only Huber channel is at most\n3/[4n(n+1)]. Both ratios to the exact U_(n+1)^ZJ tend to zero, whereas\nJ_n/U_(n+1)^ZJ tends to infinity. This is a specified schedule family,\nnot the unrestricted exact-profile theorem.\n\n## 1. A linear radial-density area estimate\n\nLet an arbitrary finite indexed planar point set contain the origin. Assume\nthat at most M nonzero indexed points lie in (R/2,R] in radius for every\nR>0, where M>=1 need not be an integer. Then EVERY simple directed cycle\nthrough these points satisfies\n\n    |Area_C| <= 3M Energy_C,                                    (3)\n    Area_C=sum_(v->w in C) Im(conj(v)w),\n    Energy_C=sum_(v->w in C)|w-v|^2.\n\nCycles with every vertex zero have area and energy zero. Otherwise let R\nbe the largest radius. Summing the squared vertex radii over dyadic annuli\ngives\n\n    sum_(v in C)|v|^2 <= (4M/3) R^2.                             (4)\n\nIf the cycle visits a radius <=R/2, follow it from a largest-radius vertex\nto the first such visit. This path has at most M edges, since every vertex\nbefore the arrival lies in (R/2,R]. The endpoint distance is at least R/2,\nso Cauchy--Schwarz gives\n\n    Energy_C >= R^2/(4M).                                      (5)\n\nRewrite the area as sum Im(conj(v)(w-v)). Cauchy--Schwarz, (4), and (5)\nthen imply\n\n    |Area_C| <= sqrt(sum|v|^2) sqrt(Energy_C)\n             <= (4M/sqrt(3)) Energy_C < 3M Energy_C.\n\nIf instead all cycle vertices have radius >R/2, their number r is at most\nM. Translate one vertex to zero; area and energy are invariant under this\ntranslation because the extra area terms telescope. Each translated vertex\nis a sum of at most r edge vectors, hence has squared norm at most\nr Energy_C. Thus the sum of their squared norms is at most r^2 Energy_C.\nThe same Cauchy--Schwarz area bound now gives\n|Area_C|<=r Energy_C<=M Energy_C. This proves (3).\n\nThe proof covers arbitrary cycles, including all reorderings of any subset\nof the prefix; no chronological-edge shortcut is used.\n\n## 2. Radial density of the increasing prefix\n\nFor this section abbreviate beta=beta_n and z=z_n, and set\n\n    a=1/4+beta^2,  h_c=1/a,\n    r(t)=|1-tz|,  ell(t)=log r(t).\n\nHere 0<beta<1/1000, 3<h_c<4, and r(t)^2=1-t+a t^2. The radii |xi_i|\ndecrease during the steps t<=h_c and increase during the steps t>h_c.\nAll are nonzero.\n\nSplit the increasing steps into the four consecutive blocks\n\n    [1,3],  (3,h_c],  (h_c,6),  [6,infinity).\n\nFor 1<=t<=3, convexity of r(t)^2 as a function of t gives\n\n    r(t)^2<=1/4+9beta^2<9/25,\n\nso two consecutive steps reduce radius by a factor less than 1/2.\nThis first block contributes at most two indexed prefix points to any\nannulus (R/2,R]. For t>=6, r(t)>=t/2-1>=2, so the fourth block contributes\nat most one. Assign each block both boundary points when counting; possible\ndouble-counting only enlarges the bound.\n\nFor 3<=t<=6,\n\n    ell'(t)=(-1+2a t)/[2(1-t+a t^2)] >= 1/20.                    (6)\n\nIndeed the numerator is at least 1/2. The quadratic in the denominator is\nincreasing for t>=3 and at t=6 is 4+36beta^2<5, so its doubled value is\nless than 10. As ell(h_c)=0, integration of (6) gives\n\n    -ell(t)>=(h_c-t)/20  for 3<=t<=h_c,\n     ell(t)>=(t-h_c)/20  for h_c<=t<=6.                          (7)\n\nThe angles defining the h_j have spacing pi/(2n+1)>1/n. On the angle\ninterval where sec^2(theta)>=3, the derivative of sec^2(theta) is\n2 sec^2(theta)tan(theta)>=6. Therefore distinct increasing steps in [3,6]\nwhose indices differ by q have values separated by at least q/n.\n\nConsider K consecutive steps entirely inside either middle block. In the\ncontracting block, their distances below h_c have sum at least\nK(K-1)/(2n): the last distance is nonnegative, and preceding distances\nincrease by at least 1/n. In the expanding block the same bound follows\nstarting at the first distance above h_c. Equation (7) gives, in either\ncase, an absolute logarithmic radial change of at least\n\n    K(K-1)/(40n).                                               (8)\n\nIf q prefix points from a middle block lie in one annulus, they are\nconsecutive within that block, since its radii are monotone. Put K=q-1.\nTheir logarithmic radial change is at most log 2<1. If q>=8sqrt(n)+2,\nthen K(K-1)>=64n>40n, contradicting (8). Each middle block thus contributes\nfewer than 8sqrt(n)+2 points.\n\nThe full indexed prefix, with boundary points counted twice if necessary,\ntherefore has fewer than 16sqrt(n)+7 points in any annulus. In particular\n\n    #{i: R/2<|xi_i|<=R} <= M_n=32sqrt(n)  for every R>0.           (9)\n\nAn empty block consists only of its boundary point for this counting\nargument and also satisfies the displayed bounds. This establishes (9)\nwithout evaluating a polynomial or any trajectory numerically.\n\n## 3. Full interpolation on the same two-dimensional data\n\nUse the transformed gradients\n\n    z'=(z-mu)/(1-mu)=1/3+(4beta/3)i,\n    g'_i=z'xi_i,\n    kappa'=Re(z')-|z'|^2=(2-16beta^2)/9,\n    beta'=Im(z')=4beta/3.\n\nThese gradients are assigned at the ORIGINAL positions P_i(z). Let\nI={star,0,...,n}. For the edge j->i set\n\n    q'_ij=<g'_j,xi_i-xi_j>+|g'_i-g'_j|^2/2.\n\nExpanding real inner products gives the accepted cycle identity in its\npurely algebraic form, valid for an arbitrary point list:\n\n    q'_ij=Re(z')(|xi_i|^2-|xi_j|^2)/2\n            +beta' Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i)\n            -kappa'|xi_i-xi_j|^2/2.                             (10)\n\nNo recurrence for z' is claimed or needed. By (3) and (9), every cycle has\n|Area_C|<=96sqrt(n) Energy_C. Moreover\n\n    kappa' >= 2beta' *96sqrt(n),                                (11)\n\nbecause multiplying by 9 reduces this to\n\n    2-16beta^2 >=2304 beta sqrt(n)=2304/4096=9/16.\n\nThe left side exceeds 1. The telescoping norm terms in (10) and (11) show\nthat every cycle has nonpositive total q'-weight.\n\nDefine ell'_i to be the maximum q'-weight of a directed path from i to\nstar, with the empty optimum path allowed, and put v'_i=-ell'_i. A path\nwith a repeated vertex contains a nonpositive cycle, whose deletion cannot\ndecrease its weight. Hence this is a finite maximum over simple paths,\nell'_star=0, and edge concatenation gives\n\n    v'_i-v'_j>=q'_ij  for ALL i,j in I.                          (12)\n\nDefine the finite convex function and its conjugate\n\n    H'(v)=max_(j in I){<g'_j,xi_j>-v'_j\n                      +<xi_j,v-g'_j>+|v-g'_j|^2/2},\n    F'(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H'(v)).\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, applied to (12), makes F' a finite convex\ndifferentiable 1-smooth interpolant of (xi_i,g'_i,v'_i), with minimum zero\nat the origin. Finally define\n\n    f_n(x)=(1-mu)F'(x)+mu|x|^2/2,\n    v_i=(1-mu)v'_i+mu|xi_i|^2/2.\n\nThis single function is mu-strongly convex, is 1-smooth, has minimum zero,\nand has gradient ((1-mu)z'+mu)xi_i=z xi_i at every xi_i. All original\nordered interpolation conditions hold because these are jets of this\none globally constructed function. All vector inner products belong to\nthe ordinary real Gram matrix in R^2 and hence are PSD; |xi_0|^2=1.\nThe polynomial recurrence is exactly xi_(i+1)=xi_i-h_i g_i.\n\n## 4. Every-anchor lower inequalities for one free terminal vector\n\nFor the original jets put\n\n    y_j=xi_j-g_j,  delta_j=v_j-|g_j|^2/2,\n    H(v)=|v|^2/2+max_(j in I)(<y_j,v>-delta_j),  F_n=H^*.\n\nThe star contributes y_star=delta_star=0. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290\nmakes F_n a pointwise maximal legal interpolant and gives F_n>=f_n.\nConjugating this inequality, or using the individual conjugate support\nquadratics, gives\n\n    H(v)<=f_n^*(v)<=|v|^2/(2mu).                                (13)\n\nThe second inequality follows from f_n(x)>=mu|x|^2/2. Write A=xi_n and\nG=g_n=z A. Define\n\n    D=A-(<A,G>/|G|^2)G=A(1-z/(2|z|^2)),\n    w_n=mu D.\n\nThen <w_n,G>=0, <A,D>=|D|^2, and\n|D|^2=beta^2|P_n(z)|^2/|z|^2. For EVERY j in I, (13) implies\n\n    2delta_j+2<A-y_j,w_n>-|w_n|^2\n        >=2<A,w_n>-|w_n|^2/mu\n        =mu|D|^2.                                              (14)\n\nThus the prescribed finite data obey all the lower affine anchor tests\non the same vector. For any real b, conjugate evaluation at w_n gives\n\n    2F_n(A-bG)>=2<A-bG,w_n>-2H(w_n)\n               >=mu beta^2 |P_n(z)|^2/|z|^2.                   (15)\n\nThis endpoint is free: no gradient at it is imposed. Its value comes from\nthe same fixed maximal interpolant, independently of b. We have not used\nan unverified minimax interchange.\n\n## 5. Quantitative evaluation and normalization\n\nAccepted factdfde2fa326c908bf gives, for this exact multiset and any order,\n\n    P_n(t)=(-1)^n T_m(sqrt(t))/(m sqrt(t)),\n    sum_j h_j=2n(n+1)/3,  m=2n+1.\n\nFor u=asinh(2beta), set s=pi/2-i u. Then cos(s)=2i beta and\nz=(1+cos(s))/2, so sqrt(z) may be chosen to be cos(s/2). The polynomial\nidentity T_m(cos t)=cos(mt) and oddness of m yield\n\n    |T_m(sqrt(z))|^2\n       =|cos(m pi/4-i m u/2)|^2\n       =1/2+sinh^2(m u/2)=cosh(m u)/2.\n\nThus |P_n(z)|^2=cosh(m u)/(2m^2|z|), and (15) is the first expression\nfor J_n in (1). Since 2beta<1,\n\n    asinh(2beta)=integral_0^(2beta) dt/sqrt(1+t^2)>=beta.\n\nAlso m>=2n, m<=3n, |z|<=1 and cosh(t)>=exp(t)/2 for t>=0. These give\n\n    m u >= sqrt(n)/2048,\n    J_n >= exp(sqrt(n)/2048)/(144*4096^2*n^3),\n\nwhich tends to infinity.\n\nFor t in [0,1], |T_m(sqrt(t))|<=1 gives tP_n(t)^2<=1/m^2, including\nt=0 by continuity. After the appended unit step, the scalar quadratic\nerror is t(1-t)^2P_n(t)^2<=1/m^2. Every finite-dimensional convex quadratic\nis a nonnegative normalized spectral average of these scalar errors,\nso the same bound holds for that entire quadratic class. The canonical\nsum-only Huber value is\n\n    1/[1+2(2n(n+1)/3+1)]<=3/[4n(n+1)].\n\nAccepted factb98bb807d514e80d gives B_N=1/U_N^ZJ<=1+4N^p for\np=log_2(3)<2 and B_N>=1. Multiplying the two channel bounds by B_(n+1)\nmakes both ratios tend to zero. Multiplying J_n by B_(n+1) preserves its\ndivergence. Multiplying the constructed functions by any specified L>0\nrestores that smoothness constant without changing normalized errors or\nthe GD points for steps h_i/L.\n\n## General usable criterion and limitation\n\nThe proof also yields a criterion for ANY schedule and nonreal z=alpha+i beta.\nIf its indexed points P_i(z), after removing exact duplicate jets when\nappropriate, have radial density at most M, and some 0<mu<1 satisfies\n\n    (alpha-mu)(1-alpha)-beta^2 >= 6M |beta|(1-mu),               (16)\n\nthen the transformed-gradient interpolation above is legal and gives one\nfixed all-final-step witness\n\n    inf_(b>=0) W_(n+1)(h,b)\n       >= mu beta^2 |P_n(z)|^2/|z|^2.                          (17)\n\nIndeed (16) is exactly the sufficient cycle condition for\nz'=(z-mu)/(1-mu), using (3); (12)-(15) give the realization and payoff.\nFor a finite indexed set one may always retain all indices instead of\nremoving duplicates; a compression is only useful when identical position\nand gradient jets would otherwise artificially enlarge M.\n\nNo horizon-independent bound on M has been proved for arbitrary orderings\nor arbitrary nonnegative steps. Equation (16) is a sufficient condition,\nnot a characterization of legality and not an assertion about the strength\nof mixtures of individually illegal orbits. The remaining exact-profile\nbridge must handle schedules whose full prefix geometry is not covered by\nthis quantitative criterion.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["72acacb33061e3ab"], "plan_id": "b15b30637c7afbba", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/ascending_chebyshev_free_terminal.md", "verification_status": "No new fact_submit; this is a complete candidate only", "scope": "Specified unbounded-horizon family and a sufficient all-schedule radial-density criterion; full exact profile unresolved."}, "glossary": {"beta_n": "Horizon-dependent imaginary part1/(4096 sqrt(n)).", "z_n": "Complex spectral point1/2+i beta_n.", "P_i": "Ordered prefix polynomial product_(r<i)(1-h_r t).", "xi_i": "Actual prefix point P_i(z_n) in the real plane.", "M_n": "Uniform upper bound32sqrt(n) on indexed prefix points in every annulus(R/2,R].", "mu": "Strong-convexity parameter1/4.", "J_n": "Exact payoff beta_n^2 cosh((2n+1)asinh(2beta_n))/(8(2n+1)^2|z_n|^3).", "h_c": "Radial unit-multiplier threshold1/(1/4+beta_n^2).", "ell(t)": "Logarithm of |1-t z_n|, used only for radial-density counting.", "F'": "Convex1-smooth interpolant of the transformed jets with gradients z'xi_i.", "f_n": "Strongly convex legal interpolant(1-mu)F'+mu||x||^2/2.", "F_n": "Pointwise maximal interpolant of the original queried prefix jets.", "w_n": "mu times the orthogonal projection of xi_n onto g_n perpendicular; independent of the appended step.", "Area_C": "Sum of Im(conj(v)w) over every directed edge of a cycle.", "Energy_C": "Sum of squared edge lengths of that cycle."}}
{"id": "64486781861952bc", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:39:38.328860+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Entire real selfadjoint polynomial-prefix class is insufficient even with arbitrary legal values and maximal free terminal completion. For ANY real symmetric A (spectrum not restricted to[0,1]) and legal jets x_i=P_i(A)x0,g_i=A x_i, every interpolant satisfies 2F(x_n-g_n)<=2 max_(0<=t<=1) t(1-t)P_n(t)^2. This covers mixtures of individually illegal real modes. On every Chebyshev permutation the whole class is O(n^-2) after one common unit step and misses the exact profile.", "evidence": "# Free completion cannot rescue any real selfadjoint polynomial prefix\n\nStatus: candidate proof, unverified; no new fact_submit allocated.\nAccepted predecessors:1252b8823bb5a290 for maximal prefix interpolation,\ndfde2fa326c908bf for the Chebyshev identity, and b98bb807d514e80d for the\nbenchmark bound. No other candidate is a premise.\n\n## Entire ansatz and bound\n\nFix any nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), including zeros and large\nsteps, and put P_0(t)=1, P_i(t)=product_(r<i)(1-h_r t). Consider ANY legal\nqueried prefix with optimum jet (0,0,0), normalized |x_0|=1, and\n\n    x_i=P_i(A)x_0,  g_i=A x_i,  i=0,...,n,\n\nwhere A is an arbitrary real symmetric operator on a finite-dimensional\nEuclidean space. In particular, its eigenvalues are NOT required to be in\n[0,1]. The values f_i are unrestricted except for complete smooth-convex\ninterpolation legality of the same prefix. They need not equal quadratic\nvalues <x_i,A x_i>/2. The function used at the appended terminal point is\nalso unrestricted among all legal convex 1-smooth interpolants of these\nprefix jets.\n\nDefine the endpoint-polynomial number\n\n    Xi_n(h)=max_(0<=t<=1) t(1-t)P_n(t)^2.\n\nThen EVERY such interpolant F satisfies the common unit-step bound\n\n    2F(x_n-g_n) <= 2Xi_n(h).                                    (1)\n\nConsequently, even optimizing all real spectral modes, their nonnegative\nmixture weights, arbitrary legal prefix values, and maximal free terminal\ncompletion cannot produce an all-final-step lower witness greater than\n2Xi_n(h). The same unit step b=1 works for the entire ansatz, so this bounds\nboth supremum-over-prefixes followed by infimum-over-b and the reversed\ninfimum-over-b followed by supremum-over-prefixes. No interchange is used.\n\n## Proof retaining arbitrary legal values and mixed-mode legality\n\nSmooth-convex interpolation between the last queried sample and the optimum\ngives\n\n    f_n <= <g_n,x_n>-|g_n|^2/2.                                (2)\n\nFor completeness, this is Q_(n,star)<=0 in accepted1252b8823bb5a290, where\nQ_(n,star)=f_n-<g_n,x_n>+|g_n|^2/2. Gradient Lipschitzness gives the descent\ninequality at a unit step:\n\n    F(x_n-g_n)<=F(x_n)-|g_n|^2/2=f_n-|g_n|^2/2.\n\nIt follows by integrating the gradient along that segment: the discrepancy\nfrom the linear term -|g_n|^2 is at most |g_n|^2/2. Combining with (2),\n\n    2F(x_n-g_n)<=2<x_n,g_n>-2|g_n|^2\n                =2<x_0,P_n(A)^2(A-A^2)x_0>.                    (3)\n\nChoose an orthonormal eigenbasis of the real symmetric operator A, let\nlambda_l be its eigenvalues and let w_l be the squared coordinates of x_0\nin that basis. Then w_l>=0, sum_l w_l=1, and the right side of (3) is\n\n    2sum_l w_l lambda_l(1-lambda_l)P_n(lambda_l)^2.               (4)\n\nIf lambda_l is outside [0,1], its term is nonpositive. If it is inside,\nthe term without w_l is at most Xi_n(h). Dropping all nonpositive terms\nand using the sum of the remaining weights <=1 proves (1).\n\nThis is also a proof directly in the finite real-mixture coordinates\nx_i=(sqrt(w_l)P_i(lambda_l))_l and\ng_i=(sqrt(w_l)lambda_l P_i(lambda_l))_l. It never assumes that an individual\nmode is interpolable. Even a mixture whose outside-[0,1] components are\nindividually illegal is covered whenever the full joint prefix is legal.\nNo constraint has been imposed on the incoming or outgoing unqueried\nterminal jets beyond F being a genuine smooth convex interpolant.\n\nThe pointwise maximal interpolant from accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is one\nsuch F, so arbitrary free terminal completion does not avoid the bound.\nThe proof does not rely on any ordering of the steps, their strict\npositivity, or a bound on their size.\n\n## Quantitative unbounded-horizon obstruction\n\nFor ANY permutation of the Chebyshev multiset\n\n    h_(j-1)=sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2n+1)]), j=1,...,n,\n\naccepteddfde2fa326c908bf gives\n\n    P_n(t)=(-1)^n T_(2n+1)(sqrt(t))/[(2n+1)sqrt(t)],\n    tP_n(t)^2<=1/(2n+1)^2,  0<=t<=1.\n\nThus (1) bounds the entire real selfadjoint ansatz at the appended unit\nstep by 2/(2n+1)^2. The canonical sum-only Huber channel there is\n\n    1/[1+2(2n(n+1)/3+1)] <=3/[4n(n+1)].\n\nAcceptedb98bb807d514e80d states B_(n+1)<=1+4(n+1)^p, p=log_2(3)<2.\nMultiplying either bound by B_(n+1) makes its ratio to U_(n+1)^ZJ tend to\nzero. Hence the whole real selfadjoint prefix class, even with unrestricted\nlegal values and a free terminal, cannot prove a uniform matching profile.\n\nThis conclusion concerns the stated ansatz, not the actual W_(n+1). The\nnonreal planar constructions in separately recorded candidates escape it\nbecause multiplication by alpha+i beta, beta!=0, has a nonzero skew-symmetric\npart as a real operator and is not selfadjoint. More general nonnormal or\nnoncommuting correlation kernels are not covered either.\n\nFor any L>0, multiply the interpolating F by L and use normalized gradients;\nthe same bounds hold for the project's normalized objective and steps h_i/L.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/real_spectral_free_terminal_obstruction.md", "related_nonreal_candidates": ["72acacb33061e3ab", "bc047aa498ba614a"], "status": "Complete unverified scoped obstruction; no new fact_submit"}, "glossary": {"A": "Any finite-dimensional real symmetric operator; even outside-[0,1] eigenvalues are allowed when the full prefix is legal.", "P_i": "Ordered prefix polynomial product_(r<i)(1-h_r t).", "Xi_n(h)": "Maximum of t(1-t)P_n(t)^2 over t in[0,1].", "lambda_l": "Real eigenvalues of A.", "w_l": "Squared spectral coordinates of normalized x0, nonnegative and summing to one.", "F": "Any globally convex1-smooth interpolant of the complete given prefix, including the pointwise maximal one."}}
{"id": "cedcf8ce28d0fe89", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:44:41.250113+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Quantitative chronological barrier: EVERY Chebyshev permutation with R monotone runs admits one legal 2D maximal prefix interpolant with inf_(b>=0) normalized terminal error >= exp(sqrt(n)/(2048R))/(144*4096^2 R^2 n^3). Hence any successful ordering must have R=Omega(sqrt(n)/log n). In particular the shared folded even/odd two-run family defeats all quadratic and every single-cut Huber channel at O(n^-2), but this full-cycle/free-terminal witness diverges as exp(c sqrt(n))/n^3. Complete unverified proof includes the old single-cut estimates anew.", "evidence": "# Monotone-run complexity of Chebyshev schedules and a free-terminal adversary\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit is allocated.\nAccepted mathematical inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 (finite interpolation and\npointwise maximal completion), dfde2fa326c908bf (polynomial identity and\ncycle algebra), and b98bb807d514e80d (single-cut functional and benchmark).\nThe earlier candidates bc047aa498ba614a and max3's f18bd083739517ba motivated\nthis proof, but neither is used as a premise; all required new steps follow.\n\n## The quantitative statement\n\nFix n>=100. Let h be ANY permutation of the n Chebyshev steps\n\n    sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2n+1)]),             j=1,...,n.\n\nSuppose its chronological list is partitioned into R nonempty consecutive\nruns, each strictly increasing or strictly decreasing. No condition is\nplaced on how the runs interleave their sets of root indices. Thus 1<=R<=n.\nSet\n\n    beta=1/(4096 R sqrt(n)),  z=1/2+i beta,  mu=1/4,\n    m=2n+1,  u=asinh(2beta),\n    P_i(t)=product_(r<i)(1-h_r t),  P_0(t)=1.\n\nThere is one explicitly defined convex differentiable 1-smooth function\nF_h on R^2, minimized at zero, whose actual ordered prefix satisfies\n\n    x_i=P_i(z),  g_i=zP_i(z),  i=0,...,n,  |x_0|=1,\n\nand which obeys for EVERY real appended step b\n\n    2F_h(x_n-b g_n) >= J_(n,R),\n    J_(n,R)=beta^2 cosh(m u)/(8m^2|z|^3)\n       >= exp(sqrt(n)/(2048R))/(C R^2 n^3),\n    C=144*4096^2.                                               (1)\n\nIt is the pointwise maximal interpolant of a concrete legal queried prefix.\nThere is a specified real vector w perpendicular to g_n whose lower affine\nvalue is at least J_(n,R) against EVERY prefix anchor. The terminal gradient\nis free; no endpoint linear-orbit condition or minimax interchange is used.\n\nIt follows that\n\n    inf_(b>=0) W_(n+1)(h,b) >= J_(n,R).                          (2)\n\nIf a sequence of such permutations has R=o(sqrt(n)/log(n)), the lower bound\nin (2) tends to infinity. More explicitly, if even one appended step b has\nW_(n+1)(h,b)<=1, then necessarily\n\n    R >= sqrt(n)/[2048(log(C)+5log(n))].                         (3)\n\nThe ordinary quadratic and canonical sum-only Huber channels at b=1 are\nO(n^-2), independently of the permutation. The explicit two-run permutation\ndefined in Section6 has the stronger property that EVERY single-cut Huber\nchannel is also O(n^-2), yet (1) grows as exp(c sqrt(n))/n^3. All these weak\nchannels divided by the exact benchmark U_(n+1)^ZJ tend to zero.\n\n## 1. A complete-cycle bound from radial density\n\nSuppose a finite indexed planar point set, containing zero, has at most M\nnonzero indexed points in every radial annulus (s/2,s], with M>=1. For every\nsimple directed cycle C in those indices define\n\n    Area_C=sum_(v->w) Im(conj(v)w),\n    Energy_C=sum_(v->w)|w-v|^2.\n\nThen\n\n    |Area_C|<=3M Energy_C.                                     (4)\n\nIf all cycle vertices are zero there is nothing to prove. Let s>0 be the\nmaximum vertex radius. The annular counts give\n\n    sum_(v in C)|v|^2 <= M s^2 sum_(j>=0)4^(-j) =4M s^2/3.       (5)\n\nIf some vertex has radius <=s/2, follow the cycle from a largest-radius\nvertex to the first such vertex. There are at most M edges before this\narrival; its endpoint distance is at least s/2. Cauchy--Schwarz for the\nedge sum gives Energy_C>=s^2/(4M). On the other hand,\n\n    Area_C=sum Im(conj(v)(w-v)),\n    |Area_C|<=sqrt(sum|v|^2)*sqrt(Energy_C).\n\nCombining with (5) yields |Area_C|<3M Energy_C. If every cycle vertex has\nradius >s/2, its vertex count r is at most M. Translate one vertex to zero;\narea and energy remain unchanged, by telescoping the added area terms.\nEvery translated vertex is the sum of at most r edges, so its squared norm\nis at most r Energy_C. The same Cauchy--Schwarz bound gives\n|Area_C|<=r Energy_C<=M Energy_C. This proves (4) for ALL cycles, in every\nordering of their selected vertices.\n\n## 2. Radial density for R monotone runs\n\nFor the prescribed z put a=1/4+beta^2, t_c=1/a, and\n\n    r(t)=|1-tz|,  ell(t)=log r(t).\n\nHere beta<1/1000, 3<t_c<4, and r(t)^2=1-t+a t^2. Factors with t<=t_c\ncontract radii; those with t>t_c expand them. None is zero.\n\nWithin a single monotone run, split the steps at 3,t_c,6. The resulting\nfour consecutive sublists are in either forward or reversed order, and\neach sublist has monotone radii. For steps 1<=t<=3,\n\n    r(t)^2<=1/4+9beta^2<9/25,\n\nso at most two prefix points of that sublist lie in a given annulus. For\nt>=6, r(t)>=t/2-1>=2, so at most one does.\n\nFor 3<=t<=6,\n\n    ell'(t)=(-1+2at)/[2(1-t+at^2)]>=1/20,                       (6)\n\nbecause the numerator is at least1/2 and the doubled denominator is less\nthan10. Since ell(t_c)=0,\n\n    |ell(t)|>=|t-t_c|/20.                                     (7)\n\nThe original root angles have spacing pi/(2n+1)>1/n. On angles for which\nsec^2(theta)>=3, its derivative 2sec^2(theta)tan(theta) is at least6.\nThus consecutive distinct steps in a monotone sublist inside [3,6] differ\nby at least1/n, even when their root indices skip many entries. For K\nconsecutive steps on the same side of t_c, their distances from t_c have\nsum at least K(K-1)/(2n). Hence (7) makes the logarithmic radial change\nin absolute value at least K(K-1)/(40n).\n\nIf q prefix points of either middle sublist lie in an annulus, its monotone\nradii make those points consecutive within that sublist. Set K=q-1. The\nlogarithmic change is at most log2<1, so q<8sqrt(n)+2. Both middle sublists\ntogether with the two extreme sublists contribute fewer than\n16sqrt(n)+7<=32sqrt(n) points. Give each sublist both its boundary points;\nthis only overcounts. Empty sublists have at most their one boundary point\nand obey the same bound. The argument applies to decreasing runs too,\nsince the sums of nonnegative distances in (7) are independent of which\nend of the sublist is read first.\n\nEach run starts at some nonzero radius; multiplying every radius in a run\nby that starting radius leaves its count bound valid for every annulus.\nSum over the R runs, again allowing repeated boundary counts, to obtain\n\n    #{i: s/2<|P_i(z)|<=s} <= M=32R sqrt(n)  for every s>0.        (8)\n\n## 3. Complete legality, values, and real realization\n\nIdentify the complex plane with R^2 and use <v,w>=Re(conj(v)w). Put\nx_i=P_i(z), g_i=zx_i, including i=n, and add x_star=g_star=0. Define\n\n    z'=(z-mu)/(1-mu)=1/3+(4beta/3)i,\n    g'_i=z'x_i,\n    beta'=4beta/3,  kappa'=Re(z')-|z'|^2=(2-16beta^2)/9.\n\nUse the SAME positions x_i=P_i(z), not P_i(z'). For every ordered pair\nof indices from I={star,0,...,n} define the edge j->i weight\n\n    q'_ij=<g'_j,x_i-x_j>+|g'_i-g'_j|^2/2.\n\nExpansion gives the complete algebraic cycle identity\n\n    q'_ij=Re(z')(|x_i|^2-|x_j|^2)/2\n              +beta' Im(conj(x_j)x_i)-kappa'|x_i-x_j|^2/2.       (9)\n\nBy (4) and (8), every cycle has |Area_C|<=96R sqrt(n) Energy_C. Moreover\n\n    kappa'>=2beta'*96R sqrt(n),\n\nbecause after multiplication by9 this is\n2-16beta^2>=2304beta R sqrt(n)=9/16, and its left side exceeds1. The norm\nterms of (9) telescope, proving that every directed simple cycle has\nnonpositive weight. Closed walks have the same property by splitting at\nrepeated vertices.\n\nLet ell'_i be the maximum q'-weight of a path from i to star and let\nv'_i=-ell'_i, allowing the empty path at star. Removing nonpositive cycles\ncannot decrease path weight. Thus the maxima are finite maxima over\nsimple paths, ell'_star=0, and concatenation gives\n\n    v'_i-v'_j>=q'_ij for EVERY ordered pair.                    (10)\n\nDefine\n\n    H'(v)=max_(j in I){<g'_j,x_j>-v'_j\n                         +<x_j,v-g'_j>+|v-g'_j|^2/2},\n    F'(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H'(v)),\n    f(x)=(1-mu)F'(x)+mu|x|^2/2,\n    v_i=(1-mu)v'_i+mu|x_i|^2/2.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 applies to (10): F' is convex1-smooth and\ninterpolates all transformed jets with minimum0 at0. It follows directly\nthat f is mu-strongly convex,1-smooth, minimized at0, and has values v_i\nand gradients ((1-mu)z'+mu)x_i=zx_i=g_i at all samples. Every original\nordered interpolation condition holds for these jets of this one function.\nTheir real Gram matrix is the ordinary Euclidean Gram matrix in R^2 and\nis PSD; |x_0|=1. Finally x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, the required recursion.\n\n## 4. One terminal vector, all anchors, and all final steps\n\nFor these original jets set\n\n    y_j=x_j-g_j,  delta_j=v_j-|g_j|^2/2,\n    H(v)=|v|^2/2+max_(j in I)(<y_j,v>-delta_j),\n    F_h=H^*.\n\nThe star has y_star=delta_star=0. By accepted1252b8823bb5a290, F_h is a\nlegal pointwise maximal prefix interpolant, F_h>=f and min F_h=F_h(0)=0.\nConjugating, or bounding each conjugate support quadratic, gives\n\n    H(v)<=f^*(v)<=|v|^2/(2mu),                                (11)\n\nwhere the second bound uses f(x)>=mu|x|^2/2. Let\n\n    D=x_n-(<x_n,g_n>/|g_n|^2)g_n=x_n(1-z/(2|z|^2)),\n    w=mu D.\n\nThen <w,g_n>=0, <x_n,D>=|D|^2, and\n|D|^2=beta^2|P_n(z)|^2/|z|^2. For EVERY j in I, (11) implies\n\n    2delta_j+2<x_n-y_j,w>-|w|^2\n       >=2<x_n,w>-|w|^2/mu\n       =mu beta^2 |P_n(z)|^2/|z|^2.                            (12)\n\nConjugate evaluation at that same w and orthogonality then give, for any\nreal b,\n\n    2F_h(x_n-bg_n)>=2<x_n-bg_n,w>-2H(w)\n                  >=mu beta^2 |P_n(z)|^2/|z|^2.               (13)\n\nThis constructs the same legal function for every b and checks every\nremote prefix anchor. The appended endpoint is otherwise unconstrained.\n\n## 5. Evaluation, divergence, and necessary run count\n\nAccepteddfde2fa326c908bf gives\n\n    P_n(t)=(-1)^n T_m(sqrt(t))/(m sqrt(t)),  m=2n+1,\n    sum_i h_i=2n(n+1)/3.\n\nFor u=asinh(2beta), take s=pi/2-i u. Then z=(1+cos(s))/2 and\nsqrt(z)=cos(s/2) is a permissible square root. The polynomial identity\nT_m(cos t)=cos(mt) and oddness of m give\n\n    |T_m(sqrt(z))|^2\n       =|cos(m pi/4-i m u/2)|^2\n       =1/2+sinh^2(m u/2)=cosh(m u)/2.\n\nThus |P_n(z)|^2=cosh(m u)/(2m^2|z|), and (13) is the exact payoff in(1).\nAlso u>=beta by integrating (1+t^2)^(-1/2) on[0,2beta], where2beta<1.\nTogether with m>=2n, m<=3n, |z|<=1 and cosh(t)>=exp(t)/2 this gives\nthe second inequality in(1).\n\nIf R=o(sqrt(n)/log n), then sqrt(n)/R grows faster than log n. Since\nR<=n, log(C R^2 n^3)<=log C+5log n. Taking logarithms of (1) proves\ndivergence. If W_(n+1)(h,b)<=1 for any b, (1) instead gives\n\n    sqrt(n)/(2048R)<=log(C)+2log(R)+3log(n)\n                     <=log(C)+5log(n),\n\nwhich rearranges to(3). This condition is necessary, not sufficient.\n\nOn the appended unit step, the ordinary quadratic error is\nt(1-t)^2P_n(t)^2<=1/m^2 for all0<=t<=1, since |T_m(sqrt(t))|<=1.\nFinite-dimensional quadratic errors are normalized spectral averages of\nthese scalar errors. The global Huber channel is\n1/[1+2(2n(n+1)/3+1)]<=3/[4n(n+1)]. By acceptedb98bb807d514e80d,\nB_(n+1)<=1+4(n+1)^p with p=log_2(3)<2; both channel-to-U ratios vanish.\n\n## 6. A two-run family defeating all single-cut channels as well\n\nPut theta=pi/(2n+1), a_k=1/sin^2(k theta),1<=k<=n. This is exactly the\nsame multiset: a_k=sec^2((2(n+1-k)-1)pi/[2(2n+1)]). Order its indices as\n\n    even k in decreasing order, then odd k in increasing order.\n\nFor example n=7 gives(6,4,2,1,3,5,7). Its first block is increasing in\nstep value and the second decreasing, so it has R=2. Formula(1) therefore\ngives, uniformly over every real final b,\n\n    2F_h(x_n-bg_n)\n      >= exp(sqrt(n)/4096)/(144*8192^2*n^3) -> infinity.          (14)\n\nFor completeness we bound every term of the accepted single-cut functional\nK_n directly on this exact ordering. For a step b with total prefix P and\ntail T, that term is (b-1)_+^2/[(1+P)^2(1+2T)].\n\nAll a_k>=1 and decrease with k. Since sin(t)/t decreases on(0,pi/2],\n\n    a_k/a_(k+2)<=((k+2)/k)^2  whenever k+2<=n.                   (15)\n\nIndeed the derivative numerator t cos t-sin t has derivative -t sin t<=0.\nThe first even index and last odd index are at least n-1; for n>=3,\n(n-1)theta>=pi/4, so both corresponding steps are at most2. Also\na_2>=a_1/4 and a_3>=a_1/9 by the same decreasing-sinc inequality.\n\nBefore the central largest step a_1, a selected step b=a_k has even k.\nIf first, b/(1+P)<=2. Otherwise the preceding step is a_(k+2), and(15)\ngives b<=4a_(k+2)<=4P. Its tail contains a_1, hence the cut is at most\n16/(1+2a_1)<=8/a_1.\n\nAt a_1, the prefix includes a_2>=a_1/4 and tail includes a_3>=a_1/9.\nThe cut is at most\n\n    a_1^2/[(a_1/4)^2(2a_1/9)]=72/a_1.\n\nAfter a_1, the prefix P>=a_1. At a nonlast odd-indexed step a_k, k>=3,\nthe tail contains a_(k+2)>=a_k/4 by(15). The cut is at most\n2a_k/a_1^2<=2/a_1. At the last step b<=2, its numerator (b-1)^2<=1,\nso the cut is at most1/a_1^2<=1/a_1. The global Huber term is at most\n1/(1+2a_1)<=1/(2a_1). Therefore\n\n    K_n(h)<=72/a_1=72sin^2(theta)<=72pi^2/(2n+1)^2.              (16)\n\nAppending b=1 only enlarges every old tail denominator, decreases the\nglobal term, and creates a new cut whose numerator is zero. Thus\nK_(n+1)(h,1)<=K_n(h). The full quadratic channel is also at most1/(2n+1)^2\nas in Section5. Multiplication by B_(n+1)<=1+4(n+1)^p makes the maximum\nof ALL these channels, divided by U_(n+1)^ZJ, tend to zero. In contrast\n(14), from a fully legal nonreal 2D prefix with a free terminal completion,\ndiverges and exceeds that benchmark eventually.\n\nThe preceding cut argument is included in full; the shared unverified\ncandidate f18bd083739517ba is credited as its source of direction, not\nused as a fact. No external paper result is invoked.\n\n## Scope\n\nThese estimates do not establish the target for arbitrary step multisets or\nfor Chebyshev permutations with arbitrarily many monotone runs. In particular,\nthe lower bound in(1) need not be large when R is comparable to sqrt(n) or\nlarger. The result identifies a quantitative chronological complexity that\nany potentially successful ordering of this special multiset must have;\nit does not say that such an ordering exists. Zeros can be inserted in a\ndisplayed schedule without changing its actual prefix or payoff, by\nrestoring duplicated jets after constructing the positive-step trajectory.\nAny resulting horizon comparison must use the resulting horizon, rather\nthan silently retaining n.
