The recursive family and\nthe real-product method were recalled from PUBLIC candidate79f6ce08b41616b5;\nno unverified result is used below. Every needed estimate is included.\n\n## Statement and exact scope\n\nPut c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, and define\n\n    H_0=(),  H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),\n    N_m=2^m-1,\n    S_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).\n\nFor a complex z, let x_i=P_i(z) be the chronological prefix polynomial\norbit of H_m, starting from x_0=1, and g_i=z x_i for i=0,...,N_m. Include\nthe optimum jet (0,0,0). Its complete-block polynomial is\n\n    p_m(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-b_j z)^(2^(m-j-1)).\n\nSuppose this FULL prefix is interpolable by a convex differentiable\n1-smooth function with minimum zero at the origin. Its values may be ANY\nlegal interpolating values, not only the maximal path values of the\ncomplex-orbit construction. At the appended final point, use ANY convex\n1-smooth interpolant F of the chosen jets, including the pointwise maximal\none and with no condition on its terminal gradient. Then the COMMON unit\nstep satisfies\n\n    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m)) <= 2c^11/(a c^m).                      (1)\n\nThis holds for every m>=1, with deliberately nonoptimal constants.\nIt also holds for any finite orthogonal mixture of such INDIVIDUALLY legal\ncomplex orbits with squared initial weights summing to one, even if the\njoint legal function values and final interpolant are not separable.\n\nThe canonical sum-only Huber channel on the appended schedule (H_m,1)\nis also O(c^(-m)). The exact recursions imply\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^(4/3),\n\nso, since c^3>16, the maximum of the entire stated witness class and that\nHuber channel, divided by U_(2^m)^ZJ, tends to zero.\n\nThis is a scoped upper bound on the proposed witness family, NOT on actual\nW_(2^m)(H_m,1). It uses the FULL schedule-dependent legality of each orbit;\nno universal spectral lens is imposed. It does NOT cover a jointly legal\nmixture whose individual complex modes are illegal. Such mixed-mode repair\nis a substantive remaining possibility, not something ruled out by this\nproof. General nonnormal kernels are also outside the scope.\n\n## 1. Universal last-query inequalities and the free unit step\n\nWrite z=alpha+i beta and sigma=|z|^2. For z=0 all gradients vanish and\nconvexity makes every queried value, and the appended unchanged value,\nzero. Hence assume z!=0. Interpolation between x_0=1 and the optimum gives\n\n    sigma<=alpha,  alpha>0,  |z|<=1,\n    f_0<=alpha-sigma/2.                                      (2)\n\nFor any queried i it likewise gives\n\n    f_i<= (alpha-sigma/2)|x_i|^2.                             (3)\n\nThe unit-step descent inequality, obtained by integrating a 1-Lipschitz\ngradient along -g_i, gives F(x_i-g_i)<=f_i-|g_i|^2/2. Thus for the last\nquery, independently of all earlier value choices,\n\n    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))\n       <=2(alpha-sigma)|p_m(z)|^2.                           (4)\n\nThis includes the maximal terminal completion in accepted1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 2. Full interpolation imposes a sector or a small-parameter case\n\nPut q=alpha/|z| in(0,1] and h_c=2alpha/sigma. Then h_c>=2 by(2), and\n|1-hz|<=1 exactly for0<=h<=h_c.\n\nIf every step in H_m is smaller than h_c, every factor contracts, so\n|p_m(z)|<=1. Also b_(m-1)<h_c implies\n\n    |z|^2 < 2alpha/b_(m-1) <=2|z|/b_(m-1),\n    |z|<2c/(a c^m).\n\nEquation(4) is then less than4c/(a c^m), which is at most the bound(1).\nThe same conclusion follows if there is no step greater than or equal to\nh_c. We now treat the remaining case.\n\nLet r be the smallest level with b_r>=h_c. Then r<=m-1, b_(r-1)<h_c,\nand r>=18 because b_17=1<h_c. The chronological prefix before the first\nb_r is exactly H_r, and every step of this prefix strictly contracts radii.\nDefine v=b_r|z|. Minimality gives\n\n    2q<=v<2c q.                                              (5)\n\nLet d=2/c=10/13. Since d^3<1/2 and d^2<4/5, d^17<1/40. Consequently\n\n    S_r/b_r=(1-d^r)/(c-2)>=13/8.                             (6)\n\nThe squared-step sum of H_r is\n\n    T_r=sum_(h in H_r) h^2=a^2(c^(2r)-2^r)/(c^2-2),\n    T_r/b_r^2<=1/(c^2-2)=25/119.                             (7)\n\nUsing log(1+t)<=t in every squared radial factor gives\n\n    log|p_r(z)|^2 <= -2alpha S_r+sigma T_r\n                   <=-(13/4)q v+v^2/(c^2-2).                 (8)\n\nThe concave quadratic (13/4)q v-v^2/(c^2-2) has its minimum on the\ninterval(5) at an endpoint. At v=2q its coefficient of q^2 is\n13/2-100/119=1347/238>11/2. At v=2c q that coefficient is\n169/10-676/119>11/2. Thus\n\n    |p_r(z)|^2 <= exp(-(11/2)q^2).                            (9)\n\nWe now use ACTUAL ordered value inequalities inside the prefix, not only\npairwise cocoercivity or radial contraction. At a step of size h with point\nx and next point x_+=(1-hz)x, interpolation in the direction from the next\nquery back to this query gives\n\n    f(x)-f(x_+)\n      >=h(1-halpha+hsigma/2)sigma |x|^2\n      =[sigma/(2alpha)](|x|^2-|x_+|^2)\n        +[h^2 sigma/(2alpha)]\n              [sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2]|x|^2.                 (10)\n\nFor the first inequality, g_+=(1-hz)g,\n<g_+,g>=(1-halpha)|g|^2, and |g-g_+|^2=h^2sigma|g|^2. Expanding gives\nthe displayed identity; no endpoint value or local oracle is guessed.\n\nSuppose q<=2/3. Then sigma-2alpha^2=sigma(1-2q^2)>0, so the second term\nin(10) is nonnegative. Sum(10) along the exact H_r prefix. Its terminal\nvalue is nonnegative and (2) bounds the initial value, so\n\n    [sigma/(2alpha)](1-|p_r(z)|^2)<=alpha-sigma/2,\n    |p_r(z)|^2>=1-2q^2+alpha>1-2q^2.                         (11)\n\nBut for0<t<=4/9,\n\n    exp(-(11/2)t)<1-2t.                                     (12)\n\nIndeed the difference 1-2t-exp(-(11/2)t) is concave, vanishes at0, and is\npositive at4/9. For the last claim,\nexp(22/9)=exp(2)exp(4/9)>(8/3)^2(1+4/9)=832/81>9;\nexp(1)>8/3 follows from the first four terms of its positive series.\nEquations(9),(11),(12) contradict each other. Therefore every legal orbit\nin the present case satisfies\n\n    q=Re(z)/|z|>2/3.                                        (13)\n\nThis is a necessary condition derived from the entire contracting prefix.\n\n## 3. Uniform damping of the dyadic product in the forced sector\n\nAssume0<|z|<=1 and q=Re(z)/|z|>=2/3. Choose the unique integer k>=17\nsuch that\n\n    c^k a|z|<=1<c^(k+1)a|z|,\n    y=c^k a|z| in(1/c,1].                                  (14)\n\nIf m<=k, every factor has b_j|z|<=1, and\n|1-b_jz|^2=1-2q b_j|z|+b_j^2|z|^2<=1; hence |p_m(z)|<=1.\n\nSuppose m>k. A vanishing factor makes the desired damping immediate, so\nassume there is none. Write z/|z|=exp(i theta). Directly from multiplicities,\n\n    log|p_m(z)|/2^(m-k-1)\n       =sum_(j=-k)^(m-k-1) 2^(-j)log|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|.    (15)\n\nFor j<0, log(1+t)<=t gives\n\n    log|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|<=-q c^j y+(c^j y)^2/2.\n\nSumming these terms and using k>=17 and (6) bounds their contribution by\n\n    -(13/4)q y + (25/119)y^2.\n\nFor j>=1, c^j y>1 and q>=2/3, so\n|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|<=c^j y. The bounds jlog c+log y are nonnegative;\nextending their weighted sum to all j>=1 gives2log c+log y. The j=0 term\nis at most(1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2). Therefore(15) is at most\n\n    E(y)=-(13/6)y+(25/119)y^2\n           +(1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2)+log y+2log(13/5).          (16)\n\nHere we used q>=2/3 also in the negative linear term. We prove the fully\nuniform elementary inequality\n\n    E(y)<-1/128  for5/13<=y<=1.                              (17)\n\nFirst let5/13<=y<=1/2. The positive quadratic term is at most25/476,\nand D(y)=1-(4/3)y+y^2 is decreasing here, so D(y)<=322/507. Also\n-(13/6)y+log y<=-1+log(6/13), by maximizing that elementary concave\nfunction. Combining yields\n\n    E(y)<=-1+25/476+log A,\n    A=(78/25)sqrt(322/507),  A^2=3864/625<(5/2)^2.\n\nWe have log(5/2)<15/16, since the first four terms in the positive\nexponential series give\n\n    exp(15/16)>\n      1+15/16+(15/16)^2/2+(15/16)^3/6\n      =61791/24576>5/2.\n\nThus E(y)<-1+25/476+15/16=-19/1904<-1/128.\n\nFor1/2<=y<=1, E is strictly decreasing. Here are the exact derivative\ncalculations to check that claim, without a numerical maximization. With\nD(y)=1-(4/3)y+y^2,\n\n    E'(y)=B(y)/[2142yD(y)],\n    B(y)=900y^4-5841y^3+11372y^2-8925y+2142.\n\nPut t=2y-1 in[0,1]. Direct expansion gives\n\n    16B((1+t)/2)\n       =-2422-11870t+15842t^2-8082t^3+900t^4\n       <=-2422+t[-11870+15842t-7182t^2]\n       <=-2422-3210t<0.\n\nFor the second inequality, 15842t-7182t^2 is increasing on[0,1] and is\nat most8660. The denominator of E' is positive. Hence E(y)<=E(1/2),\nalready controlled by the preceding interval. This proves(17).\n\nEquations(15)--(17) imply\n\n    |p_m(z)| <= exp(-2^(m-k-1)/128)  whenever m>k.             (18)\n\n## 4. The free terminal objective bound\n\nFor a legal orbit the small-parameter case in Section2 already gives\nthe bound(1). Otherwise(13) permits the product estimates of Section3.\nIf |z|<=1/(a c^m), then m<=k and(4) is at most2/(a c^m).\nIf |z|>1/(a c^m), let r=m-k>=1. By(14),(18),\n\n    2(alpha-sigma)|p_m(z)|^2\n       <=[2/(a c^m)] c^r exp(-2^(r-1)/64)\n       <=2c^11/(a c^m).                                    (19)\n\nFor1<=r<=11, discard the exponential and use c^r<=c^11. For r>=12,\n2^(r-1)/64=2^(r-7)>=r, by induction from r=12, while log c<1 because\nexp(1)>8/3>13/5. The extra factor is then at most1. This proves(19),\nand therefore(1), with complete prefix values and arbitrary terminal\ncompletion retained.\n\nFor a finite orthogonal mixture, write its components as\n\n    x_i^(l)=sqrt(w_l)P_i(z_l),\n    g_i^(l)=sqrt(w_l)z_l P_i(z_l),\n    w_l>=0, sum_l w_l=1,\n\nup to harmless rotations in the individual planes. Assume every active\nz_l individually has a legal prefix. For ANY jointly legal values and ANY\njoint smooth convex interpolant, the same last-query/unit-step inequalities\ngive\n\n    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))\n       <=2sum_l w_l(Re(z_l)-|z_l|^2)|p_m(z_l)|^2\n       <=2c^11/(a c^m).\n\nSeparability of the joint interpolant is unnecessary. Individual legality\nIS needed to apply Section2 to each z_l; it is not inferred from joint\nlegality. No such inference is claimed for mixtures of illegal modes.\n\n## 5. Exact-profile comparison, with its recurrence estimate proved here\n\nFor m>=17, (6) with r=m gives S_m>=13a c^m/8. Thus the canonical Huber\nvalue for(H_m,1) is at most\n\n    1/[1+2(S_m+1)]<=1/(2S_m)<=4/(13a c^m).                    (20)\n\nWe now derive B_n<=1+4n^(4/3) directly from the exact recursions stated in\nacceptedb98bb807d514e80d. Put C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1.\nIn these variables the primitive and dominant joins respectively are\n\n    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nThey are increasing in their nonnegative arguments. For0<=t<=1,\n\n    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.                  (21)\n\nTo prove it set x=t^(1/3),y=(1-t)^(1/3), so x^3+y^3=1. Then\n\n    1-x^4-y^4-x^4y^4\n       =x^3y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0.\n\nEach reciprocal is at least1/3, while xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3; the latter strict\ninequality follows by cubing. The endpoints also satisfy the identity.\nSince Omega is the positive root of X^2-(A+D)X-AD=0, (21) implies\n\n    Omega(A,D)<=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3).\n\nInduction in the primitive recursion gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Also\nsqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A, hence G(A,D)<=D+4A. Induction in the dominant\nrecursion, for every split k, gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4[(n-k-1)^(4/3)+(k+1)^(4/3)]<=1+4n^(4/3).\n\nThe initial values are C_0=B_0=1. The last inequality uses the elementary\nsuperadditivity of u^(4/3) on nonnegative u.\n\nThe appended horizon is N_m+1=2^m. Multiply(1),(20) by\nB_(2^m)<=1+4*2^(4m/3). Their ratios to U_(2^m)^ZJ tend to zero because\nc^3=2197/125>16. This closes the proposed all-schedule comparison for the\nentire stated individually legal orbit/direct-sum family, even after free\nterminal completion and addition of the canonical Huber channel.\n\n## Remaining possible repairs\n\n1. A jointly legal mixture of individually illegal COMPLEX modes could\n   cancel the interpolation defects used in Section2; this proof does not\n   rule it out. A correct extension must check complete joint cycles and\n   values on the same real Gram data.\n2. A definite nonnormal polynomial kernel can have correlations absent from\n   every normal spectral mixture. Replacing it by an arbitrary Gram form\n   would merely restate the original problem and is not a quantitative repair.\n3. The legal growing Chebyshev constructions remain valid scoped candidates.\n   They do not supply the missing uniform comparison on this recursive family.\n\nMultiplying every interpolant by an arbitrary prescribed L>0 restores the\nproject's smoothness normalization without changing these normalized bounds.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "plan_id": "97fa957298396810", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/full_legal_complex_recursive_obstruction.md", "related_shared_candidate": "79f6ce08b41616b5", "verification_status": "Complete unverified candidate; no new fact_submit", "scope": "All individually legal complex orbits and their orthogonal mixtures, with arbitrary legal values/free terminal; not unrestricted W or jointly repaired illegal modes."}, "glossary": {"H_m": "Recursive scheduleH_0=(), H_(m+1)=(H_m,a c^m,H_m).", "c": "Fixed scale13/5.", "a": "Fixed scale c^(-17).", "p_m(z)": "Complete H_m polynomial product_(j<m)(1-a c^j z)^(2^(m-j-1)).", "sigma": "Squared modulus|z|².", "q": "Spectral direction Re(z)/|z|.", "h_c": "Radial contraction threshold2Re(z)/|z|².", "r": "In Section2, first level with a c^r>=h_c; separately a scale difference in Section4.", "T_r": "Sum of squared steps in H_r.", "E(y)": "Explicit logarithmic upper bound in equation16; proved below-1/128 on[5/13,1].", "individually legal mode": "A complex spectral parameter whose entire queried prefix and optimum are themselves jointly interpolable by one convex1-smooth function.", "free terminal completion": "Any smooth convex interpolant of the legal prefix, including its pointwise maximal one; no linear-orbit terminal gradient constraint."}}
{"id": "edbbe4af2a2ad5c2", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:16:23.607905+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Complete two-bridge signed-value transport candidate with a final normalized objective certificate: the retained state E_theta=V-theta sum h_i Q_(i,end) transports with K=(D2/D0)^2 and theta=1-1/K, while every final coefficient on an actual interpolation inequality is nonnegative. Full earlier query-to-anchor and query-to-terminal terms remain explicit. The accepted terminal-square theorem yields the displayed initial-distance certificate. An exact five-step calibration works but is strictly dominated; no asymptotic improvement is claimed.", "evidence": "# Saturated-cut certificates retain a signed terminal-interpolation credit\n\nStatus: self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or\nfact_submit was used. The full initial-distance objective theorem is not\nbeing restricted here: the statement concerns the explicitly specified\nintrinsic primitive energy. The only accepted background used is the\nnecessity of the interpolation inequalities in fact 1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 1. Definitions and an exact identity\n\nWork at smoothness one. On a GD trajectory for a list h of sum s and\ncapacity C=1+s, write g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), and\n\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0,\n  V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)\n          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_n||^2.\n\nTake two lists a,b of capacities A,D, respectively, and set\n\n  T=A+D+r,  r>0,  rT=AD,\n  kappa=(T/D)^2=(A/r)^2,\n  theta=1-1/kappa.\n\nIn particular 0<r<min(A,D), kappa>1 and 0<theta<1. The joined\nschedule is h=(a,1+r,b). Denote its bridge query by L and the tail\nqueries by j=0,...,m; z=m is the final query. Tail indices in Q always\nmean the corresponding original joined indices. Define\n\n  J_b=sum_(j<m) b_j Q_(j,z),\n  E_theta(b)=V_b-theta J_b,\n  C_cross=sum_(j<m) b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L)+r Q_(L,z).\n\nThe exact identity, including every value term, is\n\n  V_h = V_a + kappa E_theta(b) - (kappa-1) C_cross.       (1)\n\nAll coefficients in -C_cross are nonnegative on actual inequalities\n-Q. Thus universal nonnegativity of V_a and E_theta(b) is a sufficient\ncondition for universal nonnegativity of V_h. It is not necessary here\nto assume V_b>=0. Whether this genuinely enlarges the allowable tail\nclass is a separate question.\n\nHere is a direct audit of (1). Put p=g_L, q=g_z, and\nH=sum_(j<m)b_j g_j. Then x_tail,0=x_L-(1+r)p and x_z=x_tail,0-H.\nFor C=C_cross+J_b the value part is\n\n  C_f=2sum_(j<m)b_j f_j+(r-D)f_L+(2-D-r)f_z.\n\nExpand each Q and use\n2sum_j b_j<g_j,sum_(i<j)b_i g_i>=||H||^2-sum_j b_j^2||g_j||^2.\nThe gradient part reduces exactly to\n\n  C_g=sum_(j<m)b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2\n            +[(D-r)/2-r^2]||p||^2+(D+r)||q||^2/2.\n\nAll mixed p,H,q products cancel. The identities\n\n  (kappa-1)(D-r)=2(A+r),\n  kappa r^2=A^2,  kappa D^2=T^2\n\nthen show V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)C coefficient by coefficient:\nthe f_L coefficient is 2(1+r), the f_z coefficient is -2(T-1),\nthe p-square coefficient is -r(1+r), and the q-square coefficient\nis -T(T-1). Substituting kappa theta=kappa-1 proves (1).\n\n## 2. Full intrinsic certificates force the bridge coefficients\n\nFor this part assume the entries of a and b are strictly positive;\neither list may be empty. Suppose an explicit intrinsic certificate is\ngiven:\n\n  M_h=V_h+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij,\n  lambda_ij>=0,\n\nwhere values cancel and M_h is PSD in the full independent formal\ngradient vector. Allowing the optimum index and x_0 initially does not\nenlarge this class. Indeed V_h has no x_0-square coefficient, so PSD\nforces the x_0 row to vanish. Its cross coefficients are -lambda_i,star,\nwhich forces every lambda_i,star=0. Summing the value-flow equalities\nthen gives sum_i lambda_star,i=0, so these multipliers also vanish.\nThis argument relies on the absence of an initial-distance term and\ndoes NOT apply to a full objective certificate.\n\nThere are two explicit equality trajectories for V_h. A scalar Huber\ntrajectory staying on one affine ray has every gradient equal to 1,\nall finite Q zero, and V_h=0. A second trajectory uses the derivative\n\n  f'(x)=min(1,max(x,-eta)),  eta=r/D,\n\nstarting at A. The prefix gradient is 1, its last query is 1, the\nbridge lands at -r, and the tail gradient is -eta until its endpoint\n-eta. Every incoming optimum residual is zero. Distance telescoping\ngives V_h=A^2-T^2 eta^2=0.\n\nFor completeness, if R_i is a prefix query's remaining prefix sum\nand R_j is a tail query's remaining tail sum, this second trajectory\nhas\n\n  Q_(prefix i,tail j)=-(1+eta)eta R_j,\n  Q_(tail j,prefix i)=-(1+eta)R_i.                       (2)\n\nEvery within-block Q is zero. Since M_h>=0 and all lambda Q<=0,\nequality of V_h forces multiplier complementarity and M_h to vanish\non each equality gradient vector. Those vectors span the two block\nindicators e_A,e_B, so both indicators lie in the kernel of M_h.\n\nBy (2), the only potentially nonzero cross multipliers are\n\n  nu_i=lambda_(prefix i,z),  mu_j=lambda_(tail j,L).\n\nFor a prefix index i<L, the corresponding row of M_h e_B is\n-D nu_i/2. Thus nu_i=0 for every i<L. Write nu=nu_L.\nFor a nonterminal tail index j, the corresponding row of M_h e_A\nis [r mu_j-nu b_j]/2; at z it is [r mu_z-nu]/2.\nConsequently\n\n  mu_j=(nu/r)b_j  (j<m),  mu_z=nu/r.\n\nThe sum of the prefix value-flow equations is\nsum_j mu_j-nu=2(A+r). Therefore all cross multipliers are fixed:\n\n  lambda_(j,L)=(kappa-1)b_j (j<m),\n  lambda_(z,L)=kappa-1,\n  lambda_(L,z)=r(kappa-1),                              (3)\n\nand every other cross multiplier is zero. The mixed gradient block\nof the full matrix vanishes, not merely its value on equality data.\n\n## 3. Exact cone interface and the sign that cannot be discarded\n\nLet lambda^A,lambda^B be the two within-block multiplier arrays of\nthe given full certificate. Define a signed tail array by\n\n  lambda_hat_ij=lambda^B_ij/kappa\n                 -theta b_i * 1_(i<m,j=z).             (4)\n\nCombining (1) with (3) yields the full block-diagonal identity\n\n  M_h = M_a + kappa M_b,\n  M_a=V_a+sum lambda^A_ij Q_ij,\n  M_b=V_b+sum lambda_hat_ij Q_ij.                       (5)\n\nEach summand has its own value cancellation, since its values are in\na disjoint block. Full PSD is equivalent to PSD of both summands.\nThe prefix multipliers are nonnegative. The exact tail constraints are\n\n  lambda_hat_ij>=0 except possibly i<m,j=z,\n  lambda_hat_(i,z)>=-theta b_i.                        (6)\n\nConversely, any prefix intrinsic certificate and any PSD tail identity\nwith the value cancellation and bounds (6) give a nonnegative full\ncertificate by (3),(4). Equivalently, the tail has an ordinary\nnonnegative certificate for E_theta(b), not necessarily for V_b.\n\nThus direct restriction to an ordinary primitive tail certificate would\nrequire the stronger bounds\n\n  lambda^B_(i,z)>=(kappa-1)b_i.                        (7)\n\nThey are not implied for each full certificate. The next exact example\nproves this, including full nonnegativity and PSD.\n\n## 4. Exact full certificate violating the proposed coefficient floor\n\nUse the empty prefix A=1, tail b=(1,1) of capacity D=3, and\n\n  r=sqrt(7)-2, T=sqrt(7)+2, kappa=(11+4sqrt(7))/9.\n\nThe full schedule is h=(sqrt(7)-1,1,1). Use original indices 0,1,2,3,\nwhere 0 is the bridge query and 1,2,3 are the tail queries. Set\n\n  lambda_10=lambda_20=lambda_30=kappa-1,\n  lambda_03=r(kappa-1),\n  lambda_12=5kappa/2, lambda_21=17kappa/4,\n  lambda_23=37kappa/4-1, lambda_32=12kappa,\n  lambda_13=3kappa/4-1,\n\nwith every unlisted multiplier zero. All are nonnegative, since\nkappa>4/3. Direct substitution, or (1) and the audited tail identity\nbelow, gives\n\n  V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n   =kappa { ||(g_1-g_2)-(g_2-g_3)/8||^2\n                         +(255/64)||g_2-g_3||^2 } >=0. (8)\n\nFor the tail alone, write u=g_0-g_1 and v=g_1-g_2 using local indices.\nIts signed multiplier list is\n\n  lambda_hat_01=5/2, lambda_hat_10=17/4,\n  lambda_hat_12=33/4, lambda_hat_21=12,\n  lambda_hat_02=-1/4, lambda_hat_20=0.\n\nThe value coefficients cancel, and expansion gives\n\n  V_(1,1)+sum lambda_hat_ij Q_ij\n      =||u||^2-<u,v>/4+4||v||^2\n      =||u-v/8||^2+(255/64)||v||^2.\n\nNevertheless lambda_13=(kappa-1)-kappa/4 is strictly below the\nproposed floor kappa-1. The restricted terminal coefficient is -1/4.\nThis refutes the inference of (7) from full nonnegativity and PSD.\n\nThere IS another ordinary primitive certificate for this tail:\nlambda_01=2,lambda_10=4,lambda_12=8,lambda_21=12, all other\nmultipliers zero, gives ||u||^2+4||v||^2. Accordingly this example\ndoes not disprove existence of a suitable re-selection, arbitrary-tail\nheredity, or the original asymptotic minimax problem. It identifies\nthe exact missing sign assertion rather than replacing it with PSD.\n\n## 5. A particular nonprimitive tail cannot exploit this credit\n\nThe previously considered b=(17/10,2,6/5) fails even E_theta for\nevery 0<=theta<=1. Here is a self-contained witness.\nPut m=7/34, ell=21/85. Define the continuous derivative\n\n  f'(x)=x                  for x<=m,\n         m                 for m<=x<=m+ell,\n         x-ell             for x>=m+ell,\n\nand integrate with f(0)=0. Its derivative is nondecreasing and\n1-Lipschitz, so f is convex differentiable 1-smooth with minimizer 0.\nStarting at x_0=106/85 gives\n\n  (x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)=(106/85,-77/170,77/170,7/34),\n  (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(1,-77/170,7/34,7/34).\n\nThe endpoint is core: x_3=g_3=m, f_3=m^2/2. Define\nI_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Direct scalar evaluation gives\n\n  I_0=ell(1-m)=567/2890, I_1=I_2=I_3=0,\n  sum_(i<3)b_i I_i=567/1700,\n  V_b=-13581/23120.\n\nAt a core endpoint, expansion gives Q_(i,3)=-I_i. Thus\n\n  E_theta(b)=-13581/23120+theta*567/1700\n           <= -29349/115600 <0.                       (9)\n\nThe value of V_b can also be checked without integrating every term:\ndistance telescoping gives\nV_b=x_0^2-D^2g_3^2-2sum b_i I_i, with D=59/10.\nThis proves (9) exactly.\n\nConsequently this specific tail cannot acquire a valid E_theta\ncertificate at any saturated join parameter. A rank-one coefficient\nmatching attempt on this same fixed tail also failed, but (9) is the\nstronger actual-function obstruction. Neither statement excludes other\nnonprimitive tails. No numerical schedule screen was reopened.\n\n## 6. Complete two-bridge identity with retained earlier anchors\n\nThe identity also provides a closed, explicitly signed value state\nthrough two bridges. Let a_1,a_2 be any nonnegative physical prefix\nlists of capacities A_1,A_2. Starting with the physical tail b of\ncapacity D_0, define\n\n  H_1=(a_1,1+r_1,b), D_1=A_1+D_0+r_1, r_1 D_1=A_1 D_0,\n  H_2=(a_2,1+r_2,H_1), D_2=A_2+D_1+r_2, r_2 D_2=A_2 D_1,\n  kappa_1=(D_1/D_0)^2, kappa_2=(D_2/D_1)^2,\n  K=kappa_1 kappa_2=(D_2/D_0)^2, theta_2=1-1/K.\n\nLet L_1,L_2 be the respective bridge query indices in the final\noriginal indexing and z the final query. For each physical step in\na_1 or a_2, its gradient/value index is also its original index in H_2.\nDefine the following sums of actual inequalities, without collapsing\nany old query or replacing it by a new anchor:\n\n  C_1=sum_(j in b steps)b_j Q_(j,L_1)+Q_(z,L_1)+r_1 Q_(L_1,z),\n  C_2=sum_(i in H_1 steps)(H_1)_i Q_(i,L_2)\n                                      +Q_(z,L_2)+r_2 Q_(L_2,z),\n  J_left1=sum_(i in a_1 steps)(a_1)_i Q_(i,z)\n                                      +(1+r_1)Q_(L_1,z).\n\nThen the complete identity is\n\n  V_(H_2)=V_(a_2)+kappa_2 V_(a_1)+K E_theta2(b)\n       -kappa_2(kappa_1-1)C_1-(kappa_2-1)C_2\n       -(kappa_2-1)J_left1.                            (10)\n\nEvery coefficient displayed on -Q is nonnegative. In particular the\nearlier a_1 queries still have their actual outgoing edges to z, and\nevery query inside H_1 has its actual edge to L_2. These are not removed\nby the unqueried-terminal theorem.\n\nTo verify (10) algebraically, apply the audited expansion in Section 1\nat each cut. The weighted incoming-terminal sum for H_1 is exactly\nJ_b+J_left1. Substitution gives a J_b coefficient\nkappa_2(kappa_1-1)+(kappa_2-1)=K-1=K theta_2; all other terms are\nthose displayed in (10). This derivation is part of this same candidate\nproof, not an invocation of an unverified finding as a theorem.\n\nConsequently, if explicit nonnegative intrinsic certificates are given\nfor a_1 and a_2, and an explicit nonnegative certificate is given for\nE_theta2(b), (10) assembles an intrinsic certificate for H_2. Its PSD\nremainder is the sum of the three input PSD remainders with scales\n1,kappa_2,K. All original actual multipliers are sums of nonnegative\nnumbers. No positive remainder is presumed for a benchmark block.\n\n## 7. Final objective certificate, including initial distance\n\nHere is the exact final conversion for any intrinsic certificate\nproduced by (10), and in particular for the example below. Write H=H_2,\nlet its terminal query be n, its capacity be D=1+sum_(i<n)H_i, and let\n\n  M_H=V_H+sum_(i!=j<=n)lambda_ij Q_ij >=0\n\nbe the assembled PSD identity, with all lambda_ij>=0. Define\n\n  t=(-1+sqrt(8D+1))/4, beta=1+t,\n  gamma=1/(2t+1), q=gamma^2,\n  x_(n+1)=x_n-beta g_n.\n\nThe arithmetic identities are t=gamma D, 1-gamma=2qD, and\nq=1/[1+2(sum H_i+beta)]. The accepted terminal-square parameterization\nin fact 1252b8823bb5a290(c) applies with terminal probabilities\nalpha_star=gamma, alpha_n=1-gamma, and all other alpha_i=0.\nGive the queried prefix the following additional optimum multipliers:\n\n  lambda_pre_(i,star)=2q H_i  (i<n),\n  lambda_pre_(n,star)=2q(D+1),\n\nand multiply every intrinsic lambda_ij by q. All these coefficients\nare nonnegative and their flows are exactly the required 2alpha_i.\nThere are no optimum-outgoing multipliers in this particular\nconstructed certificate; none is dropped from an arbitrary certificate.\nThe full identity supplied by the accepted parameterization is\n\n  q||x_0||^2-2f_(n+1)\n    +q sum_(i!=j<=n)lambda_ij Q_ij\n    +2q sum_(i<n)H_i Q_(i,star)+2q(D+1)Q_(n,star)\n    +2(1-gamma)Q_(n+1,n)+2gamma Q_(n+1,star)\n  =||g_(n+1)-[gamma x_n-(t+gamma)g_n]||^2+q M_H.       (11)\n\nThe optimum is translated to x_star=0,f_star=0 in this display.\nIt follows that W_(n+1)(H,beta)<=q. The factor on ||x_0||^2 is retained,\nso this is a certificate in the original objective normalization.\n\nTo audit its application rather than re-prove the accepted component,\nthe prefix remainder of that component is\n\n  R=q||x_0||^2-2(1-gamma)(f_n-||g_n||^2/2)\n       -||gamma x_n-(t+gamma)g_n||^2\n       +2q sum_(i<n)H_i Q_(i,star)+2q(D+1)Q_(n,star)\n       +q sum lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nUsing t=gamma D and 1-gamma=2qD, its mixed <x_n,g_n> coefficient\ncancels. Its f_n and ||g_n||^2 coefficients are respectively\n-2q(D-1) and -qD(D-1). Distance telescoping then gives R=q M_H,\nwhich is exactly (11). Every queried-prefix inequality is retained.\n\n## 8. A full two-bridge calibration and its exact scope\n\nTake b=(1,1), and take both a_1,a_2 empty. Then\n\n  D_0=3, D_1=2+sqrt(7), r_1=sqrt(7)-2,\n  D_2=[1+D_1+sqrt((1+D_1)^2+4D_1)]/2, r_2=D_2-1-D_1.\n\nThus H_2=(1+r_2,sqrt(7)-1,1,1). For E_theta2(b), use the signed\ntail coefficients in Section 4 and add theta_2 b_i to each incoming\nterminal coefficient. The only initially negative coefficient becomes\ntheta_2-1/4>0: already kappa_1>4/3 and K>kappa_1. All other\ncoefficients are nonnegative. The PSD remainder is\n||u-v/8||^2+(255/64)||v||^2, because the added interpolation terms\nexactly cancel the -theta_2 J_b term in E_theta2.\n\nEquations (10),(11) therefore give a completely explicit certificate\nfor this five-step objective schedule after appending beta. This is a\ncalibration of signed coordinates with nonnegative actual multipliers,\nnot an improved asymptotic schedule. In particular, the physical tail\ncapacity 3 is already smaller than that of the legal two-step primitive\nschedule (3/2,3/2), whose capacity is 4. The old tail also has an ordinary\nprimitive certificate, so this example does not establish strict\nenlargement by the E_theta state.\n\nThe comparator's primitive property has a direct identity, so no\nunverified external premise is needed: at h=(3/2,3/2), take\nlambda_01=1,lambda_10=7/2,lambda_12=17/2,lambda_21=14,\nlambda_02=1/2,lambda_20=1. Expanding gives V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0.\nAll six multipliers are positive. Replacing the capacity-3 tail by this\ncapacity-4 tail in the same two saturated joins strictly increases both\nsuccessive capacities, since the positive root\nOmega(A,D)=[A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD)]/2 is increasing in D for A,D>0.\nThe terminal q in (11) strictly decreases. The displayed calibration\nis therefore strictly dominated already at its own finite horizon.\n\nThe unresolved bridge toward an improvement is concrete: find an actual\ntail with a nonnegative certificate for E_theta at some theta<1 and with\na capacity gain large enough to pay for the physical prefix lengths.\nNeither the existence of that tail nor the required infinite-horizon\ncomparison with U_n^ZJ is asserted here.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_two_bridge_terminal_credit", "plan_id": "90d0a912690abdbc", "attempt_type": "direct", "status": "conditional interface complete; profitable tail and amplification blocked", "artifact": "local_memory/saturated_certificate_credit.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["f7cee3034df8cb7f", "00244596ecb825bc"], "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "refuted for one supplied tail; general existence open", "summary": "The rational tail(17/10,2,6/5) fails E_theta throughout[0,1]. The concrete certified tail(1,1) has a stronger ordinary certificate and yields a dominated calibration."}, "key_stuck_points": ["Need an actual useful E_theta-certified tail; signed coordinates alone do not show strict enlargement.", "A gain must pay physical prefix lengths and then admit rigorous infinite-horizon comparison."], "verification_status": "unverified; no fact_submit allocation used"}, "glossary": {"E_theta(b)": "Intrinsic primitive energy V_b minus theta times its actual weighted incoming-terminal interpolation sum.", "D_0,D_1,D_2": "Capacities of the physical tail, once-prepended block, and twice-prepended block in the two-bridge identity.", "a_1,a_2": "The two actual physical prefix step lists, which may be empty.", "r_1,r_2": "Positive bridge excesses satisfying r_i D_i=A_i D_(i-1).", "kappa_1,kappa_2": "Capacity-square ratios (D_i/D_(i-1))^2.", "K": "Product kappa_1 kappa_2=(D_2/D_0)^2, local to this candidate.", "theta_2": "Actual tail credit1-1/K, in(0,1).", "C_1,C_2": "Displayed nonnegative-coefficient sums of actual cross-block signed interpolation residuals Q<=0 at the two cuts.", "J_left1": "Incoming-terminal sum retaining every physical step of the inner prefix and its bridge.", "gamma": "Terminal outgoing optimum probability1/(2t+1), with t=(-1+sqrt(8D+1))/4.", "beta": "Appended final step1+t.", "q": "Normalized objective certificate level gamma^2."}}
{"id": "22347ffeb25f8830", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:26:26.128171+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "ENTIRE normal spectral-mixture ansatz is insufficient, even with jointly repaired illegal modes, arbitrary horizon-dependent dimension/weights/spectra, arbitrary legal values, and fully free terminal completion. On H_(m+1)=(H_m,a(13/5)^m,H_m), a=(13/5)^(-17), every legal prefix x_i=P_i(A)x0,g_i=A x_i with ANY real normal A obeys a common appended-unit-step error <=C_norm/[a(13/5)^m]=o(U_(2^m)^ZJ). One SAME complete terminal-block interpolation cycle controls all modes simultaneously. The exact defect recurrence propagates positivity across every larger block; no individual-mode legality is assumed. Complete unverified candidate, not an upper bound on unrestricted W.", "evidence": "# Complete normal spectral mixtures remain insufficient after free completion\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit is allocated.\nAccepted inputs:1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation and maximal completion),\ndfde2fa326c908bf (complex orbit notation and necessary interpolation), and\nb98bb807d514e80d (the exact benchmark recursions). The recursive family and\nthe real-product method were recalled from PUBLIC candidate79f6ce08b41616b5;\nno unverified result is used below. Every needed estimate is included.\n\nThe final result is stronger than the supporting individual-mode statement\nin Sections1--5: Sections6--7 prove a common unit-step O(c^(-m)) upper bound\nfor EVERY jointly legal finite normal spectral mixture, with arbitrary complex\nspectra, arbitrary horizon-dependent weights and dimension, arbitrary legal\nprefix values, and a fully free terminal completion. NO individual-mode\nlegality is assumed in that final result. A single common interpolation\ncycle controls all troublesome modes simultaneously. The exact benchmark\ncomparison is included, so this is a full obstruction for the stated normal\npolynomial-prefix ansatz, not just for separately realizable modes.\n\n## Supporting individual-mode result\n\nPut c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, and define\n\n    H_0=(),  H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),\n    N_m=2^m-1,\n    S_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).\n\nFor a complex z, let x_i=P_i(z) be the chronological prefix polynomial\norbit of H_m, starting from x_0=1, and g_i=z x_i for i=0,...,N_m. Include\nthe optimum jet (0,0,0). Its complete-block polynomial is\n\n    p_m(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-b_j z)^(2^(m-j-1)).\n\nSuppose this FULL prefix is interpolable by a convex differentiable\n1-smooth function with minimum zero at the origin. Its values may be ANY\nlegal interpolating values, not only the maximal path values of the\ncomplex-orbit construction. At the appended final point, use ANY convex\n1-smooth interpolant F of the chosen jets, including the pointwise maximal\none and with no condition on its terminal gradient. Then the COMMON unit\nstep satisfies\n\n    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m)) <= 2c^11/(a c^m).                      (1)\n\nThis holds for every m>=1, with deliberately nonoptimal constants.\nIt also holds for any finite orthogonal mixture of such INDIVIDUALLY legal\ncomplex orbits with squared initial weights summing to one, even if the\njoint legal function values and final interpolant are not separable.\n\nThe canonical sum-only Huber channel on the appended schedule (H_m,1)\nis also O(c^(-m)). The exact recursions imply\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^(4/3),\n\nso, since c^3>16, the maximum of the entire stated witness class and that\nHuber channel, divided by U_(2^m)^ZJ, tends to zero.\n\nThis supporting result is a scoped upper bound on the stated witness family,\nNOT on actual W_(2^m)(H_m,1). It uses full schedule-dependent legality, with\nno universal spectral lens. By itself it does not cover jointly legal\nmixtures of individually illegal modes. Sections6--7 remove that restriction\nusing one common cycle. General nonnormal kernels remain outside the scope\neven after that extension.\n\n## 1. Universal last-query inequalities and the free unit step\n\nWrite z=alpha+i beta and sigma=|z|^2. For z=0 all gradients vanish and\nconvexity makes every queried value, and the appended unchanged value,\nzero. Hence assume z!=0. Interpolation between x_0=1 and the optimum gives\n\n    sigma<=alpha,  alpha>0,  |z|<=1,\n    f_0<=alpha-sigma/2.                                      (2)\n\nFor any queried i it likewise gives\n\n    f_i<= (alpha-sigma/2)|x_i|^2.                             (3)\n\nThe unit-step descent inequality, obtained by integrating a 1-Lipschitz\ngradient along -g_i, gives F(x_i-g_i)<=f_i-|g_i|^2/2. Thus for the last\nquery, independently of all earlier value choices,\n\n    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))\n       <=2(alpha-sigma)|p_m(z)|^2.                           (4)\n\nThis includes the maximal terminal completion in accepted1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 2. Full interpolation imposes a sector or a small-parameter case\n\nPut q=alpha/|z| in(0,1] and h_c=2alpha/sigma. Then h_c>=2 by(2), and\n|1-hz|<=1 exactly for0<=h<=h_c.\n\nIf every step in H_m is smaller than h_c, every factor contracts, so\n|p_m(z)|<=1. Also b_(m-1)<h_c implies\n\n    |z|^2 < 2alpha/b_(m-1) <=2|z|/b_(m-1),\n    |z|<2c/(a c^m).\n\nEquation(4) is then less than4c/(a c^m), which is at most the bound(1).\nThe same conclusion follows if there is no step greater than or equal to\nh_c. We now treat the remaining case.\n\nLet r be the smallest level with b_r>=h_c. Then r<=m-1, b_(r-1)<h_c,\nand r>=18 because b_17=1<h_c. The chronological prefix before the first\nb_r is exactly H_r, and every step of this prefix strictly contracts radii.\nDefine v=b_r|z|. Minimality gives\n\n    2q<=v<2c q.                                              (5)\n\nLet d=2/c=10/13. Since d^3<1/2 and d^2<4/5, d^17<1/40. Consequently\n\n    S_r/b_r=(1-d^r)/(c-2)>=13/8.                             (6)\n\nThe squared-step sum of H_r is\n\n    T_r=sum_(h in H_r) h^2=a^2(c^(2r)-2^r)/(c^2-2),\n    T_r/b_r^2<=1/(c^2-2)=25/119.                             (7)\n\nUsing log(1+t)<=t in every squared radial factor gives\n\n    log|p_r(z)|^2 <= -2alpha S_r+sigma T_r\n                   <=-(13/4)q v+v^2/(c^2-2).                 (8)\n\nThe concave quadratic (13/4)q v-v^2/(c^2-2) has its minimum on the\ninterval(5) at an endpoint. At v=2q its coefficient of q^2 is\n13/2-100/119=1347/238>11/2. At v=2c q that coefficient is\n169/10-676/119>11/2. Thus\n\n    |p_r(z)|^2 <= exp(-(11/2)q^2).                            (9)\n\nWe now use ACTUAL ordered value inequalities inside the prefix, not only\npairwise cocoercivity or radial contraction. At a step of size h with point\nx and next point x_+=(1-hz)x, interpolation in the direction from the next\nquery back to this query gives\n\n    f(x)-f(x_+)\n      >=h(1-halpha+hsigma/2)sigma |x|^2\n      =[sigma/(2alpha)](|x|^2-|x_+|^2)\n        +[h^2 sigma/(2alpha)]\n              [sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2]|x|^2.                 (10)\n\nFor the first inequality, g_+=(1-hz)g,\n<g_+,g>=(1-halpha)|g|^2, and |g-g_+|^2=h^2sigma|g|^2. Expanding gives\nthe displayed identity; no endpoint value or local oracle is guessed.\n\nSuppose q<=2/3. Then sigma-2alpha^2=sigma(1-2q^2)>0, so the second term\nin(10) is nonnegative. Sum(10) along the exact H_r prefix. Its terminal\nvalue is nonnegative and (2) bounds the initial value, so\n\n    [sigma/(2alpha)](1-|p_r(z)|^2)<=alpha-sigma/2,\n    |p_r(z)|^2>=1-2q^2+alpha>1-2q^2.                         (11)\n\nBut for0<t<=4/9,\n\n    exp(-(11/2)t)<1-2t.                                     (12)\n\nIndeed the difference 1-2t-exp(-(11/2)t) is concave, vanishes at0, and is\npositive at4/9. For the last claim,\nexp(22/9)=exp(2)exp(4/9)>(8/3)^2(1+4/9)=832/81>9;\nexp(1)>8/3 follows from the first four terms of its positive series.\nEquations(9),(11),(12) contradict each other. Therefore every legal orbit\nin the present case satisfies\n\n    q=Re(z)/|z|>2/3.                                        (13)\n\nThis is a necessary condition derived from the entire contracting prefix.\n\n## 3. Uniform damping of the dyadic product in the forced sector\n\nAssume0<|z|<=1 and q=Re(z)/|z|>=2/3. Choose the unique integer k>=17\nsuch that\n\n    c^k a|z|<=1<c^(k+1)a|z|,\n    y=c^k a|z| in(1/c,1].                                  (14)\n\nIf m<=k, every factor has b_j|z|<=1, and\n|1-b_jz|^2=1-2q b_j|z|+b_j^2|z|^2<=1; hence |p_m(z)|<=1.\n\nSuppose m>k. A vanishing factor makes the desired damping immediate, so\nassume there is none. Write z/|z|=exp(i theta). Directly from multiplicities,\n\n    log|p_m(z)|/2^(m-k-1)\n       =sum_(j=-k)^(m-k-1) 2^(-j)log|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|.    (15)\n\nFor j<0, log(1+t)<=t gives\n\n    log|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|<=-q c^j y+(c^j y)^2/2.\n\nSumming these terms and using k>=17 and (6) bounds their contribution by\n\n    -(13/4)q y + (25/119)y^2.\n\nFor j>=1, c^j y>1 and q>=2/3, so\n|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|<=c^j y. The bounds jlog c+log y are nonnegative;\nextending their weighted sum to all j>=1 gives2log c+log y. The j=0 term\nis at most(1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2). Therefore(15) is at most\n\n    E(y)=-(13/6)y+(25/119)y^2\n           +(1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2)+log y+2log(13/5).          (16)\n\nHere we used q>=2/3 also in the negative linear term. We prove the fully\nuniform elementary inequality\n\n    E(y)<-1/128  for5/13<=y<=1.                              (17)\n\nFirst let5/13<=y<=1/2. The positive quadratic term is at most25/476,\nand D(y)=1-(4/3)y+y^2 is decreasing here, so D(y)<=322/507. Also\n-(13/6)y+log y<=-1+log(6/13), by maximizing that elementary concave\nfunction. Combining yields\n\n    E(y)<=-1+25/476+log A,\n    A=(78/25)sqrt(322/507),  A^2=3864/625<(5/2)^2.\n\nWe have log(5/2)<15/16, since the first four terms in the positive\nexponential series give\n\n    exp(15/16)>\n      1+15/16+(15/16)^2/2+(15/16)^3/6\n      =61791/24576>5/2.\n\nThus E(y)<-1+25/476+15/16=-19/1904<-1/128.\n\nFor1/2<=y<=1, E is strictly decreasing. Here are the exact derivative\ncalculations to check that claim, without a numerical maximization. With\nD(y)=1-(4/3)y+y^2,\n\n    E'(y)=B(y)/[2142yD(y)],\n    B(y)=900y^4-5841y^3+11372y^2-8925y+2142.\n\nPut t=2y-1 in[0,1]. Direct expansion gives\n\n    16B((1+t)/2)\n       =-2422-11870t+15842t^2-8082t^3+900t^4\n       <=-2422+t[-11870+15842t-7182t^2]\n       <=-2422-3210t<0.\n\nFor the second inequality, 15842t-7182t^2 is increasing on[0,1] and is\nat most8660. The denominator of E' is positive. Hence E(y)<=E(1/2),\nalready controlled by the preceding interval. This proves(17).\n\nEquations(15)--(17) imply\n\n    |p_m(z)| <= exp(-2^(m-k-1)/128)  whenever m>k.             (18)\n\n## 4. The free terminal objective bound\n\nFor a legal orbit the small-parameter case in Section2 already gives\nthe bound(1). Otherwise(13) permits the product estimates of Section3.\nIf |z|<=1/(a c^m), then m<=k and(4) is at most2/(a c^m).\nIf |z|>1/(a c^m), let r=m-k>=1. By(14),(18),\n\n    2(alpha-sigma)|p_m(z)|^2\n       <=[2/(a c^m)] c^r exp(-2^(r-1)/64)\n       <=2c^11/(a c^m).                                    (19)\n\nFor1<=r<=11, discard the exponential and use c^r<=c^11. For r>=12,\n2^(r-1)/64=2^(r-7)>=r, by induction from r=12, while log c<1 because\nexp(1)>8/3>13/5. The extra factor is then at most1. This proves(19),\nand therefore(1), with complete prefix values and arbitrary terminal\ncompletion retained.\n\nFor a finite orthogonal mixture, write its components as\n\n    x_i^(l)=sqrt(w_l)P_i(z_l),\n    g_i^(l)=sqrt(w_l)z_l P_i(z_l),\n    w_l>=0, sum_l w_l=1,\n\nup to harmless rotations in the individual planes. Assume every active\nz_l individually has a legal prefix. For ANY jointly legal values and ANY\njoint smooth convex interpolant, the same last-query/unit-step inequalities\ngive\n\n    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))\n       <=2sum_l w_l(Re(z_l)-|z_l|^2)|p_m(z_l)|^2\n       <=2c^11/(a c^m).\n\nSeparability of the joint interpolant is unnecessary. Individual legality\nIS needed to apply Section2 to each z_l; it is not inferred from joint\nlegality. No such inference is claimed for mixtures of illegal modes.\n\n## 5. Exact-profile comparison, with its recurrence estimate proved here\n\nFor m>=17, (6) with r=m gives S_m>=13a c^m/8. Thus the canonical Huber\nvalue for(H_m,1) is at most\n\n    1/[1+2(S_m+1)]<=1/(2S_m)<=4/(13a c^m).                    (20)\n\nWe now derive B_n<=1+4n^(4/3) directly from the exact recursions stated in\nacceptedb98bb807d514e80d. Put C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1.\nIn these variables the primitive and dominant joins respectively are\n\n    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nThey are increasing in their nonnegative arguments. For0<=t<=1,\n\n    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.                  (21)\n\nTo prove it set x=t^(1/3),y=(1-t)^(1/3), so x^3+y^3=1. Then\n\n    1-x^4-y^4-x^4y^4\n       =x^3y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0.\n\nEach reciprocal is at least1/3, while xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3; the latter strict\ninequality follows by cubing. The endpoints also satisfy the identity.\nSince Omega is the positive root of X^2-(A+D)X-AD=0, (21) implies\n\n    Omega(A,D)<=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3).\n\nInduction in the primitive recursion gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Also\nsqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A, hence G(A,D)<=D+4A. Induction in the dominant\nrecursion, for every split k, gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4[(n-k-1)^(4/3)+(k+1)^(4/3)]<=1+4n^(4/3).\n\nThe initial values are C_0=B_0=1. The last inequality uses the elementary\nsuperadditivity of u^(4/3) on nonnegative u.\n\nThe appended horizon is N_m+1=2^m. Multiply(1),(20) by\nB_(2^m)<=1+4*2^(4m/3). Their ratios to U_(2^m)^ZJ tend to zero because\nc^3=2197/125>16. This closes the proposed all-schedule comparison for the\nentire stated individually legal orbit/direct-sum family, even after free\nterminal completion and addition of the canonical Huber channel.\n\n## 6. Analytic estimates for supporting modes, independent of legality\n\nThe estimates in this section assume only0<|z|<=1 and\nq=Re(z)/|z|>=2/3; they do not assume that the orbit is individually legal.\nRetain k from(14), and define\n\n    R_u(z)=p_u(z)(1-b_u z),\n    E_u(z)=sum_(i in H_u) h_i^2 |z|^2 |P_i(z)|^2.\n\nHere P_i are the local chronological prefix polynomials of H_u starting\nat1. The recursion of the actual ordered list gives\n\n    E_(u+1)=(1+|R_u|^2)E_u+b_u^2|z|^2|p_u|^2.               (22)\n\nFor u<=k all factors contract, so E_k<=|z|^2 T_k<1. At u=k both\n|R_k|^2 and b_k^2|z|^2|p_k|^2 are at most1, hence E_(k+1)<=3.\nFor u=k+t,t>=1, the damping estimate(18) and b_u|z|<=c^t give\n\n    |R_u|^2, b_u^2|z|^2|p_u|^2\n          <=c^(2t)exp(-2^(t-7)).                            (23)\n\nFor1<=t<=12 this is at most c^24. For t>=13,2^(t-7)>=3t, by induction\nfrom t=13, so(23) is at most exp(-t)<=2^(-t), using log c<1. Applying(22)\nto E_u+1 therefore gives the uniform bound\n\n    E_u(z)+1<K_E:=12(1+c^24)^12  for every u>=0.               (24)\n\nIndeed E_(k+1)+1<=4. The next twelve updates multiply this upper bound\nby at most(1+c^24)^12. Every subsequent update multiplies E_u+1 by at\nmost1+2^(-t); their product is at most exp(sum_(t>=13)2^(-t))<exp(1)<3.\nFor exp(1)<3, sum n!>=2^(n-1), with strictness when n>=3, in the\nexponential series. The earlier u<=k already satisfy(24).\n\nThere is also the weighted block-amplitude bound\n\n    |z||R_u(z)|^2<=c^36/b_u.                                (25)\n\nIf u<=k, |z|<=1/b_u and |R_u|<=1. If u=k+t>k, multiply(23) by\n|z|<=c^t/b_u. For t<=12 the result is at most c^36/b_u; for t>=13 it\nis at most1/b_u because2^(t-7)>=3t and log c<1. In particular\n\n    Re(z)|R_(m-1)(z)|^2<=c^37/b_m.                          (26)\n\nThese are quantitative bounds on the same chronological orbit, with no\ndimension, mixture-weight or function-value assumptions.\n\n## 7. One common complete cycle controls ALL normal modes\n\nConsider ANY finite list of complex numbers z_l and weights w_l>=0 with\nsum_l w_l=1. Form the real direct-sum data for the entire H_m prefix by\n\n    x_i=(sqrt(w_l)P_i(z_l))_l,\n    g_i=(sqrt(w_l)z_l P_i(z_l))_l,\n    x_star=g_star=0.                                        (27)\n\nReal scalar modes are included as beta_l=0. Constant phases in individual\nplanes can be absorbed by orthogonal rotations. There is no assumption on\nthe individual spectra or their separate legality. Assume only that these\nJOINT data admit actual convex1-smooth interpolation values f_i, with the\ngiven optimum, and let F be ANY legal interpolant of them.\n\nDefine constants depending only on c by\n\n    C_aux=K_E c^37+2c[1+4c^2/(c^2-2)],\n    C_norm=2c^11+256 C_aux.                                  (28)\n\nWe prove the uniform upper bound\n\n    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))<=C_norm/(a c^m).                     (29)\n\nThe spectra, weights, number of modes, and legal values may all depend on\nm. This upper bound includes the pointwise maximal terminal completion.\n\n### 7a. The common cycle and its exact recurrence\n\nFor any mode z=alpha+i beta, put sigma=|z|^2, kappa=alpha-sigma, and let\n\n    L_r(z)=sum_(i in H_r) h_i |P_i(z)|^2,\n    E_r(z)=sum_(i in H_r) h_i^2 sigma |P_i(z)|^2.\n\nDefine its reverse-chronological optimum cycle weight by\n\n    D_r(z)=sum_(i in H_r)\n                 h_i sigma(1-h_i alpha+h_i sigma/2)|P_i(z)|^2\n                  +(sigma/2)|p_r(z)|^2-alpha+sigma/2.        (30)\n\nThe cycle goes from the optimum to the end of H_r, backwards through its\nqueries to its start, then to the optimum. The reverse query edges have\nthe algebraic weights in(10). Its area and energy alternatively give\n\n    D_r(z)=beta^2 L_r(z)\n                 -(kappa/2)[1+|p_r(z)|^2+E_r(z)].            (31)\n\nTo check(31), each forward GD edge has area\nIm(conj(P_i)P_(i+1))=-h_i beta|P_i|^2. Reversing the path changes the\nsign, and the two optimum edges contribute zero area. The cycle energy is\n1+|p_r|^2+E_r. The complex cycle identity of accepteddfde2fa326c908bf is\npurely algebraic and therefore applies even to an individually illegal mode.\n\nIf alpha>0, telescoping squared norms yields\n\n    1-|p_r|^2=2alpha L_r-E_r.\n\nPut d= sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2. Substitution into(31) gives\n\n    D_r=[d/(2alpha)](1+E_r)\n                    -[sigma(1-alpha)/(2alpha)]|p_r|^2.\n\nUsing(22), p_(r+1)=R_r p_r, and\nb_r^2sigma|p_r|^2-|R_r|^2=(2b_r alpha-1)|p_r|^2, one obtains the EXACT\nrecurrence\n\n    D_(r+1)=(1+|R_r|^2)D_r\n                         +|p_r|^2[kappa+b_r d].             (32)\n\nFor clarity, in the substitution the constant-coefficient difference is\n[sigma(1-alpha)-d]/(2alpha)=kappa. Thus no sign in(32) is inferred from\nlegality or a guessed potential.\n\nNow use the LAST copy of H_(m-1) inside H_m. Its start polynomial is\nR_(m-1), and its end polynomial is p_m=R_(m-1)p_(m-1). The SAME indexed\ncycle on the full joint prefix has weight\n\n    sum_l w_l D_(m-1)(z_l)|R_(m-1)(z_l)|^2.\n\nJoint interpolation requires\n\n    sum_l w_l D_(m-1)(z_l)|R_(m-1)(z_l)|^2<=0.               (33)\n\nNo different cycle is selected for different spectral modes in(33).\n\n### 7b. Classification of every complex parameter\n\nPartition the active modes into the following three groups.\n\nOUTSIDE MODES: kappa<=0. Formula(31) gives D_r>=0 for every r, because\nbeta^2 L_r>=0 and the bracketed energy is nonnegative. These modes can\nsupply NO negative credit in(33), and their unit-step objective contribution\n2w_l kappa_l|p_m(z_l)|^2 is nonpositive. This includes arbitrary real or\ncomplex parameters outside the cocoercivity disk and the zero mode.\n\nSAFE MODES: kappa>0 and either q=alpha/|z|>=2/3, or every step of H_m is\nsmaller than h_c=2alpha/sigma. Here alpha>=sigma>0 implies0<|z|<=1.\nIn the sector case, Sections3 and4 are purely polynomial bounds and give\n\n    2kappa|p_m|^2<=2c^11/b_m.                               (34)\n\nAlso each term in(30) is at least-alpha h_i^2sigma|P_i|^2, so\n-D_r<=alpha(1+E_r). With(24),(26), this yields\n\n    [-D_(m-1)]|R_(m-1)|^2<=K_E c^37/b_m.                   (35)\n\nIn the all-contracting case, every prefix modulus is at most1 and\n|z|<2c/b_m. Then E_(m-1)<=|z|^2 T_(m-1)<=4c^2/(c^2-2), and the left\nside of(35) is at most2c[1+4c^2/(c^2-2)]/b_m. The objective bound(34)\nalso holds, by the small-parameter calculation of Section2. Thus the total\nnegative cycle credit from ALL safe modes, after their weights are applied,\nis at most C_aux/b_m, while their total objective contribution is at most\n2c^11/b_m.\n\nTROUBLESOME MODES: kappa>0, q<2/3, and there is a level r<=m-1 with\nb_r>=h_c. Take the first such r for the following ANALYSIS of the mode,\nwithout changing the common cycle(33). It has r>=18, and all of H_r\nstrictly contracts. Estimates(5)--(9) and the algebraic identity in(10)\ndo not need individual legality. They imply\n\n    D_r(z)>=alpha[(1-exp(-(11/2)q^2))/(2q^2)-1].              (36)\n\nThe bracket decreases as q^2>0 increases: differentiating\n(1-exp(-at))/t and using exp(at)>=1+at proves this. Since q^2<=4/9,\n\n    D_r(z)/alpha\n       >=(9/8)(1-exp(-22/9))-1\n       >(9/8)(1-81/832)-1\n       =103/6656>1/128.                                    (37)\n\nHere we used the explicit exponential bound in(12). Moreover\nd=sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2>0 and kappa>0, so(32) implies for every u>=r\n\n    D_(u+1)>=|R_u|^2 D_u.\n\nPropagating the positive defect up to m-1 gives\n\n    D_(m-1)(z)\n       >=D_r(z) product_(u=r)^(m-2)|R_u(z)|^2\n       >=[alpha/128]|p_(m-1)(z)|^2.                        (38)\n\nFor the second inequality, p_(m-1)=p_r product_(u=r)^(m-2)R_u and\n|p_r|<=1. The empty product when r=m-1 is allowed. Multiplying(38) by\n|R_(m-1)|^2 proves that EVERY troublesome mode contributes at least\n\n    [alpha/128]|p_m(z)|^2                                 (39)\n\nto the SAME cycle(33). Thus troublesome modes cannot cancel each other's\npositive defects on this cycle, even if their first violating scales differ.\n\nThese three groups cover every complex number. Boundary kappa=0 modes\nwere placed outside, and q=2/3 modes in the safe sector, so no limiting case\nis omitted.\n\n### 7c. Joint legality pays for the whole problematic spectral mass\n\nInsert the classification into(33). Outside modes contribute nonnegative\ncycle weights; all troublesome modes have the simultaneous lower bound(39).\nThe total possible negative credit of safe modes is at most C_aux/b_m.\nTherefore\n\n    sum_(troublesome l) w_l alpha_l |p_m(z_l)|^2\n                                      <=128 C_aux/b_m.       (40)\n\nFor ANY joint legal values, interpolation to the optimum followed by the\ncommon unit-step descent gives\n\n    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))\n      <=2<x_(N_m),g_(N_m)>-2|g_(N_m)|^2\n      =2sum_l w_l kappa_l |p_m(z_l)|^2.                      (41)\n\nOutside modes have nonpositive terms. Safe modes contribute at most\n2c^11/b_m in total. In every troublesome mode kappa_l<=alpha_l, so(40)\nbounds their total by256 C_aux/b_m. This proves(29).\n\nNo assumption of separable function values, separate legality, a bounded\nnumber of modes, or a spectrum fixed across horizons was made. The Gram\ndata in(27) are real and PSD by actual direct-sum realization; joint\nlegality, when it holds, is the only hypothesis used for(33),(41).\n\n## 8. Exact scope of the completed normal-mixture obstruction\n\nEvery real finite-dimensional normal operator is orthogonally decomposable\ninto real scalar blocks and planar complex-multiplication blocks. For a\nnormalized initial vector, the squared block-coordinate norms give exactly\nthe weights in(27); initial phases are harmless planar rotations. Therefore\n(29) applies equivalently to EVERY jointly legal prefix of the form\n\n    x_i=P_i(A)x_0,  g_i=A x_i,\n\nwith one real normal operator A, arbitrary real Gram dimension, arbitrary\nlegal values, and arbitrary free terminal completion. No spectral restriction\non A is imposed. The finite direct-sum formulation(27) also states the\nwhole result without using operator terminology.\n\nThe horizon after the unit step is2^m. Multiplying(29) and the canonical\nHuber bound(20) by B_(2^m)<=1+4*2^(4m/3), proved in Section5, makes both\nratios tend to zero, since c^3>16. This is a full rigorous candidate\nobstruction for the chosen NORMAL spectral-mixture ansatz. It does not\nupper-bound unrestricted W or exclude a genuinely NONNORMAL kernel.\n\n## Remaining possible repairs\n\n1. Jointly repairing illegal normal modes works in the finite3D calibration,\n   but Sections6--7 show it cannot restore the benchmark uniformly on H_m.\n   More such normal modes or altered legal values do not avoid(29).\n2. A definite nonnormal polynomial kernel can have correlations absent from\n   every normal spectral mixture. Replacing it by an arbitrary Gram form\n   would merely restate the original problem and is not a quantitative repair.\n3. The legal growing Chebyshev constructions remain valid scoped candidates.\n   They do not supply the missing uniform comparison on this recursive family.\n\nMultiplying every interpolant by an arbitrary prescribed L>0 restores the\nproject's smoothness normalization without changing these normalized bounds.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "plan_id": "2a07cc504b9e18c5", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/normal_mixture_recursive_obstruction.md", "extends_findings": ["6e098f91c4a13444", "8273524595704c66"], "related_positive_candidate": "cedcf8ce28d0fe89", "verification_status": "Complete candidate only; no new fact_submit allocated or called", "scope": "All finite normal polynomial Gram kernels, with only JOINT prefix legality; dimension and spectra may depend on horizon. Genuinely nonnormal kernels remain open.", "new_mechanism": "One common last-block cycle: D_(r+1)=(1+|R_r|²)D_r+|p_r|²[kappa+b_r(sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha²)]."}, "glossary": {"A": "Any finite-dimensional real normal operator, equivalently an orthogonal direct sum of real and complex-multiplication blocks.", "H_m": "Recursive listH_0=(), H_(m+1)=(H_m,a c^m,H_m), length2^m-1.", "c": "Fixed scale13/5.", "a": "Fixed scale c^(-17).", "p_m(z)": "Complete H_m product polynomial.", "R_u(z)": "Polynomial of the block(H_u,b_u):p_u(z)(1-b_u z).", "E_u(z)": "Accumulated squared displacement sum h_i²|z|²|P_i(z)|² on H_u.", "L_r(z)": "Sum h_i|P_i(z)|² on H_r, used in the cycle area identity.", "D_r(z)": "Exact interpolation cycle weight along optimum->end of H_r->reverse chronological queries->start->optimum.", "kappa": "Re(z)-|z|², the scalar cocoercivity coefficient.", "q": "Re(z)/|z| for nonzero modes inside the cocoercivity disk.", "K_E": "Constant12(1+c^24)^12 bounding sector-mode accumulated energy plus one.", "C_aux": "K_E c^37+2c[1+4c²/(c²-2)], bounding all negative supporting cycle credit.", "C_norm": "2c^11+256C_aux, independent of horizon, spectra, weights and dimension.", "normal spectral mixture": "Real datax_i=(sqrt(w_l)P_i(z_l))_l,g_i=(sqrt(w_l)z_lP_i(z_l))_l with nonnegative weights summing to one; individual modes need not be legal."}}
{"id": "7ae9117f7b476ed0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:35:48.472554+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "First entire genuinely nonnormal obstruction: ANY legal prefix whose gradients are affine in cumulative steps (including every A^3 x0=0 polynomial prefix, with arbitrary dimension/conditioning) has common free-unit-step payoff <=1/[2(S+1)] whenever its last positive step is <=1. An explicit fixed convex1smooth nonconstant-gradient 3D realization shows the class is nonempty. On prefixes(1,2,...,n-1,1) the whole class is O(n^-2)=o(U_(n+1)^ZJ).", "evidence": "# An entire nonnormal affine-gradient obstruction\n\nStatus: complete candidate argument, not verifier-accepted. No new verification\nallocation is used. Accepted facts 1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d are the\nonly graph inputs; all new algebra and the example are proved below. In\nparticular no earlier unverified obstruction is a premise.\n\n## 1. Class and result\n\nFix a finite nonnegative prefix h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), allowing zero steps. Set\n\n    s_i=sum_(t<i) h_t,       S=s_n,\n    e_i=sum_(0<=r<t<i) h_r h_t.\n\nSuppose real vectors x_0,v,w give queried data at every index 0<=i<=n by\n\n    x_i=x_0-s_i v+e_i w,        g_i=v-s_i w.             (1)\n\nAssume there are values f_i such that all these jets and (0,0,0) have a convex\ndifferentiable 1-smooth interpolant. The values are arbitrary, and the terminal\ncompletion after query n is unrestricted. Normalize ||x_0||<=1.\n\nAssume either S=0, or the LAST POSITIVE entry of h is at most one. Then EVERY\ninterpolant F of the queried jets with F(0)=min F=0 satisfies\n\n    2 F(x_n-g_n) <= 1/[2(S+1)].                         (2)\n\nThus (2) applies to the maximal interpolant in fact1252b8823bb5a290 and bounds\nthe best uniform-in-final-stepsize lower witness in this entire class. It does\nnot impose any linear relation at the new endpoint x_n-g_n.\n\nFor any real linear operator A with A^3 x_0=0, the usual polynomial prefix\nx_i=P_i(A)x_0, g_i=A x_i, P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), has exactly (1), with\nv=A x_0 and w=A^2 x_0. Therefore (2) covers every such nilpotent Krylov prefix,\nwithout any bound on dimension, operator norm, conditioning, or dependence of\nthe data on n. More generally it covers (1) even when no single A realizes it.\n\nThis class genuinely contains nonconstant gradients and nonnormal operators;\nSection 4 supplies an actual fixed legal function for every nonempty schedule.\n\n## 2. Proof retaining the actual last positive step\n\nWrite r=g_n=v-Sw and\n\n    M=sum_(t<n) h_t(S-s_t)=(S^2+sum_(t<n)h_t^2)/2>=0.\n\nDirect substitution in (1), or summation of the actual GD steps, gives\n\n    g_i=r+(S-s_i)w,       x_n=x_0-Sr-Mw.               (3)\n\nThe GD recursion itself holds because e_(i+1)=e_i+h_i s_i.\n\nFor any two legal query indices i,j, add the two interpolation inequalities\nQ_ij<=0 and Q_ji<=0 from fact1252b8823bb5a290. Their function values cancel,\nyielding\n\n    <g_i-g_j,x_i-x_j> >= ||g_i-g_j||^2.                (4)\n\nSuppose S>0 and let ell be the index of the last positive step, with\ndelta=h_ell in (0,1]. Trailing zero steps repeat the same point and gradient.\nHence\n\n    g_ell=r+delta w,\n    x_ell=x_n+delta r+delta^2 w.\n\nUse (4) on indices ell,n and divide by delta^2>0. This gives exactly\n\n    <r,w> >= (1-delta)||w||^2 >=0.                    (5)\n\nNo other gradient correlation was assumed. In particular it is the final\nsmall step, rather than nilpotence by itself, that supplies the sign.\n\nThe optimum interpolation inequality at index n gives\n\n    f_n <= <r,x_n>-||r||^2/2.\n\nFor completeness, 1-smoothness gives the one-step descent estimate\nF(x_n-r)<=f_n-||r||^2/2: integrate the gradient along x_n-t r, 0<=t<=1,\nand bound <grad F(x_n-t r)-r,-r> by t||r||^2 using Lipschitz continuity.\nConsequently (3) and (5) imply\n\n    2F(x_n-r)\n      <=2<r,x_0>-2(S+1)||r||^2-2M<r,w>\n      <=2||r||-2(S+1)||r||^2\n      =1/[2(S+1)]-2(S+1)(||r||-1/[2(S+1)])^2.\n\nThis proves (2). If S=0, (3) has M=0 and the same last calculation applies\nwithout (5). It also covers r=0 and w=0. All trailing and internal zero steps\nwere retained, and no division by a gradient or operator parameter occurred.\n\n## 3. Quantitative unbounded-horizon consequence\n\nFor each n>=2 take the explicit n-step prefix\n\n    h^(n)=(1,2,...,n-1,1).\n\nIts sum is S=n(n-1)/2+1 and its final step is one. For ANY legal prefix (1),\nincluding all horizon-dependent nilpotent operators above, the same final\nchoice b=1 therefore obeys\n\n    2F(x_n-g_n)<=1/[n(n-1)+4].                        (6)\n\nThe exact benchmark in accepted factb98bb807d514e80d is U_N^ZJ=1/B_N, with\nB_N<=1+4N^p and p=log_2(3)<2. Divide (6) by U_(n+1)^ZJ to obtain\n\n    (1+4(n+1)^p)/[n(n-1)+4] ->0.\n\nThus this entire nonnormal polynomial class cannot supply a uniform matching\nlower bound for arbitrary schedules. This is an ansatz obstruction, not an\nupper bound on unrestricted W_(n+1)(h^(n),1).\n\nMore broadly, (2) applies to EVERY permutation of a multiset whose final\npositive element is at most one. Its significance is that adding the second\nelementary symmetric moment and a nonconstant affine gradient still leaves a\nsum-scale bound whenever the supplied chronology ends with a small step.\n\n## 4. Explicit nonconstant legal realization for every finite schedule\n\nWe construct unnormalized data in R^3, then normalize. For any S>=0 put\n\n    q=v0=1/[4(S+1)],          eta=v0/[2(S+1)],\n    X0=(1,1,1),              V=(q,v0,0),   W=(0,eta,0),\n    X_i=(1-q s_i, 1-v0 s_i+eta e_i, 1),\n    G_i=(q,v0-eta s_i,0).\n\nSet Y_i=1-v0 s_i+eta e_i and V_i=v0-eta s_i. We have Y_i>=3/4,\n0<V_i<=v0<=1/4, and the first coordinate of X_i is at least3/4>q.\n\nIf s_i<s_j, then\n\n    0<Y_i-Y_j=(s_j-s_i)[v0-eta (e_j-e_i)/(s_j-s_i)],\n    V_i-V_j=eta(s_j-s_i).\n\nThe increment identity for e gives\n0<=(e_j-e_i)/(s_j-s_i)<=S. It follows that\n\n    0 < (V_i-V_j)/(Y_i-Y_j)\n      <= eta/(v0-eta S)=1/(S+2)<=1.\n\nRepeated values of s_i give identical Y_i,V_i and can be merged in the\nfollowing scalar definition. Let phi be the continuous piecewise affine\nfunction through the knots (0,0),(Y_n,V_n),...,(Y_0,V_0), listed in increasing\nfirst coordinate. Set phi(y)=0 for y<=0 and phi(y)=v0 for y>=1. The first\nsegment has slope V_n/Y_n<=1/3; every other segment has slope in [0,1] by\nthe preceding calculation. Thus phi is nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz.\n\nDefine\n\n    C_q(u)=u^2/2                 for u<=q,\n           q u-q^2/2            for u>=q,\n    B(y)=integral_0^y phi(t)dt,\n    F0(u,y,z)=C_q(u)+B(y).\n\nBoth scalar derivatives are nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. Squaring the two\ncoordinate gradient differences shows that F0 is convex and 1-smooth, with\nminimum zero at the origin. Its gradient at X_i is exactly G_i. Hence all\nsample values F0(X_i) satisfy EVERY ordered smooth-convex interpolation\ninequality, not only the adjacent inequalities. The actual recursion for\nthe prescribed steps holds by the displayed increment identity.\n\nNormalize using x_i=X_i/sqrt(3), g_i=G_i/sqrt(3), and\nF(x)=F0(sqrt(3)x)/3. Then ||x_0||=1, the smoothness remains one, and the\nwhole construction is still a single fixed function.\n\nThe three vectors X0,V,W are linearly independent: W has a nonzero second\ncoordinate, V has a nonzero first coordinate, and X0 has a nonzero third\ncoordinate. Define a linear operator A on this basis by\n\n    A X0=V,      A V=W,      A W=0.\n\nThen A^3=0, A^2 X0=W!=0, and A X_i=G_i. It is a nonzero nilpotent operator\nand cannot be real normal: a real normal nilpotent matrix has all zero\neigenvalues and hence is zero by its orthogonal spectral decomposition.\nIf S>0, gradients change at every positive step. This proves that the\nobstructed class has actual curved, nonconstant-gradient legal examples.\n\n## 5. Scope and retrieval\n\nThe last-positive-step assumption is essential to the sign proof; nothing\nhere bounds all nilpotent prefixes ending with a large jump. The result does\nnot cover higher-degree gradient clocks, nonzero-eigenvalue Jordan blocks,\nor an arbitrary changing-generator kernel. It does not use the unverified\nnormal-mixture candidate as a lemma.\n\nMatlas retrieval for affine-gradient/nilpotent interpolation returned\narXiv:2003.04201 Corollary3.9 (self-contracted small-step sequences),\narXiv:1405.7741 Proposition3 (pseudocontractivity), and arXiv:2111.07720\nLemma5.1 (projected gradient). They do not supply the asserted class bound\nand none is imported. A web search located operator-interpolation work and\nthe standard smooth-convex interpolation paper, but no external result is\nused: the needed interpolation inequalities come from the accepted fact.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max2_nilpotent_affine_gradient", "attempt_type": "direct", "subgoal": "Entire-class quantitative verdict and actual nonnormal realization", "status": "complete_unverified_candidate", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/nilpotent_affine_gradient_obstruction.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": false, "result": "not_needed", "summary": "All announced subgoals have explicit candidate proofs."}, "scope": "Last positive prefix step <=1, arbitrary previous nonnegative steps, free terminal completion. Not all nonnormal kernels or unrestricted W.", "verification_allocation": "No new fact_submit."}, "glossary": {"s_i": "Cumulative sum of prefix steps before query i.", "S": "Total prefix step sum.", "e_i": "Second elementary symmetric sum of the steps before query i.", "v": "Initial gradient in the affine-gradient ansatz.", "w": "Fixed slope vector for the affine-gradient ansatz g_i=v-s_i w.", "r": "Final queried gradient g_n.", "M": "Sum over t of h_t(S-s_t).", "A": "A real linear generator, nilpotent on the Krylov space with A^3x0=0."}}

An explicit genuine nonnormal fixed-function realization and all-anchor orthogonal terminal vector are included.", "evidence": "# A complete candidate obstruction for one real Jordan eigenvalue\n\nStatus: unverified candidate. No new fact_submit allocation is used. All new\nlemmas needed below are proved in this document; no previous unverified\nfinding is used as a premise. Graph inputs are accepted1252b8823bb5a290\n(smooth-convex interpolation and maximal completion) and b98bb807d514e80d\n(the exact benchmark recursions).\n\n## 1. Exact class, recursive family, and conclusion\n\nSet c=13/5, a=c^(-18), b_j=a c^j, and define ordered schedules\n\n    H_0=(),       H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m).\n\nTheir length is N_m=2^m-1 and their sum is\n\n    S_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).\n\nFor every m>=1 consider ANY finite real Euclidean space, a unit initial\nvector x0, a real number lambda, and a real linear operator\n\n    A=lambda I+N,          N^2 x0=0.\n\nThe condition is only on the initial Krylov space; arbitrary other action\nof A is allowed. At all N_m+1 query indices use the exact polynomial jets\n\n    x_i=P_i(A)x0,       g_i=A x_i,\n    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z),       h=H_m.\n\nSuppose these jets and the optimum sample (0,0,0), with SOME values f_i,\nare jointly interpolable by a convex differentiable 1-smooth function.\nThe values are arbitrary. Let F be ANY such interpolant, including the\nmaximal one from fact1252b8823bb5a290. Write x_end=x_(N_m), g_end=g_(N_m).\n\nWith K=3250 and the finite absolute constant\n\n    C_J=1020+(16/7)c^11(1+8K)(1+9K),\n\nthe SAME appended step b=1 satisfies\n\n    2F(x_end-g_end) <= C_J/b_m.                            (1)\n\nNo linear-operator constraint is imposed at the new endpoint. Dimension,\nlambda, N, conditioning and values may all depend on m. The canonical\nsum-based Huber channel is also O(1/b_m). Section7 proves directly from\nthe accepted benchmark recurrence that B_n<=1+4n^(4/3), so the ratio of\nthese bounds to U_(2^m)^ZJ tends to zero because c^3>16.\n\nThis is a bound on the specified Jordan class, not on unrestricted W.\nIt does NOT include jointly legal mixtures of different Jordan eigenvalues.\n\n## 2. Exact polynomial identities and a necessary eigenvalue interval\n\nPut u=N x0 and U=||u||. Finite binomial expansion, using N^2 x0=0, gives\n\n    x_i=P_i(lambda)x0+P_i'(lambda)u,\n    g_i=lambda P_i(lambda)x0+[P_i(lambda)+lambda P_i'(lambda)]u.  (2)\n\nThese identities do not divide by P_i and include repeated polynomial roots.\nIn particular g0=lambda x0+u and\n\n    A g0=2lambda g0-lambda^2 x0.                           (3)\n\nLet d0=<x0,g0> and e0=||g0||^2. The two optimum interpolation inequalities\nimply d0>=e0. The first step of H_m is a>0. Adding its two ordered\ninterpolation inequalities and dividing by a^2 gives\n\n    <A g0,g0> >= ||A g0||^2 >=0.                          (4)\n\nBy (3), the left side is 2lambda e0-lambda^2 d0. If e0=0, then g0=0 and\n(3) implies lambda=0; all jets are then stationary. If e0>0, lambda<0\nis impossible. For lambda>0 the inequalities d0>=e0 and (4) give\n\n    0<=lambda(2-lambda)e0,\n\nso 0<=lambda<=2. We use this closed interval and do not require endpoint\nstrictness. Neither an operator-norm bound nor individual eigendirection\nlegality was assumed.\n\n## 3. A short actual prefix controls the nilpotent component\n\nConsider any initial nonnegative prefix of the same jets, with total sum\nT>0 satisfying lambda T<=1/20. For its query indices define\n\n    p_i=product_(t<i)(1-lambda h_t),\n    t_i=sum_(t<i)h_t/(1-lambda h_t).\n\nAll denominators are positive, and\n\n    19/20<=p_i<=1,       0<=lambda t_i<=1/19.\n\nThe lower product bound follows inductively from product(1-r_j)>=1-sum r_j\nwhen r_j>=0 and their sum is at most1. Equation(2) now reads\n\n    g_i=p_i[lambda x0+(1-lambda t_i)u],\n    A g_i=p_i[lambda^2 x0+lambda(2-lambda t_i)u].            (5)\n\nSuppose U>=4lambda and U>0. Then\n\n    ||g_i|| >=(9/10)U-(19/20)lambda >=U/2,\n    ||g_i|| <=lambda+U <=5U/4,\n    ||A g_i||<=lambda^2+2lambda U <=(9/4)lambda U.\n\nAt an actual step h_i, reverse ordered interpolation gives\n\n    f_i-f_(i+1)\n      >= <g_(i+1),h_i g_i>+||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2/2\n       =h_i||g_i||^2-h_i^2<A g_i,g_i>+h_i^2||A g_i||^2/2\n      >=h_i U^2/4-3lambda h_i^2 U^2\n      >=h_i U^2/10.                                     (6)\n\nThe last step uses lambda h_i<=1/20; zero steps cause no division. Sum(6)\nover the full chosen prefix. Its terminal value is nonnegative, while\noptimum interpolation at the initial query gives\n\n    f0<=<g0,x0>-||g0||^2/2<=lambda+U<=5U/4.\n\nThus U<=25/(2T). Combining with the alternative U<4lambda proves\n\n    U<=max{4lambda,25/(2T)}<=4lambda+13/T.                 (7)\n\nIf lambda=U=0 this conclusion is immediate. The estimate comes from the\nactual full-prefix function-value budget, not an imposed normality or\nbounded-conditioning hypothesis.\n\nApply this to H_m with tau=1/100. There are two cases.\n\nLOW FREQUENCY: b_m lambda<tau. Then lambda S_m<tau/(c-2)=1/60, and\nS_m>=b_m/c for m>=1. Using(7) on the entire prefix gives\n\n    U<=4lambda+13/S_m,\n    ||x_end||<=1+(20/19)S_m U<15,\n    ||g_end||<=lambda+U<=5lambda+13/S_m<34/b_m.             (8)\n\nThe position bound uses x_end=p_end(x0-t_end u), t_end<=20S_m/19,\nand lambda S_m<=1/20. The gradient bound follows directly from(5).\n\nSCALE REACHED: b_m lambda>=tau. Since 2a<tau, there is a first integer\nr in[1,m] with b_r lambda>=tau, and b_r lambda<c tau. The actual initial\nblock of H_m of this depth is H_r. Its sum satisfies\n\n    1/260=tau/c<=lambda S_r\n            <c tau/(c-2)=13/300<1/20.\n\nIndeed S_r/b_r=(1-(2/c)^r)/(c-2) lies between1/c and1/(c-2).\nApply(7) to this initial block to obtain the horizon-independent bound\n\n    U<=K lambda,       K=3250.                           (9)\n\n## 4. Complete sector polynomial estimate\n\nDefine the full-block polynomial\n\n    p_m(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-b_j z)^(2^(m-j-1)).\n\nFor EVERY complex z with 0<|z|<=9/4 and Re(z)/|z|>=2/3, we prove\n\n    |z||p_m(z)|^2<=c^11/b_m.                            (10)\n\nThis is an algebraic estimate, independent of any interpolation legality.\nChoose the unique integer k with b_k|z|<=1<b_(k+1)|z| and set\ny=b_k|z| in(5/13,1]. Because a=c^(-18), |z|<=9/4<c, we have k>=17.\nWrite q=Re(z)/|z| and z/|z|=exp(i theta).\n\nIf m<=k, each factor has magnitude at most one: for t=b_j|z|<=1,\n1-2qt+t^2<=1 because q>=2/3. Hence |z||p_m(z)|^2<=1/b_m.\n\nNow m>k. A zero factor proves(10) immediately. Otherwise multiplicities give\n\n    log|p_m(z)|/2^(m-k-1)\n      =sum_(j=-k)^(m-k-1)2^(-j)log|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|.  (11)\n\nFor j<0, log(1+t)<=t applied to the squared magnitude bounds each term\nby -q c^j y+(c^j y)^2/2. Put d=2/c=10/13. We have d^17<1/40: d^3<1/2\nand d^2<4/5 imply d^17=(d^3)^5 d^2<1/40. Thus the sum of negative-index\nterms is at most\n\n    -(13/4)q y+(25/119)y^2.\n\nHere sum_(r=1)^k(2/c)^r>=13/4 and\n(1/2)sum_(r>=1)(2/c^2)^r=25/119. For j>=1, c^j y>1 and q>=2/3 give\n|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|<=c^j y. The nonnegative upper bounds\nj log c+log y can be extended to an infinite sum; their weighted sum is\n2log c+log y. The j=0 term is at most (1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2).\nConsequently(11) is at most\n\n    E(y)=-(13/6)y+(25/119)y^2\n          +(1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2)+log y+2log(13/5).\n\nWe give exact details for E(y)<-1/128 on[5/13,1]. Since |z|<=c^r/b_m,\n\n    |z||p_m(z)|^2<=(1/b_m)c^r exp(-2^(r-1)/64).\n\nFor1<=r<=11 the extra factor is at mostc^11. (14)\n\nUnit-step descent follows by integrating the1-Lipschitz gradient along the\nunit gradient step; it does not assume that the new gradient equals A times\nthe new point.
