An explicit legal genuinely nonnormal calibration\n\nThis section is not used in the upper bound. It shows that the class has\nnontrivial legal prefixes for every supplied nonnegative schedule, with a\nsingle fixed strongly convex function and a uniform final-step lower witness.\n\nFor total sum S choose lambda=1/[10(S+1)], eta=lambda/10,\nx0=(0,1), and A=[[lambda,eta],[0,lambda]]. Thus N^2=0 and N!=0.\nWrite p_i=P_i(lambda), t_i=sum_(t<i)h_t/(1-lambda h_t). The exact positions are\n\n    x_i=(s_i,tcoord_i)=(-eta p_i t_i,p_i).\n\nThe use of tcoord_i as a coordinate is distinct from the sum t_i above.\nWe have p_i>=9/10 and lambda t_i<=1/9. Delete only repeated knots caused\nby zero steps. Along every positive step,\n\n    (s_(i+1)-s_i)/(tcoord_(i+1)-tcoord_i)\n          =(eta/lambda)(1-lambda t_i) in[0,1/10].\n\nJoin these query knots and(0,0) by a continuous piecewise affine graph\ns=psi(t). The segment from(0,0) to the last query has slope\n-eta t_end with magnitude at most1/90. Extend psi constantly outside the\nextreme knots. Then psi(0)=0 and psi is globally1/10-Lipschitz.\n\nDefine on R^2 the single function\n\n    F0(s,t)=lambda(s^2+t^2)/2+eta s t-eta integral_0^t psi(v)dv.\n\nIts gradient is (lambda s+eta t,lambda t+eta(s-psi(t))). Thus at every query\nit equals A x_i exactly. On an affine piece of psi its Hessian is\n\n    [[lambda,eta],[eta,lambda-eta psi'(t)]].\n\nFor any real vector(a0,b0), the absolute value of the perturbation quadratic\n2eta a0 b0-eta psi'(t)b0^2 is at most(11/10)eta(a0^2+b0^2). Integrating\nthe piecewise Hessian along line segments proves that F0 is globally\n(89lambda/100)-strongly convex and has gradient Lipschitz constant at most\n111lambda/100<1. Its gradient at0 is zero, so F0(0)=min F0=0. All query\nvalues are its actual values; therefore all ordered interpolation inequalities\nand the unit initial-distance normalization hold for the SAME data.\n\nFor clarity the resulting all-final-step lower bound can also be quantified.\nLet x=x_end, g=g_end and z be the orthogonal projection of x onto g-perp.\nHere g!=0 and\n\n    |det(x,g)|=eta p_end^2,\n    ||g||<=lambda+eta,\n    ||z||^2>=p_end^4/121>=1/242.\n\nThe determinant identity follows by expanding(2); the terms with t_end\ncancel. Strong convexity gives, for every real final b and hence for b>=0,\n\n    2F0(x-bg)>= (89lambda/100)||z||^2\n               >=89/[242000(S+1)]>1/[3000(S+1)].           (15)\n\nThere is also one test vector satisfying every maximal-interpolant anchor:\nput mu=89lambda/100 and w=mu z. Then <w,g>=0. Since F0>=mu||x||^2/2,\nits conjugate is at most||w||^2/(2mu). Every quadratic conjugate anchor\nH_j from accepted1252b8823bb5a290 lies below F0*, because F0 interpolates\nthe queried data. Thus, with y_j=x_j-g_j and delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2,\n\n    2delta_j+2<x-y_j,w>-||w||^2\n       =2<x,w>-2H_j(w)>=mu||z||^2\n\nfor EVERY query and optimum anchor. The maximal completion inherits(15).\nThis merely calibrates the sum-scale strength of this class; it is weaker\nthan existing general sum-based lower bounds and is not a new target claim.\n\n## 7. Exact-profile comparison derived from the accepted recursions\n\nWrite C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 as in acceptedb98bb807d514e80d.\nTheir two increasing join maps become\n\n    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nFor0<=t<=1,\n\n    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.                (16)\n\nIndeed put x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3), so x^3+y^3=1. Direct factorization is\n\n    1-x^4-y^4-x^4y^4\n      =x^3 y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0.\n\nEach reciprocal is at least1/3 and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3, the latter checked\nby cubing. The endpoint cases hold as well. Since Omega is the positive\nroot of X^2-(A+D)X-AD, inequality(16) yields\n\n    Omega(A,D)<=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3).\n\nInduction in the primitive recurrence gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Also\nsqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A implies G(A,D)<=D+4A. The dominant recurrence therefore\ngives, for each split k and by induction,\n\n    B_n<=1+4[(n-k-1)^(4/3)+(k+1)^(4/3)]<=1+4n^(4/3).\n\nThe last inequality follows from superadditivity of t^(4/3), obtained by\nintegrating its increasing derivative. Initial values are C_0=B_0=1.\nConsequently\n\n    (C_J/b_m)/U_(2^m)^ZJ\n       <=(C_J/a)[c^(-m)+4(2^(4/3)/c)^m] ->0,\n\nbecause c^3=2197/125>16. The canonical Huber value on(H_m,1) is at most\n1/(2S_m)<=c/(2b_m), so adding it does not change this conclusion.\n\n## 8. Neither this\nexample nor C29 decides primitive capacity for arbitrary schedules or\nthe original unrestricted Zhang--Jiang exact-profile question.\n\n## 1. External construction, constants, and hypotheses\n\nSource: Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for\nGradient Descent, arXiv:2609.09152v2, Section 3.1, Lemmas 3.1--3.2 and\nthe pre-weakening final-value calculation proving Lemma 2.2; proofs in\nAppendix A.1--A.3. URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09152v2 . The PDF\nand extracted text are in downloads/ye-liu-2609.09152.{pdf,txt}. The\narc construction, projection proof, displacement bound, zero regions,\nassembly, and terminal calculation were reread for this application.\nA fresh Matlas checkpoint-transfer query returned unrelated fixed-step\nresults, none used. The direct source's identity and version were checked.\n\nThe exact input, also given in PROBLEM.md Section 7, is this. Fix positive\nepsilon and gamma, and chi=sqrt(1+2gamma+epsilon^2). There are finite\nconstants A_YL and B_YL, depending only on these fixed parameters, such\nthat any nonnegative finite schedule with selected checkpoint sizes\nz_i>=B_YL admits a finite-dimensional legal function, minimizer zero,\nand initial point e_1, with\n\n    2f(x_n)>=1/[2(1+T)]\n               product_i [z_i/(chi(A_YL+u_i)+epsilon z_i)]^2. (3)\n\nHere u_i is the sum after the preceding selected checkpoint and before\ncheckpoint i, and T is the sum after the last selected checkpoint.\nThe first u_i includes the whole initial ordinary block.\n\nFor completeness, the source's local functions are translated/scaled\nparameter-one Moreau envelopes of finite support functions. Their\ngradients are projections onto finite convex hulls. The backward arc\nconstruction yields the prescribed trajectory for arbitrary intervening\nnonnegative steps and a bounded second-coordinate displacement. For a\nlocal checkpoint b>=1+epsilon^(-2), its first coordinate stops at U>=0,\nand the function vanishes both when x<=0,y>=0 and when x=U,y>=Y.\nThose regions keep every future and past component inactive during the\nappropriate part of the concatenated trajectory. The global function is\nhalf the sum of adjacent-coordinate local functions and a terminal\none-sided Huber function. Each coordinate occurs in at most two terms,\nso the global smoothness bound is one. Its local steps are h/2, accounting\nfor B_YL=2(1+epsilon^(-2)). The initial point is e_1 and all activation\noffsets are nonnegative, so zero is a common minimizer. There are one\nmore coordinates than selected checkpoints. If the final transferred\ndistance is D, the terminal Huber value is D^2/[4(1+T)], yielding (3)\nafter multiplication by two. No inference from the paper's notation\nfor its performance measure is used.\n\nChoose epsilon=1/100 and gamma=2099/20000, so chi=11/10 and\nB_YL=20002. Choose any permitted fixed finite A_YL. Then choose a\nfixed integer r>=18 sufficiently large that\n\n    b_r>=20002,  chi A_YL/b_r<=1/100.\n\nIn particular s_r>1. Indeed s_r/b_r>=13/8 for r>=17:\n(10/13)^17<1/40 and (c-2)^(-1)=5/3 give this estimate.\nAll constants are fixed independently of m.\n\n## 2. The one changed ordinary block at the copy boundary\n\nChoose every step of level at least r in EACH copy of H_m. Put M=m-r.\nWithin each copy, every ordinary block between selected checkpoints is\nexactly H_r. The initial and terminal ordinary blocks are also H_r.\nAt the junction of the two copies, the one intervening ordinary block\nis (H_r,H_r), of sum 2s_r. There is exactly one such exceptional block.\nThe selected level-ell step b_r c^ell occurs 2^(M-ell-1) times in each\ncopy, for 0<=ell<M. The terminal ordinary sum of the full word is s_r.\n\nFor a usual ordinary block,\n\n    chi(A_YL+s_r)/b_r\n       <=1/100+(11/10)(5/3)=553/300<37/20.\n\nHence each transfer factor at level ell is at least\n\n    q_ell=c^ell/[37/20+c^ell/100].\n\nThis increases with ell, and direct rational comparisons give\n\n    q_0>=1/2, q_1>=4/3, q_2>=7/2, q_3>=8;\n    q_ell>=1 for ell>=4.\n\nWhen M>=4 the transfer product over one copy, using ordinary block sum\ns_r throughout, is therefore at least\n\n    [(1/2)^8(4/3)^4(7/2)^2 8]^(2^(M-4))\n       =(98/81)^(2^(M-4)).                         (4)\n\nFor the exceptional block, at its actual following checkpoint z, the\nratio of the actual factor to the factor using ordinary sum s_r is\n\n    [chi(A_YL+s_r)+epsilon z]\n      /[chi(A_YL+2s_r)+epsilon z]>=1/2.              (5)\n\nAll summands are nonnegative, so the denominator is at most twice the\nnumerator. The full transfer product is thus at least\n\n    (1/2)(98/81)^(2^(M-3)).\n\nSubstituting its square and T=s_r in (3) proves (1). All selected\ncheckpoints exceed B_YL. The special ordinary block is accepted by the\nlocal construction with its actual doubled chronological step list;\nno replacement of that list by a single artificial step is made.\n\n## 3. From the objective blowup to a negative primitive energy\n\nFor any normalized legal run with minimizer zero and f(0)=0, smooth\nconvex interpolation (accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290) gives\n\n    I_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=0.\n\nExpanding ||x_(i+1)||^2=||x_i-h_i g_i||^2 and summing gives the exact\nidentity\n\n    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2-2sum_(i<n)h_i I_i\n                      -2S f_n-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nFor nonnegative steps and S this is at most ||x_0||^2-2S f_n. Using\n||x_0||=1, S=2s_m, and (1) proves the first inequality in (2).\nSince s_m>=2^M s_r, M>=4, and s_r>1, its subtracted term is greater\nthan 4s_r/(1+s_r)>2. Thus (2) is strictly negative at every stated\nhorizon, not just eventually.\n\n## 4. A finite strict simplex witness, with normalization loss visible\n\nHere is the conversion in full, without invoking an unverified reduction.\nWrite n=L_m; every step of G_m is positive. Let the energy of the actual\nnonlinear run above be -v<0. For ell=0,...,n choose\ntheta_ell=(ell+1)/(2(n+2)), let A_aux=diag(theta_ell), and run\npsi(z)=<z,A_aux z>/2 from z_0=(1,...,1) under the same schedule.\nThe gradients w_i have components theta_ell P_i(theta_ell), where\nP_i(t)=product_(j<i)(1-h_j t). Distinct degrees 0,...,n and n+1\ndistinct theta_ell imply that the w_i are linearly independent. They\nalso imply that the z_i are pairwise distinct. Since 0<A_aux<I,\n\n    Q_ij^aux=-(1/2)<z_i-z_j,A_aux(I-A_aux)(z_i-z_j)><0, i!=j,\n\nin the convention Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLet E_aux be the finite auxiliary energy and put\ndelta=v/[2(v+|E_aux|)]. The actual product function\nf_mix(x,z)=f(x)+delta psi(z/sqrt(delta)), initialized at\n(x_0,sqrt(delta)z_0), has jets\n(x_i,sqrt(delta)z_i), (g_i,sqrt(delta)w_i), f_i+delta psi_i.\nIt remains 1-smooth, its gradient vectors G_i are independent, all its\nfinite Q_ij are strict, and its energy is at most -v/2. Denote its\npoints and values by Z_i,F_i for the remainder of this section.\n\nChoose f_* = min_i(F_i-||G_i||^2/2)-1. Independence makes the following\nlinear equations soluble for z_* in the span of the G_i:\n\n    <G_i,z_*>=f_*-F_i+<G_i,Z_i>-||G_i||^2/2.\n\nSet X_i=Z_i-z_*, V_i=F_i-f_*. Then\nV_i=<G_i,X_i>-||G_i||^2/2 and\n\n    <X_i-G_i,G_j-G_i>=Q_ji<0,  i!=j,\n    <X_i-G_i,-G_i>=-V_i+||G_i||^2/2<=-1.\n\nConsequently G_i is the projection of X_i onto\nC=conv{0,G_0,...,G_n}: the displayed inequalities are the normal\ncondition against EVERY vertex, hence by convex combinations against\nevery point of C. Orthogonally project the X_i to the gradient span;\nthe GD equations, inner products, and all normal inequalities are\nunchanged. That span has dimension n+1 and the listed n+2 vertices\nare affinely independent.\n\nThe function F_C(x)=max_(u in C)(<x,u>-||u||^2/2) is differentiable,\nconvex, and 1-smooth with gradient P_C: the projection variational\ninequalities imply firm nonexpansiveness, and comparison of the two\nmaxima gives its derivative. It has minimum zero at zero and takes\nvalues V_i at X_i. Translation and common value shifts leave the\nintrinsic energy unchanged, so the new energy is at most -v/2.\n\nFinally F_C(X_i)=<G_i,X_i>-||G_i||^2/2 makes every I_i zero, and\nexpansion of the preceding telescope gives\n\n    V_h=||X_0||^2-||X_n+S G_n||^2<0.\n\nThe perturbation and z_* shift need not preserve ||x_0||. Thus the\nlarge normalized objective in (1) belongs to the original source\ninstance; only the negative sign is asserted for the simplex instance.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/c30_nonlinear_failure_of_quadratic_safe_double_ruler.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "related_unverified": ["c58273e20409955e", "846c8ad5e763dfb7", "d1d7cfb25e10bfc5"], "external_refs": [{"key": "YL26", "authors": ["Yuhan Ye", "Kaizhao Liu"], "title": "Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent", "arxiv": "2609.09152", "year": 2026, "cited_for": "Section3.1 and AppendixA.1--A.3, pre-weakening Lemma2.2 final objective formula"}], "status": "No fact_submit allocated; unverified source-based candidate"}, "glossary": {"G_m": "Two consecutive copies of H_m.", "H_m": "Ruler word H_0=(), H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m).", "b_j": "Checkpoint scale a c^j with c=13/5,a=c^(-17).", "s_m": "Sum a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2) of H_m.", "r": "One fixed cutoff level dominating the fixed Ye–Liu constants.", "V_h": "Intrinsic primitive energy defined in the attached complete proof.", "A_YL": "Finite additive checkpoint transfer cost for epsilon1/100 and gamma2099/20000.", "B_YL": "Checkpoint threshold20002.", "chi": "11/10, equal to sqrt(1+2gamma+epsilon^2).", "C": "Simplex containing zero whose projection yields a strictly negative primitive energy; this conversion need not preserve normalized objective error."}}
{"id": "a39f96a2ed9cdb20", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:09:21.154574+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Positive elliptic cross-scale gluing is realizable by ONE fixed2D strongly convex1smooth function on an infinite trajectory: h=1001/1000,b=1001 repeat, A=[[1/2,-4/5],[1/5,1/2]], (I-bA)(I-hA)=R I with R=-40982041/100000. Every query is R^k times a strictly legal two-query core; all remote cross inequalities and the infinite conjugate construction are proved. For every k and every final beta>=0, the same function gives error>=|R|^(2k)/4000000, with an orthogonal vector satisfying every anchor. Quadratics already detect this family; it calibrates the positive gluing mechanism only.", "evidence": "# A fixed two-dimensional elliptic function with infinitely many legal copies\n\nStatus: complete unverified candidate. This is a positive cross-scale gluing\nmechanism and calibration, not the unrestricted exact-profile result. The\nalternating test below is already unstable on quadratics. No previous\nunverified calibration is used as a premise: all core inequalities, cross\ninequalities and the infinite realization are proved here. The accepted\ninterpolation convention and conjugate construction are fact1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 1. Explicit statement\n\nSet\n\n    A=[[1/2,-4/5],[1/5,1/2]],     x0=(1,0),\n    h=1001/1000,    b=1001,    R=-40982041/100000,\n    mu=1/10000.\n\nThere is ONE everywhere-defined convex differentiable function F:R^2->R\nwith1-Lipschitz gradient, strong-convexity modulus mu, F(0)=min F=0, and\nthe following infinite GD trajectory for the fixed alternating steps h,b:\n\n    x_(2k)=R^k x0,       g_(2k)=R^k g0,\n    x_(2k+1)=R^k x1,     g_(2k+1)=R^k g1,\n\nwhere\n\n    g0=(1/2,1/5),\n    x1=x0-hg0=(999/2000,-1001/5000),\n    g1=A x1=(40991/100000,-1/5000).\n\nIts values at these points are R^(2k) times respectively17/50 and1/10.\nThe initial norm is one. For every k>=0 and EVERY replacement final step\nbeta>=0 after the query at time2k+1, this same function satisfies\n\n    2F(x_(2k+1)-beta g_(2k+1))\n                   >= |R|^(2k)/4000000.                  (1)\n\nThe proof also supplies an explicit orthogonal test vector for each such\nterminal query and verifies every maximal-completion anchor inequality.\n\n## 2. A strict finite core, with all perturbation bounds\n\nFirst use h_old=1 and keep f0=17/50,f1=1/10. The old next point and gradient\nare x1_old=(1/2,-1/5), g1_old=(41/100,0). With\nQ_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2, direct calculation gives\n\n    Q_(0,star)=-3/200,       Q_(star,0)=-39/200,\n    Q_(1,star)=-419/20000,   Q_(star,1)=-319/20000,\n    Q_(0,1)=-519/20000,      Q_(1,0)=-219/20000.\n\nHere ||g0||^2=29/100, ||g1_old||^2=1681/10000, and\n||g0-g1_old||^2=481/10000, which verify the displayed residuals directly.\n\nNow set epsilon=1/1000 and h=1+epsilon. We have\n\n    x1=x1_old-epsilon g0,\n    g1=g1_old-epsilon A g0,      A g0=(9/100,1/5).\n\nAll old vectors x1_old,g0,g1_old,A g0,g0-g1_old have norm at most1.\nThus ||x1-x1_old|| and ||g1-g1_old|| are at most epsilon. The two\nresiduals involving only query0 and the optimum are unchanged. Expanding\nthe bilinear and squared-norm terms bounds the absolute changes in the\nother four residuals as follows:\n\n    Q_(star,1):  epsilon+epsilon^2/2 <.001001,\n    Q_(1,star):  3epsilon+3epsilon^2/2 <.003002,\n    Q_(0,1):     2epsilon+epsilon^2/2 <.002001,\n    Q_(1,0):     3epsilon+3epsilon^2/2 <.003002.\n\nThese estimates respectively use the expansions of ||g1||^2/2,\n-<g1,x1>+||g1||^2/2, <g0,x1>+||g0-g1||^2/2, and\n<g1,x0-x1>+||g0-g1||^2/2. Therefore the new core satisfies\n\n    Q_(i,star),Q_(star,i)<=-delta,     delta=14/1000,\n    Q_(0,1),Q_(1,0)<=-eta,            eta=7/1000.          (2)\n\nAlso ||x_i||<=1 and ||g_i||<=1 for i=0,1. For query1 this follows from\n||x1_old||<3/5, ||g1_old||<1/2, and the preceding perturbation bounds.\nWrite y_i=x_i-g_i; then ||y_i||<=2.\n\n## 3. Every cross-copy inequality, in both directions\n\nFor every integer k>=0 prescribe scaled core data\n\n    X_(k,i)=R^k x_i,       G_(k,i)=R^k g_i,\n    V_(k,i)=R^(2k) f_i,       i=0,1.\n\nAdd the optimum (0,0,0). Within one copy, all Q residuals are R^(2k)\ntimes the corresponding core residuals. Now take two distinct copies\nwith absolute scale factors L>s>0; their signs can be either positive or\nnegative. Since |R|>400, we have L>=400s.\n\nDefine the positive optimum margins of any jet by\n\n    delta_i^+=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\n    delta_i^-=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nBoth core margins are at least delta by(2), and scaling multiplies them\nby the squared scale. The exact cross residual for a large jet u and\nsmall jet v is\n\n    Q_uv=-delta_u^- -delta_v^+ +<G_u,X_v-G_v>.\n\nConsequently, regardless of signs and chosen core indices,\n\n    Q_uv<=-delta(L^2+s^2)+2Ls,\n    Q_vu<=-delta(L^2+s^2)+2Ls.                           (3)\n\nNo old/future plane or cross-copy inequality has been dropped.\n\nWe will need strong convexity as well. For any sampled jets define\n\n    G_i'=(G_i-mu X_i)/(1-mu),\n    V_i'=(V_i-mu||X_i||^2/2)/(1-mu).\n\nDirect expansion of the interpolation residual gives the exact identity\n\n    (1-mu)^2 Q_ij'\n       =(1-mu)Q_ij+(mu/2)||(X_i-G_i)-(X_j-G_j)||^2.       (4)\n\nWithin one nonzero copy the right side is at most\n\n    [-eta(1-mu)+8mu] L^2<0,\n\nbecause the difference of the two core y vectors has norm at most4.\nAgainst the optimum it is at most[-delta(1-mu)+2mu] L^2<0. For two\ndistinct copies, ||L y_i-(+/-s)y_j||^2<=8(L^2+s^2), so(3),(4) give\n\n    (1-mu)^2 Q_uv'\n      <=-[delta(1-mu)-4mu](L^2+s^2)+2Ls\n      <=-(27/2000)(L^2+s^2)+2Ls<0.                       (5)\n\nHere delta(1-mu)-4mu=.0135986>27/2000=.0135, and\n2Ls<=L^2/200 because L>=400s. The same estimate holds for Q_vu'.\nThis proves ALL transformed interpolation inequalities for the entire\ncountably infinite set of scaled jets and the optimum.\n\n## 4. One global interpolant for the infinite set\n\nFor each transformed sample put\n\n    y_i'=X_i-G_i',          d_i'=V_i'-||G_i'||^2/2,\n    H_i'(v)=||v||^2/2+<y_i',v>-d_i'.\n\nThe optimum anchor is H_star'(v)=||v||^2/2. The transformed strict optimum\nresiduals proved above imply d_i'>0 on the two unscaled core jets. For the\nkth copy, y_i' scales by R^k and d_i' by R^(2k). Hence, for each fixed v,\n\n    <R^k y_i',v>-R^(2k)d_i' -> -infinity.\n\nThe same convergence is uniform on any bounded set of v. Therefore\n\n    H'(v)=sup_i H_i'(v),       F'(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H'(v))     (6)\n\ndefines an everywhere finite continuous1-strongly convex H'. Locally it\nis the maximum of finitely many anchors. Moreover H'(v)>=||v||^2/2,\nH'(0)=0, and H' is minimized at0.\n\nFor each x the conjugate supremum has a unique maximizer g(x): existence\nfollows from the quadratic lower bound on H', and uniqueness from its\nstrong convexity. The optimality inequalities for two points x,y imply\n<g(x)-g(y),x-y>>=||g(x)-g(y)||^2, hence ||g(x)-g(y)||<=||x-y||.\nThe two supremum inequalities also bound the remainder in\nF'(x+d)-F'(x)-<g(x),d> between0 and\n<g(x+d)-g(x),d>, which is at most||d||^2. Thus F' is differentiable\nwith gradient g and1-Lipschitz gradient. It is convex and has minimum0 at0.\n\nAll interpolation inequalities Q_ij'<=0 say that at v=G_i' the i-th\nanchor attains the supremum in(6). Its gradient there is X_i. Thus\nX_i belongs to the subgradient of H' at G_i'; conjugate equality gives\nF'(X_i)=V_i' and grad F'(X_i)=G_i'. This checks every sample, including\nthe infinite collection. Finally set\n\n    F(x)=(1-mu)F'(x)+mu||x||^2/2.                        (7)\n\nIt interpolates all ORIGINAL scaled jets, is mu-strongly convex, and has\ngradient Lipschitz constant at most(1-mu)+mu=1. It is one fixed function,\nindependent of the horizon at which the trajectory is inspected.\n\n## 5. The actual alternating trajectory\n\nThe characteristic identity for A is\n\n    A^2-A+(41/100)I=0.\n\nSince h+b=hb, we obtain the exact matrix identity\n\n    (I-bA)(I-hA)=[1-(41/100)hb]I=R I.                  (8)\n\nIndeed hb=1002001/1000 and 1-(41/100)hb=-40982041/100000. Starting\nat x0, the h step reaches x1 and the b step reaches R x0. Repetition\nof(8) proves the full trajectory in Section1. Equation(7) is the SAME\nfunction at all these query times; no oracle or function is reset.\n\n## 6. Uniform terminal payoff and all anchors\n\nThe complex representation A=T B T^(-1), with T=diag(1,1/2) and\nz=1/2+(2/5)i, gives\n\n    |det(x1,g1)|=(1/5)|1-hz|^2.\n\nBoth |Re(1-hz)|=999/2000 and |Im(1-hz)|=1001/2500 exceed2/5.\nThus |1-hz|^2>8/25 and |det(x1,g1)|>8/125>1/20. Because ||g1||<=1,\nthe distance from x1 to span(g1) is greater than1/20.\n\nLet x=R^k x1, g=R^k g1 and let z_perp be the orthogonal projection of\nx onto g-perp. Then ||z_perp||^2>|R|^(2k)/400. Strong convexity of(7)\nand its minimizer0 imply, for every real beta,\n\n    2F(x-beta g)>=mu||x-beta g||^2\n                  >=mu||z_perp||^2>|R|^(2k)/4000000,\n\nwhich proves(1). In particular the final gradient is not forced to follow\nthe linear operator beyond this last query.\n\nFor the finite prefix through this query, define the ordinary conjugate\nanchors H_j(v)=||v||^2/2+<x_j-g_j,v>-(f_j-||g_j||^2/2). Since F is\nan actual1-smooth interpolant, H_j<=F* for every query and optimum anchor,\nas in accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Strong convexity gives\nF*(w)<=||w||^2/(2mu). With w=mu z_perp we therefore have <w,g>=0 and\n\n    2(f_j-||g_j||^2/2)+2<x-(x_j-g_j),w>-||w||^2\n       =2<x,w>-2H_j(w)>=mu||z_perp||^2\n\nfor EVERY anchor. Hence the same lower bound is visible in the requested\northogonal-terminal-vector formulation as well as in the fixed function.\n\n## 7. What this proves and what it does not\n\nThe proof gives a concrete way to glue an infinite family of legal scaled\ncopies while retaining every crossing constraint and a common strong\nconvexity modulus. Strict optimum margins pay for the cross terms when\nradial scale ratios are large. This is a sufficient condition with an actual\nfixed realization; it is not lossless gluing of arbitrary boundary jets.\n\nThe alternating schedule already has huge quadratic error, for example\nits pair multiplier at scalar curvature1/2 is\n(1-h/2)(1-b/2)=-(999/2000)(999/2), whose magnitude exceeds one.\nTherefore this example does not establish a new family where the quadratic\nchannel misses the benchmark. Its role is to make the positive elliptic\ncopy mechanism concrete. For the geometric ruler family at a dyadic\nresonance, the still-missing step is a legal expanding finite core and\nquantitative control of its rotated copies.\n\nMatlas returned unrelated statements in arXiv:1812.04874 and2109.09377.\nNeither is imported. {"id": "295385c862b91a07", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:19:49.260131+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Quantitative obstruction on the ASSIGNED near-critical ruler: the ENTIRE elliptic parabolic regime z_m=1-u_m/m+i v_m/sqrt(m), with u_m bounded and v_m in any fixed positive compact interval, has superexponentially small free-terminal payoff for every horizon-dependent SPD metric. Full legality forces u_m>=[1+sqrt2/(3-sqrt2)]v_m²-o(1), and then every final b in[0,2], including the original d0, gives error <=(2304/v_-²)m exp[-2^(m+1)/(3m)]=o(U_(2^m-1)^ZJ). Finite-horizon averaging includes exact dyadic resonances; no universal isotropization is assumed.", "evidence": "# Near-critical ruler: a quantitative parabolic elliptic obstruction\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or new\nfact_submit is used. Accepted facts1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d\nsupply the interpolation convention and the benchmark definition/bound.\nEvery new phase, averaging, polynomial and conditioning estimate is proved\nbelow. In particular the earlier isotropization counterexample and the\nearlier elliptic interface are not invoked as unverified lemmas.\n\n## 1. The actual assigned schedule and the entire parameter regime\n\nFor each integer m>=128 put\n\n    s=1+sqrt(2),      c=c_m=s(1+1/m),\n    a=a_m=(1-1/m)/c,  d_j=1+a c^j,\n    H_0=(),          H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1.\n\nAll parameters remain fixed as j varies within this horizon. Let h_pre be\nH_m with its last d_0 removed, so it has n-1 steps. All n queried prefix\npoints, including the last point x_(n-1), will be retained.\n\nFix arbitrary finite constants U>0 and 0<v_-<=v_+. For each m allow ANY\nreal u_m and v_m with |u_m|<=U and v_-<=v_m<=v_+, and set\n\n    alpha_m=1-u_m/m,     beta_m=v_m/sqrt(m),\n    z_m=alpha_m+i beta_m.\n\nChoose ANY real invertible2x2 matrix T_m with ||T_m e1||=1. Its norm and\ncondition number may depend arbitrarily on m. At the actual prefix query\nindices use the elliptic polynomial data\n\n    x_i=T_m(Re P_i(z_m),Im P_i(z_m)),\n    g_i=T_m(Re[z_m P_i(z_m)],Im[z_m P_i(z_m)]),\n    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_pre,t z).\n\nAssume that SOME values f_i make these same real jets and(0,0,0) fully\ninterpolable by a convex differentiable1-smooth function. For every such\ninterpolant F, with F(0)=min F=0, and every final step b in[0,2], we prove\nthe uniform estimate, for all sufficiently large m,\n\n    2F(x_(n-1)-b g_(n-1))\n      <= (2304/v_-^2)m exp[-2^(m+1)/(3m)].                (1)\n\nThe threshold in m depends only on U,v_-,v_+. Thus even with arbitrary\nhorizon-dependent metrics, values and free terminal completion, this\nENTIRE compact parabolic regime has payoff o(U_n^ZJ). The interval[0,2]\nincludes both the common comparison step1 and the original final d_0.\n\nA necessary condition for legality, uniformly over the same regime, is\n\n    u_m >= (1+A0)v_m^2-o(1),\n    A0=sqrt(2)/(3-sqrt(2)).                              (2)\n\nThis is not an exclusion of all elliptic frequencies, an upper bound on\nunrestricted W, or a resolution of the original exact-profile problem.\n\n## 2. Complete-cycle averaging over actual early copies\n\nFor this proof abbreviate alpha=alpha_m, beta=beta_m, z=z_m and\nM=T_m^T T_m. Then M is positive definite and M_11=1. Multiplication by z\nhas real matrix B=[[alpha,-beta],[beta,alpha]]. Define the full partial\nword polynomials and copy multipliers\n\n    p_j(z)=product_(ell<j)(1-d_ell z)^(2^(j-ell-1)),\n    R_j(z)=p_j(z)(1-d_j z).\n\nEvery query position in H_j, in complex coordinates, is a product of a\nsubset of R_0,...,R_(j-1). Indeed the right copy in H_(j+1) multiplies\nevery old position by R_j, whereas the left copy leaves it unchanged.\n\nThe entire first H_(m-1) is contained in h_pre. It contains2^(m-2)\ncopies of the two-query word H_1=(d_0), with starting multipliers equal\nto all subset products of R_1,...,R_(m-2). All these multipliers are\nnonzero because z is nonreal and all d_j are real.\n\nConsider the directed triangle star -> next query -> first query -> star\nin any one of these copies. Sum the full interpolation inequalities on\nthat triangle, eliminating the values. Divide by the squared modulus of\nits starting multiplier. Its legality becomes the same base triangle\ntested with the rotated metric R_theta^T M R_theta, where theta is the\nargument of the starting multiplier and R_theta is planar rotation.\nAveraging all these ACTUAL cycle inequalities therefore gives legality\nof the base triangle under one effective metric M_bar.\n\nPut tau=tr(M)/2. Rotation averaging preserves its scalar part tau I. On\nthe traceless part, each binary copy average multiplies its norm by\n|cos(arg R_j)|. To verify this directly, identify a traceless symmetric\nmatrix[[q,r],[r,-q]] with q+i r. Rotation conjugation multiplies it by\nexp(+/-2i theta), and averaging identity and this rotation multiplies\nits modulus by |(1+exp(2i theta))/2|=|cos theta|. Consequently\n\n    M_bar=tau I+E,\n    ||E||_op<=tau Gamma_m,\n    Gamma_m=product_(j=1)^(m-2)|cos(arg R_j(z))|.          (3)\n\nThe bound on the initial traceless part uses only positive definiteness:\nits eigenvalues have magnitude at most tau. It is uniform in conditioning.\n\n## 3. A finite-horizon averaging estimate that includes resonances\n\nFor m>=128, the inequalities12/5<s<17/7 give\nc<=2193/896<5/2 and a>=(127/128)/(5/2)=127/320>3/8;\nalso c>12/5. The bounds on s follow by squaring7/5 and10/7 to bound\nsqrt(2). For all sufficiently large m, uniformly in our parameter range, alpha>=3/4,\nalpha<=2, beta<=1/8, and |1-alpha|<=a/4. Thus\n\n    alpha d_j-1=alpha a c^j-(1-alpha)>=a c^j/2>0.\n\nChoose the principal factor phases\n\n    phi_j=arg(1-d_j z)\n          =-pi+arctan[beta d_j/(alpha d_j-1)],\n    Phi=sum_(j>=0)2^(-j-1)phi_j=-pi+delta.\n\nThe series converges by the bounded phases and summable weights. Since\nalpha<=2, alpha>=3/4 and a>=3/8, the arctangent arguments are bounded\nbetween beta/2 and8beta. For sufficiently small beta, arctan t is between\nt/2 and t for this range. Therefore\n\n    beta/4<=delta<=8beta.                                (4)\n\nAs j grows, phi_j decreases to phi_infinity=-pi+arctan(beta/alpha).\nThe difference of its two arctangent arguments is exactly\n\n    beta[d_j/(alpha d_j-1)-1/alpha]\n      =beta/[alpha(alpha d_j-1)]<=8beta c^(-j).\n\nSince arctan is1-Lipschitz,\n0<=phi_j-phi_infinity<=8beta c^(-j). Direct summation of polynomial\nmultiplicities gives an unwrapped phase identity\n\n    arg R_j=2^j Phi+epsilon_j,\n    epsilon_j=phi_j-sum_(ell>=0)2^(-ell-1)phi_(j+ell),\n    0<=epsilon_j<=8beta c^(-j).                          (5)\n\nChoose k so that2^k delta lies in[pi/4,pi/2). For large m this integer\nsatisfies1<=k<=m-2 and2^k<=8/beta, by(4). Discarding earlier cosine\nfactors only increases the product. Because2^j Phi differs from2^j delta\nby an integer multiple of pi, the1-Lipschitz property of |cos| and a\ntelescoping difference of products of numbers in[0,1] give\n\n    Gamma_m\n      <=product_(j=k)^(m-2)|cos(2^j delta)|\n                          +sum_(j=k)^(m-2)epsilon_j\n      <=[1/(2^(m-1-k)|sin(2^k delta)|)]\n                          +8beta c^(-k)/(1-c^(-1)).      (6)\n\nThe product identity in the last line follows by iterating\nsin(2t)=2sin(t)cos(t); its denominator is nonzero because\n2^k delta is in[pi/4,pi/2). This calculation works unchanged at exact\ndyadic resonance, where the numerator may vanish.\n\nLet r0=log_2(12/5)>0. Since2^(-k)<=4delta/pi<=11beta, c<=5/2 and\nc>=12/5, the bounds above imply, for an absolute constant C,\n\n    Gamma_m<=C[2^(-m)/beta+beta^(1+r0)].                  (7)\n\nFor example c^(-k)=(2^(-k))^(log_2 c)<=C beta^r0, since beta<1 and\nlog_2 c lies in a fixed compact interval. Because beta is bounded above\nand below by positive constants times m^(-1/2), (7) yields\n\n    Gamma_m/beta ->0                                    (8)\n\nuniformly in all the stated parameters. This is a FINITE-HORIZON bound\nin this specific regime, not the false assertion that every fixed nonreal\nparameter has a vanishing infinite tail product.\n\n## 4. The averaged triangle forces the parabolic legality constant\n\nWrite d=d_0, e1=(1,0), v0=B e1, r1=e1-d v0 and v1=B r1. The base\ntriangle weight under a metric M is tr(M Z), with the symmetric matrix\n\n    Z=(v1 v1^T)/2+d sym(v0 v1^T)\n          +(v0-v1)(v0-v1)^T/2-sym(e1 v0^T)+(v0 v0^T)/2,\n    sym(C)=(C+C^T)/2.                                  (9)\n\nThis follows by writing its three edge weights: ||v1||_M^2/2,\nd<v1,v0>_M+||v0-v1||_M^2/2, and-<v0,e1>_M+||v0||_M^2/2.\nFor M=I, direct expansion gives\n\n    tr(Z)=d beta^2-(alpha-|z|^2)(1-d alpha+d^2|z|^2).    (10)\n\nWe also need an error bound uniform in M. At z=1, Z=0 for every d.\nWhen |z|<=2 and1<=d<=2, put w=z-1. Then\n||v0-e1||<=|w|, ||r1-(1-d)e1||<=2|w|, and\n||v1-(1-d)e1||<=7|w|; also||v0||<=2, ||v1||<=10.\nApplying ||aa^T-bb^T||_op<=(||a||+||b||)||a-b|| to(9), and expanding\nits mixed product, bounds its five differences from z=1 by respectively\n38.5|w|,34|w|,56|w|,|w| and1.5|w|. Thus\n\n    ||Z||_op<=132|w|<=264beta                            (11)\n\nfor all sufficiently large m, since |alpha-1|=O(m^(-1))=o(beta).\n\nThe averaged legal triangle from Section2 satisfies tr(M_bar Z)<=0.\nUsing(3),(11), and |tr(E Z)|<=2||E||_op||Z||_op, gives\n\n    d beta^2-(alpha-|z|^2)(1-d alpha+d^2|z|^2)\n                  <=528 Gamma_m beta=o(beta^2).         (12)\n\nHere tau>0 cancels; no condition-number loss occurs. Now d tends to\nsqrt(2), alpha tends to1, and |z|^2 tends to1, so the denominator\n1-d alpha+d^2|z|^2 tends to3-sqrt(2)>0. Moreover\n\n    alpha-|z|^2=(u_m-v_m^2)/m-u_m^2/m^2.\n\nDivide(12) by beta^2=v_m^2/m. This proves precisely the uniform\nnecessary condition(2).\n\n## 5. The full polynomial potential, with a uniform remainder\n\nPut w=z-1, t_j=d_j/(d_j-1)=1+1/(a c^j), and N=2^m-1. For m>=128,\n1<=t_j<4. Factor each complex magnitude exactly as\n\n    |1-d_j z|=a c^j |1+t_j w|.\n\nThe level multiplicities satisfy sum_j2^(m-j-1)=N and\nsum_j j2^(m-j-1)=N-m. Define\n\n    L_m(w)=log(ac)+Re sum_(j>=0)2^(-j-1)log(1+t_j w),\n\nwhere the logarithms use their convergent power series near1. For\n|w|<=1/8, their cubic remainders are at most128|w|^3: bound the terms\nof degree>=3 by2|t_j w|^3. Summing the linear and quadratic terms yields\n\n    L_m(w)=log(1-1/m)+D_m Re(w)-(J_m/2)Re(w^2)+O(|w|^3),\n    D_m=1+1/[a(2-c^(-1))],\n    J_m=1+2/[a(2-c^(-1))]+1/[a^2(2-c^(-2))].            (13)\n\nAll remainder constants are uniform over the compact parameter regime.\nBecause w=-u_m/m+i v_m/sqrt(m),\n\n    L_m(w)=[-1-u_m D_m+(v_m^2/2)J_m]/m+O(m^(-3/2)).     (14)\n\nAt the limits c=s, a=1/s, use s^2=2s+1 to obtain\n\n    D=lim D_m=2+2/(2s-1)>5/2,\n    J=lim J_m=6+4/(2s-1)+2/(4s+1)<15/2.                (15)\n\nFor the inequalities,12/5<s<5/2 implies2s-1<4 and\n4/(2s-1)+2/(4s+1)<20/19+10/53<3/2. Also\n\n    1+A0=3/(4-s)>15/8.\n\nBy(2),(15), the coefficient in(14) is at most\n\n    -1-[(1+A0)D-J/2]v_m^2+o(1)\n          <=-1-(15/16)v_m^2+o(1).\n\nIn particular legality forces L_m(w)<=-1/(2m) for sufficiently large m.\n\nThe exact finite product differs from its infinite weighted sum only by\na controlled tail:\n\n    log|p_m(z)|\n      =2^m L_m(w)-log(ac)-m log c\n             -2^m Re sum_(j>=m)2^(-j-1)log(1+t_j w).\n\nThe absolute value of the last term is at most8|w|, since\n|log(1+t_j w)|<=2t_j|w|<=8|w|. Therefore, uniformly over all legal\nparameters under consideration,\n\n    log|p_m(z)|<=-2^m/(3m)                              (16)\n\nfor all sufficiently large m. This includes zeros without a division;\nin fact nonreal z makes every factor nonzero here.\n\n## 6. Conditioning, the deleted last step, and free completion\n\nThe two initial optimum interpolation inequalities give\n<x0,g0>>=||g0||^2. Since ||x0||=1, this implies ||g0||<=1. But\n\n    g0=alpha T_m e1+beta T_m e2,\n\nso ||T_m e2||<=(1+|alpha|)/beta<=3/beta. Hence\n\n    ||T_m||_op<=1+3/beta<=4/beta.                       (17)\n\nThis bounds the actual two-vector realization despite arbitrary initial\nconditioning. Let p_pre(z)=p_m(z)/(1-d_0 z), the polynomial of the\nactual queried prefix. From Section3,\n|1-d_0 z|>=alpha d_0-1>=a/2>=3/16, so\n\n    |p_pre(z)|<=6|p_m(z)|,\n    ||x_(n-1)||<=4|p_pre(z)|/beta,\n    ||g_(n-1)||<=8|p_pre(z)|/beta.                      (18)\n\nNo condition at a later gradient is used. For any convex1-smooth\ninterpolant of these queried data, optimum interpolation gives\nf_(n-1)<=<x_(n-1),g_(n-1)>-||g_(n-1)||^2/2. Smoothness along a final\nstep b in[0,2] gives F(x-bg)<=f_(n-1)-b(1-b/2)||g||^2<=f_(n-1).\nEquations(16)--(18) now imply\n\n    2F(x-bg)<=2||x||||g||\n       <=2304|p_m(z)|^2/beta^2\n       <=(2304/v_-^2)m exp[-2^(m+1)/(3m)],\n\nproving(1) for EVERY completion and every final b in the stated interval.\n\n## 7. Exact benchmark comparison and remaining scope\n\nAcceptedb98bb807d514e80d gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3). Since n<2^m,\nthe ratio of(1) to U_n^ZJ=1/B_n is at most\n\n    (2304/v_-^2)m(1+4*3^m) exp[-2^(m+1)/(3m)] ->0.\n\nThe exponential dominates because2^m/m grows faster than any constant\nmultiple of m, which follows for example by successive doubling after\na fixed sufficiently large index. This uses the actual accepted B_n\nbound, not a guessed dyadic benchmark constant.\n\nThe obstruction covers all horizon-dependent elliptic metrics in the\nentire compact regime alpha=1-O(1/m), beta comparable to m^(-1/2).\nIt specifically closes the natural near-unit polynomial-growth attempt:\nthe same cycles that allow a free terminal completion force damping of\nthe chronological polynomial before that completion. Other imaginary\nscales, other real parts, multiple modes and changing generators are not\ncovered. The arbitrary-schedule exact-profile target is unchanged.\n\nMatlas retrieval returned unrelated statements in arXiv:2002.05991 and\n2009.11657; none is used. All calculations are symbolic, with no numerical\nsearch, isotropization assumption, or additional verifier allocation.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max2_near_critical_parabolic_elliptic", "status": "Complete unverified quantitative candidate", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/near_critical_parabolic_elliptic_obstruction.md", "guidance": "0150cd2519656ebc", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "related_findings": ["102610bc6e0e464e", "4a6bed048e4235d9", "892ca165436ef59c"], "scope": "All metrics/values/completions for the compact parabolic unit-eigenvalue regime on the exact near-critical family, with the original last step deleted before completion. Not all elliptic frequencies or unrestricted W.", "new_mechanism": "Average one actual triangle over copies inside the retained first half. Gamma<=C(2^-m/beta+beta^(1+log_2(12/5)))=o(beta), so its anisotropic contribution is o(beta²), independently of conditioning.", "verification_allocation": "No new fact_submit or numerical computation."}, "glossary": {"s": "Silver ratio1+sqrt(2).", "c_m": "s(1+1/m), fixed within each horizon.", "a_m": "(1-1/m)/c_m, fixed within each horizon.", "d_j": "Level step1+a_m c_m^j.", "H_j": "Ordered ruler with H_0 empty and H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).", "u_m": "Bounded real parameter of alpha_m=1-u_m/m.", "v_m": "Positive parameter in a fixed compact interval, with beta_m=v_m/sqrt(m).", "T_m": "Arbitrary real invertible2x2 elliptic realization with ||T_m e1||=1.", "M": "Common metric T_m^T T_m.", "Gamma_m": "Exact traceless metric attenuation over copies of H_1 inside the first H_(m-1).", "A0": "Triangle legality constant sqrt(2)/(3-sqrt(2)).", "L_m": "Weighted full polynomial logarithmic potential defined in the proof."}}

Retain the old jet (x_j,g_j,f_j)=(y+q,q,||q||²/2), and suppose the next checkpoint places x_tail=y+epsilon d. For physical tail steps t_l>=0 and gradients g_l, let T=sum t_l,sigma=2T+1,g=g_end,Delta=sum t_l(g-g_l). The terminal location is x_end=y+epsilon d-sum t_l g_l. The outgoing inequality Q_(end,j)<=0 expands exactly to 2f_end<=2epsilon<d,g>-sigma||g||²+2<g,Delta>. Completing a square gives 2f_end<=epsilon²||d||²/sigma+2<g,Delta>-sigma||g-epsilon d/sigma||². Thus if every <g,g_l>>=||g||², the drift term is nonpositive. In particular g_l=c_l g,c_l>=1 gives nonpositive drift; g_l=g+w_l,<w_l,g>=0 gives zero drift. These are two different direct attempts that cannot increase the constant-tail upper budget. On the assigned ruler, the same JOINT package research/max_near_critical_ruler_audit.md derives the complete first-term bound: ri*rj/(p*x)<=qpair=c(c-2)²<1/2 in both ruler orders; with t=1+tailmass, total M=p+x+t+ri+rj and ri<=(c-2)p,rj<=(c-2)t, this gives R<=min(qpair,Dpair*t/M), Dpair=2(c-1)qpair+(c-2)². Maximizing min(qpair²,Dpair²*t²/M²)/(2t-1) gives qpair*Dpair/(2M-Dpair/qpair) <=(5949/6088)/(2M-1) for M>=8, by qpair*Dpair<=661/800 and Dpair/qpair<=199/60. Hence a successful value >=1/B needs drift>=1/B-gamma/(2M-1), plus the exact square loss. {"id": "dc4e251619c30c58", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:27:25.900957+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C32's necessary asymmetric box transfers to the actual E_theta cone, because every witness used in its derivation has every incoming-terminal residual exactly zero. This is a domain-transfer candidate, not a cone-separation result; the bounded probe still has zero new evaluations.", "evidence": "Use the definitions and complete witness checks in the appended C32 text. For EVERY endpoint interval-projection witness, full-curvature quadratic, middle-cut interval, one-sided Huber witness in its stated domain, and orthogonal mixed witness, the assigned values obey f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2 and the final query obeys x_3=g_3. Expanding Q_(i,3)=<g_i-g_3,x_3-g_3> proves Q_(i,3)=0 for every i<3. The orthogonal sum preserves both displayed identities, and its sole illegal auxiliary edge is explicitly canceled in C32. Thus E_theta=V_h on EACH of these witnesses for every real theta, so the same cut inequalities and F_Q,F_B,F_C,F_N conditions are necessary for E_theta validity or for a full nonnegative certificate (evaluation of its PSD identity suffices; no certificate-validity equivalence is assumed). All the subsequent scalar deductions in Sections3-4 use only those necessary inequalities, so they apply unchanged. This reproduces the mathematical ingredients below rather than adopting an unverified finding as a fact.\n\n# A necessary asymmetric region for a three-step capacity improvement\n\nStatus: complete candidate reductions and a scoped optimization result;\nNOT verifier-accepted. No solver or fact_submit. The public max4 candidates\n5b4359baa88bf3e4 and 500d209094028aef suggested the witnesses; all needed\nlegal data and deductions are included here, not used as unverified facts.\nAccepted input: smooth-convex interpolation in 1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 1. Statement\n\nFor h=(u,v,w) in [0,infinity)^3 put T=1+u+v+w. Write V_h for the\nintrinsic primitive energy defined below. Call h primitive if V_h>=0\non every finite-dimensional convex differentiable 1-smooth GD run with\na minimizer. Put t=sqrt(2), lambda=1+t, and T_0=lambda^2=3+2t.\n\nIf h is primitive and T>=T_0, then either\n\n    h=(t,2,t), T=T_0,\n\nor necessarily\n\n    t<u<7/4,   1<v<2,   1<w<t,\n    T_0<=T<7,  u+w>=2T/lambda-2.                   (1)\n\nThe scalar constraints below further restrict the remaining region.\nThis does NOT exclude region (1) and does NOT prove the global\nthree-step capacity bound. It is a global, orientation-sensitive\nreduction beyond the existing local and fixed-middle-step results.\n\nFor the remaining region, the one-parameter completion of the mixed\ntwo-dimensional witness in Section5 cannot strengthen its original\nendpoint choice. Every finite interpolation inequality is checked.\n\n## 2. Actual witnesses and all their legality conditions\n\nFor any legal run use\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<3)h_i(f_i-f_3)\n         +sum_(i<3)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n         -(T-1)T||g_3||^2.\n\nFor data with f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2 and x_3=g_3, set\ny_i=x_i-g_i. Expansion gives\n\n    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>, Q_(i,3)=0,\n    V_h=||x_0||^2-T^2||g_3||^2.                   (2)\n\nAll witnesses used below have these properties and include the legal\noptimum jet (0,0,0). Besides the finite Q conditions, optimum legality\nrequires <g_i,y_i>>=0; the other direction is saturated by the value\nformula. A feasible finite array is realized by the conjugate maximum\nof quadratics in accepted 1252b8823bb5a290. Thus the arrays specify\nfixed globally smooth convex functions with a minimizer.\n\nThe interval-projection witness at any selected step b, with ordinary\nsums P,Q, has gradient 1 before b and -(b-1)/(Q+1) after it, starting\nat P+1 and ending at its negative threshold, when b>1. Formula (2)\ngives the necessary cut condition\n\n    T(b-1)_+<=(P+1)(Q+1).                         (3)\n\nThis is a direct fixed clipped-derivative construction, including zero\nordinary steps. Apply it at the first and last step to obtain\n\n    u,w<=M(T):=2T/(T+1)<2.                        (4)\n\nFor u,v,w>1 abbreviate\n\n    a=u-1, A=u+1, r=v-1, D=w+1, eta=r/D;\n    T=A+D+r.\n\nThe full-curvature quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, gives\n\n    F_Q=1-T^2 a^2 r^2(w-1)^2>=0.                  (5)\n\nThe middle-cut interval, with x=(A,1,-r,-eta) and\ng=(1,1,-eta,-eta), gives\n\n    F_B=A^2-T^2 eta^2>=0, or Tr<=AD.              (6)\n\nIf r(w-1)<=1, the one-sided Huber derivative min(x,1), started at A,\nhas x=(A,1,-r,r(w-1)) and g=(1,1,-r,r(w-1)). It gives\n\n    F_C=A^2-T^2 r^2(w-1)^2>=0.                   (7)\n\nWhen eta a<1, a two-dimensional mixed witness gives\n\n    F_N=a u F_B+(1+eta)u^2(2-u^2)>=0.             (8)\n\nHere is its complete verification. Combine the middle-cut base array\nfrom (6) with the auxiliary scalar array\n\n    x_aux=(1,-a,ra,eta a),\n    g_aux=(1,-a,eta a,eta a),\n    f_aux,i=x_aux,i*g_aux,i-g_aux,i^2/2.\n\nThe auxiliary proximal points are (0,0,w eta a,0). Its only positive\nfinite residual is Q_aux,(0,2)=(1-eta a)w eta a. Every optimum\ninequality is legal because g_aux,i*y_aux,i>=0. The base proximal\npoints are (u,0,-w eta,0); its Q_(0,2)=-(1+eta)w eta and all its\nresiduals are nonpositive. With\n\n    epsilon=(1+eta)/[a(1-eta a)],\n\ntake the orthogonal sum of the base and sqrt(epsilon) times the\nauxiliary points and gradients, with the summed values. Residuals add;\nthe sole positive auxiliary edge is exactly canceled and every other\nedge is nonpositive. The energy equals\n\n    F_B+epsilon(1-T^2 eta^2 a^2)\n       =F_N/[a(1-eta a)].                         (9)\n\nFor the algebra use 1-a^2 A^2=u^2(2-u^2). This proves (8) with\nall actual edges, rather than an assumed two-dimensional interpolant.\n\n## 3. A uniform box where all four witnesses apply\n\nAssume T>=T_0 and all the cut conditions (3). First v>1. Otherwise\nT=1+u+v+w<=2+2M(T), so T^2-5T-2<=0. At T=T_0 this polynomial\nequals 2t>0 and it increases thereafter, a contradiction.\n\nThus r>0 and the middle cut (6) applies. As T=A+D+r,\n\n    T(T-A-D)<=AD<=(A+D)^2/4,\n    T<=lambda(A+D)/2<=lambda(M(T)+1).\n\nConsequently\n\n    T^2+(1-3lambda)T-lambda<=0.                    (10)\n\nIts left side increases for T>=T_0, and at T=7 equals\n56-22lambda>0 because lambda<5/2. Hence T<7 and (4) gives\nu,w<7/4.\n\nBoth u and w exceed1. If, for example, u<=1, then A<=2, D<11/4,\nso r=T-A-D>T-19/4. But\n\n    Tr>T(T-19/4)>=T_0(T_0-19/4)\n       =11/4+5t/2>11/2>AD,\n\ncontradicting the middle cut. The argument for w is identical.\n\nOn this entire necessary box the extra hypotheses of (7) and (8) hold:\n\n    r(w-1)<=A(w^2-1)/T<363/(64T_0)<1,\n    eta a<=Aa/T=(u^2-1)/T<33/(16T_0)<1.           (11)\n\nFor the first strict numerical comparison, 363<64T_0 is equivalent\nto 171<128sqrt(2), and squaring gives 29241<32768. Thus no local\nneighborhood restriction is being silently used in this global screen.\nThe earlier inequality T<=lambda(A+D)/2 also gives\n\n    u+w>=2T/lambda-2>=2t.                         (12)\n\n## 4. Excluding three of the four endpoint orientations\n\nSuppose u<=t. By (12), w>=t. Since T>=T_0 and w<2, the difference\nbetween the two sides of the necessary inequality Tr(w-1)<=A is\nat least\n\n    T_0(T_0-t-w-2)(w-1)-(t+1)\n       =T_0(w-t)(2-w)>=0.                         (13)\n\nIt is strictly positive unless u=w=t and T=T_0. In that exceptional\ncase v=2. Hence every other candidate must have u>t.\n\nSuppose next that also w>=t. On the rectangle [t,M(T)]^2 consider\n\n    q_T(u,w)=T(u-1)(w-1)(T-u-w-2).\n\nThis function is strictly concave in either endpoint with the other\nfixed. Its minimum is attained at a corner. At T=T_0, M(T_0)=1+t/2,\nthe corner values are\n\n    q_(T_0)(t,t)=1,\n    q_(T_0)(t,M(T_0))=q_(T_0)(M(T_0),t)=lambda/2>1,\n    q_(T_0)(M(T_0),M(T_0))=lambda/2>1.\n\nEach corner value strictly increases with T>=T_0. All its positive\nfactors increase; in particular the derivatives of\nT-t-M(T)-2 and T-2M(T)-2 are respectively\n1-2/(T+1)^2 and 1-4/(T+1)^2, both positive.\nThus q_T>1 except at the single point T=T_0,u=w=t. This contradicts\nthe quadratic condition (5). Every nonexceptional candidate therefore\nsatisfies u>t>w.\n\nFinally its middle step is less than2. For u>t the expression F_N in\n(8), with u,w held fixed, strictly decreases as r>0 increases: the\nnegative squared term in F_B decreases, while u^2(2-u^2)<0.\nAt r=1 a direct expansion gives\n\n    D^2 F_N/u\n       =P_u(D):=u^2 D^2+(4-2u^2-u^3)D-(u-1)(u+2)^2.\n\nThis polynomial has one positive and one negative root, since its\nleading coefficient is positive and its constant term negative. Also\n\n    P_u(lambda)=-(lambda+1)u^3-2u^2+4(lambda+1)<0\n\nfor u>t: the right side vanishes at t and has strictly negative\nderivative for u>0. Its positive root is therefore beyond lambda,\nand P_u(D)<0 for 0<D<=lambda. Since w<t gives D<lambda, F_N<0\nat r=1 and at every r>=1. Necessarily r<1, proving (1).\n\nThe remaining constraints can be written without a mixed-energy\nabbreviation. In the box (1), a primitive candidate must satisfy\n\n    Tr<=AD,\n    T a r(w-1)<=1,\n    u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta-(u-1)T^2 eta^2>=0.        (14)\n\nThe last expression is F_N/u after expansion. No sufficiency of (14)\nis asserted, and no global feasibility or infeasibility conclusion for\nthis remaining box is claimed.\n\n## 5. Optimizing a continuous auxiliary completion does not improve F_N\n\nWork in box (1) and assume the scalar tests F_B,F_Q>=0. Keep the same\nbase array in (6), but vary the auxiliary third gradient. Put k=ra<1\nand choose z in [k/(w+1),k]. Use\n\n    x_aux=(1,-a,k,k-wz),\n    g_aux=(1,-a,z,k-wz),\n    f_aux,i=x_aux,i*g_aux,i-g_aux,i^2/2.\n\nEvery GD equation is exact. The sole possibly nonzero proximal point\nis y_aux,2=k-z>=0. The residuals entering query2 are\n\n    Q_aux,(0,2)=(1-z)(k-z),\n    Q_aux,(1,2)=(-a-z)(k-z)<=0,\n    Q_aux,(3,2)=(k-(w+1)z)(k-z)<=0.\n\nEvery other finite residual is zero, and every optimum constraint is\nvalid. For z<k the complete legal range of mixing coefficients is\n\n    0<=epsilon<=epsilon_max(z)\n       =(1+eta)w eta/[(1-z)(k-z)].                 (15)\n\nThe same active edge is exactly canceled at the upper endpoint.\nThe mixed energy is\n\n    F_B+epsilon[1-T^2(k-wz)^2].                    (16)\n\nLet q=k-wz, A_0=w-k>0, B_0=k(w-1)>0. The quadratic test says\nT B_0<=1. Since q ranges from q_0=k/(w+1) down to -B_0, the bracket\nin (16) can be negative only when q>1/T. In that case epsilon_max\nis optimal. Apart from its positive constant factor, the improvement\nover F_B is\n\n    R(q)=(T^2 q^2-1)/[(q+A_0)(q+B_0)].\n\nFor q>1/T its derivative has numerator\n\n    T^2(A_0+B_0)q^2+2(T^2 A_0B_0+1)q+(A_0+B_0)>0.\n\nThe greatest improvement is therefore at the largest q=q_0, namely\nz=k/(w+1)=eta a. This is exactly the auxiliary choice in (8)--(9).\nIf q_0<=1/T there is no negative auxiliary energy anywhere in the\nfamily. At z=k all auxiliary proximal points are zero and its energy\nis F_Q>=0, so this boundary supplies no additional improvement.\n\nThus the original mixed witness is already optimal in this whole\none-parameter completion family, with arbitrary admissible mixture\nweight. Repairing the remaining region (1) requires another change of\ngeometry or a separate algebraic argument, rather than this retuning.\n\n## 6. Reversal caution\n\nThe downloaded and read Grimmer--Shu--Wang paper arXiv:2410.16249v2,\nSection3.1, and Zhang--Jiang arXiv:2410.12395, Proposition5.5, establish\nreversal properties for their recursively constructed basic schedules.\nThe checked statements do not supply reversal invariance for every\nprimitive schedule. The new legality bound is1-Re(z)>=(8/5)Im(z)²-5000000000/n; an actual early query handles tiny imaginary parts, and polynomial divided differences remove the1/beta conditioning loss.", "evidence": "# Near-critical ruler: the whole elliptic unit-eigenvalue neighborhood\n\nStatus: complete unverified candidate. No numerical computation or new\nfact_submit is used. The entire proof is given here, rather than invoking\nthe earlier unverified parabolic result as a lemma. Accepted facts\n1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d provide the interpolation convention\nand benchmark bound only.\n\n## 1. Uniform statement, including every imaginary scale\n\nFor each m>=128 put\n\n    s=1+sqrt(2),      c=s(1+1/m),      a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    d_j=1+a c^j,      H_0=(),         H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1.\n\nThese parameters stay fixed over the whole horizon. Let h_pre be H_m\nwith its last d_0 removed. At its n queried indices0,...,n-1 put\n\n    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_pre,t z),\n    x_i=T(Re P_i(z),Im P_i(z)),\n    g_i=T(Re[zP_i(z)],Im[zP_i(z)]),\n\nwhere T is ANY invertible real2x2 matrix with ||T e1||=1. The metric,\nits conditioning, z, and the values f_i may all depend on m.\n\nSet epsilon0=10^(-6). There is an absolute integer m0 such that the\nfollowing holds for every m>=m0 and EVERY z=alpha+i beta in the fixed rectangle\n\n    |alpha-1|<=epsilon0,       |beta|<=epsilon0.           (1)\n\nIf the same queried real jets, some f_i, and the optimum(0,0,0) are fully\ninterpolable by a convex differentiable1-smooth function, then EVERY\ninterpolant F with minimum0 at0 satisfies, for every final b in[0,2],\n\n    2F(x_(n-1)-b g_(n-1))\n          <=100000 n^2 exp[-2^(m+1)/(3m)].                (2)\n\nThe ratio of this bound to the exact U_n^ZJ tends to zero. The interval\nof final steps includes the original d_0 and the common comparison step1.\nThere is no restriction tying the new endpoint gradient to the operator.\n\nIn particular this excludes a WHOLE fixed neighborhood of the natural\nunit-eigenvalue mechanism, not just beta proportional to m^(-1/2). The\nresult still does not cover frequencies outside(1), multiple modes,\nchanging generators, or unrestricted W.\n\nWe prove the stronger intermediate legality restriction, with the explicit\nconstant C=5000000000 and all large m,\n\n    1-alpha >= (8/5)beta^2-C/n.                          (3)\n\nNo fitted numerical threshold or compactness assumption on T is used.\n\n## 2. A common finite-cycle average and a uniform phase bound\n\nConjugating z and replacing T by T diag(1,-1) leaves the physical data\nunchanged, so first take beta>0. Put M=T^T T, tau=tr(M)/2, and\nB=[[alpha,-beta],[beta,alpha]]. The full partial-word polynomial is\n\n    p_j(z)=product_(ell<j)(1-d_ell z)^(2^(j-ell-1)),\n    R_j(z)=p_j(z)(1-d_j z).\n\nThe query positions of H_j in complex coordinates are precisely the subset\nproducts of R_0,...,R_(j-1): the left copy is unchanged and the right copy\nis multiplied by R_j. The first H_(m-1) lies inside h_pre, and its2^(m-2)\ncopies of H_1=(d_0) start at all subset products of R_1,...,R_(m-2).\n\nIn each copy sum the ordered interpolation inequalities on the triangle\nstar -> second query -> first query -> star. Function values cancel.\nDivide by the squared modulus of the nonzero starting multiplier. The\nresult is the base triangle evaluated in the metric R_theta^T M R_theta.\nAverage these actual inequalities over all copies. Its effective metric is\n\n    M_bar=tau I+E_M,\n    ||E_M||_op<=tau Gamma_m,\n    Gamma_m=product_(j=1)^(m-2)|cos(arg R_j(z))|.          (4)\n\nIndeed a traceless symmetric matrix[[q,r],[r,-q]], represented by q+i r,\nis multiplied under rotation conjugation by exp(+/-2i theta); binary\naveraging multiplies its modulus by |cos theta|. The traceless norm of\nthe original positive definite M is at most tau. This argument uses one\ncommon metric and is uniform over all its condition numbers.\n\nHere are bounds valid on a fixed small box around1. The inequalities\n12/5<s<17/7 imply, for m>=128,\n\n    12/5<c<5/2,       3/8<a<1/2,\n    11/8<d_0<3/2.                                    (5)\n\nFor example c<=2193/896<5/2 and a>=127/320>3/8. The upper bound a<1/2\nfollows from c>12/5. Our epsilon0 is less than1/100. Then\nalpha d_j-1>=a c^j/2. Define\n\n    phi_j=arg(1-d_j z)\n           =-pi+arctan[beta d_j/(alpha d_j-1)],\n    Phi=sum_(j>=0)2^(-j-1)phi_j=-pi+delta.\n\nThe series converges uniformly by its bounded terms and summable weights.\nThe arctangent arguments lie between beta/2 and8beta. Since arctan t lies\nbetween t/2 and t on[0,1],\n\n    beta/4<=delta<=8beta.                              (6)\n\nThe phases decrease to phi_infinity=-pi+arctan(beta/alpha), and\n\n    0<=phi_j-phi_infinity\n      <=beta/[alpha(alpha d_j-1)]<=8beta c^(-j).\n\nThe first inequality uses the1-Lipschitz property of arctan. Exact\nmultiplicities give\n\n    arg R_j=2^j Phi+epsilon_j,\n    epsilon_j=phi_j-sum_(ell>=0)2^(-ell-1)phi_(j+ell),\n    0<=epsilon_j<=8beta c^(-j).                         (7)\n\nChoose an integer k with2^k delta in[pi/4,pi/2). If1<=k<=m-2,\ndiscard the earlier factors in(4), compare each remaining |cos| with its\nunperturbed value using its1-Lipschitz property, and telescope the\ndifference of products of numbers in[0,1]. This gives\n\n    Gamma_m<=product_(j=k)^(m-2)|cos(2^j delta)|\n                                  +sum_(j=k)^(m-2)epsilon_j\n       <=1/[2^(m-1-k)|sin(2^k delta)|]\n                                  +8beta c^(-k)/(1-c^(-1)).\n\nThe unperturbed identity follows by repeated sin(2t)=2sin(t)cos(t);\nit includes exact dyadic resonances. From(6),2^k<=8/beta and\n2^(-k)<=11beta. Let r0=log_2(12/5)>5/4, the last inequality following\nfrom(12/5)^4>32. We can take C1=32 and C2=1000 in the bound\n\n    Gamma_m<= C1/(n beta)+C2 beta^(1+r0)                (8)\n\nwhenever k<=m-2. Indeed the first term is at most16sqrt(2)/(n beta),\nand the second is at most14beta c^(-k). Since11beta<1 and\n1<r0<=log_2 c<3/2, we have c^(-k)<=44beta^r0; hence1000 is a valid\nsecond constant. Here sin(2^k delta)>=1/sqrt2. Take K=2000000.\nIf beta>=K/n, the bound2^k<=8/beta ensures k<=m-2 for all large m.\nOur epsilon0 ensures k>=1. Thus(8) is valid throughout\nthe large-beta case beta>=K/n; it does not make an infinite-tail claim.\n\n## 3. The triangle estimate for beta>=K/n\n\nPut xi=1-alpha, d=d_0, v0=B e1, r1=e1-d v0 and v1=B r1. For a metric\nM the triangle weight is tr(M Z), where\n\n    Z=(v1v1^T)/2+d sym(v0v1^T)+(v0-v1)(v0-v1)^T/2\n                            -sym(e1v0^T)+(v0v0^T)/2,\n    sym(C)=(C+C^T)/2.\n\nExpanding its three edge weights gives\n\n    tr(Z)=d beta^2-(alpha-|z|^2)E,\n    E=1-d alpha+d^2|z|^2.                              (9)\n\nAt z=1, Z=0 for every d. For |z|<=2 and1<=d<=2, write w=z-1.\nThe differences of v0,r1,v1 from their z=1 values have norms at most\n|w|,2|w|,7|w|. Also||v0||<=2,||v1||<=10. Bounding the five outer or\nmixed products in Z gives respectively38.5,34,56,1,1.5 times |w|.\nThus\n\n    ||Z||_op<=132(|xi|+beta).                           (10)\n\nFor epsilon0<=1/100, E>=1 and1+d/E>=7/4. Here is a direct uniform check.\nAt alpha=1,beta=0, E0=1-d+d^2>=97/64. In the small box,\n|E-E0|<=7epsilon0. Also4d-3E0=7d-3d^2-3>=3/4 for\nd in[11/8,3/2]. Hence4d-3E>=3/4-21epsilon0>0, as required.\n\nThe averaged legal triangle gives tr(M_bar Z)<=0. Equations(4),(9),(10),\nwith |tr(E_M Z)|<=2||E_M||_op||Z||_op, imply\n\n    -xi+xi^2+(1+d/E)beta^2\n                   <=264 Gamma_m(|xi|+beta).             (11)\n\nFor beta>=K/n, (8) is at most\n32/2000000+1000epsilon0^(1+r0)<1/26400; the second term is at most10^(-9).\nWrite q=264Gamma_m<=1/100. Drop\nxi^2 in(11). If xi>=0, divide by1+q; if xi<0, divide by1-q. In both\ncases the inequalities(7/4)/(1.01)>5/3 and1/(1-q)<=2 give\n\n    xi >=(5/3)beta^2-528Gamma_m beta.\n\nSince r0>5/4, epsilon0^r0<10^(-7), so\n528C2 epsilon0^r0<.0528<1/15. Using(8),\n\n    xi >=(8/5)beta^2-C3/n,\n    C3=528C1,              beta>=K/n.                  (12)\n\nNo lower bound on the smallest eigenvalue of M was needed.\n\n## 4. Tiny imaginary parts: two actual optimum inequalities suffice\n\nAssume0<beta<=K/n. If alpha<=1, then xi>=0 and(3) follows for this\ncase with the stated C and n>=32000. Suppose alpha>1\nand write e=alpha-1>0. Put\n\n    u=g0-alpha x0=beta T e2,       V=||u||.\n\nThe initial optimum inequalities give<x0,g0>>=||g0||^2, so||g0||<=1\nand V<=3. Expanded at alpha=1+e, the same condition gives\n\n    alpha e+V^2<=-(2alpha-1)<x0,u><=3V,\n    e<=3V.                                             (13)\n\nChoose the actual prefix query index\n\n    ell=min(n-1,floor(1/(100beta))).\n\nOur epsilon0 is below1/200, so the floor is at least half its argument.\nFor large n, ell>=n/(200K). Every factor of its prefix is\n-(positive modulus) times exp(i arctan[beta h/(alpha h-1)]). By the\nsame bounds as(6), the sum theta of these small positive angles obeys\n\n    ell beta/4<=theta<=8ell beta<=.08.\n\nLet t=tan(theta)/beta. Since theta<=tan(theta)<=2theta in this interval,\n\n    ell/4<=t<=16ell.                                   (14)\n\nAfter dividing its point and gradient by their common nonzero scalar\n(-1)^ell |P_ell(z)| cos(theta), the query has vectors\n\n    X=x0+t u,\n    G=(alpha-beta^2 t)X+(1+beta^2 t^2)u.                 (15)\n\nThis is obtained by expanding real and imaginary parts of P_ell and\nzP_ell. Its optimum cocoercivity inequality remains<G,X>>=||G||^2.\n\nIf e<=beta^2 t, then(14) and ell<=1/(100beta) give\ne<=.16beta<=.16K/n. Otherwise put A0=alpha-beta^2t>1 and\nk0=1+beta^2t^2>0. Substitution of(15) into the optimum inequality gives\n\n    A0(1-A0)||X||^2+(1-2A0)k0<X,u>-k0^2 V^2>=0.\n\nThis forces<X,u><0: if it were nonnegative, all terms would be\nnonpositive, and equality would require X=u=0, which would contradict\nx0=X-tu having norm one. Hence<x0,u>+t V^2<0, so V<1/t. Equations\n(13),(14) now yield\n\n    e<3/t<=12/ell<=2400K/n.\n\nThus in every case xi>=-2400K/n. Since beta<=K/n, the choice\nC=5000000000 gives, for n>=32000,\n\n    xi >=(8/5)beta^2-C/n,              0<beta<=K/n.      (16)\n\nTogether(12),(16) prove(3) for all imaginary scales. The argument used\nactual prefix query indices; it did not invent intermediate points.\n\n## 5. Uniform damping throughout the vertical segment\n\nOur radius also satisfies512epsilon0<=1/100 and512epsilon0^2<=1/100.\nFor every0<=bta<=beta,\n\n    xi >=(8/5)bta^2-C/n.                               (17)\n\nThis inequality, once proved at the legal endpoint, is algebraically\nvalid along the whole vertical segment; no legality at other parameters\nis assumed. Write w=-xi+i bta and t_j=1+1/(a c^j)<4. The exact factor is\n|1-d_j(1+w)|=a c^j|1+t_j w|. Define\n\n    L_m(w)=log(ac)+Re sum_(j>=0)2^(-j-1)log(1+t_j w).\n\nFor |w|<=1/8 the cubic remainder in each logarithm is at most128|w|^3,\nby summing its power series after degree two. Therefore\n\n    L_m(w)=log(ac)-D_m xi+(J_m/2)bta^2-(J_m/2)xi^2+R,\n    D_m=1+1/[a(2-c^(-1))],\n    J_m=1+2/[a(2-c^(-1))]+1/[a^2(2-c^(-2))],\n    |R|<=512(|xi|^3+bta^3)<=.01|xi|+.01bta^2.           (18)\n\nThe power-sum identity behind the last remainder bound is\n(p+q)^3<=4(p^3+q^3) for p,q>=0. At c=s,a=1/s the limits are\n\n    D=2+2/(2s-1)>5/2,\n    J=6+4/(2s-1)+2/(4s+1)<15/2.\n\nThe bounds follow from12/5<s<5/2 and\n20/19+10/53<3/2. Thus for all large m,\n\n    5/2<=D_m<=4,       J_m<=15/2.\n\nPut xi_+=max(xi,0), xi_-=max(-xi,0). By(17), xi_-<=C/n and\nxi_+>=(8/5)bta^2-C/n. Dropping the nonpositive quadratic term in(18),\n\n    L_m(w)<=log(ac)-2.49xi_++3.76bta^2+4.01C/n\n           <=log(ac)-.224bta^2+6.5C/n\n           <=-1/(2m)                                  (19)\n\nfor all sufficiently large m. The last step uses ac=1-1/m and the fact\nn=2^m-1 eventually exceeds every fixed multiple of m.\n\nThe finite product has the exact expression\n\n    log|p_m(1+w)|\n      =2^m L_m(w)-log(ac)-m log c\n          -2^m Re sum_(j>=m)2^(-j-1)log(1+t_j w).\n\nThe absolute value of its last term is at most8|w|, since\n|log(1+t_j w)|<=8|w|. Equations(18),(19) yield the uniform bound\n\n    |p_m(alpha+i bta)|<=E_m:=exp[-2^m/(3m)]\n                       for EVERY0<=bta<=beta.            (20)\n\nThus no division by an extremely small beta will be needed.\n\n## 6. A divided difference eliminates the conditioning loss\n\nLet p_pre be the polynomial of the actual n-1 step prefix. Since\n|1-d_0(alpha+i bta)|>=alpha d_0-1>=a/2>=3/16,\n\n    |p_pre(alpha+i bta)|<=6E_m.                         (21)\n\nEvery scalar ratio h/[h alpha-1] is at most8. Differentiate the finite\nproduct on this root-free segment to get\n\n    |p_pre'(alpha+i bta)|\n      <=8n |p_pre(alpha+i bta)|<=48n E_m.               (22)\n\nThe coefficients of p_pre are real. Its imaginary part at alpha is\ntherefore zero, and integration of its derivative along the segment gives\n\n    |Im p_pre(z)|/beta<=48n E_m,\n    |Im[z p_pre(z)]|/beta<=102n E_m.                    (23)\n\nFor the second inequality differentiate z p_pre(z); its derivative is\np_pre+z p_pre', and |z|<=2. These bounds remain uniform as beta tends\nto zero arbitrarily fast.\n\nUse u=g0-alpha x0=beta T e2 with||u||<=3. The physical endpoint vectors\nare exactly\n\n    x_(n-1)=Re p_pre(z) x0+[Im p_pre(z)/beta]u,\n    g_(n-1)=Re[zp_pre(z)]x0+[Im[zp_pre(z)]/beta]u.\n\nEquations(21)--(23) imply\n\n    ||x_(n-1)||<=150n E_m,       ||g_(n-1)||<=320n E_m.  (24)\n\nThe operator T itself need not have bounded norm or conditioning.\n\nFor beta=0, the physical data are x_i=P_i(alpha)x0 and g_i=alpha x_i.\nInitial optimum interpolation forces0<=alpha<=1. Hence(17) holds with\nbta=0, the real version of(20)--(21) follows, and(24) holds directly.\nNegative beta was reduced to positive beta in Section2. This covers all(1).\n\n## 7. Every completion and the exact benchmark\n\nFor any interpolant F of the actual queried data, optimum interpolation\nat its last query gives f<=<x,g>-||g||^2/2. For b in[0,2], integrating\nthe1-Lipschitz gradient along x-tg gives\n\n    F(x-bg)<=f-b(1-b/2)||g||^2<=f.\n\nThus(24) gives\n\n    2F(x-bg)<=2||x||||g||\n       <=96000 n^2 E_m^2\n       <=100000 n^2 exp[-2^(m+1)/(3m)],\n\nwhich proves(2), including maximal free terminal completion. Accepted\nb98bb807d514e80d gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3). Multiply(2) by this bound.\nIts logarithmic positive terms are O(m), whereas2^m/m grows faster\nthan m, so the ratio to U_n^ZJ tends to zero.\n\nThe explicit constants epsilon0=10^(-6), C1=32, C2=1000,\nK=2000000 and C=5000000000 satisfy all displayed restrictions.\nOnly a sufficiently large absolute starting horizon m0 remains to be\nchosen for the convergent coefficient bounds and exponential comparisons.\nNo horizon-dependent radius or condition-number bound is hidden in the statement.\n\nThe result closes the entire near-unit elliptic mechanism on the assigned\nconstant-level diagnostic. A positive elliptic witness, if one exists,\nmust use other frequencies. Low-frequency behavior and other parts of\nthe complex plane still require a quantitative full-cycle analysis.\n\nMatlas retrieval returned unrelated statements in arXiv:1304.7921 and\n2101.11386; neither is used. No\nexternal theorem is a premise of this proof.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max2_near_critical_full_unit_neighborhood", "status": "Complete unverified quantitative candidate", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/near_critical_full_unit_neighborhood.md", "guidance": "0150cd2519656ebc", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "extends_finding": "295385c862b91a07", "related_findings": ["4a6bed048e4235d9", "892ca165436ef59c", "102610bc6e0e464e"], "scope": "Whole fixed unit-eigenvalue rectangle, all frequencies/metrics/values/completions inside it; other complex frequencies and unrestricted W remain open.", "new_mechanism": "Combine actual-copy triangle averaging for beta>=2000000/n with two actual optimum inequalities for beta<=2000000/n.
