{"id": "f8b4d9f258ab98c0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:43:29.206761+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C34 explicit correlated simplex family closed quantitatively: every dyadic-depth coordinate-path relay on the near-critical H_m has optimized objective <=global Huber K, with strict inequality for every multitransition relay. Exact all-normal inequalities, closest-minimizer cost, objective and primitive defect formulas are included; no unrestricted W upper bound.", "evidence": "# C34. Exact coordinate-path projection relay and its near-critical obstruction\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No verifier or numerical solver was used.\nThis is an exact obstruction for the defined coordinate-flow family, not an\nupper bound on unrestricted W and not a primitive-capacity theorem.\n\n## 1. Actual correlated real vectors and the whole trajectory\n\nPartition a nonnegative schedule into N ordinary blocks of masses S_i,\nseparated by checkpoints b_i>1, 0<=i<N-1. Each ordinary block can be an\narbitrary ordered list with that mass. Set M_i=S_i+1, L_i=b_i-1 and\nT_i=S_i+b_i=M_i+L_i for i<N-1. Choose an artificial terminal parameter\nT_(N-1)>=M_(N-1). Let e_0,...,e_(N-1) be the standard orthonormal basis.\n\nChoose alpha_i>0, beta_i>=0 for i<N-1, with beta_(N-1)=0, and impose\n\n    T_(i+1) alpha_(i+1)=T_i beta_i.\n\nFor a nonzero terminal payoff all beta_i must be positive. Define\n\n    p_i=alpha_i e_i-beta_i e_(i+1)  (i<N-1),\n    p_(N-1)=alpha_(N-1)e_(N-1),\n    C=conv(0,p_0,...,p_(N-1)),\n    F(x)=max_{p in C} (<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nThe strict concavity of the maximization gives its unique maximizer P_C(x).\nThe projection variational inequality proves\n||P_C(x)-P_C(z)||^2<=<P_C(x)-P_C(z),x-z>, hence F is convex and\ndifferentiable with 1-Lipschitz gradient P_C. Differentiability follows by\nbounding the two maximization differences between the corresponding linear\nforms and then using the displayed Lipschitz estimate. F>=0 and F(0)=0.\n\nStart at x_init=T_0 alpha_0 e_0. At phase i, after ordinary partial mass\nu in [0,S_i], prescribe\n\n    x_i(u)=(T_i-u)alpha_i e_i+u beta_i e_(i+1).\n\nAt the checkpoint, subtracting b_i p_i gives T_i beta_i e_(i+1), which is\nthe next prescribed start. Thus the SAME whole GD trajectory is realized\nif P_C(x_i(u))=p_i at every actual partial mass.\n\nWrite y_i(u)=x_i(u)-p_i. Its two coordinates are\n(T_i-u-1)alpha_i and (u+1)beta_i, both nonnegative. Put\n\n    delta_i(u)=<y_i(u),p_i>\n              =(T_i-u-1)alpha_i^2-(u+1)beta_i^2.\n\nFor j<=i-2 and j>=i+2, <y_i(u),p_j>=0. For j=i-1 this inner\nproduct is -beta_(i-1)(T_i-u-1)alpha_i<=0. For j=i+1 it is\n(u+1)beta_i alpha_(i+1). The comparison with zero is 0<=delta_i(u).\n\nConsequently ALL normals against ALL vertices and zero hold if and only if\n\n    L_i alpha_i^2-M_i beta_i^2\n                    >= M_i beta_i alpha_(i+1),     i<N-1,       (1)\n\ntogether with T_(N-1)>=M_(N-1). Necessity uses the actual last query in\neach ordinary block, including an empty block's only query. Sufficiency\nuses the fact that delta_i(u)-(u+1)beta_i alpha_(i+1) decreases with u.\nThe terminal phase has only its nonnegative own normal and nonpositive\ncomparison with its preceding vertex. Thus no remote condition is omitted.\n\nUsing the transfer relation, (1) is precisely\n\n    (beta_i/alpha_i)^2\n          <= L_i T_(i+1)/[M_i(T_i+T_(i+1))].                    (2)\n\nThe term M_i beta_i^2 in (1) records the mass already accumulated in the\nnext coordinate during the ordinary block. Replacing it by beta_i^2\nwould give a false, much stronger relay.\n\nThe correlations are explicit:\n<p_i,p_(i+1)>=-beta_i alpha_(i+1)<0, all nonadjacent correlations zero.\nThe p_i are linearly independent because their coordinate matrix is\ntriangular with positive diagonal. Thus C is an actual N-simplex,\nnot a formal Gram matrix.\n\n## 2. Actual normalization and exact optimal payoff\n\nThe minimizer set is C^polar={z:<p_i,z><=0 for all i}: these inequalities make every maximand nonpositive, while if <p_i,z>>0, a sufficiently small positive multiple of p_i in C gives a positive maximand. The terminal\ninequality forces z_(N-1)<=0. Backward substitution then forces every\ncoordinate z_i<=0. Therefore 0 is a closest minimizer to x_init, whose\ntrue squared distance to the minimizer set is T_0^2 alpha_0^2.\n\nAt the final point, with D=2S_(N-1)+1,\n\n    2F_end / dist(x_init,argmin F)^2\n      = (2T_(N-1)-D)/T_(N-1)^2\n                         product_(i=0)^(N-2)(beta_i/alpha_i)^2. (3)\n\nFor fixed T_(N-1), equality in every bound (2) maximizes this expression.\nIt gives legal positive parameters recursively from any alpha_0>0. Thus\nthe exact optimum for this family is\n\n    E(T)=(2T-D)/T^2\n        product_(i=0)^(N-2) [L_i T_(i+1)/(M_i(T_i+T_(i+1)))].   (4)\n\nFor N>=2 let C_last=T_(N-2). All T dependence in (4), up to a positive\nconstant, is\n\n    phi(T)=(2T-D)/[T(T+C_last)],  T>=M_(N-1).\n\nIts derivative has numerator -2T^2+2DT+D C_last. Therefore its unique\nmaximum occurs at\n\n    T_opt=(D+sqrt(D^2+2D C_last))/2 >= D >= M_(N-1),\n\nand\n\n    phi(T_opt)=2/[D+C_last+sqrt(D^2+2D C_last)].\n\nFor N=1, (3) is maximized at T=D and gives 1/D, the global Huber value.\n\nThe primitive defect is tracked on the same normalized trajectory. Let s be\nthe mass of the ENTIRE schedule and let\nV=2 sum_t h_t(F_t-F_end)+sum_t h_t(1-h_t)||g_t||^2\n  -s(s+1)||g_end||^2.\nAt every projection query F_t=<x_t,g_t>-||g_t||^2/2, so\n2h_t F_t+h_t(1-h_t)||g_t||^2\n=||x_t||^2-||x_(t+1)||^2. Telescoping gives\n\n    V=||x_init||^2-||x_end+s g_end||^2,\n\nand therefore, with T=T_(N-1),\n\n    V/dist(x_init,argmin F)^2\n      =1-[(T-S_(N-1)+s)/T]^2\n                   product_(i<N-1)(beta_i/alpha_i)^2.           (5a)\n\nEquations (3) and (5a) report BOTH the normalized objective and the normalized\nprimitive defect; neither is inferred from the sign of the other.\n\n## 3. Every dyadic-depth version fails on the near-critical word\n\nFix m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\nc=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, and\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j).\nLet S_j be its mass and M_j=S_j+1. Direct geometric summation gives\n\n    M_j=A c^j+(1-A)2^j,  A=a/(c-2).\n\nHere 0<A<1: c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1 while ac=1-1/m<1.\nAlso 2<c<49/20 and c-2<9/20. For example sqrt(2)<99/70 gives\nlambda<169/70, and (169/70)(129/128)<49/20.\n\nChoose any r in [0,m-1]. Partition H_m into N=2^k copies of H_r, where\nk=m-r, separated by its actual higher-level checkpoints. Then all M_i=M=M_r\nand\n\n    L_i=a c^(r+nu_2(i+1)),  0<=i<N-1,\n    q=a c^r/M <= c-2 < 9/20,\n    T_i=M(1+q c^(nu_2(i+1))).\n\nHere nu_2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing the positive integer j.\nIn particular the first and last nonterminal T_i equal T_0=M(1+q).\nPut D=2M-1. Exact counting of binary valuations gives\n\n    product_(i<N-1)(L_i/M)=q^(N-1)c^(N-k-1)\n                         =(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k).                   (5)\n\nFor internal transitions i<N-2, every checkpoint of level ell>=1\nis flanked by two level0 checkpoints. Therefore\n\n    product_(i<N-2)(1+T_(i+1)/T_i)\n      = product_(ell=1)^(k-1)\n          [(T_0+T_ell)^2/(T_0 T_ell)]^(2^(k-ell-1))\n      >= 2^(N-2),                                           (6)\n\nwhere T_ell=M(1+q c^ell). Each bracket is at least4 by\n(T_0-T_ell)^2>=0. There are (N-2)/2 brackets, with multiplicity.\n\nTelescoping the T_(i+1)/T_i factors in (4) and retaining the terminal\nfactor gives the exact formula\n\n    E_opt = phi(T_opt) (qc)^(N-1)c^(-k) /\n            product_(ell=1)^(k-1)\n             [(T_0+T_ell)^2/(T_0 T_ell)]^(2^(k-ell-1)).        (7)\n\nIn particular phi(T)<= (2T-D)/T^2 <=1/D, so\n\n    E_opt <= [2/(D c^k)] (qc/2)^(N-1).                       (8)\n\nLet K=1/(2M_m-1). From the formula for M_j and q<=c-2,\n\n    M_m=M[(1-q/(c-2))2^k+(q/(c-2))c^k] <=M c^k.\n\nConsequently for k>=2,\n\n    E_opt/K <= 4 (qc/2)^(N-1)\n             <4 (441/800)^(N-1)\n             <=4 (441/800)^3 <1.                            (9)\n\nEvery genuinely multitransition dyadic-depth relay in this family is thus\nstrictly below K, uniformly in its free scalar parameters. If k tends to\ninfinity, its ratio to K tends to0 at least geometrically in 2^k.\n\nFor completeness k=1 is also below K. Set epsilon=1/M in (0,1]. The exact\ntwo-phase optimum divided by K is\n\n    2q[2(2+q)-epsilon] /\n     [3+q-epsilon+sqrt((2-epsilon)(4+2q-epsilon))].\n\nIt is bounded above by\n\n    R(q)=4q(2+q)/[2+q+sqrt(3+2q)].\n\nThis function increases for q>=0: write it as\n4q/[1+sqrt(3+2q)/(2+q)]; the ratio in the denominator decreases,\nsince its derivative has numerator -(1+q). Therefore\n\n    R(q)<R(9/20)=441/[245+10sqrt(390)]<1;\n\nthe last inequality follows from 196^2<39000.\n\nAllowing r=m gives the single-phase Huber construction and attains K.\nThus the optimal payoff of the UNION of all these dyadic-depth incidence\nrelays is exactly K. This does not claim that all simplex walks, all\ncorrelated walks, or all smooth-convex functions have payoff at most K.\n\n## 4. Why the obstruction is useful and what it does not close\n\nThe family is legal in dimension N with every old/future normal retained,\nuses N distinct mutually overlapping gradients, and has an exact\nclosest-minimizer calculation. Its failure is quantitative; no translated\nnegative-energy sign is used. The penalty in (6) comes from adjacent\ncoordinate transfer and survives the near-critical perturbation qc=1+O(1/m).\n\nA tree transfer can avoid following N consecutive fresh coordinates, but it\nmust retain waiting coordinates. Every waiting future vertex then contributes\na positive normal comparison throughout the intervening phases. That is a\ndifferent construction class, not covered by (9).\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["e865afe9e0f7093c", "bf591e52671f6d33"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "research/c34_coordinate_path_nearcritical_obstruction.md"}, "glossary": {"E_path": "Optimized normalized last-iterate objective of the explicit coordinate-path projection family defined in the evidence.", "p_path_i": "Triangular incidence normal alpha_i e_i-beta_i e_(i+1); the terminal beta is zero.", "T_path_i": "Total phase transfer mass S_i+b_i, with a freely optimized artificial terminal parameter.", "nu_2": "Exponent of2 in a positive integer; used to describe the chronological ruler checkpoints."}}
{"id": "05f35c4b34104f68", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:44:52.455236+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Independent focused attack on the EXACT frozen C33 proof6306bdd00914df38: no mathematical gap or counterexample found. This is NOT verifier acceptance. Probe final usage remains0 new calls/0 seconds.", "evidence": "# Independent focused attack on frozen C33\n\nWorker max4. Result: no mathematical gap or counterexample found in the\nspecified finished object. This is an independent-check report, NOT a\nverifier verdict or a Danus fact. No fact_submit was called.\n\n## Exact object and scope\n\nI read the frozen public file\n`/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/6306bdd00914df38.md`\nin full. Its SHA256 is\n`63d42bba86dd4d19fd70bfe37a207af24dcd273fb8f4fd0065f26592c49c369b`,\nmatching TASK.md. I did not change that file or replace its Section 5 by a\ndifferent argument. Its public finding is 6306bdd00914df38.\n\nThe checked conclusion is exactly the global THREE-step capacity and unique\nSilver equality statement, together with the negative-witness extension\nthat makes all incoming-terminal residuals zero. The report does not infer\nan all-length result, general primitive-cone re-selection, objective-profile\noptimality, or sufficiency of arbitrary negative credits at Silver.\n\n## Witness legality, all directions and degenerate schedules\n\nWith y_i=x_i-g_i and the assigned values f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2,\ndirect expansion gives Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>. The optimum incoming constraints\nare equalities; the outgoing constraints are precisely <g_i,y_i>>=0.\nThe terminal condition x_3=g_3 gives every Q_(i,3)=0. Squared-distance\ntelescoping gives V_h=||x_0||^2-T^2||g_3||^2. I checked these identities\nwith the stated orientation Q_ij<=0.\n\nThe selected-cut clipped derivative is one fixed continuous, nondecreasing,\n1-Lipschitz derivative. For b>1, the query before the selected step is1,\nand the last query is -(b-1)/(Q+1), its negative threshold. Every intervening\npartial sum lies between0 and the corresponding full nonnegative sum, so\nevery asserted constant-gradient segment is legal. Zero ordinary steps\nonly duplicate identical jets. For b<=1 the cut inequality is automatic;\nno zero denominator or nonzero-threshold construction is invoked.\n\nThe quadratic witness is globally legal. The one-sided Huber witness uses\nthe explicitly proved condition r(w-1)<=1; its last positive point is in\nthe quadratic region, including the endpoint case. The global box proves\nthis condition strictly before that witness is used to exclude orientations.\n\nFor the mixed witness, I checked all finite residuals by their target normal.\nThe base gradients and normals are\n(1,1,-eta,-eta) and (u,0,-w eta,0). Its only nonzero residuals are\n\n Q_base,20=Q_base,30=-(1+eta)u,\n Q_base,02=Q_base,12=-(1+eta)w eta.\n\nThe auxiliary gradients and normals are\n(1,-a,eta a,eta a) and (0,0,w eta a,0). Its only nonzero residuals are\n\n Q_aux,02=(1-eta a)w eta a,\n Q_aux,12=-a^2(1+eta)w eta.\n\nThe chosen positive epsilon cancels Q_02 exactly and cannot make any other\nfinite edge positive. At the optimum, the base gradient-normal pairings are\n(u,0,w eta^2,0), and the auxiliary pairings are\n(0,0,w eta^2 a^2,0). Thus both optimum directions hold as stated. All GD\nupdates, terminal equality and energy scaling hold componentwise. The\nfinite interpolation construction in accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, proof\nSection2, applies to the ENTIRE checked finite array plus its zero optimum\njet. No remote constraint is omitted and no adaptive interpolant is needed.\n\n## Global domain and equality checks\n\nThe initial cut inequalities apply to every nonnegative schedule before\ndividing by u-1 or assuming a positive middle bridge. The proof then excludes\nv<=1, obtains T<7 and u,w<7/4, and excludes either endpoint<=1. Thus every\nsubsequent division by a, eta, D or 1-eta a occurs only in the proved positive\ndomain. This handles all zero-step and unit-step boundary cases.\n\nI checked the two upper-bound polynomials and their monotonicities, the\nstrict numerical inequalities in (11), and the derivation u+w>=2t.\nFor u<=t, the comparison in (13) is strict unless u=w=t,T=T_0, which then\nforces v=2. For u,w>=t, separate strict concavity places the minimum at the\nlisted rectangle corners; at T_0 only (t,t) attains1, and all corners\nincrease with T. Positivity of every factor and of both displayed derivatives\nholds throughout the stated range. This excludes mixed interior/edge equality\ncases too. No reversal symmetry is used.\n\nFor u>t>w, F_N decreases with r at fixed endpoints. The displayed polynomial\nat r=1 has positive leading coefficient, negative constant term, and negative\nvalue at lambda; its unique positive root is beyond lambda. This really\nforces v<2. I checked the expansion of that polynomial and of F_N/u.\n\n## Original Section 5, including every polynomial identity\n\nThe replacement T->max(T_0,u/(2-u)) weakens precisely the needed inequalities:\nF_N decreases with T and T a(T-A-D)(D-2) increases, while a>0,D>2.\nThe reduced r=T-A-D remains positive by the explicit lower bound t-3/4.\nThis is an algebraic weakening of necessary inequalities, not a claim that\nthe modified schedule has an existing certificate. No such claim is used.\n\nI separately derived N-kappa Q=ell D+c_0 from the quadratic coefficients.\nIn particular the coefficient simplification is\n(u-1)(u+1)^2+u(2-u^2)=u^2+u-1. All claimed positive factors are positive:\nT>A, K>2, a>0, B>0, ell>0. Thus N>=0,Q<=0 correctly implies D>=-c_0/ell.\nQ' is positive for every D<=lambda, including any smaller D_L, because\nK+2-2lambda>=2-u>0. This justifies the comparison even without presuming\nthat D_L lies inside the original feasible interval.\n\nI checked the exact switch M_0=1+t/2, the overlap of the two subintervals,\nand the strict lower endpoint u>t. On the second interval, q=2-u>0,\nell_num=q^3 ell>0, Y=pq ell_num>0, and every denominator being cleared is\nnonzero and positive. The inclusions [M_0,7/4] subset [5/3,2] are valid.\n\nUsing only a fixed, tiny exact coefficient calculation over Q and Q(sqrt2),\nI independently expanded and matched:\n\n1. Identity (18), including all nine supplied P_8 coefficients.\n2. The degree-eight Bernstein identity, including ALL nine beta coefficients.\n3. Identity (19), including the full degree-thirteen factor and all cleared\n   factors in the degree-twenty-one polynomial.\n4. The degree-twelve Bernstein identity for 29229255 P_13', including ALL\n   thirteen listed integer coefficients and the affine interval map.\n5.
